\begin{minipage}{0.86\textwidth}\small
\textbf{Status.} AI-assisted research manuscripts, unrefereed, and not formalized in a proof assistant. The language theorem of Part~I is conditional on precisely pinned imports from the Hilbert's-tenth research tree of this repository; the theorems of Parts~III and~IV are conditional on, or inherit, pinned native-source theorems as each Part states; Part~V inherits the compiler contract and the sign-free bootstrap as premises, and its exact predicate decides neither existence nor emptiness of a negative zero. Parts~VI--VIII inherit the same compiler contract; Part~VI's existence theorems rest on two estimates from the literature and on an analytic argument that the programme's review did not recertify, and the two candidates of Part~VIII are refuted, so no Part lowers the research tree's 84-operation record. The exact-degree theorems of Parts~I and~II are unconditional algebra on the stated sources. Section~\ref{fup:fm:sec:status} lists what is and is not claimed.

\medskip
\textbf{Merge.} Text marked \wtag\ was written when the eleven manuscripts were merged (five in batch~82, Part~V in batch~83, Parts~VI--VIII in batch~88); all other text is the source's own. Section~\ref{fup:fm:sec:provenance} gives the provenance.
\end{minipage}
\vfill
\end{titlepage}

\section{\wtag\ What this report contains}\label{fup:fm:sec:contents}
This report prints eleven research manuscripts that share one object: the fixed universal Diophantine polynomials of the Hilbert's-tenth research programme in this repository (\rw{Computability/HilbertTenthProblem/Papers/research-wip/native-stream-queue/}, below \emph{the research tree}) and the native Pell arithmetic block inside them. They share one spine: first the exact resources and exact degree of one literal instance, then the structure of the witness fibers of the native block. The sixth manuscript, Part~V, was added in batch~83; it continues the last appendix of Part~IV. Five more, Parts~VI--VIII, were added in batch~88: they continue Part~V and test cheaper candidates for the research tree's universal polynomial, whose record is now 84 operations.
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Part~\ref{fup:part:gp}} (Report~23, revision~1): one literal Grill program with 397,488 run phases and one complete integer polynomial circuit with an ordinary positive input, five program parameters fixed for each represented language, 797,135 positive witnesses and 3,600,546 binary operations. Its language theorem is conditional on pinned imports; its exact total degree 69,339,973 is proved unconditionally.
\item \textbf{Part~\ref{fup:part:dl}} (Report~24): for every nonempty run table of the same native template, with $N=2m+g+5$, the native unit has exact degree $63N-4$, the largest residual $12N+10$, and the complete native polynomial $87N+16$; residual gluing, recoder and loader maxima, and fresh polynomial inputs. Part~I's degree is the instance $N=797{,}011$.
\item \textbf{Part~\ref{fup:part:nf}} (Report~25): the entire positive fiber of the retained twenty-two-coordinate native component at fixed ports: seventeen coordinates forced, five parametrized by two Pell indices, and the two-term height law with a $\zeta(1/2)$ second term.
\item \textbf{Part~\ref{fup:part:ir}} (Reports~33 and~34): the complete74 signed19 index-eliminated child has infinitely many positive zeros with negative restored index at every positive input (Report~33), and the height and counting law of one canonical family of these zeros, with a Kesten-type obstruction to a smooth second term (Report~34).
\item \textbf{Part~\ref{fup:part:nz}} (Report~37): for the two positive interfaces raw29 and positive21 that Report~33 leaves open, an exact necessary-and-sufficient arithmetic predicate for a full positive zero with negative restored index, with explicit positive reconstruction of every witness in both interfaces, a finite wrapped odd-input sector, sharper even-input bounds and a certified sub-unit Binet window. Whether the predicate ever passes is open.
\item \textbf{Part~\ref{fup:part:nr}} (Report~39): for every valid compiler and input, infinitely many positive tuples satisfying every condition of Part~V's predicate except the main congruence, a fixed-modulus Jacobi filter for that congruence, and a thinned progression on which it fails; hence the raw29 main projection and the positive21 main norm are nonredundant comparisons. No full negative zero is constructed; Part~V's question stays open.
\item \textbf{Part~\ref{fup:part:fd}} (Report~41): deleting the first-index quotient from the research tree's 85- and 86-operation universal polynomials gives 81- and 82-operation candidates that accept every positive input on every genuine compiler slice. The tree has since carried the construction to its 84-operation polynomial (an 80-operation candidate, also refuted).
\item \textbf{Part~\ref{fup:part:cand}} (Reports~43, 45 and~46): two reductions of the 84-operation polynomial. For the free-coefficient 83 candidate, structural divisor obstructions and nonnative inner families (Report~43; its open language question has since been answered by the research tree, which refutes the candidate); for the square/product 82 candidate, a full counterfamily at every input (Report~45, a second construction of a refutation the tree also proved); and the exact height and fixed-outer minimality of Report~45's certificates, with an even-rank obstruction to extending them to the free-coefficient candidate (Report~46).
\end{itemize}

\section{\wtag\ Sources, editions and provenance}\label{fup:fm:sec:provenance}
\subsection{The manuscripts}
All seven archives of this cluster arrived in commit \texttt{db37d18c8} (3 October 2026, ``New research reports'') under \rw{docs/incoming/}; they were placed by commit \texttt{2f58ab4e9} (batch~82, cluster M3), which retired the archives. The manuscripts are numbered ``Research Report NN'' by their pipeline; the batch numbers are the manuscript numbers of batch~82. Report~37 arrived later, in commit \texttt{3051d1446} (batch~83, manuscript~09), and was placed by commit \texttt{a51a439cd} (cluster 83H2) as Part~V; its local source number, 15, continues this report's own sequence after the highest prefix, 14. Table~\ref{fup:fm:tab:sources} lists them. Each manuscript records its own pins, which are the commits of this repository whose research-tree files it imports or cites; they are kept here as provenance. \emph{(Batch~88.)} Reports~39, 41, 43, 45 and~46 arrived in commit \texttt{c5612efa1} (3 October 2026, 21:05~PDT, ``New research reports'', ten archives, manuscripts 01--10 of batch~88; they are manuscripts 01, 02, 05, 06 and~08) and were placed by commit \texttt{992aaafb3} (cluster~88T2), which retired their archives, as sources~16--20, continuing this report's own sequence after its highest prefix, 15. The other five archives of that arrival, Reports~40, 42, 44, 47 and~48 on a periodic Langton ant, went to \rw{periodic-turmite-first-revisits} (cluster~88T1).

\paragraph{What each contributed.} Source~11 is the base: its \rw{article.tex} and \rw{README.md} were staged as delivered, and its text is Part~I. Sources~14, 07, 10 and~05 contributed their manuscripts (Parts~II--IV) and their code, data and audit files; their delivered READMEs and PDFs are not shipped. Source~15 (Report~37) contributed its manuscript, printed as Part~V, and its code, data and audit files; its delivered README and PDF are not shipped. Sources~16--20 (Reports~39, 41, 43, 45 and~46, batch~88) contributed their manuscripts, printed as Parts~VI, VII and~VIII, and their code, data and audit files; their delivered READMEs and PDFs are not shipped. Every result, proof, remark, question and limitation of the eleven manuscripts is printed.

\paragraph{The edition of Report~23.} Two editions of Report~23 arrived. Archive~11 is revision~1: its \rw{11-grill-poly-REVISION.md} calls it ``a versioned extension of Report~23'' and anchors the SHA-256 digests of the original PDF, LaTeX source, release inventory and of archive~12 itself (\texttt{467e2b47\ldots bf9b}, measured equal). Revision~1 changes no gate, coefficient, witness or comparison; it replaces the original's propagated upper bound 71,731,007 by the exact degree 69,339,973 (new Section~\ref{fup:gp:sec:exactdegree}) and keeps the original's QA files as \rw{qa/v0-*}. Archive~12 is therefore superseded and nothing of it is staged. Its one open question, ``Can the true degree be determined?'', is answered by Section~\ref{fup:gp:sec:exactdegree} and is not printed as open; revision~1 replaces it by ``Can a different representation reduce the exact degree?''.

\paragraph{The series citation graph.} Report~24 cites Report~23 revision~1 (its optional full regression reads that report's arithmetic DAG). Report~25 is the sequel of Report~22, ``Canonical outer histories and infinite native fibers'' (archive \rw{Canonical_Histories_and_Infinite_Fibers_Package.zip} of batch~82, cluster~M2, placed by commit \texttt{7d2b1b245} as source~23 of the collection report \rw{signal-machine-collision-certificates}, in its new Part~VIII); it uses only Report~22's entire-fiber projection theorem, in the corollary of Section~\ref{fup:nf:sec:transport}. Report~34 cites Reports~33 and~25 and carried their LaTeX sources, byte-identical to Parts~III and~IV, as context (not staged). Reports~23, 24, 25, 33 and~34 all pin research-tree commits. Report~37 continues Report~33's Appendix~\ref{fup:ir:app:raw}, cites its reduction as ``prior'', and carried two of Report~33's evidence files as context (byte copies, shipped once under \texttt{10-index-restore-}); it does not cite Reports~23--25 or~34. The sibling Report~32 of the same batch, a fixed universal matrix semigroup, is printed as Part~V of \rw{group-theoretic-substrates}; it mentions Report~23 only in its delivered audit. \emph{(Batch~88.)} Report~39 continues Report~37 and carried its manuscript as context (a byte copy, not shipped). Report~41 cites Reports~33, 37 and~39. Report~43 cites Report~41; Report~45 cites Reports~41 and~43; Report~46 imports Report~45's construction and Report~43's interface and small-norm lemma, and carried Report~45's complete release and a selection of Report~43's sources (byte copies, shipped once). All five pin research-tree commits.

\paragraph{Where the merge had to choose.}
\begin{enumerate}
\item The edition of Report~23 (above).
\item Placement of Report~25. Report~25 continues Report~22, which goes to \rw{signal-machine-collision-certificates}; Report~25 is printed here because its mathematics is purely the native Pell block that Parts~I, II and~IV also use, and it uses Report~22 in one corollary only.
\item Part~IV prints two manuscripts: Report~34 is a direct sequel of Report~33, studies one family inside Report~33's construction, and shipped Report~33 as its context.
\item Numbering. Every source keeps its own numbering, prefixed by its Report number: statement $k.m$ of Report~NN is printed as NN.$k.m$, its Section~$k$ as Section~NN.$k$, its equation~($k$) as (NN.$k$), and its Appendix~X as Section~NN.X. Tables are numbered consecutively through the whole report. Front-matter sections are numbered 0.$k$.
\item Bibliographies. Each source's bibliography is printed at the end of its own Part, with the source's numbering; entries naming a sibling Report gained a \wtag\ pointer to its place.
\item Macros. Only macro names changed, never renderings: Report~24's \verb|\sha| and \verb|\fname| are \verb|\shasmall| and \verb|\fnameB|; Report~33's \verb|\MF| (the native mask $\MFnat$) is \verb|\MFnat| and its \verb|\MFS| (the paid mask $\MFsrc$) is \verb|\MFsrc|; Report~34's \verb|\MF|, which denotes the paid mask, is \verb|\MFsrc|. No mathematical symbol was renamed.
\item Labels. Every label carries the prefix \texttt{fup:}; the labels of Reports~23, 24, 25, 33 and~34 carry \texttt{fup:gp:}, \texttt{fup:dl:}, \texttt{fup:nf:}, \texttt{fup:ir:} and \texttt{fup:hc:} before their delivered names, and the front matter uses \texttt{fup:fm:}. Report~37's labels carry \texttt{fup:nz:} (batch~83).
\item \emph{(Batch 83.)} Placement of Report~37. It continues Appendix~\ref{fup:ir:app:raw} of Report~33, but it is printed as its own Part~V rather than as a third source of Part~IV, because Part~IV is about the signed19 child and Report~37 treats only raw29 and positive21. Its Section~\ref{fup:nz:sec:prior} restates Appendix~33.B and the research tree's refinement note, as the source itself says; it is printed in full (its equations are cited later) with a \wtag\ note giving the correspondence, and with no novelty claim. Its re-proof of Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:projection} and its use of the tree's auxiliary construction are marked where they occur.
\item \emph{(Batch 83.)} Report~37's macros \verb|\MF| and \verb|\MFnative| are \verb|\MFsrcV| and \verb|\MFnatV| here; their renderings $\MFsrcV$ and $\MFnatV$ are kept and denote the masks written $\MFsrc$ and $\MFnat$ in Part~IV. Unlike the batch-82 merge, Part~V renames some mathematical symbols of its source, to keep one meaning per letter inside the Part; the note at the start of Part~V lists every rename.
\item \emph{(Batch 88.)} Placement. The five manuscripts are additions to this report, the only report of the collection that treats these interfaces and the programme's operation record. They form three Parts in report order: Report~39 continues Part~V (Part~VI); Report~41 is a failure of index elimination like Part~IV, for the 85- and 86-operation parents (Part~VII); and Reports~43, 45 and~46 form one Part, because Report~46 imports both of the others (Part~VIII, after the precedent of Part~IV).
\item \emph{(Batch 88.)} Duplicates. Report~45's main theorem was also proved by the research tree, by a different construction, about forty minutes later and without either text citing the other; Report~45 is printed whole as a second route, with the timing stated and no priority asserted. The small-norm lemma is proved in Reports~43 and~46; both proofs are printed, the second marked as such. The odd-quotient, projection and Pell-growth lemmas that several sources re-prove are printed in each, with pointers to Report~33's Lemmas~\ref{fup:ir:lem:projection} and~\ref{fup:ir:lem:oddquotient} and no novelty claim; Report~39's restatement of Part~V's predicate and reconstruction is printed as a recap with pointers; Report~46's recap of Report~45's construction is printed as delivered.
\item \emph{(Batch 88.)} Macros and symbols. The sources' \verb|\MF|, \verb|\MFnative| and \verb|\MFo| are \verb|\MFsrcV| and \verb|\MFnatV|, their \verb|\ya| is \verb|\yaux| and their \verb|\Pos| is \verb|\pos|, all with unchanged renderings. Report~46's factor names $N_{\mathrm a}$ and $N_{\mathrm s}$ are printed $\Na$ and $\Ns$, the renderings of Reports~43 and~45 for the same factors; this is the one rendering change. Report~45's two input files (its pin table and row list) are printed in place. Unlike Part~V, Parts~VI--VIII rename no mathematical symbol; their opening notes and Table~\ref{fup:cand:tab:letters} list the letters that collide.
\item \emph{(Batch 88.)} Stale statements. Report~41's 85-operation record, Report~43's open language question and Report~45's repetition of it, and a sentence in Report~39's first-stage evidence README are kept as delivered, with dated \wtag\ notes; none of them is a retraction of a theorem.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Not shipped.} PDFs, checksum ledgers and release seals, the non-base manuscripts and delivery READMEs, byte copies of research-tree files (73 members of the five archives; they match the current files byte for byte and are cited by path), in-archive and cross-archive byte copies (shipped once), Report~34's context copies of Reports~25 and~33, two heavy regenerable files of Report~23 (the 61,209,290-byte arithmetic DAG and the 1,589,952-byte run table; the README gives the rebuild and the retrieval), and the third-party copies of the Cocke--Minsky (1964) and Neary--Woods (2009) papers that archive~11 contained (PDFs, page scans and text extractions; they are cited in Part~I's bibliography~\cite{fup:gp:NW,fup:gp:CM} and are not redistributed). All of these survive in the arrival commit. From Report~37's archive (source~15, batch~83) the manuscript, delivery README and PDF, its two checksum ledgers, three in-archive duplicate receipts, and seven byte copies of repository files (five research-tree files and two of Report~33's evidence files, cited by path) are not shipped; they survive in its arrival commit \texttt{3051d1446}. From the five archives of batch~88 (sources~16--20) the manuscripts, delivery READMEs and seven PDFs (including the copies of Reports~43 and~45 inside Report~46's archive), 20 checksum ledgers, 63 byte copies of research-tree files (cited by path), 40 in-batch byte copies (Report~46's copy of the complete Report~45 release and of Report~43's sources, and Report~39's copy of the Report~37 manuscript) and 33 duplicate receipts are not shipped; they survive in the arrival commit \texttt{c5612efa1}.

\section{\wtag\ Notation across the Parts}\label{fup:fm:sec:notation}
Each Part keeps its source's letters, and inside a Part a letter always has that Part's meaning; Part~V renames a few symbols of its source to achieve this, as the note at its start lists. Parts~VI--VIII rename no symbol: Parts~VI and~VII keep their sources' letters (each source flags its own collisions), and each of the three sources of Part~VIII keeps its own letters inside its sections, with Table~\ref{fup:cand:tab:letters} comparing them. Many letters change meaning between Parts. Table~\ref{fup:fm:tab:notation} lists those that do, with the reading that would be wrong.

\section{\wtag\ Relation to the Hilbert's-tenth research tree}\label{fup:fm:sec:tree}
The six manuscripts take the research tree's own artifacts, at the pinned commits, as their inputs. The tree is maintained separately; this section records what the Parts re-prove or overlap and what is new relative to it at the time of this write (batch~82 for Parts~I--IV, batch~83 for Part~V, both 3 October 2026).

\paragraph{Byte copies.} 73 members of the five batch-82 archives are byte copies of 51 distinct files of \rw{Computability/HilbertTenthProblem/Papers/} (research tree, \rw{1980/} and \rw{verification/}); every one equals the current file. Report~37's archive holds five more (all in \rw{native-stream-queue/}: the projection receipt, the refinement note, the compiler note, the positive-elimination source and the bootstrap note), equal to the current files, and two byte copies of Report~33's evidence files. None of these is shipped; the README lists each with its repository path. \emph{(Batch~88.)} The five archives of Parts~VI--VIII hold 63 more byte copies, of 33 distinct files of \rw{Computability/HilbertTenthProblem/Papers/} (\rw{research-wip/native-stream-queue/}, \rw{1980/} and \rw{verification/}); every one equals the current file, no pinned file has changed since its pin, and none is shipped. The README lists each with its repository path. The programme's review counts 48 byte-identical copies of current research-tree files; the difference is the scope of its count, not a disagreement about bytes.

\paragraph{The programme's reviews.} The research tree has reviewed these archives, after their arrival:
\begin{itemize}
\item \rw{review_incoming_matrix_grill.md} (commit \texttt{32da04296}, with a helper and receipt) authenticates both editions of Report~23, Report~24 and Report~32 as data, without running their code. It independently parses every row of the 3,600,546-gate DAG, propagates the syntactic degree 71,731,007, checks liveness of every gate and of all 797,141 supplied coordinates and the 260 finalizer rows, and recomputes $87N+16=69{,}339{,}973$ at $N=797{,}011$. Its verdict: a ``useful full numerical Grill milestone'', not an operation improvement on the programme's 85-operation universal polynomial (the record at that review; 84 operations since commit \texttt{20aafb9a5}, below). It did not rerun the exact-degree verifiers.
\item \rw{review_incoming_negative_index_fibers.md} (commit \texttt{0055e1c4d}) reviews Reports~33, 25 and~22. It finds no gap in Report~33's construction, independently expands the eight residuals, fourteen definitions and the 23-gate finalizer of the signed19 child, and records the refutation of the signed19 candidate in the programme's status; it finds Report~25's classification consistent, notes a domain distinction (Part~III, Section~\ref{fup:nf:sec:mainindex}), and does not certify Report~25's second-term or total-bit-length theorems.
\item \rw{review_incoming_counterexample_heights.md} (commit \texttt{ceb7222e9}, 3 October 2026, with a helper and receipt) reviews Report~34. \emph{(Correction, batch~83: the batch-82 write, two minutes later, still called Report~34 unreviewed.)} Report~34 ``passes this scoped analytic and source-interface review'': the review confirms the eventual height by the auxiliary ordinate $y$, the Laurent normal-form factorization, the all-orders asymptotic and inverse reasoning and the exact rotation-discrepancy count; it keeps the unproved discrepancy condition $D(R)=o(\log R)$ open, while a two-term law with $O(1)$ remainder is excluded. It confirms the transfer of Appendix~\ref{fup:hc:sec:transfer} to all eight matching signed19 children as a forward transfer only, with no new gate ledgers, whole-fiber count or parent refutation, and notes that the report improves no universal arithmetic bound.
\item \rw{review_exact_negative_obstruction37.md} (commit \texttt{308f14072}, with a helper and receipt) reviews Report~37 (Part~V): ``PASS within the stated fixed-compiler scope''. The predicate is necessary and sufficient, existence and emptiness both remain unresolved, and the programme's normalized85 polynomial is unaffected because its positive zeros have $F+Z<q$ while every Report~37 negative solution has $F\ge q$. Part~V prints its findings after Lemma~\ref{fup:nz:lem:packing} and Theorem~\ref{fup:nz:thm:main}.
\item \emph{(Batch 88.)} \rw{review_incoming_arithmetic_reports_39_46.md} (commit \texttt{5d8889017}, 3 October 2026, 21:43~PDT, with a receipt \rw{.json}) reviews Reports~39, 41, 43, 45 and~46 (Parts~VI--VIII) within a stated scope: it authenticates the five archives and their 408 members, reads the five delivery READMEs and eleven further proof and source notes in full, executes no archived program and reproduces no package test count; a second reviewer re-verified the hashes and cross-read the note. It is, in its words, ``not approval of every manuscript, analytic appendix or saved diagnostic''. It checks Report~39's Jacobi filter but does not recertify its analytic existence argument; reads Report~41's full counterfamily and finds its arithmetic consistent; finds Report~43's descent and divisor classification consistent and its open-language status historical, without reviewing its inner-family and other squarefree addenda in full; reads Report~45's counterfamily without replaying its receipts; and reads Report~46's even-rank, height and minimality proofs but not its analytic expansions. Its conclusion: the five reports give ``a full obstruction to first-index deletion, exact filters for free-coefficient factor values, and controlled witness-size information for one already-refuted chart. None changes the established84 bound or resolves gamma83.'' The same commit adds the tree's transfer of Report~41 to the 84-operation polynomial, \rw{complete80_first_index_deletion_collapse.md}, with its independent review. Each of Parts~VI--VIII prints the findings for its sources.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{What the Parts re-prove or overlap.}
\begin{itemize}
\item The norm cancellation behind the exact degrees, identity~\eqref{fup:gp:eq:normidentity} of Part~I and~\eqref{fup:dl:eq:cancel} of Part~II, is identity~(4) of \rw{gpcp_shared_selectors774.md} (commit \texttt{ee507599f}), also used in three other notes of the tree; neither Report cites it.
\item Part~II's law $87N+16$ is proved by the same method as the tree's exact law $58N+28$ for the PCP affine slope-class history (\rw{pcp_affine_slope_class_history.md}, shipped inside archive~11), for a different template.
\item Sections~\ref{fup:gp:sec:grill} and~\ref{fup:gp:sec:native} of Part~I re-state and re-certify the tree's corrected Grill halt bridge and its composed native history; Part~I says so and imports them.
\item Report~33's input-index dichotomy (Section~\ref{fup:ir:app:raw}) is proved independently in the tree's \rw{complete74_negative_index_refinement.md} (commit \texttt{813c1cff4}), which goes further: it also proves $4^d>X\ge q^3$ for the defect $d=2n-p$ on the negative branch, and that for fixed $q,X,Y$ and a fixed bounded representative at most one main index $p$ fits the ratio interval; Report~33's bounded-window reduction imports the tree's bootstrap (\texttt{93ad43375}) and says so.
\item Report~37 (Part~V) takes the bounded reduction of Section~\ref{fup:ir:app:raw} and the refinement note's defect bound and uniqueness as prior results, and re-uses the auxiliary construction of Section~5 of the tree's bootstrap note \rw{review_complete74_nonlinear_index_bootstrap.md}; it credits all three. Its Section~\ref{fup:nz:sec:prior} is a recap of them, and its identity~\eqref{fup:nz:eq:E-star} is Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:projection} of Report~33.
\item \emph{(Batch 88.)} Report~45's main theorem is the tree's \rw{complete82_all_input_outer_collapse.md} (commit \texttt{dc03992d9}), proved by a different construction; Report~43's open language question is answered by the tree's \rw{free_coefficient83_prime_outer_collapse.md} (commit \texttt{8cf6239b6}); both postdate the sources' pins and are not cited by Reports~43 and~45. Report~43's completion theorem extends the tree's \rw{free_coefficient83_native_alias.md} (commit \texttt{53c34b012}), Report~45's auxiliary lemma extends the tree's odd-$c$ extension (commit \texttt{6ce2dcaaf}), and Report~41's scaled subsystem family is of the kind of the tree's \rw{first_index_scaled_obstruction.md} (commit \texttt{93c34e817}); the sources credit all three. The odd-quotient, projection and Pell-growth lemmas that Reports~39, 41, 43, 45 and~46 prove are Report~33's Lemmas~\ref{fup:ir:lem:projection} and~\ref{fup:ir:lem:oddquotient} and standard estimates, and Report~39 restates Part~V. Conversely, the tree's \rw{complete80_first_index_deletion_collapse.md} (commit \texttt{5d8889017}) is a transfer of Report~41's theorem, which it credits.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{What is new relative to the tree.} A literal fixed universal Grill program with a paid ordinary-input composition (conditional on the pinned imports), where the tree recorded that ``a numerical universal source and its input-language theorem remain open'' for the exact-width Grill loader; the exact degree of a Grill polynomial (the tree recorded only upper bounds, with \texttt{exact\_degree} \texttt{None}); the parametric laws of Part~II; the classification and counting of the entire native fiber in Part~III; the signed19 counterexample of Report~33, which answers negatively the open obligation of the tree's nonlinear-index scout for the signed child (raw29 and positive21 stay open); and the canonical-family counting of Report~34 with its Kesten obstruction. From Part~V: the deterministic packing recovery, the equivalence of a negative zero in raw29, a negative zero in positive21 and a passing predicate, with complete positive reconstruction, the finite wrapped odd-input sector, the even-input bounds, the $3\nmid q$ filter and the sub-unit Binet window. Before Report~37 the tree had the bounded reduction and left the question open; it is still open, since raw29 and positive21 are reduced to the predicate, not decided. From Parts~VI--VIII: Report~39's shrinking-target family and its Jacobi nonredundancy theorem; Report~41's full counterfamily for first-index deletion, which the tree then transferred to its 84-operation polynomial; Report~43's structural divisor theorems and nonnative inner families; Report~45's second construction of the square/product 82 refutation; and Report~46's exact height, fixed-outer minimality and even-rank nonextension.

\paragraph{Records.} Part~I is not an operation record. At the batch-82 write the tree's current universal polynomials by other routes were far smaller: 85 operations with 18 positive witnesses and exact degree 175 (commit \texttt{109aaca15}), and 86 operations with exact degree 131 (commit \texttt{55d248dc5}); it also has a 557-operation fixed binary-tag universal polynomial and a 97-tile U15 GPCP construction. Part~I does not mention these, and disclaims optimality itself. \emph{(Batch~83.)} Since commit \texttt{20aafb9a5} (3 October 2026, \rw{complete84_scaled_strong_output.md}) the tree's record is 84 operations ($84=47M+37A$), with 18 positive witnesses and uniform exact degree 187; the new polynomial is $F_{84}=\Delta\cdot F_{85}$ with $\Delta>0$ on every allowed positive tuple, so its positive zeros are those of the 85-operation polynomial. The lower-degree 85/175 and 86/131 constructions remain available, and the comparison-certificate bound remains 74. No Part of this report changes these bounds.

\paragraph{Candidates below the record (batch 88).} Parts~VII and~VIII study candidate polynomials with fewer operations than the record; all those they treat are refuted on their inherited compiler recipe. Several share an operation count, so in this report a candidate is always named by its construction, as in Table~\ref{fup:fm:tab:candidates}, which extends the naming table of the programme's review \rw{review_incoming_arithmetic_reports_39_46.md}. The record is still 84 operations at this write.

\section{\wtag\ Relation to other reports of the collection}\label{fup:fm:sec:relation}
No other report of the collection holds the programme's universal polynomial, its native block, the Grill route or the complete74 family. Several name the target that Part~I approaches, an instantiated audited universal polynomial: \rw{stochastic-and-thermal-exactness}, question \rw{ste:ta:q:instantiate}; \rw{liveness-beyond-halting}, the explicitness boundary of Section \rw{lbh:qd:sec:universal} (``no numerical expanded universal polynomial''); \rw{signal-machine-collision-certificates}, near \rw{smc:cg:sec:barrier}; and \rw{polynomial-witness-histories}, question \rw{pwh:oc:q:numerical}. Part~I bears on them without answering them: it gives a literal circuit, conditional on its imports, with exact degree, not an expanded polynomial, and not their particular source machines. Report~22, the predecessor of Part~III, is printed in \rw{signal-machine-collision-certificates}; Report~32 in \rw{group-theoretic-substrates}. No other report of the collection treats the raw29 and positive21 interfaces of Parts~V and~VI, the first-index deletion of Part~VII or the candidates of Part~VIII.

Placement of this report beside the Lean development of \rw{Computability/HilbertTenthProblem} confers no formal status: no statement of this report has been formalized, and no source contains or cites Lean or Rocq code.

\section{\wtag\ Status, claims and non-claims}\label{fup:fm:sec:status}
The eleven manuscripts are AI-assisted research reports (author lines ``Research report 23/24/25'', ``Mathematical research report'' and ``Research Report 43/45/46''), unrefereed, and not formalized. Each Part keeps all its source's disclaimers; the main ones are collected here.
\begin{itemize}
\item Part~I: the language theorem is conditional on pinned full native-history, binary-recoder and finite-input U15 universality theorems; only the explicit valid five-parameter slices have the machine interpretation; no new general MRDP theorem, no optimality claim, and no materialized full Pell witness or universal accepting execution. The exact degree is unconditional.
\item Part~II: source-specific algebraic theorems; no universality theorem, no semantic correctness of arbitrary loader attachments, no minimal degree among equivalent representations.
\item Part~III: native soundness and positive completeness are inherited from pinned source proofs; no change to the native circuit, no minimality, no finite-fold MRDP result, no numerical Pell witness, no literature-wide novelty claim.
\item Part~IV: Report~33 refutes only the proposed positive-domain restoration for the signed19 child; no improved operation bound, no machine-specific false acceptance, no materialized full child witness; raw29 and positive21 remain open (Part~V reduces both to one exact predicate without deciding it). Report~34 is about one explicit canonical subfamily, not the whole signed19 fiber; its finer second term is equivalent to an unresolved discrepancy estimate.
\item Part~V: an exact necessary-and-sufficient predicate, not a proof of global restored-index positivity and not a full negative zero; its emptiness and nonemptiness are both unresolved; only $q,w$ are unbounded, and this is not a globally finite decision procedure. The compiler contract and the sign-free bootstrap are inherited premises, not reproved; auxiliary witnesses need not be unique. The finite checks (a 3,536-case relaxed scale grid, auxiliary-only tuples) are not valid-compiler instances, counterexamples or an unbounded search certificate; the Binet window is a necessary test, not an exact acceptance test, with no complexity or separation bound. No operation count, universal polynomial bound, new witness bound or global novelty is claimed.
\item Part~VI: nonredundancy of two comparisons on positive tuples, in the comparison-deleted systems only; no full negative zero, no main-congruence hit, no unrestricted logical independence, global positivity, complete parametrization, density bounded away from zero, uniformity in a growing compiler or $q$, or feasible threshold; no large genuine tuple is materialized. The symbol $-1$ sector and Part~V's question remain open. The existence argument rests on two estimates from the literature and was not recertified by the programme's review; finite fixtures are not genuine compiler tuples.
\item Part~VII: a symbolic existence proof, not a materialized tuple; the counts 81 and 82 and the degrees 168 and 124 describe rejected candidates. No new universal bound, no claim that any count is a lower bound over all circuits, no effect on the parents or on Part~V's question. Its ``85-operation'' record is now 84 (dated note). The smooth radix bounds the radix only.
\item Part~VIII: Report~43's structural theorems hold on genuine slices and normalize only the stated factors; its inner families are not compiler zeros, and it supplies no zero at a rejected input (the tree has since refuted the candidate by another family). Report~45 refutes the square/product candidate for the inherited compiler, not for a different unprovided compiler, and claims no bound or priority. Report~46's height is exact for its family and minimal only with the outer tuple fixed; its even-rank theorem is not a soundness theorem; its growth is doubly exponential in $t$ and in the numerical input, not in the input's bit length; its analytic inverse is a convergent algorithm, not a canonical inverse transseries, with no interval certification, and was not reviewed by the programme.
\end{itemize}
The suites delivered with the sources were rerun on copies at placement (README); passing them supports the implementations, not the unbounded theorems.

\fupstart{23}{Part I: Report 23 revision 1}
\part{A fixed universal Grill polynomial: paid ordinary input and exact width through a literal source program}\label{fup:part:gp}
\noindent\wtag\ Source~11 of batch~82: \emph{Research report 23, revision 1} (``Revision 1 with the exact degree theorem''), dated 3 October 2026; archive \rw{Fixed_Universal_Grill_Polynomial_Package (1).zip}; pin \texttt{2d887f0fa}; the base of this report. Its sections, statements and equations are numbered 23.$k$ here.
\begin{sourceabstract}
We construct one literal Grill program with 397,488 run phases and one complete integer polynomial circuit attached to it. The circuit accepts an ordinary positive integer input, pays for its conversion into the finite input convention of a fixed universal machine, and binds the initial queue to its exact bit width. Its six external coordinates are the ordinary input and five program parameters fixed for each represented language. It has 797,135 positive existential coordinates and 3,600,546 binary arithmetic operations, comprising 803,517 multiplications and 2,797,029 additions or subtractions. Its exact total degree is 69,339,973, established by an all-tuple norm cancellation and an explicit nonzero leading coefficient on a univariate ray.

The language theorem is conditional on precisely pinned full native-history, binary-recoder, and finite-input U15 universality theorems. The exact-degree theorem is unconditional algebra on the unchanged pinned circuit. The new contribution is the explicit composition: corrected right and left tag macros, a unique accepting-head event, phase-preserving normalization, two distinct complete recoders, signed finite-frame arithmetic, exact unary loading, and a unit-safe finalizer. A complete 61,209,290-byte arithmetic DAG and independent whole-source checks accompany the report. The costs describe this actual emitted circuit in a literal-integer arithmetic model; they are not bit complexity or a minimality claim.
\end{sourceabstract}
\begin{writenote}
The title states universality before its qualifier: the language theorem, Theorem~\ref{fup:gp:thm:main}, is conditional on the pinned imports of Section~\ref{fup:gp:sec:imports}, as its name, the abstract and the conclusion say. The exact-degree theorem, Theorem~\ref{fup:gp:thm:degree}, is unconditional. The programme's review \rw{review_incoming_matrix_grill.md} (commit \texttt{32da04296}) authenticated this source and its original edition as data and checked the DAG independently (Section~\ref{fup:fm:sec:tree}); it did not rerun the exact-degree verifiers. The source's files are shipped with the prefix \texttt{11-grill-poly-}.
\end{writenote}

\section{Result and its mathematical meaning}
The purpose of this report is to close the ordinary-input interface for a concrete Grill route to Diophantine representation. A native queue-history relation by itself is insufficient: it may permit different terminal-zero paddings of the same integer, and a substrate-universality slogan does not identify what language a particular numerical input represents. Here both interfaces are explicit and paid.

\begin{theorem}[Conditional fixed universal polynomial]\label{fup:gp:thm:main}
Assume the pinned theorem imports in Section~\ref{fup:gp:sec:imports}. There exists a single polynomial
\[
 F\in\Z[x,a,p,s,C,L,y_1,\ldots,y_{797135}]
\]
represented by the complete arithmetic DAG in the accompanying archive, such that for every computably enumerable set $S\subseteq\Np$ there is a fixed tuple
\[
 \theta_S=(a_e,p_e,s_e,C_e,L_e)\in\Np^5
\]
with, for every $x\in\Np$,
\[
 x\in S\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \exists(y_1,\ldots,y_{797135})\in\Np^{797135}:
 F(x,a_e,p_e,s_e,C_e,L_e,\mathbf y)=0.
\]
All five coordinates of $\theta_S$ have the finite program-dependent recipes in Section~\ref{fup:gp:sec:parameters}. The polynomial has 3,600,546 emitted binary arithmetic gates and exact total degree 69,339,973 when all six external coordinates and every witness have degree one. The degree assertion is proved independently of these theorem imports in Section~\ref{fup:gp:sec:exactdegree}.
\end{theorem}

The program parameters are not existentially varied with $x$. An arbitrary positive tuple $(a,p,s,C,L)$ still gives a polynomial slice, but only the explicitly constructed valid tuples have the stated machine interpretation. The theorem covers positive integer inputs and positive integer witnesses. No statement about $x=0$, negative inputs, or real zeros is intended.

The construction establishes a concrete representation through a selected computational substrate. It is not a new general MRDP theorem, and the operation or witness counts are not asserted to be optimal. The finite audits establish exact source facts and implementation identities; the unbounded existential equivalence additionally uses the imported complete theorems. In particular, no astronomical full Pell witness tuple or complete universal accepting execution has been materialized.
\begin{writenote}
\emph{Not an operation record.} The research tree's universal polynomials by other routes are far smaller: 85 operations, 18 positive witnesses and exact degree 175 (commit \texttt{109aaca15}), and 86 operations with exact degree 131 (commit \texttt{55d248dc5}), besides a 557-operation fixed binary-tag polynomial. This Part does not mention them. It is the Grill-route instance, and its 3,600,546 operations are about 42,000 times the 85-operation record; the programme's intake review \rw{review_incoming_matrix_grill.md} (commit \texttt{32da04296}) calls it a ``useful full numerical Grill milestone'' and not an operation improvement. \emph{(Batch~83.)} These were the figures at the batch-82 write; since commit \texttt{20aafb9a5} the tree's record is 84 operations at exact degree 187 (Section~\ref{fup:fm:sec:tree}).
\end{writenote}

\subsection{An overview of the chain}
Fix a language $S$ and its finite semidecider. For the varying $x>0$, the construction follows
\[
\begin{gathered}
x\longmapsto u=x+1\longmapsto \text{canonical LSB bits of }u\longmapsto N_e(x),\\
\text{U15 finite tape}\longmapsto\text{corrected deletion-2 tag word}
\longmapsto\text{normalized Genera word}\\
\longmapsto\text{exact Grill E word and width}\longmapsto
\text{complete native history relation}\longmapsto F=0.
\end{gathered}
\]
The two recoders serve different jobs. The first selects the canonical bit length of $u$. The second extracts the unrestricted exponent $N_e(x)+1$ used to load a unary tag block. Neither exponent is the native history duration. Keeping these three quantities separate is essential.

\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Finite-input universality.} The note \fname{neary_woods_explicit_universal_tm.md} supplies the U15 table and effective finite-frame initialization of a selected one-tape semidecider through the bi-tag construction. \item \emph{Complete binary recoding.} The input-dilation130 source and the generic-width bridge, with their complete parent geometry/AND theorem chain, supply all 34 comparisons and 49 positive auxiliaries at every fixed width $k\ge4$. \section{A concrete finite U15 input language}\label{fup:gp:sec:u15}
Use $c=0$, $b=1$, and blank zero outside a finite tape. Refine a U15 control state $u$ and already-read symbol $r$ to
\[
 i=2(u-1)+r\in\{0,\ldots,29\}.
\]
The initial refined state is $0=(u_1,0)$; the accepting state is $19=(u_{10},1)$. The unique absent instruction in primary Table~16 is $(u_{10},b)$. A primary prose sentence instead names $c$; the literal construction follows the table, and the discrepancy is recorded rather than silently reconciled.

For a nonhalting instruction $(u,r)\mapsto(v,\epsilon,D)$, the refined machine writes $\epsilon$, moves in direction $D$, reads $r'$, and selects $j_{r'}=2(v-1)+r'$. There are 15 left-moving and 14 right-moving nonhalting instructions.

Let $M,N$ be the left and right binary tape integers, with the square nearest the head as the least significant bit. The scanned symbol is omitted because the refined control remembers it. A right move changes
\[
 (i,M,N)\mapsto(j_{N\bmod2},\,2M+\epsilon,\,\lfloor N/2\rfloor),
\]
and a left move changes
\[
 (i,M,N)\mapsto(j_{M\bmod2},\,\lfloor M/2\rfloor,\,2N+\epsilon).
\]
These formulas include zero tape integers and indefinitely blank tails.

\subsection{The chosen semidecider and physical input blocks}
For each $S\subseteq\Np$ that is computably enumerable, choose a finite semidecider that reads the canonical LSB-first spelling of $u=x+1$, decodes it, subtracts one, and recognizes $S$. This is a fixed program choice. It is not an uncharged external algorithm applied anew to $x$.

Write the physical binary blocks
\[
 \mathsf A_i=(01)^{8i-5}11,\qquad
 \mathsf B_0=\mathsf A_2\mathsf A_1,\quad
 \mathsf B_1=\mathsf A_1\mathsf A_2.
\]
Both $\mathsf B_0,\mathsf B_1$ have 32 bits. The U15 head scans the final $c$ of the start marker $bc$. Its left word is the finite encoded program followed by $b$; define $M_e$ by reversing that word and taking its LSB value. Its right word is
\begin{equation}\label{fup:gp:eq:frame}
 \mathsf P_e\mathsf B_{w_0}\cdots\mathsf B_{w_{n-1}}\mathsf S_e,
 \qquad w_i=\operatorname{bit}_i(x+1),\quad n=\bits(x+1),
\end{equation}
where
\[
 \mathsf P_e=(01)^{8q_B},\qquad
 \mathsf S_e=\mathsf A_{\rm right}\mathsf A_{\rm left},\qquad q_B\ge2.
\]
The alphabet size $q_B$ and the two boundary indices depend only on the selected finite program. All squares beyond the displayed words are blank. Put $N_e(x)=\val(\text{word in \eqref{fup:gp:eq:frame}})$. The suffix ends in $11$, hence $N_e(x)>0$.

\begin{writenote}
Added 3~October 2026 (batch~82, reciprocal note). Report~32, printed as Part~V of \rw{group-theoretic-substrates}, cites this Report's ordinary-input frame~\eqref{fup:gp:eq:frame} for the finite $U_{15,2}$ input convention in its delivered dependency audit, for the input hardness of its fixed matrix semigroup only (Theorem \rw{gts:um:thm:semigroup} there); the audit calls this Report's conditional theorem unnecessary for that purpose.
\end{writenote}

\section{Corrected tag macros and the accepting head}\label{fup:gp:sec:tag}
A deletion-2 tag step reads the first symbol, deletes two symbols, and appends that first symbol's production. The canonical word is
\[
 \mathcal C(i,M,N)=A_i\xi_i(a_i\xi_i)^M B_i\xi_i(b_i\xi_i)^N.
\]
Internal names are local to the nonhalting refined state. Their numerical identifiers and every production appear in the literal packet.

\subsection{The right macro}
For an instruction writing $\epsilon$ and moving right, use the complete rules below. On internal symbols suppress the state index $i$.
\begin{align*}
 A_i&\to C\xi_i(c\xi_i)^\epsilon,& a_i&\to c\xi_i c\xi_i,\\
 B_i&\to S,&b_i&\to s,\\
 C&\to D_1D_0,&c&\to d_1d_0,\\
 S&\to T_1T_0,&s&\to t_1t_0,\\
 D_1&\to A_{j_1}\xi_{j_1},&d_1&\to a_{j_1}\xi_{j_1},\\
 D_0&\to\xi_{j_0}A_{j_0}\xi_{j_0},&d_0&\to a_{j_0}\xi_{j_0},\\
 T_1&\to B_{j_1}\xi_{j_1},&t_1&\to b_{j_1}\xi_{j_1},\\
 T_0&\to B_{j_0}\xi_{j_0},&t_0&\to b_{j_0}\xi_{j_0}.
\end{align*}
Put $M'=2M+\epsilon$. Exact intermediate queue cuts are
\begin{align*}
 &B_i\xi_i(b_i\xi_i)^N C\xi_i(c\xi_i)^{M'},\\
 &C\xi_i(c\xi_i)^{M'}S s^N,\\
 &S s^N D_1D_0(d_1d_0)^{M'}.
\end{align*}
If $N=2k+1$, the shrinking traversal leaves
\[
 D_1D_0(d_1d_0)^{M'}T_1T_0(t_1t_0)^k,
\]
which rotates through the $D$ and $T$ blocks to exactly $\mathcal C(j_1,M',k)$.
If $N=2k$, its last deletion consumes $D_1$, leaving
\[
 D_0(d_1d_0)^{M'}T_1T_0(t_1t_0)^k.
\]
The $D_0/d_0$ traversal consumes $T_1$ in its last deletion, including when $M'=0$, and gives
\[
 T_0(t_1t_0)^k\xi_{j_0}A_{j_0}\xi_{j_0}(a_{j_0}\xi_{j_0})^{M'}.
\]
The last $T_0/t_0$ deletion consumes the extra filler and yields precisely $\mathcal C(j_0,M',k)$, including $k=0$.

The final filler in $t_0\to b_{j_0}\xi_{j_0}$ is deliberate. The inspected printed Cocke--Minsky production omits it although the next displayed target retains paired repetitions. With $M=0,N=2,\epsilon=0$, the printed rule produces a five-symbol target after ten steps, missing its sixth filler. The construction here proves its own corrected rule; it does not present an asserted published erratum.

\subsection{The left macro and explicit rotation}
For a left move replace the initial four right rules by
\begin{align*}
 A_i&\to L_i\xi_i,& a_i&\to l_i\xi_i,\\
 B_i&\to C\xi_i(c\xi_i)^\epsilon,&b_i&\to c\xi_i c\xi_i,\\
 L_i&\to S,&l_i&\to s.
\end{align*}
One traversal gives
\[
 L_i\xi_i(l_i\xi_i)^M C\xi_i(c\xi_i)^{2N+\epsilon}.
\]
Keep the $C/c/S/s$ preparation. Replace target $A_j,a_j$ in the $D/d$ rules by temporary $B'_j,b'_j$, and replace target $B_j,b_j$ in the $T/t$ rules by actual $A_j,a_j$. Add
\[
 B'_j\to B_j\xi_j,\qquad b'_j\to b_j\xi_j.
\]
The right parity calculation with the tape roles exchanged yields
\[
 B'_j\xi_j(b'_j\xi_j)^{2N+\epsilon}
 A_j\xi_j(a_j\xi_j)^{\lfloor M/2\rfloor},
 \qquad j=j_{M\bmod2}.
\]
Moving the leading prime block to the tail gives the exact canonical left-move target. This finite rotation is the missing content behind an informal appeal to left/right symmetry.

\subsection{Safety before acceptance and uniqueness at acceptance}
Every canonical word has at least four symbols. All preacceptance productions except the shrinking $B_i/b_i$ or $L_i/l_i$ rules have length at least two. During the shrinking phase an untouched $C\xi_i(c\xi_i)^{M'}$ block of length at least two remains, and an $S$ remains at the finish. Therefore every preacceptance intermediate queue has length at least three. Prime rotation preserves length. No valid rejecting run can halt by an accidental short queue.

The exact cuts create one next canonical head. It is read only after all old-macro symbols preceding it are consumed; at that read the whole word is canonical. In particular the first active $A_{19}$ occurs at a genuine accepting boundary, with no delayed old head lineage elsewhere in the queue.

Set $A_{19}\to H$, with a fresh halt symbol $H$. Give the accepting suffix symbols inert productions $ZZ$, and $Z\to ZZ$. No other nonhalting rule emits $H$. Once $A_{19}$ is read, the remaining accepting-state symbols cannot recreate either $A_{19}$ or $H$. Thus exactly one fresh $H$ is emitted. An occurrence in the second deleted position is never mistaken for an active head event.

\section{Normalization with a persistent phase}\label{fup:gp:sec:normalization}
Give each original tag symbol width one. Its unnormalized Genera production is its tag production at phase zero and empty at phase one. Two FIFO reads implement one deletion-2 step. The phase is persistent across generation boundaries, so a generation ending after an odd number of original reads is not reset.

Introduce a width-zero dummy $d$, with $d\to dd$. Pad each production to four symbols using trailing $d$, and replace its two consecutive pairs by fresh width-zero pair symbols. A pair $[u,v]$ expands to $uv$ at either phase. Every normalized production now has exactly two outputs.

\begin{lemma}[Two-layer projection]
Starting in an original-symbol layer, erasing dummies after two normalized generations gives exactly one unnormalized generation, with exactly its final persistent phase.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Only original symbols have width one, so only the first pass changes the phase. Its outputs are pairs and width-zero dummies. The second pass expands each pair, preserves order, and leaves the phase unchanged. Dummy expansion erases to the empty word. Applying these facts successively proves both the word and phase identities, also for odd erased word length.
\end{proof}

A first literal $H$ occurs only when its containing pair expands. The preceding prefix of that output contains original symbols and dummies. Their hypothetical next outputs contain only pairs and dummies, never $H$. This verifies the extra prefix condition in the Genera halt convention, not merely the existence of a halt symbol.

The literal normalized alphabet has 569 nonhalting original symbols, 442 ordered pairs, one dummy, and one halt symbol, for $N_G=1013$. There are 2,024 nonhalting phase rows, each with two outputs; the two ignored halt rows are also total. Its unique halt-containing pair is $(H,d)$, numeric ID 694; the halt has ID 1012. Only phase-zero $A_{19}$ produces that pair. The complete literal table has SHA-256
\begin{center}\sha{3c8924dbb1b5d6e6b8897e59550b0e38a703654b87442c73487f411e94ae355b}.\end{center}

\section{The literal corrected Grill program}\label{fup:gp:sec:grill}
Set
\[
 a=28(N_G+1)=28392,\qquad b=7a=198744,\qquad m=14a=397488.
\]
At one run step a zero is removed without output; a one is removed and appends $g(n_p)=0(10)^{n_p}$; the phase advances modulo $m$. An exponent $n_p$ denotes $n_p$ commands $11$ followed by command $10$. All source times below count run steps.

For a nonhalting source symbol $y$ of width $w_y\in\{0,1\}$ put $e_y=7a(1-w_y)$, and put $e_H=3a/2$. The corrected blocks are
\begin{align*}
 E_y&=0^{14y+7}g(a-3)g(7)g(a-4)0^{3a-14y-10+e_y},\\
 L_y&=0^{a-3}g(14y+7)g(3)g(3a-14y-10)(10)^{e_y},\\
 R_y&=0g(14y+7)g(3)g(3a-14y-10+e_y)0^{a-4}.
\end{align*}
For $y=H$, the displayed $E_H$ is only a cleanup intermediate $E_H^*$, not an initial source block. The second long E grill uses $a-4$, correcting the earlier informal $a-2$ expression.

\subsection{Every run exponent is specified}
Use zero-based positions relative to the start $7ap$ of each half $p\in\{0,1\}$ of length $7a$. Place the values $a-3,7,a-4$ in the listed order at
\[
 28y+\{17,19,21\}\quad\hbox{and}\quad 28y+a+\{13,15,17\},
\]
respectively, for every symbol $y$, including $H$. If nonhalt $y$ outputs $(u,v)$ at source phase $p$, place
\[
 14u+7,\ 3,\ 3a-14u-10+e_u,\ 0,\
 14v+7,\ 3,\ 3a-14v-10+e_v
\]
at the seven positions $14y+2a+\{3,5,7,9,11,13,15\}$, in that order. All other exponents are zero. There are no E-production positions for $H$. The source rows therefore determine the entire little-endian uint32 array; its hash is
\begin{center}\sha{fa658fcdfe1dae2be3a8e2bf97bd613db59558949ea23ca03f549b77201dad01}.\end{center}
Independent reconstruction agrees at every entry and all 1,589,952 bytes.

\subsection{Phase alignment and exact cleanup}
At a normal start phase $7ap$, a nonhalt E block produces the appropriate $L_uR_v$. Its phase advance is $7aw_y\pmod{14a}$. A nonhalt L/R block has length $7a$ or $21a$, both congruent to $7a$ modulo $14a$. A complete two-output LR generation is phase-neutral. These facts preserve the source's persistent phase.

Every active run position modulo $7a$ is odd and below $5a/2$. Translating it by $3a$ lands in the zero-run region. At a start phase shifted by $3a$, every one in an E/L/R block therefore selects exponent zero, producing one zero, while every zero produces nothing.

Suppose the first halting source output is an even word $v$ with its unique $H$ at zero-based index $j$. The H block has length $10a$ and shifts all later blocks by $3a$. The three remaining generation lengths from that LR boundary are
\begin{align*}
 L_1&=10a+\sum_{i\ne j}\bigl[7a+14a(1-w_{v_i})\bigr],\\
 Z&=\sum_{i>j}\bigl[3a+7a(1-w_{v_i})\bigr],\\
 L_2&=17a/2+\sum_{i<j}\bigl[7a+7a(1-w_{v_i})\bigr]+Z,\\
 L_3&=2a(j+1).
\end{align*}
The last produced word is all zeros. All three lengths are positive, and a FIFO queue cannot empty while an initial-generation suffix remains. Thus $L_1+L_2+L_3$ is the exact first-empty time from that boundary. For the actual H-producing pair, the lengths are 880,152, 525,252, and 56,784, totaling 1,462,188 further runs.

Nonhalting E boundaries stay nonempty. Consequently the correctly normalized source halts if and only if the Grill queue from its exact E word empties. This equivalence is at the literal width. Allowing arbitrary high-zero padding of the same numeral would be a different and unjustified interface.
\begin{writenote}
\emph{Re-statement.} The corrected E/L/R compiler, the persistent phase alignment, the $3a$ cleanup shift and the exact first-empty lengths $L_1,L_2,L_3$ are those of the research tree's \rw{grill_tag_halt_bridge.md} (commit \texttt{8ae0b7c1f}), imported in Section~\ref{fup:gp:sec:imports}; its $a-2\to a-4$ correction of the E encoding is the tree's \rw{review_grill_encoding_e.md}. New here are the literal 397,488-entry table and its instantiated totals 880,152, 525,252 and 56,784. The tree's halt bridge itself does not give an ordinary-input universal Grill recognizer; this Part supplies that composition.
\end{writenote}

\section{All five valid program parameters}\label{fup:gp:sec:parameters}
The first three parameters record the finite U15 frame:
\begin{equation}\label{fup:gp:eq:params1}
 a_e=\val(\mathsf P_e)=\frac{2(4^{8q_B}-1)}3,
 \qquad p_e=2^{16q_B},\qquad s_e=\val(\mathsf S_e).
\end{equation}
Each is positive and fixed once the finite program is chosen.

For original symbols define $E_y^\#=\val(E_y)$. Let
\begin{align*}
 \gamma(t)&=2\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}4^j=\frac{2(4^t-1)}3,\\
 A_*&=2^7\bigl[\gamma(a-3)+2^{2a-5}\gamma(7)
                    +2^{2a+10}\gamma(a-4)\bigr].
\end{align*}
Then $E_y^\#=A_*2^{14y}$ and every original block has width $b$. Put
\[
 K=2^{2b},\qquad W_y=E_y^\#+2^bE_{539}^\#.
\]
\needspace{8\baselineskip}
The literal start IDs are $A_0=0$, $a_0=270$, $B_0=30$, $b_0=300$, and $\xi_0=539$. The remaining parameters are
\begin{align}
 C_e&=W_0+K W_{270}\sum_{j=0}^{M_e-1}K^j+K^{M_e+1}W_{30},\label{fup:gp:eq:params2}\\
 L_e&=K^{M_e+2}.\nonumber
\end{align}
The empty sum when $M_e=0$ is zero. These are the value and dyadic width scale of
\[
 E\bigl(A_0\xi_0(a_0\xi_0)^{M_e}B_0\xi_0\bigr).
\]
Equations~\eqref{fup:gp:eq:params1}--\eqref{fup:gp:eq:params2} are finite recipes for fixed program integers. Their powers and finite sums are not runtime operations of the universal polynomial. By contrast, every multiplication involving any of these five \emph{external coordinates} in the universal DAG is charged.

The full initial tag word is the displayed fixed prefix followed by $(b_0\xi_0)^{N_e(x)}$. That repetition count varies with the ordinary input and must be paid inside the polynomial; it is the task of the second recoder.

\section{Two complete recoders and the signed frame}\label{fup:gp:sec:recoders}
The complete generic recoder at fixed width $k\ge4$, on positive input $z<2^h$ and exponent $h\ge2$, retains 49 positive auxiliaries and 34 comparison pairs. At a complete zero its ports satisfy
\begin{gather}
 q=2^h,\quad Q=q^k,\quad B=2^{k-1}Q,\quad P=B^h,\label{fup:gp:eq:recoder}\\
 J=\sum_{j<h}B^j,\quad K_{\rm mask}=\sum_{j<h}(2B)^j,\quad
 z_{\rm out}=\sum_{j<h}\operatorname{bit}_j(z)2^{kj}.\nonumber
\end{gather}
The full converse supplies every retained positive auxiliary at every such exponent. Selected outer equations alone would not justify this interface.

Typing does not depend circularly on the conclusions. Before the recoder equalities, $B=2^{k-1}q^k\ge8q^2$ for positive $q$ and $k\ge4$, and the conceptual prescribed-scale AND ports $zJ+1$, $K_{\rm mask}+1$, its positive supplied output hat, and $qP$ are positive. Generic width changes only the fixed power chain and coefficient in the complete 130-row source; all remaining comparisons and witnesses remain.

\subsection{Canonical input with an inclusive upper endpoint}
Pay $u=x+1$ and apply the width-32 recoder with positive spread output $R$. Its existing positive slack $s_0$ satisfies $u+s_0=q_0$. Supply positive $\beta$ and impose
\[
 s_0+\beta=u+1.
\]
Then $u<q_0\le2u$. Since $q_0$ is dyadic, it is exactly $2^n$ for $n=\bits(u)$. For dyadic $u$ the upper endpoint is necessary and $\beta=1$. Conversely, $s_0=2^n-u>0$ and $\beta=2u+1-2^n>0$ give the required extension. As $x>0$, $u\ge2$, so $n\ge2$ is in the imported domain.

With $K_0=2^{32}$ and $m_0=K_0-1$, the literal block values are
\[
 c_0=3941247658,\qquad c_1=3937053418,\qquad c_1-c_0=-4194240.
\]
Supply positive $N$ and impose the denominator-cleared equation
\begin{align}\label{fup:gp:eq:N}
 m_0N={}&m_0a_e+p_ec_0(Q_0-1)\\
       &+p_em_0(c_1-c_0)R+p_em_0s_eQ_0.\nonumber
\end{align}
The recoder gives $Q_0=K_0^n$ and $R=\sum_{i<n}w_iK_0^i$. Hence \eqref{fup:gp:eq:N} is exactly the LSB concatenation formula for \eqref{fup:gp:eq:frame}. The signed coefficient is retained; it must not be changed to a presumed positive block difference. $N$ is a positive supplied witness, so the off-zero sign of the computed right side does not become an illicit native positivity assumption.

\subsection{An unrestricted exponent equal to the tape integer plus one}
Use a disjoint second recoder at fixed width $k=2b=397488$, with input and spread output both equal to one. Its theorem gives $h\ge2$ and \eqref{fup:gp:eq:recoder}. It receives no canonical-length guard and no dyadic-duration AND predicate. The two namespaces share no private witness.

Supply positive $\ell,v,g$ and impose
\begin{equation}\label{fup:gp:eq:extract}
 (B-1)v+\ell=J,\qquad \ell+g=B-1,\qquad \ell=N+1.
\end{equation}
Since $J\equiv h\pmod{B-1}$, the first equation gives $\ell\equiv h$. The second bounds $1\le\ell\le B-2$. Also
\[
 B=2^{kh+k-1}\ge2^{4h+3}>h+1,
\]
so $2\le h\le B-2$. The representatives lie in one modulus interval and are equal: $h=\ell=N+1$. No modular alias survives.

For the converse choose the full recoder extension at $h=N+1$ and take
\[
 v=\sum_{j=1}^{h-1}\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}B^i>0,
 \qquad g=B-1-h>0,\qquad\ell=h.
\]
The case $N=1,h=2$ is already valid; the inner quotient is positive. $N=0$ would require the excluded $h=1$, but the physical suffix has proved $N>0$. Nondyadic exponents are allowed and are essential.

\section{Exact unary loading and positive width}
The fixed repeated pair has value
\[
 V=E_{300}^\#+2^bE_{539}^\#
   =A_*2^{14\cdot300}+A_*2^{b+14\cdot539}
\]
and exact width $k=397488$. Its value has 319,867 bits. Those two numbers are different because its terminal zeros are part of the word.

The second recoder has $Q_1=K^{N+1}$. Supply positive $T,X$ and impose
\begin{align}
 KT&=Q_1,\label{fup:gp:eq:unary}\\
 (K-1)X&=(K-1)C_e+L_eV(T-1),\nonumber\\
 P_0&=L_eT.\label{fup:gp:eq:width}
\end{align}
The first equation gives $T=K^N$. The second uniquely gives
\[
 X=C_e+L_eV\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}K^j,
\]
precisely the numeral of the full initial E word. Equation~\eqref{fup:gp:eq:width} fixes its exact dyadic width scale.

The native source already has a positive width slack $Z_0$ and computes $P_0=X+Z_0$. This coordinate is retained, and \eqref{fup:gp:eq:width} is an additional comparison. Substituting an arbitrary signed computed difference for a positive input would not be a valid replacement.

Every original E block is nonzero and ends with at least two zero bits. In base $2^b$, each block value is below one quarter of that base. A finite geometric sum therefore gives $0<3X<P_0$ for the exact word. On a genuine halt the strong native converse applies at that same width, with positive slack $P_0-3X$. The established strong-to-weak map yields $Z_0=P_0-X>0$ while preserving the word, width, and history. No optional padding is used.

\section{A complete scalable native history}\label{fup:gp:sec:native}
The complete native component is much larger than the loader. Its correctness transfer must therefore cover its entire source, not just a few ports or small instances. The accompanying \fname{native_history_proof.md} proves the scalable emitter identically equal to the pinned complete weak native polynomial after a bijective coordinate renaming. The following formulas expose the main identities.
\begin{writenote}
\emph{Re-certification.} The native history polynomial of this section is the research tree's word-closure, weak-cone, phase-sharing, phase-residual206 and composed205 polynomial for a fixed program; the new content is a scalable emitter proved identical to it over all integer tuples. The tree states for its own small programs that ``no ordinary-input decoder or fixed universal Grill recognizer has been supplied'' (\rw{grill_tag_native_weak_cone.md}) and that its word closure ``supplies no fixed universal Grill program'' (\rw{review_grill_native_word_closure.md}).
\end{writenote}

\subsection{Coordinates and grouped affine transports}
For a fixed program $n_0,\ldots,n_{m-1}$, let $s=2m$ and let $g$ be the number of distinct exponents. The two tiles at phase $i$ are
\[
 (2,0,1,0),\qquad (2,1,b(n_i),d(n_i)),
\]
where $b(n)=2^{2n+1}$ and $d(n)=2(4^n-1)/3$. These are fixed integer coefficients. Distinct exponent classes retain separate selected-history coordinates, while every original chronological selector remains present.

There are $s$ positive selector hats $\widehat S_i$, $g$ positive history hats $\widehat Z_j$, seven positive outer coordinates, and sixteen positive native-kernel coordinates. Thus the native count is $s+g+23$. For the literal program, $s=794976$ and $g=2030$, giving 797,029.

Let $I_j=\{2i+1:n_i=e_j\}$, with groups ordered by first occurrence, and put
\[
 \sigma_i=\widehat S_i-1,\quad G_j=\sum_{i\in I_j}\sigma_i,\quad
 z_j=\widehat Z_j-1,\quad J=\sum_i\sigma_i.
\]
To avoid collision with the loader's $K,B$, write the native radix multiplier as $\mathcal K$ and radix as $\mathcal B$. Its exact fixed value is
\[
 \mathcal K=2^{\max(3,\lceil\log_2(s+4)\rceil,\,2\max n_i+2)}=2^{551890}.
\]
The implementation computes the integer ceiling by bit length, without floating point. Define
\begin{gather*}
 P_0=X+Z_0,\qquad U_f=P_0V_f+X,\\
 D=U_f+q_{\rm init}+h_{\rm slack},\qquad\mathcal B=\mathcal K D,
 \qquad P=(\mathcal B-1)J+1.
\end{gather*}
Grouped transports are
\begin{align*}
 \operatorname{next}U&=2H_U+\sum_jG_j,\\
 \operatorname{next}V&=H_V+\sum_j(b(e_j)-1)z_j+\sum_jd(e_j)G_j.
\end{align*}
Distributivity gives exactly the ungrouped template. The first three outer comparisons are
\begin{align*}
 H_U+H_V+\sum_j\widehat Z_j+g_{\rm bound}&=P,\\
 \mathcal B\operatorname{next}U+1&=H_U+PU_f,\\
 \mathcal B\operatorname{next}V+1&=H_V+PV_f.
\end{align*}
Positive hats in the first equality are intentional; they implement the precise global bound, including its $g$ offset.

\subsection{The exact phase residual}
Let $\pi_i=\widehat S_{2i}+\widehat S_{2i+1}$, $S_i=\pi_i-2$, and
\[
 T_{\rm ph}=\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}(m-i)S_i,\qquad J=\sum_iS_i.
\]
The streamed nested prefix sum gives coefficient $m-i$ on $\pi_i$; subtracting $m(m-1)$ removes its hat offsets. Every prefix and accumulator addition is an emitted gate.

For proof purposes write $\operatorname{Next}=mJ-T_{\rm ph}$ and
$Q=(m+1)J-mS_0-T_{\rm ph}$. Substitution yields the polynomial identity
\begin{align*}
 &\mathcal B\operatorname{Next}+q_{\rm init}-Q-mP\\
 &\hspace{1em}=m\pi_0-(\mathcal B-1)T_{\rm ph}+q_{\rm init}-(J+3m).
\end{align*}
This is an all-tuple identity over a commutative ring. It assumes neither Boolean selectors nor a valid phase path nor the vanishing of another residual. For $m=1$ it reduces to $q_{\rm init}-1$.

\subsection{Exact packed ports and the fixed kernel}
Use $\pack(v)=\sum_i v_iP^i$ and $\rep(r)=\sum_{i<r}P^i$. Horner packing and the paid identities
\[
 \rep(2r)=\rep(r)(1+P^r),\qquad
 \rep(2r+1)=\rep(2r)+P^{2r}
\]
are polynomial identities also at $P=0$ and $P=1$. All exponents are fixed by the literal program; square-and-multiply emits ordinary paid products.

Define
\begin{gather*}
 S=\pack(\sigma),\quad M_c=J\rep(s),\quad T_c=H_U+PH_V,\\
 G_*=\pack(G),\quad H_b=H_V\rep(g),\quad
 M_b=(\mathcal B-1)G_*,\quad Z_b=\pack(z),\\
 R_M=(D-1)J(1+P).
\end{gather*}
With $e_c=g$, $e_r=g+s$, $e_t=g+s+2$, $e_i=g+s+4$, put
\begin{align*}
 W&=P^{e_c}S+P^{e_r}T_c,\\
 H&=H_b+W+P^{e_t}\mathcal B+P^{e_i}P_0,\\
 M&=M_b+P^{e_c}M_c+P^{e_r}R_M
            +P^{e_t}(\mathcal B-1)+P^{e_i}(P_0-1),\\
 Z&=Z_b+W,\qquad \mathrm{scale}=P^{g+s+5}.
\end{align*}
These are the exact controller, range, top/radix, and initial-width lanes of the pinned native adapter. Here the scale exponent is 797,011.

The frozen 67-row kernel receives exactly $(H,M,Z,\mathrm{scale})$. It retains sixteen positive coordinates, all six native comparison pairs, and a four-factor integer unit
\[
 U=R_{15}P_{17}\,\mathrm{first\_unit}\,\mathrm{bs}_q.
\]
Together with the four outer comparisons this gives ten nonunit native comparisons. In particular the strong norm-coefficient equality is retained. Induction over the frozen rows, combined with the displayed port identities, proves equality of every kernel register, residual, unit factor, and complete native polynomial on all integer tuples.

\subsection{The semantic import at its positive domain}
On supplied positive coordinates and $X>0$, $P_0\ge2$, $D\ge5$, $\mathcal B>D$, and the decoded hats are nonnegative. At a complete zero the global equality forces $P>1$, $J>0$ and the history bounds. Native classification gives dyadic scale and width and separates the lanes. The retained transports and phase relation recover a common reverse affine history. Its endpoint yields the word closure, hence an actual halt at or before the proposed closure length.

Conversely a genuine finite halt at the specified admissible $P_0$ supplies reversed sentinel histories and selector hats. Choose an adequate dyadic height, pack at $\mathcal B=\mathcal K D$ and $P=\mathcal B^t$, and use the positive global-bound estimate from the complete theorem. The prescribed-scale native AND converse supplies its full positive witnesses; the unit projection supplies all sixteen retained coordinates.

A native closure may include a post-halt suffix. Soundness gives a first actual halt no later than the closure length; it does not claim legal physical execution after an empty queue. The separate Grill cleanup theorem identifies the actual first-empty boundary. There is no externally imposed time cutoff.

\section{One unit-safe finalizer and the full proof}
The complete residual list is shown in Table~\ref{fup:gp:tab:residuals}. It includes both full recoders, all loader constraints, all native comparisons, and the exact-width equality once.
\begin{table}[ht]
\centering
\begin{tabular}{lr}
\toprule
Source of nonunit residuals & Number\\\midrule
Complete native history & 10\\
Canonical width-32 recoder & 34\\
Canonical length guard and signed frame & 2\\
Unrestricted width-397488 recoder & 34\\
Exponent extraction and binding & 3\\
Unary scale, unary value, exact native width & 3\\\midrule
Total & 86\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{All 86 residuals occur inside the same unit product.}\label{fup:gp:tab:residuals}
\end{table}

The emitted polynomial is exactly
\begin{equation}\label{fup:gp:eq:finalizer}
 F=U\left(1+\sum_{j=0}^{85}R_j^2\right)-1.
\end{equation}
For any integer tuple, the second factor is a positive integer. If $F=0$, it divides one, so it equals one, every $R_j=0$, and $U=1$. Conversely those conditions give $F=0$. This argument does not assume that $U$ or a previously finalized native polynomial is nonnegative away from its zero set. Adding an unweighted external sum of squares to a signed native polynomial would not provide the same implication.

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{fup:gp:thm:main}]
Fix a valid $\theta_S$ from Section~\ref{fup:gp:sec:parameters}. Suppose $F$ has a positive zero at $x>0$. The finalizer restores every comparison and the native unit. The first recoder fixes the canonical bits of $x+1$ and the frame equation fixes $N=N_e(x)$. The second recoder and the bounded residue extraction fix $h=N+1$, hence $T=K^N$. The unary equation and width comparison identify the exact literal E word, with no independent padding choice. Native soundness implies a finite Grill halt. The corrected halt bridge, phase-preserving normalization, and accepting-head invariant imply U15 acceptance. The selected finite program recognizes $S$ on the decoded input $x$, so $x\in S$.

Conversely let $x\in S$. Its selected U15 computation accepts. The corrected tag macros avoid premature short queues and emit one halt from the accepting head. Normalization satisfies the source halt convention, and the corrected Grill bridge gives a genuine finite first empty time from the exact E word. Choose the full first recoder extension at $n=\bits(x+1)$ and its positive guard. Set the positive genuine $N$, then choose the full second extension at $h=N+1$ and the positive $\ell,v,g$ in Section~\ref{fup:gp:sec:recoders}. Set $T=K^N$ and the literal positive $X$. The E-word margin permits the strong native converse at the same width and its positive transfer to the retained weak slack. All private witness sets are disjoint except for the deliberately shared ports. Thus every added coordinate can be positive simultaneously, every residual vanishes, and $U=1$. Equation~\eqref{fup:gp:eq:finalizer} vanishes.
\end{proof}

\section{What the arithmetic ledger counts}\label{fup:gp:sec:cost}
The source is a straight-line DAG with binary addition, subtraction, and multiplication over integers. Reading an exact literal coefficient is free in this model, but multiplying a runtime register by any nontrivial coefficient is an emitted multiplication. A fixed-exponent runtime power is emitted as binary multiplication gates. There are no runtime division or variable-exponentiation gates.
