\section{The exact polynomial interface}\label{fup:ir:sec:interface}
We state the entire child interface so that neither its scope nor a hidden sign convention can be imported from a different presentation. The ordinary input $x$ and precisely the coordinates
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:witnesses}
 J,F,\alpha,z,f,h,i,j,o,s,w,\tau,\eta,\zeta,y,Z,\delta,\rho,\sigma
 \quad\text{belong to }\pos.
\end{equation}
All other quantities below are computed expressions; they are not additional existential coordinates. Define
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:defscale}
 q=(B-1)J+1,\qquad X=wq^3,\qquad Y=sq^3,\qquad E=XY.
\end{equation}
\begin{align}
 k&=\eta+\zeta,&a&=Y(X+1),&c&=kY+\eta,\label{fup:ir:eq:defmain}\\
 A&=a+2,&H&=4a+3,&\Delta&=a^2+H=A^2-1,\notag\\
 \gamma&=\rho+\sigma,&D&=X+ac+\gamma H,&R&=k-hE-1,\label{fup:ir:eq:defrest}\\
 u&=2dx+b,&\kappa&=u+\delta\Delta,&C&=q-\alpha-2dx,\label{fup:ir:eq:definput}\\
 W&=C-Z,&\mu&=W+a\kappa+\rho H.&&\notag
\end{align}
For clarity, the source's register named \code{A} is our $\Delta$, whereas our $A=a+2$ is a mathematical abbreviation. The source's \code{Jrep}, \code{zquot}, and \code{y_aux} are $J,z,y$; its \code{R10b}, \code{R10a}, \code{R12}, and \code{R14} are $k,c,a,D$. Its paid field-mask port is $\MFsrc$, not the native mask $\MFnat$.

The eight retained equations are exactly
\begin{align}
 (K_0+X)C&=F+z(q-1),\label{fup:ir:eq:transport}\\
 R&=(q^2-Z-qF)(q^2-1)+(\MC+q\MFsrc)J,\label{fup:ir:eq:packing}\\
 \tau^2-XY^2(XY^2+1)k^2&=1,\label{fup:ir:eq:first}\\
 D^2-\Delta c^2&=1,\label{fup:ir:eq:mainnorm}\\
 (ic^2)^2&=\Delta(f^2-1),\label{fup:ir:eq:strong}\\
 (ic^2)^2\big((jc-R)^2-y^2\big)&=1-y^2,\label{fup:ir:eq:auxnorm}\\
 jc-R&=of-c,\label{fup:ir:eq:auxlinear}\\
 \mu^2-\Delta\kappa^2&=1.\label{fup:ir:eq:inputnorm}
\end{align}
The child polynomial $F_{19}$ is the sum of the squares of their residuals. Over the integers, it vanishes if and only if all eight equations hold. The first factored paid comparison is
\[
 (EkY)(EkY+k)=\tau^2-1.
\]
Because $E=XY$, its left side equals $k^2XY^2(XY^2+1)$, exactly as in \eqref{fup:ir:eq:first}. No weaker auxiliary square has been substituted in \eqref{fup:ir:eq:strong} or \eqref{fup:ir:eq:auxnorm}.

\subsection{Why the graph identity does not supply positivity}
In the signed20 parent, $R$ is itself a positive supplied coordinate, and there is a ninth equation $k=R+1+hE$. Its index-eliminated descendant substitutes $R=k-hE-1$ in both packing and $jc-R$, then removes only that now-identical ninth residual. Consequently
\[
 F_{19}(v)=F_{20}\bigl(v,R=k(v)-hE(v)-1\bigr)
\]
as a polynomial identity over every commutative ring. On integer zero sets it gives inverse deletion and restoration maps without sign restrictions. On positive zero sets, the inverse is valid exactly on the slice $k>hE+1$. Theorem~\ref{fup:ir:thm:main} supplies positive child zeros outside this slice.

The source proof for the parent uses positive $R$ before deriving a packing bound. More explicitly, its shifted-mask rewrite is
\begin{align*}
 S'&=Z+qF-1,\\
 T'&=\MC J+1+q(\MFnat J-1),\\
 R&=(q^2-S')(q^2-1)+T',\qquad 0<T'<q^2-1.
\end{align*}
Positive $R$ rules out $S'>q^2$. Without that hypothesis the same formula permits negative representatives. Importing the parent's consequent bounds into the eliminated child would be circular.

\section{Elementary Pell identities used in the proof}\label{fup:ir:sec:pell}
For an integer $A\ge2$ and an integer $r\ge0$, define $\chi_A(r),\psi_A(r)$ by
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:pell}
 \chi_A(r)+\psi_A(r)\sqrt{A^2-1}
   =\bigl(A+\sqrt{A^2-1}\bigr)^r.
\end{equation}
These are integer polynomials in $A$. Their initial values are $(\chi_A(0),\psi_A(0))=(1,0)$ and $(\chi_A(1),\psi_A(1))=(A,1)$; both obey $v_{r+1}=2Av_r-v_{r-1}$. The positive coefficient sequence $\psi_A(r)$ is strictly increasing for $r\ge1$.

\begin{lemma}[Norms and congruences]\label{fup:ir:lem:pell}
Write $\Delta=A^2-1$. For nonnegative indices,
\begin{align}
 \chi_A(r)^2-\Delta\psi_A(r)^2&=1,\label{fup:ir:eq:pellnorm}\\
 \psi_A(r+s)&=\psi_A(r)\chi_A(s)+\chi_A(r)\psi_A(s),\label{fup:ir:eq:addition}\\
 \psi_A(r)&\equiv rA^{r-1}\pmod\Delta\quad(r\ge1).\label{fup:ir:eq:discrimcong}
\end{align}
If $A$ is even, $\psi_A(r)\equiv r\pmod2$. If $A\equiv1\pmod m$, then $\psi_A(r)\equiv r\pmod m$. If $r\ge3$ is odd, then $\psi_A(r)>r$ and $\psi_A(r)\equiv r\pmod\Delta$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Multiplication of \eqref{fup:ir:eq:pell} proves the addition law; conjugation proves the norm. In the binomial expansion the only term surviving in the square-root coefficient modulo $\Delta$ is $rA^{r-1}$. For odd $r$, $A^{r-1}\equiv1\pmod\Delta$. The stated parity and modulus-$m$ formulas follow from the recurrence and initial values. Finally $\psi_A(2)=2A>2$, and induction in the recurrence gives $\psi_A(r+1)>\psi_A(r)+1$ for $r\ge2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Projection congruence and growth]\label{fup:ir:lem:projection}
Let $a\ge1$, $A=a+2$, $H=4a+3$, and set
\[
 e_A(r)=\chi_A(r)-a\psi_A(r).
\]
Then $e_A(0)=1$, $e_A(1)=2$, and
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:projectionidentity}
 e_A(r)\equiv2^r\pmod H,\qquad
 e_A(r)=2\psi_A(r)-\psi_A(r-1)>\psi_A(r)\quad(r\ge1).
\end{equation}
In particular $e_A(r)$ grows exponentially with $r$ for fixed $A$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The sequence $e_A$ has recurrence coefficient $2A$. The candidate $2^r$ satisfies that recurrence modulo $H$ because
$2A\cdot2-1=4a+7\equiv4\pmod H$, and its initial values agree. The standard recurrence identity $\chi_A(r)=A\psi_A(r)-\psi_A(r-1)$ gives the second formula; strict growth of $\psi_A$ gives the inequality. Formula~\eqref{fup:ir:eq:pell}, or its conjugate, gives exponential growth.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Odd quotient polynomial]\label{fup:ir:lem:oddquotient}
There is an integer polynomial $\mathcal Q_b(z)$ for each $b\ge0$ such that
\begin{align}
 \chi_Q(2b+1)/Q&=\mathcal Q_b(Q^2),\label{fup:ir:eq:oddq}\\
 \mathcal Q_b(0)&=(-1)^b(2b+1),\label{fup:ir:eq:oddqzero}\\
 \mathcal Q_b(1-A^2)&=(-1)^b\psi_A(2b+1).\label{fup:ir:eq:oddqeval}
\end{align}
The first identity is a polynomial identity, with the quotient interpreted polynomially even at $Q=0$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The odd polynomial $\chi_Q(2b+1)$ is divisible by $Q$. Its quotient is a polynomial in $Q^2$. More explicitly,
\[
 \mathcal Q_0(z)=1,\quad \mathcal Q_1(z)=4z-3,\quad
 \mathcal Q_{b+1}(z)=(4z-2)\mathcal Q_b(z)-\mathcal Q_{b-1}(z).
\]
This follows from the two-step Pell recurrence. At $z=0$ the initial values and recurrence give \eqref{fup:ir:eq:oddqzero}. At $z=1-A^2$, its recurrence coefficient is $2-4A^2$. The sequence $(-1)^b\psi_A(2b+1)$ has this coefficient and the same initial values $1,1-4A^2$, proving \eqref{fup:ir:eq:oddqeval}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[A divisibility lift]\label{fup:ir:lem:divisibility}
If $c=\psi_A(p)$ and $m=cp$ with $p,c\ge1$, then $c^2\mid\psi_A(m)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Write $D=\chi_A(p)$. The square-root coefficient in
$(D+c\sqrt\Delta)^c$ is $\psi_A(cp)$. Its term with one square root is $c^2D^{c-1}$. Every higher odd term contains at least $c^3$. Thus each term is divisible by $c^2$.
\end{proof}

\section{Fixing the input and a compatible scale}\label{fup:ir:sec:scale}
Fix an arbitrary $x\in\pos$ and an actual fixed compiler export. Choose a power of five $N$ large enough that
\[
 q=B^N>2dx+1.
\]
Put
\begin{align}
 t&=dN,& J&=\frac{q-1}{B-1},&p_0&=12t+7,\label{fup:ir:eq:context}\\
 X&=2^{p_0},& w&=2^{p_0-3t},&Q&=q^2-1.\notag
\end{align}
Here $t$ is a power of five, $q=2^t$, $p_0\equiv3\pmod4$, and $p_0\equiv1\pmod6$. Thus $J,w$ are strictly positive integers and $X=wq^3$. Set
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:outerconstants}
 C=1,\quad\alpha=q-1-2dx,\quad z=1,\quad
 F=K_0+X-q+1,\quad M=(\MC+q\MFsrc)J.
\end{equation}
The coordinates $\alpha,z,F$ are positive, since $X\ge q^3>q$ and $K_0\ge0$. The definition of $C$ and the transport equation \eqref{fup:ir:eq:transport} are now exact. The fixed input index
\[
 u=2dx+b
\]
is odd and at least three.

\subsection{A prime progression removes the remaining congruence obstruction}
Define
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:D0}
 D_0=\frac{4q^3(X+1)}3.
\end{equation}
This is an integer because $p_0$ is odd.

\begin{lemma}\label{fup:ir:lem:scalegcd}
One has $\gcd(D_0,Q)=1$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Since $t$ is a power of five,
$\gcd(p_0,2t)=\gcd(12t+7,2t)=1$.
We show $\gcd(2^{p_0}+1,2^{2t}-1)=3$. Any common divisor $g$ is odd. Choose integers $r,s$ with $rp_0+2st=1$. The integer $r$ is odd, so, using inverses modulo $g$ if necessary,
$2=(2^{p_0})^r(2^{2t})^s\equiv(-1)^r=-1\pmod g$.
Thus $g\mid3$, and both expressions are divisible by three. Moreover $p_0\equiv1\pmod6$ gives $X+1\equiv3\pmod9$. Removing its sole factor of three makes $(X+1)/3$ coprime to $Q$. Since $Q$ is odd and coprime to $q$, \eqref{fup:ir:eq:D0} proves the assertion.
\end{proof}
For each prime divisor $r$ of the odd integer $Q$, avoid the two distinct residues $0$ and $-D_0^{-1}$ modulo $r$. This is possible because $r\ge3$. The Chinese remainder theorem, followed by any lift to the prime powers if needed, provides $s_0$ with
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:s0}
 \gcd(s_0,Q)=\gcd(1+D_0s_0,Q)=1.
\end{equation}
The progression
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:primeAP}
 \ell\equiv1+D_0s_0\pmod{D_0Q}
\end{equation}
is primitive: its residue is one modulo $D_0$ and a unit modulo $Q$. Dirichlet's theorem therefore supplies arbitrarily large primes in it \cite{fup:ir:dirichlet}. Fix one with $\ell>3$ and put
\begin{align}
 s&=(\ell-1)/D_0,&Y&=sq^3,&E&=XY,\label{fup:ir:eq:Y}\\
 a&=Y(X+1),&A&=a+2,&H&=4a+3=3\ell,\notag\\
 \Delta&=A^2-1,&P&=2XY^2+1.&&\notag
\end{align}
The coordinate $s$ is a positive integer congruent to $s_0$ modulo $Q$. In particular it is coprime to $Q$ and not divisible by three. The latter follows because $3\mid Q$. As $D_0$ is also a unit modulo three and $\ell$ is a unit modulo three, $\ell-1=D_0s$ is nonzero modulo three; consequently $\ell\equiv2\pmod3$.

Let $O=\ord_H(2)$, which exists because $H$ is odd. Since $H=3\ell$,
\[
 O\mid\lcm(2,\ell-1)=\ell-1=D_0s.
\]
The number $D_0s$ is coprime to $Q$, to three, and to $\ell$, so $\gcd(QH,O)=1$. Separately $\gcd(QH,2E)=1$: the factors of $Q$ do not divide $s$ or the powers of two $X,q$; neither three nor $\ell$ divides $s$, and both are odd. Therefore, with
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:TL}
 T=\lcm(4,2E,O),\qquad L=E/2,
\end{equation}
we have the crucial compatibility
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:coprime}
 \gcd(QH,T)=1.
\end{equation}
The scale, including this one chosen prime, is now held fixed. Dirichlet's theorem is used only here; the infinitely many full child zeros below come from varying the main Pell index.

\section{The entire outer and input block in one free progression}\label{fup:ir:sec:crt}
Before choosing the main index, set
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:inputchoices}
 \kappa=\psi_A(u),\quad\mu_0=\chi_A(u),\quad
 e_u=e_A(u),\quad\delta=\frac{\kappa-u}{\Delta}.
\end{equation}
Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:pell} makes $\delta$ a strictly positive integer. Impose the two congruences
\begin{align}
 p&\equiv p_0\pmod T,\label{fup:ir:eq:pAP1}\\
 p&\equiv-M-Q(q^2-qF+e_u-1)\pmod{QH}.\label{fup:ir:eq:pAP2}
\end{align}
By \eqref{fup:ir:eq:coprime}, their solutions form one nonempty arithmetic progression
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:freeAP}
 p=p_*+Vr,\qquad V=TQH,\qquad r\in\Z.
\end{equation}
In particular $p$ remains a freely varying linear index. It has not been restricted to values of a Pell sequence or to another nonlinear sparse set. Every solution has
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:pconditions}
 p\equiv3\pmod4,\qquad 2^p\equiv X\pmod H,\qquad p\equiv p_0\pmod{2E}.
\end{equation}
Choose an integer $n_0$ representing $(1-p_0)/2$ modulo $L=E/2$. For every
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:nAP}
 n=n_0+Lv
\end{equation}
and every $p$ satisfying \eqref{fup:ir:eq:pAP1},
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:kncong}
 2n\equiv1-p\pmod E.
\end{equation}

For sufficiently large $p$ in \eqref{fup:ir:eq:freeAP}, define
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:Zrho}
 Z=q^2-qF+\frac{p+M}{Q},\qquad
 \rho=\frac{Z+e_u-1}{H},\qquad W=1-Z.
\end{equation}
Reduction of \eqref{fup:ir:eq:pAP2} modulo $Q$ makes $Z$ integral; division by $Q$ in the same congruence makes $\rho$ integral. Both are eventually strictly positive: as functions of $p$, their slopes are respectively $1/Q$ and $1/(QH)$. The computed root is
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:mupositivity}
 W+a\kappa+\rho H=e_u+a\kappa=\chi_A(u)=\mu_0>0.
\end{equation}
Hence the complete input definition, the definition $\kappa=u+\delta\Delta$, and the input norm \eqref{fup:ir:eq:inputnorm} all hold. Also
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:packingnegative}
 (q^2-Z-qF)Q+M=-p.
\end{equation}
Thus the packing equation is exact with $R=-p$. Notice that $W=1-Z$ is eventually negative. It is a computed intermediate, not one of the nineteen supplied coordinates in \eqref{fup:ir:eq:witnesses}. No positivity condition on $W$ may be added to this child.

\section{An irrational rotation supplies the strict Pell ratio}\label{fup:ir:sec:density}
The only missing coupling between the main and first Pell indices is the strict interval
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:ratio}
 Y<\frac{\psi_A(p)}{2\psi_P(n)}<Y+1.
\end{equation}
It gives the two positive decomposition witnesses $\eta,\zeta$. We must obtain it on the already imposed progressions, without discarding any outer equation.

\begin{lemma}[Independent quadratic growth rates]\label{fup:ir:lem:irrational}
For the fixed parameters above, put
\[
 \lambda=A+\sqrt{\Delta},\qquad
 \nu=P+\sqrt{P^2-1},\qquad
 \alpha_0=\log\lambda,\quad\beta_0=\log\nu.
\]
Then $\alpha_0/\beta_0$ is irrational.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The integer $A$ is even, hence $\Delta$ is odd. On the other hand
\[
 P^2-1=4XY^2(XY^2+1),\qquad
 v_2(P^2-1)=2+p_0+2v_2(Y)
\]
has odd 2-adic valuation. Thus the squarefree part of $\Delta$ is odd and that of $P^2-1$ is even. Both numbers are nonsquares: for any integer $z\ge2$, $z^2-1$ lies strictly between consecutive squares. Their real quadratic fields are therefore distinct and have intersection $\Q$.

If $\alpha_0/\beta_0$ were rational, there would be positive integers $r,s$ with $\lambda^r=\nu^s$. This common positive number would belong to the intersection $\Q$. Its norm in either quadratic field is one; the norm of a rational number $q$ is $q^2$, so positivity would force it to be one. This contradicts $\lambda,\nu>1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Density on the imposed progressions]\label{fup:ir:lem:ratio}
There are infinitely many pairs of positive integers $(p,n)$ satisfying \eqref{fup:ir:eq:freeAP}, \eqref{fup:ir:eq:nAP}, and \eqref{fup:ir:eq:ratio}, with both $p$ and $n$ tending to infinity.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For a positive irrational $\theta$, every forward tail of the rotation $r\theta$ modulo one is dense. One elementary proof is to use the pigeonhole principle to obtain nonzero integer multiples arbitrarily close to zero modulo one; successive steps by such a multiple approximate any point. The closure of the integer orbit is thus the entire circle. The closure of a forward tail is the same, because positive multiples approach zero and hence approximate the negative multiples as well. In particular every nonempty open arc is visited infinitely often.

Apply this fact to
\[
 \theta=\frac{V\alpha_0}{L\beta_0},
\]
which is irrational by Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:irrational}. Write
\[
 c_0=\log\frac{\sqrt{P^2-1}}{2\sqrt\Delta}.
\]
Choose a nondegenerate closed interval $I=[a_0,b_0]$ strictly inside
$(\log Y,\log(Y+1))$, small enough that its length is less than $L\beta_0$. The affine expression
\[
 (p_*+Vr)\alpha_0-n_0\beta_0+c_0
\]
is dense modulo $L\beta_0$ as positive integers $r$ vary. Infinitely many such $r$ admit an integer $v$ for which
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:affine}
 (p_*+Vr)\alpha_0-(n_0+Lv)\beta_0+c_0\in I.
\end{equation}
To see that the closed interval causes no endpoint issue, apply density to its nonempty interior. Since the left side is bounded in \eqref{fup:ir:eq:affine},
\[
 v=\frac{V\alpha_0}{L\beta_0}r+O(1),
\]
so $v$, and then $n=n_0+Lv$, tend to positive infinity along the selected hits. So does $p=p_*+Vr$.

The exact Binet formula gives
\begin{align*}
 \log\frac{\psi_A(p)}{2\psi_P(n)}
 &=p\alpha_0-n\beta_0+c_0+\varepsilon_{p,n},\\
 \varepsilon_{p,n}
 &=\log(1-e^{-2p\alpha_0})-\log(1-e^{-2n\beta_0}).
\end{align*}
The error tends to zero. The positive distance between $I$ and the complement of $(\log Y,\log(Y+1))$ absorbs this error at every sufficiently large selected hit. Exponentiating proves \eqref{fup:ir:eq:ratio}.
\end{proof}
No density assertion about prime indices or Pell-valued indices is being used. The prime fixes the scale once; the simultaneous congruences leave a full ordinary arithmetic progression for $p$.

\section{All main and first witnesses are positive}\label{fup:ir:sec:mainwitnesses}
For each sufficiently large selected pair from Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:ratio}, define
\begin{align}
 c&=\psi_A(p),&D&=\chi_A(p),& k&=2\psi_P(n),&\tau&=\chi_P(n),\label{fup:ir:eq:mainchoices}\\
 \eta&=c-kY,&\zeta&=k-\eta,&
 \gamma&=\frac{D-ac-X}{H},&h&=\frac{k+p-1}{E},\label{fup:ir:eq:slacks}\\
 \sigma&=\gamma-\rho,&R&=-p.&&&&\notag
\end{align}
The strict ratio \eqref{fup:ir:eq:ratio} says exactly $0<c-kY<k$. Thus $\eta,\zeta$ are positive integers, $k=\eta+\zeta$, and $c=kY+\eta$. The two Pell norms imply
\[
 D^2-\Delta c^2=1,\qquad
 \tau^2-1=(P^2-1)\psi_P(n)^2=XY^2(XY^2+1)k^2.
\]
This establishes the two norms \eqref{fup:ir:eq:first} and \eqref{fup:ir:eq:mainnorm}.

By Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:projection} and \eqref{fup:ir:eq:pconditions},
\[
 D-ac=e_A(p)\equiv2^p\equiv X\pmod H.
\]
Hence $\gamma$ is integral. Moreover $e_A(p)>c$ grows exponentially with $p$, so $\gamma$ is eventually positive. The already fixed expression $\rho$ is affine in $p$. Therefore $\sigma=\gamma-\rho$ is also a strictly positive integer for all sufficiently large selected pairs. In particular the actual stronger source definition $\gamma=\rho+\sigma$, with both summands positive, holds exactly.

Because $P\equiv1\pmod E$, Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:pell} gives $\psi_P(n)\equiv n\pmod E$. Consequently
\[
 k\equiv2n\equiv1-p\pmod E,
\]
so $h$ is a positive integer and
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:restored}
 k-hE-1=-p=R.
\end{equation}
Thus $R$ is exactly the eliminated source's restored expression, not an independently imposed variable. Equation~\eqref{fup:ir:eq:packingnegative} is therefore its retained packing comparison.

\section{The complete strong auxiliary block}\label{fup:ir:sec:aux}
We now choose the four remaining auxiliary supplied coordinates $f,i,j,o$ and the Pell coordinate $y$, verifying their integrality, positivity, and all three retained auxiliary equations.

Since $A$ is even and $p\equiv3\pmod4$, Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:pell} makes $c=\psi_A(p)$ odd. Define
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:auxchoices}
 m=cp,\qquad f=\chi_A(m),\qquad
 \Qa=\Delta\psi_A(m),\qquad i=\Qa/c^2.
\end{equation}
By Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:divisibility}, $i$ is a strictly positive integer. The norm at index $m$ gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:strongverify}
 \Qa=ic^2,\qquad \Qa^2=\Delta(f^2-1).
\end{equation}
This is precisely the actual strong square required by \eqref{fup:ir:eq:strong}.

Let
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:oddindex}
 s_a=p+2m,\qquad b_a=(s_a-1)/2,\qquad
 U=\frac{\chi_{\Qa}(s_a)}{\Qa},\qquad y=\psi_{\Qa}(s_a).
\end{equation}
Both $m$ and $p$ are odd, and $p\equiv3\pmod4$, so $s_a\equiv1\pmod4$ and $b_a$ is even. Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:oddquotient} proves that $U$ is a positive integer. We need two exact congruences.

First, $c^2\mid\Qa$, so \eqref{fup:ir:eq:oddqzero} gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:Umodc}
 U=\mathcal Q_{b_a}(\Qa^2)\equiv s_a\equiv p\pmod c.
\end{equation}
Second, \eqref{fup:ir:eq:strongverify} gives $\Qa^2\equiv-\Delta=1-A^2\pmod f$. Therefore \eqref{fup:ir:eq:oddqeval}, with $b_a$ even, gives
\[
 U\equiv\psi_A(p+2m)\pmod f.
\]
The doubling formulas are
\[
 \chi_A(2m)=2f^2-1,\qquad
 \psi_A(2m)=2f\psi_A(m).
\]
Using the addition law \eqref{fup:ir:eq:addition}, we obtain
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:Umodf}
 U\equiv-\psi_A(p)=-c\pmod f.
\end{equation}
Define
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:jo}
 j=\frac{U-p}{c},\qquad o=\frac{U+c}{f}.
\end{equation}
Equations~\eqref{fup:ir:eq:Umodc}--\eqref{fup:ir:eq:Umodf} prove their integrality. For all sufficiently large selected $p$, one has $c>p$, $\Qa\ge c^2$, and $s_a\ge3$. Monotonicity of the positive Pell root then gives
\[
 U\ge\frac{\chi_{\Qa}(3)}{\Qa}=4\Qa^2-3>c>p.
\]
Thus $j,o$ are strictly positive. Since the restored index is $R=-p$,
\[
 U=jc+p=jc-R=of-c,
\]
which proves \eqref{fup:ir:eq:auxlinear}. Finally the norm at parameter $\Qa$ is
\[
 (\Qa U)^2-(\Qa^2-1)y^2=1.
\]
Rearranging and using $\Qa=ic^2$ yields
\[
 (ic^2)^2(U^2-y^2)=1-y^2,
\]
exactly \eqref{fup:ir:eq:auxnorm}. This completes the strong auxiliary block, including the sign that is essential for a negative restored index.

\section{Completion and domain audit}\label{fup:ir:sec:conclusion}
Every definition in Section~\ref{fup:ir:sec:interface} has now been checked. To collect the strict supplied-coordinate signs without suppressing a domain condition:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
 \item $J,w$ are positive integer powers or repunit expressions; $s$ is positive by the prime choice
 \item $\alpha,z,F$ are positive by \eqref{fup:ir:eq:outerconstants} and $q>2dx+1$
 \item $\delta>0$ by the odd input Pell congruence and strict growth
 \item $Z,\rho>0$ eventually by their positive affine slopes in $p$
 \item $\tau>0$ and $\eta,\zeta>0$ by the Pell norm and strict ratio
 \item $h>0$ by its positive integral quotient in \eqref{fup:ir:eq:slacks}
 \item $\sigma>0$ eventually because exponential $\gamma$ dominates affine $\rho$
 \item $f,i,j,o,y>0$ by the full auxiliary construction
\end{itemize}
These are precisely the nineteen coordinates in \eqref{fup:ir:eq:witnesses}. Finitely many eventual lower bounds on $p$ can be met simultaneously because Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:ratio} gives unbounded selected pairs. Equations~\eqref{fup:ir:eq:transport}--\eqref{fup:ir:eq:inputnorm} all hold, and therefore $F_{19}=0$. Yet \eqref{fup:ir:eq:restored} gives $R=-p<0$. Different selected values of $p$ yield different $Z$, whose affine slope is $1/Q>0$. Thus there are infinitely many distinct witness tuples at the same arbitrary fixed $x$. This proves Theorem~\ref{fup:ir:thm:main} and Corollary~\ref{fup:ir:cor:projection}.

The computed $\mu=\chi_A(u)$ is positive, while $W=1-Z$ is eventually negative. All supplied variables remain positive. Neither a signed input root nor a nonpositive supplied auxiliary coordinate is used to evade a hypothesis.

\subsection{What remains outside this result}
The positive21 child retains a positive supplied $W$ and a stronger positive input-gap structure; raw29 has its own positive supplied interface. The signed19 construction cannot be transferred to those domains because its $W$ is negative. The general kernel family alone also does not settle their additional outer conditions. The signed restoration failure established here should therefore not be relabelled as a failure of every complete74 presentation.

The proof does not derive $X=2^R$, an accepting typed word, or a no-wrap exponent estimate. Those are conclusions of a positive-index theorem whose sign hypothesis is unavailable here. It also does not produce a new universal Diophantine operation bound. The invalidated signed shortcut and the established positive parent occupy different mathematical domains.

\section{Proof provenance and reproducibility}\label{fup:ir:sec:repro}
The mathematical theorem is established by Sections~\ref{fup:ir:sec:interface}--\ref{fup:ir:sec:conclusion}. Finite arithmetic tests do not prove Dirichlet's theorem, the density step, eventual positivity, or the quantified conclusion. In particular they are not a numerical construction of one full giant witness tuple.

\subsection{Pinned source evidence}
The source definitions are taken from the signed-projection note \cite{fup:ir:signed}; the exact substitution, literal domains, and candidate costs are taken from the nonlinear-index scout and its independent source review \cite{fup:ir:scout,fup:ir:review}. The complete74 factored parent receipt supplies the literal arithmetic interface \cite{fup:ir:factored}. The half-binomial compiler note explicitly preserves powers-of-five $b,L,d$ and $B=2^d$ \cite{fup:ir:compiler}. The unchanged nonnegative sparse definitions of $D_C,D_R$ also support $K_0\ge0$; the packet includes the relevant compiler source files as inert text.

A later repository contribution at commit
\code{93ad43375a4a5610de0cfb25d64f84ec018d02f7} records the sign-free bootstrap $p\ge13$, positivity of the auxiliary argument, raw/positive exact-sign reductions, and a positive auxiliary realization of $R=-p$ at arbitrarily large indices \cite{fup:ir:newbootstrap}. We inspected that note and credit the overlapping auxiliary construction. It expressly leaves the full child/compiler system unresolved. Theorem~\ref{fup:ir:thm:main} goes beyond that auxiliary-only scope by satisfying the transport, literal packing, first norm, and complete signed input block simultaneously. This later contextual note does not change the earlier equation pin; its separate cached bytes and manifest are included under \code{context/}.

All sixteen cached upstream files have their repository paths, exact pinned URLs, SHA256 hashes, and Git blob SHA1 identities recorded in the packet's source manifest. The Git blob hash is computed as SHA1 of the canonical bytes \code{blob <length>} followed by a zero byte and the file bytes. Reading these files as text or JSON is distinct from executing the upstream programs. No upstream compiler, historical checker, or emitted arithmetic schedule was executed for this report, and no upstream repository file was modified.

\subsection{Independent proof review}
The full all-input proof was independently audited against the exact source interface, including all eight literal residuals, all nineteen signs, the shifted native mask, the CRT coprimality, the unrestricted linear main-index progression, and the strong auxiliary sign. The reviewed proof and audit are byte-bound in the accompanying evidence:
\begin{description}[style=nextline,leftmargin=1em,labelindent=0pt]
 \item[Proof SHA256]\small\code{b109e2fd1142cdad84a5b519acc5dc5055160f1d8e7617d648a561538fd956cd}
 \item[Independent audit SHA256]\small\code{cd62529a6efcdfcbab43f1426c6b3fe5930575fda2cc865f12c40a5ec6c3bda6}
\end{description}
The audit verdict is PASS for the full signed19 theorem stated here. The article supplies the supporting identities directly, rather than requiring the reader to combine chronological exploratory reports.

\subsection{Finite checks and limits}
The portable replay uses newly authored Python, the standard library, explicit checks that remain active with Python optimization, and exact-type receipt comparison. Upstream source bytes are only authenticated and read as data. Its identity gate rejects a changed packet before running the authored check program. Normal and optimized replay run from an unrelated working directory with isolated imports and temporary outputs.

The finite evidence comprises an exact recursive Lucas primality certificate for a 98-bit prime, scale and CRT coprimality checks, and 32 complete outer/input progression fixtures at toy fixed numerals. These toy masks are not represented as actual compiler exports. Additional bounded exact identities corroborate the elementary Pell and auxiliary congruence algebra: 451 main Pell/projection cases, 160 first-norm factoring cases, 25 coefficientwise odd-quotient identities and 200 evaluations, and six strong-auxiliary congruence cases. One small auxiliary-only block is materialized; it is not a full child zero. The replay does not numerically locate a density hit or materialize the full main and auxiliary coordinates. The unbounded proof applies to the actual arbitrary fixed exports; the toy fixtures test algebraic components only.

The packet README provides the exact replay commands and scope of each receipt. A failed identity or receipt check is an error rather than a silently regenerated expected result. The article's LaTeX source and a local build script are included; PDF compilation is separate from arithmetic replay and requires a standard LaTeX installation. The archive contains no third-party PDF cache and requires no network access to replay its arithmetic evidence.

\fupappendix
\section{A broader algebraic parameter theorem}\label{fup:ir:app:generalized}
The powers-of-five convention is sufficient for the actual-compiler result, but is not necessary for failure of restoration in the same algebraic interface. The following extension is separately independently reviewed. It does not assert that arbitrary numeral ports implement a compiler.

\begin{theorem}\label{fup:ir:thm:generalized}
Consider exactly the definitions and eight equations in Section~\ref{fup:ir:sec:interface}, but assume only $d\in\pos$, $B=2^d$, $b$ a positive odd integer, $K_0\in\Z_{\ge0}$, and arbitrary fixed integers $\MC,\MFsrc$. At every $x\in\pos$, there are infinitely many strictly positive supplied-witness zeros with restored $R<0$ and computed $\mu>0$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Choose any positive integer $N$ large enough that $q=B^N>2dx+1$, put $t=dN$, and retain $J=(q-1)/(B-1)$. Write
\[
 t=2^{a_2}3^{a_3}t_0,\qquad\gcd(t_0,6)=1.
\]
Because $\gcd(12,t_0)=1$, CRT provides $p_0$ with
\[
 p_0\equiv7\pmod{12},\qquad p_0\equiv1\pmod{t_0}.
\]
When $t_0=1$ the second condition is vacuous. Increase $p_0$ by a positive multiple of $12t_0$ until $p_0>3t$. This replaces only the specific choice $p_0=12t+7$ in the main proof. Every prime factor of $t$ either is two or three, or divides $t_0$. Therefore
\[
 \gcd(p_0,2t)=1,\quad p_0\equiv3\pmod4,\quad p_0\equiv1\pmod6.
\]
Set $X=2^{p_0}$ and $w=2^{p_0-3t}$ as before. The proof of Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:scalegcd} uses exactly these congruences, so $D_0=4q^3(X+1)/3$ is integral and coprime to $Q=q^2-1$. The primitive prime progression construction works without change.

There is one parity detail worth making explicit. Here
$D_0\equiv(-1)^t\pmod3$, which need not be two. No particular nonzero residue is needed: both $D_0$ and $s$ are units modulo $Q$, and $3\mid Q$, so $\ell-1=D_0s$ is nonzero modulo three. Since $\ell$ is also a unit modulo three, $\ell\equiv2\pmod3$ follows. Thus all coprimalities culminating in $\gcd(QH,T)=1$ remain valid.

The input $u=2dx+b$ is odd and at least three, so the same $\delta>0$ works without an upper bound on $b$. Also $F=K_0+X-q+1>0$. Arbitrary signs of the fixed masks affect only the constant
$M=(\MC+q\MFsrc)J$. CRT still accepts any resulting residue in \eqref{fup:ir:eq:pAP2}; the positive slopes of $Z,\rho$ in \eqref{fup:ir:eq:Zrho} are unchanged.

Finally $q,Y$ are even for every $t\ge1$, whereas $p_0$ is odd. Hence the quadratic-field separation in Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:irrational} remains true even when $t$ is even. The free progression, density interval, all Pell definitions, the exponential dominance over $\rho$, and the auxiliary construction use only the properties just established. Every subsequent step of Sections~\ref{fup:ir:sec:crt}--\ref{fup:ir:sec:conclusion} therefore applies and proves the claim.
\end{proof}
This extension changes the algebraic parameter range, not the main theorem's source attribution. For actual exports the paid mask remains $\MFsrc=\MFnat+B-1$ and all compiler conditions remain in force. The independent supplement review is bundled separately from the originally approved main proof and audit.

\section{The unresolved raw29 and positive21 domains}\label{fup:ir:app:raw}
This appendix records a separate audited necessary-condition reduction. It does not prove positivity of the restored index and does not construct a full negative zero in either domain. The source receipts call the parent modes \code{raw30} and \code{positive22}; after deletion the children have 29 and 21 supplied coordinates. Unlike signed19, both retain a positive supplied $W$ and a positive input-gap witness. The theorem of the main text has $W<0$ and therefore cannot settle these cases.

\subsection{Literal assumptions and imported bootstrap}
In addition to the common Pell and scale equations, a positive zero in either domain has
\begin{align*}
 q&=(B-1)J+1,& C+\alpha+2dx&=q,\\
 C&=Z+W,&(K_0+X)C&=F+z(q-1),\\
 R&=(q^2-Z-qF)(q^2-1)+(\MC+q\MFsrc)J,\\
 \kappa&=u+\delta\Delta,&c&=\kappa+\phi,\\
 \mu&=W+a\kappa+\rho H,&\mu^2-\Delta\kappa^2&=1,
\end{align*}
where $W,Z,\alpha,\phi$ and the supplied coordinates are strictly positive. In raw29, the appropriate raw quantities are supplied and the equations are retained comparisons; in positive21 the triangular quantities are computed positive expressions. Thus
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:rawouter}
 q\ge B\ge16,\quad0<Z<C<q,\quad0<W<C<q,\quad
 F<(K_0+X)C,\quad0<u<2q.
\end{equation}
The last bound uses the actual compiler's $b<B\le q$.

The sign-free bootstrap is an imported, independently audited prerequisite here, as also recorded in the later source note \cite{fup:ir:newbootstrap}; it is not needed for the constructive main theorem. Its precise conclusions are
\begin{align}
 c&=\psi_A(p),& p&\ge13\text{ odd},& R&\equiv\pm p\pmod c,\label{fup:ir:eq:bootstrapraw}\\
 k&=2\psi_P(n),&\frac{p+1}{2}&\le n\le p-1,&2n&\equiv R+1\pmod E,\notag\\
 c&>2p,&c&>A^{12},&P&=2XY^2+1.\notag
\end{align}
This bootstrap derives positivity of the auxiliary argument and the rank hypotheses without $R>0$. It invokes the ordinary strong norm, not a weakened replacement. We cite its rank/step-down argument rather than claiming to reprove it in this appendix; its reviewed snapshot and audit are included with the packet.

\subsection{Why the actual compiler gives the required coefficient bound}
The reduction needs $K_0<B^2$ for actual compiler exports; it would not be legitimate to impose that inequality on arbitrary evaluator ports. Here is the source provenance. Write $R_{\mathrm{rad}}=2^b$ for the inner radix, and $B=R_{\mathrm{rad}}^{L_{\mathrm{comp}}}$. If $S$ is the sum of the positive center coefficients, the complete76 sparse $D_C$ consists of four unit monomials, two copies of those coefficients, and at most one optional unit monomial. Every coefficient is therefore at most $2S+5$. All exponents, including the optional high monomial, are below $L_{\mathrm{comp}}$. The half-binomial wrapper raises the radix to at least
\[
 R_{\mathrm{rad}}\ge4(K+2)(2S+6)+8>2S+5,
\]
where $K$ is the number of native positions. Hence there are no radix carries and $0<D_C<B$. The inherited $D_R=R_{\mathrm{rad}}^{H_{\mathrm{comp}}}$ has $0<H_{\mathrm{comp}}<L_{\mathrm{comp}}$, so $0<D_R<B$ too.

The materializers are inherited from complete78 through complete77 and complete76. Complete77 changes only the masks/export wrapper. The sparse construction does not access the cached properties $B,D_C,D_R$ before the half-binomial wrapper raises the radix; hence the export contains no stale smaller-radix cached value. Its \code{new_constants} function changes only $\MC,\MFnat$ after materializing the other numerals. The literal alias chain identifies \code{Kconstant} with $D_C+BD_R$ and the paid \code{MF} with $\MFnat+B-1$. These facts are checked against the pinned source files in the separate raw/positive audit. Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:Kbound}
 0<K_0=D_C+BD_R\le(B-1)+B(B-1)=B^2-1\le q^2-1.
\end{equation}

\subsection{Only two exact index representatives survive}
The shifted-mask identity from Section~\ref{fup:ir:sec:interface} holds with $0<T'<q^2-1$ using only the native mask ranges and the repunit equation; it does not require $q=B^N$. It implies $R\ne0$ and $R<q^4$. If $R<0$, dropping the positive mask contribution in the original packing formula and using \eqref{fup:ir:eq:rawouter} gives
\begin{align*}
 -R&<(Z+qF)q^2\\
 &<Cq^3(X+K_0+1/q)\\
 &<CXq^3(1+1/q)\\
 &\le(q-1)Xq^3(1+1/q)<Xq^4.
\end{align*}
The third step follows from $K_0+1/q<q^2\le X/q$, by \eqref{fup:ir:eq:Kbound} and $X\ge q^3$. The positive branch already has $0<R<q^4<Xq^4$. Thus $|R|<Xq^4$ in either case. Since $A>XY\ge Xq^3$, one has $A^2>2Xq^4$, and \eqref{fup:ir:eq:bootstrapraw} gives $c>2Xq^4$. Both $|R|$ and $p$ are therefore less than $c/2$. The congruence $R\equiv\pm p\pmod c$ can have only its zero multiple of $c$, so
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:exactsigned}
 R=p\quad\hbox{or}\quad R=-p.
\end{equation}
Both alternatives are odd. Were $q$ odd, the repunit equation with even $B$ would make $J$ even; both terms in the literal packing formula would then be even. Hence $q$ is even, so $X,Y,A$ are even and $\Delta$ is odd. This is a parity conclusion, not a proof that $q$ is a power of two.

\subsection{A negative zero must lie in a bounded window}
If $R=-p$, the first-index congruence in \eqref{fup:ir:eq:bootstrapraw} becomes
\[
 2n+p-1=t_-E
\]
for a positive integer $t_-$. This quotient is not the supplied witness $h$ and is unrelated to the main proof's binary exponent $t=dN$. The interval for $n$ gives
\[
 2p\le t_-E\le3p-3.
\]
Combining with $p<Xq^4$ yields the necessary restrictions
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:negativewindow}
 E/3+1\le p<Xq^4,\qquad s<3q,\qquad1\le t_-<3q/s.
\end{equation}
Packing further requires
\[
 p=(Z+qF-1-q^2)(q^2-1)-T',\qquad F\ge q.
\]
At $F=q$ it requires $Z\ge2$. Thus $F<q$, a conclusion of the old positive-index proof, cannot be silently imposed here. The window is finite for fixed $q,X,Y$ and compiler numerals, but is not uniformly finite as the witnesses vary.

\subsection{The retained positive input gives an exact dichotomy}
Since the input root and coefficient are positive, the Pell norm supplies an index $v\ge1$ with $\kappa=\psi_A(v)$ and $\mu=\chi_A(v)$. For $A\ge2$ the unit $A+\sqrt{A^2-1}$ is fundamental: a smaller positive solution would have square-root coefficient at least one and root at least $A$. Thus the standard positive Pell parametrization applies. The retained $c>\kappa$ gives $v<p$. Also
\[
 p=|R|<Xq^4<\Delta,
\]
because $\Delta>(XY)^2\ge X^2q^6$. From $0<u<2q<A-1$, both $u$ and $uA$ belong to $(0,\Delta)$. The discriminant congruence of Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:pell} now gives
\[
 \psi_A(v)\equiv
 \begin{cases}v&v\text{ odd},\\vA&v\text{ even}\end{cases}\pmod\Delta.
\]
As $\kappa\equiv u\pmod\Delta$, uniqueness of the representatives in $(0,\Delta)$ proves
\begin{equation}\label{fup:ir:eq:inputdichotomy}
 v=u\quad\hbox{or}\quad v=uA.
\end{equation}
For the even case, multiply $vA\equiv u$ by $A$ and use $A^2\equiv1\pmod\Delta$ before selecting representatives. Since $u$ is odd and $A$ even, $uA$ is indeed even. On the negative branch this alternative forces $uA<p<Xq^4$, hence $uY<q^4$ and $us<q$.
\begin{writenote}
\emph{The same dichotomy in the research tree.} The tree's \rw{complete74_negative_index_refinement.md}, Section~2 (commit \texttt{813c1cff4}, 3 October 2026), proves for raw30 and positive22 that the input Pell index is exactly $e=u$ or $e=uA$, by the same discriminant-representative argument. It is later than this Report's pins and not cited by it, and this Report is not cited there: the two proofs are independent, and no priority is claimed either way. The tree's note goes further: on $r=-p$ it proves $4^d>X\ge q^3$ for the defect $d=2n-p$ (so $d\ge7$), and that for fixed $q,X,Y$ and a fixed bounded representative at most one main index $p$ satisfies the ratio interval. The programme's review of this Report notes the same coincidence.
\end{writenote}

The positive branch actually excludes $v=uA$: it has $p=R<q^4<A$, whereas $v=uA\ge A$. On $R=-p$, no such exclusion has been proved. The remaining projections are only
\[
 W\equiv2^v\pmod H,\quad0<W<q,\qquad X\equiv2^p\pmod H.
\]
Neither $p<\Delta$ nor $v<p$ rules out exponent wrapping. The unresolved task is to exclude or realize the negative branch while simultaneously meeting \eqref{fup:ir:eq:negativewindow}, \eqref{fup:ir:eq:inputdichotomy}, the exact Pell ratio, and every outer and input equation. This appendix establishes neither outcome.
\begin{writenote}
\emph{Re-scoped, 3 October 2026 (batch~83).} Report~37, printed as Part~\ref{fup:part:nz}, converts this task into an exact predicate: Theorem~\ref{fup:nz:thm:main} proves, at each fixed valid compiler slice and input $x$, that raw29 has a full positive zero with $R<0$ if and only if positive21 has one, if and only if the arithmetic predicate of Section~\ref{fup:nz:sec:predicate} passes for some $q,w$ (the only unbounded parameters) and finitely many choices of $s,t,p,e$; every passing predicate reconstructs full positive witnesses in both interfaces. The task stays open in its yes-or-no form: neither outcome is established there either. Part~V also sharpens the bound $us<q$ above to~\eqref{fup:nz:eq:even}, isolates a finite wrapped odd-input sector (Section~\ref{fup:nz:sec:sectors}), and gives a certified sub-unit Binet window (Theorem~\ref{fup:nz:thm:window}).
\end{writenote}

\fupstart{34}{Part IV: Report 34}
\fupsourcehead{Report 34. Height and Counting of a Signed19 Family: canonical witnesses with negative restored index}
\noindent\wtag\ Source~05 of batch~82: \emph{Research Report 34}, author line ``Mathematical research report'', dated 3 October 2026; archive \rw{Counterexample_Height_Expansions_and_Rotation_Discrepancy_Package.zip}; pins \texttt{2dde7850f} (construction) and \texttt{9fa99be93} (transfer appendix). Its sections, statements and equations are numbered 34.$k$ here.
\begin{sourceabstract}
We fix the input, compiler export, repunit and one prime-scale choice in the signed19 construction of Report 33, and count only its prescribed canonical auxiliary witnesses. Each main index in one Chinese-remainder progression admits at most one first-Pell index in the required strict ratio window. The maximum of the nineteen supplied coordinates is eventually the last auxiliary Pell coordinate, whose double logarithm has an explicit exponentially corrected expansion and a Lambert $W$ inverse. The height count satisfies $N(B)=\kappa\log\log B+O((\log\log B)^{1-\varepsilon})$ for some effective fixed-parameter $\varepsilon>0$. A logarithmic-form bound makes the exact selection eventually identical to a fixed irrational-rotation test. The resulting exact discrepancy term is unbounded by Kesten's theorem, ruling out a smooth two-term count with bounded remainder. The finer two-term law with $o(\log\log\log B)$ remainder is equivalent to a specific unresolved $o(\log R)$ discrepancy estimate. This is a theorem about one explicit subfamily, not the whole signed19 fiber.
\end{sourceabstract}
\begin{writenote}
Report~34 has no executable mathematical checker by design. \emph{(Corrected in batch~83: the batch-82 write said it had not been reviewed.)} The programme reviewed it in \rw{review_incoming_counterexample_heights.md} (commit \texttt{ceb7222e9}) and passes it within its scope (Section~\ref{fup:fm:sec:tree}). Its letters differ from Part~III's: here $\lambda=A+\sqrt\Delta$ and $\alpha=\log\lambda$; $\kappa$ is a counting coefficient and $\kin$ is Report~33's $\kappa$; $\asrc,\dsrc$ are Report~33's coordinates $\alpha,\delta$; and its paid mask is written $\MFsrc$ as in Report~33. The source's files are shipped with the prefix \texttt{05-signed19-heights-}.
\end{writenote}

\section{The result and the object being counted}\label{fup:hc:sec:result}
Report 33 proves that, for every positive ordinary input and every actual fixed compiler export in its pinned source, the complete74 signed19 index-eliminated child has infinitely many positive supplied-witness zeros with negative restored index~\cite{fup:hc:r33}. We study the size and number of one canonical family within that construction. Report 33 and its source are unchanged.

The term \emph{negative restoration} means that the omitted, algebraically computed index is $R=-p<0$. It does not mean that any of the nineteen supplied coordinates is negative. A negative computed intermediate is allowed by the signed presentation. Nothing here identifies a machine input's acceptance status or changes a positive-parent theorem.

Fix all construction data in Section~\ref{fup:hc:sec:family}. In particular the prime used to choose the scale is fixed once and for all. Write
\begin{gather}\label{fup:hc:eq:constants}
 \Delta=A^2-1,\qquad D_P=P^2-1,\qquad
 \lambda=A+\sqrt\Delta,\qquad \nu=P+\sqrt{D_P},\\
 \alpha=\log\lambda,\qquad \beta=\log\nu,\qquad
 L=E/2,\qquad V=TQH_0,\notag\\
 \delta=\log(1+1/Y),\qquad
 \drot=\frac{\delta}{L\beta},\qquad
 \kappa=\frac{\delta}{2\alpha V L\beta},\qquad
 C_0=\frac{\alpha}{2\Delta}.\notag
\end{gather}
All logarithms are natural. The growth logarithm $\alpha$ is distinct from the source coordinate $\asrc$; $\delta$ and $\kappa$ likewise differ from $\dsrc$ and $\kin$. The letter $B$ below is a height bound, whereas $B_{\mathrm{comp}}$ denotes the fixed compiler radix.

For each eligible pair $(p,n)$, let $\mathbf w(p,n)$ be the full tuple in Section~\ref{fup:hc:sec:family}, and put
\[
 \htop(\mathbf w)=\max\{\text{its nineteen supplied coordinates}\}.
\]
We count a sufficiently late tail $\mathcal C$ of these tuples, retaining all pairs in that tail which satisfy the exact congruence and strict ratio test. Define
\[
 N(B)=\#\{\mathbf w\in\mathcal C:\htop(\mathbf w)\le B\}.
\]
The tail begins at one fixed main-index progression representative $p_b$. Its onset may be enlarged to ensure positivity, height dominance and the exact rotation reduction. Finite changes to this convention affect $N$ by $O(1)$ only.

\begin{theorem}[Canonical family height and count]\label{fup:hc:thm:main}
For the fixed data just specified, the following statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item Each $p=p_b+Vr$, $r\in\Z_{\ge0}$, has at most one positive $n\equiv n_0\pmod L$ satisfying
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:ratio}
 Y<\frac{\psi_A(p)}{2\psi_P(n)}<Y+1.
\end{equation}
The proportion of progression terms admitting such an $n$ is $\drot>0$.
\item On $\mathcal C$, the height is exactly $y=\psi_{\qaux}(S)$ and strictly increases with $p$. With $b_0=(\log\Delta)/\alpha-1$,
\begin{align}\label{fup:hc:eq:heightmain}
 \log y&=C_0p^2e^{2\alpha p}\bigl(1+O(e^{-\alpha p})\bigr),\\
 \log\log y&=2\alpha p+2\log p+\log C_0
   +\sqrt\Delta\left(1+\frac{b_0}{p}\right)e^{-\alpha p}
   +O(e^{-2\alpha p}).\label{fup:hc:eq:heightfine}
\end{align}
\item If $B_j$ is the $j$th height in increasing order, then
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:leading}
 N(B)\sim\kappa\log\log B,\qquad
 \log\log B_j\sim j/\kappa.
\end{equation}
For some effectively computable $\varepsilon>0$ depending on the fixed data,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:effective}
 N(B)=\kappa\log\log B+O\bigl((\log\log B)^{1-\varepsilon}\bigr),\quad
 \log\log B_j=\frac j\kappa+O(j^{1-\varepsilon}).
\end{equation}
\item There is an exact fixed-rotation discrepancy $D(R)$, defined in Section~\ref{fup:hc:sec:exact}, such that, for $t=\log\log B$,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:decomposition}
 N(B)=\kappa t-2\kappa\log t+D(R(B))+O(1),\qquad
 R(B)\sim\frac{t}{2\alpha V}.
\end{equation}
The smooth all-height formula $N(B)=\kappa t-2\kappa\log t+O(1)$ is false. The weaker formula with $o(\log t)$ in place of $O(1)$ holds if and only if $D(R)=o(\log R)$; that condition is not established here.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}
The height inverse is given more precisely in Section~\ref{fup:hc:sec:inverse}. All constants and thresholds may depend on the fixed input, compiler, repunit and prime-scale choice. No parameter-uniform exponent or useful numerical onset is asserted. Equations~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:leading} and~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:effective} are statements on the double-logarithmic scale, not multiplicative equivalents for the heights.

\section{The canonical family and its source interface}\label{fup:hc:sec:family}
This section defines every tuple being counted. We inherit from the audited construction in Report 33 its integrality, positivity on a sufficiently late tail, and satisfaction of the full child equations. We recall the exact formulas and the relevant hypotheses rather than reproducing its whole existence proof.

\subsection{Fixed compiler and scale choices}
For an integer $D\ge2$ define the Pell sequences by
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:pell}
 \chi_D(v)+\psi_D(v)\sqrt{D^2-1}
   =\bigl(D+\sqrt{D^2-1}\bigr)^v\qquad(v\in\Z_{\ge0}).
\end{equation}
Their norm is $\chi_D(v)^2-(D^2-1)\psi_D(v)^2=1$.

Fix $x\in\pos$ and an actual compiler export from Report 33: $B_{\mathrm{comp}}=2^d\ge16$, with $d$ a power of five, $b$ positive and odd, $K_0\ge0$, and the fixed masks $M_C,M_F^{\mathrm{native}}$. Put $\MFsrc=M_F^{\mathrm{native}}+B_{\mathrm{comp}}-1$. All further compiler requirements remain those of the inherited source; no toy mask replaces these numerals.

Choose and fix a power of five $N_0$ such that $q=B_{\mathrm{comp}}^{N_0}>2dx+1$. Define
\begin{align}\label{fup:hc:eq:scale1}
 t_0&=dN_0,&p_0&=12t_0+7,& X&=2^{p_0},&Q&=q^2-1,\notag\\
 J&=\frac{q-1}{B_{\mathrm{comp}}-1},&w&=2^{p_0-3t_0},&
 \asrc&=q-1-2dx,&z&=1,\\
 F&=K_0+X-q+1,&M&=(M_C+q\MFsrc)J,& C&=1.&\notag
\end{align}
Thus $q=2^{t_0}$ and $X=wq^3$. Set $D_0=4q^3(X+1)/3$. The construction proves $D_0\in\pos$ and $\gcd(D_0,Q)=1$. Choose $s_0$ with
\[
 \gcd(s_0,Q)=\gcd(1+D_0s_0,Q)=1,
\]
and fix one prime $\ell>3$ in the primitive progression $1+D_0s_0\pmod{D_0Q}$, large enough that $s=(\ell-1)/D_0>0$. Dirichlet's theorem supplies such primes; this report neither varies nor counts them. Define
\begin{gather}\label{fup:hc:eq:scale2}
 Y=sq^3,\quad E=XY,\quad a=Y(X+1),\quad A=a+2,\quad
 H_0=4a+3=3\ell,\\
 \Delta=A^2-1=a^2+H_0,\qquad P=2XY^2+1,\notag\\
 O=\ord_{H_0}(2),\qquad T=\lcm(4,2E,O),\qquad L=E/2.\notag
\end{gather}
The inherited scale argument gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:crtcompat}
 \gcd(QH_0,T)=1.
\end{equation}
This fact is what allows a free main-index progression after imposing all outer constraints.

\subsection{Indices and all nineteen coordinates}
The positive odd input index $u=2dx+b\ge3$ is fixed. Set
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:inputfixed}
 \kin=\psi_A(u),\qquad \mu_0=\chi_A(u),\qquad
 e_u=\mu_0-a\kin,\qquad \dsrc=\frac{\kin-u}{\Delta}.
\end{equation}
Choose $p$ in the single simultaneous progression
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:crt}
 p\equiv p_0\pmod T,\qquad
 p\equiv-M-Q(q^2-qF+e_u-1)\pmod{QH_0}.
\end{equation}
By~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:crtcompat}, its step is $V=TQH_0$. In particular $p\equiv3\pmod4$. Fix $n_0\equiv(1-p_0)/2\pmod L$. Retain the pairs $(p,n)$ with $n\in\pos$, $n\equiv n_0\pmod L$, and the exact test~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:ratio}.

For each retained pair define
\begin{align}\label{fup:hc:eq:maincoords}
 c&=\psi_A(p),&D&=\chi_A(p),&k&=2\psi_P(n),&\tau&=\chi_P(n),\notag\\
 \eta&=c-kY,&\zeta&=k-\eta,&h&=\frac{k+p-1}{E},&R&=-p,\\
 Z&=q^2-qF+\frac{p+M}{Q},&\rho&=\frac{Z+e_u-1}{H_0},&&&\notag\\
 \gamma&=\frac{D-ac-X}{H_0},&\sigma&=\gamma-\rho,&W&=1-Z.&&\notag
\end{align}
The \emph{canonical auxiliary choice} in this report is exactly
\begin{align}\label{fup:hc:eq:auxcoords}
 m&=pc,&f&=\chi_A(m),&\qaux&=\Delta\psi_A(m),&i&=\frac{\qaux}{c^2},\notag\\
 S&=p+2m,&U&=\frac{\chi_{\qaux}(S)}{\qaux},&y&=\psi_{\qaux}(S),&&\\
 j&=\frac{U-p}{c},&o&=\frac{U+c}{f}.&&&&\notag
\end{align}
The supplied tuple, in source order, is
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:tuple}
 \mathbf w(p,n)=(J,F,\asrc,z,f,h,i,j,o,s,w,\tau,\eta,\zeta,y,Z,\dsrc,\rho,\sigma).
\end{equation}
All its coordinates are positive integers for sufficiently large eligible $p$. This is the construction theorem inherited from Report 33, Sections 4--9, and its independent audit~\cite{fup:hc:construction,fup:hc:audit33}. In particular, $\qaux/c^2$, $U$, $j$, and $o$ are integers by the divisibility and odd-quotient Pell identities proved there. Their integrality is not inferred from the asymptotic analysis below.
\begin{writenote}
Report~33's Sections~4--9 are Sections~\ref{fup:ir:sec:scale}--\ref{fup:ir:sec:conclusion} of this Part. The construction packet and audit cited here are shipped once, as source~10's \rw{10-index-restore-repro-evidence-FULL-SIGNED-COUNTEREXAMPLE.md} and \rw{10-index-restore-repro-evidence-INDEPENDENT-AUDIT.md}; source~05's copies, \rw{evidence/FULL-SIGNED-COUNTEREXAMPLE.md} and \rw{evidence/CONSTRUCTION-INDEPENDENT-AUDIT.md}, are byte-identical and not shipped.
\end{writenote}

There is no choice of a second auxiliary index, a different multiple of $pc$, another prime, or a new scale hidden in $N(B)$. Once $(p,n)$ is selected, every entry of~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:tuple} is prescribed. Distinct $p$ already yield distinct tuples because $Z$ is affine in $p$ with positive slope.

\subsection{Link to the complete child equations}
For clarity, the child consists of the sum of squares of the following eight residuals, with the computed definitions in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:scale1}--\eqref{fup:hc:eq:auxcoords} and $\mu=W+a\kin+\rho H_0$:
\begin{align}\label{fup:hc:eq:child}
 (K_0+X)C&=F+z(q-1),\notag\\
 R&=(q^2-Z-qF)(q^2-1)+(M_C+q\MFsrc)J,\notag\\
 \tau^2-XY^2(XY^2+1)k^2&=1,\qquad D^2-\Delta c^2=1,\notag\\
 (ic^2)^2&=\Delta(f^2-1),\\
 (ic^2)^2\bigl((jc-R)^2-y^2\bigr)&=1-y^2,\notag\\
 jc-R&=of-c,\qquad \mu^2-\Delta\kin^2=1.\notag
\end{align}
The restored expression is $R=k-hE-1=-p$. Also $c=kY+\eta$, $\gamma=\rho+\sigma$, $D=X+ac+\gamma H_0$, $\kin=u+\dsrc\Delta$, and $\mu=\mu_0>0$. Thus the family satisfies the whole signed19 interface, rather than just an auxiliary subsystem. The validity and positivity proof of these statements is the inherited theorem. Only the size and count of this prescribed family are newly analyzed here.

\section{The exact first index window}\label{fup:hc:sec:window}
The two quadratic fields are distinct. Indeed $A$ is even, so $\Delta$ is odd, while
\[
 D_P=4XY^2(XY^2+1)
\]
has odd $2$-adic valuation $2+p_0+2v_2(Y)$. The squarefree part of $D_P$ is therefore even and that of $\Delta$ is odd. Both define real quadratic fields, and their intersection is $\Q$. Consequently $\alpha/\beta$ is irrational: a rational relation would put equal positive powers of $\lambda$ and $\nu$ in that intersection, and the norm-one property would force such a positive rational power to be $1$, which is impossible.

Put $c_p=\psi_A(p)$ and
\[
 g=\log\frac{\sqrt{D_P}}{2\sqrt\Delta}.
\]
The hyperbolic Pell formulas make~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:ratio} exactly equivalent to
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:window}
 a_p<n\beta<b_p,\qquad
 a_p=\arsinh\frac{c_p\sqrt{D_P}}{2(Y+1)},\qquad
 b_p=\arsinh\frac{c_p\sqrt{D_P}}{2Y}.
\end{equation}
The derivative of $t\mapsto\arsinh(e^t)$ lies strictly between $0$ and $1$. Integrating across a logarithmic interval of width $\delta$ gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:width}
 0<b_p-a_p<\delta<L\beta.
\end{equation}
Here $\delta\le\log2$, $L\ge1$, and $\beta=\arcosh P>\log2$. Distinct allowed $n$ have spacing $L$, so at most one can satisfy~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:window}. This is an exact uniqueness result, before any limiting approximation. Both endpoints tend to infinity, and any large-index solution has
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:nsize}
 n=(\alpha/\beta)p+O(1)>0.
\end{equation}

For $v\in\{Y,Y+1\}$ define
\[
 \epsilon_v(p)=\arsinh\frac{c_p\sqrt{D_P}}{2v}
       -(\alpha p+g-\log v).
\]
Binet's formula and $\arsinh z=\log(2z)+(4z^2)^{-1}+O(z^{-4})$ give
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:endpoint}
 \epsilon_v(p)=\left(\frac{4v^2\Delta}{D_P}-1\right)e^{-2\alpha p}
                    +O(e^{-4\alpha p}).
\end{equation}
The $-1$ term is the Binet correction. It is essential to the first endpoint coefficient.

Write the main progression as $p=p_b+Vr$, $r\ge0$, and set
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:rotation}
 \theta=\frac{V\alpha}{L\beta},\qquad
 \xi=\frac{\alpha p_b+g-\log Y-n_0\beta}{L\beta},\qquad
 I=(0,\drot)\pmod1.
\end{equation}
The limiting interval contains a point of $n_0\beta+L\beta\Z$ precisely when
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:limtest}
 \{\xi+r\theta\}\in I.
\end{equation}
To check the phase convention, put $z=(\alpha p+g-\log Y-n_0\beta)/(L\beta)$. The condition is that some integer lies strictly between $z-\drot$ and $z$, equivalent to $0<\{z\}<\drot$.

Because $\theta$ is irrational, the proportion of fixed-interval hits is $\drot$. This transfers to the exact moving window without a logarithmic-form theorem: for any $\epsilon>0$, after finitely many indices the exact and limiting indicators can differ only within $\epsilon$-neighborhoods of the two endpoints of $I$. Equidistribution bounds the upper density of these discrepancies by $4\epsilon$. Letting $\epsilon$ tend to zero proves assertion (i) and positive density. This argument alone does not imply finitely many discrepancies; Section~\ref{fup:hc:sec:exact} supplies that stronger fact separately.

\section{The maximum supplied coordinate and its growth}\label{fup:hc:sec:height}
\subsection{Eventual exact height and no ties}
The strict ratio gives $k<c/Y$, hence $\eta,\zeta=O(c)$ and $\tau=O(c)$ by the first norm. Also $h=O(c+p)$; $Z$ and $\rho$ are affine in $p$; and $\sigma=O(c+p)$ by the formula for $\gamma$. The remaining nonauxiliary coordinates in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:tuple} are fixed. All these estimates have fixed-data implied constants.

For the auxiliary coordinates, $\qaux=ic^2$ with $i\ge1$, and
\[
 \qaux^2=\Delta(f^2-1),\qquad
 y\ge\psi_{\qaux}(3)=4\qaux^2-1>\qaux\ge c^2.
\]
Since $\Delta\ge3$ and $f\ge2$, we have $f<\qaux$ and $i\le\qaux$. The auxiliary norm is exactly
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:unorm}
 U^2=(1-\qaux^{-2})y^2+\qaux^{-2}<y^2.
\end{equation}
Therefore $j=(U-p)/c<y$ and
\[
 o=\frac{U+c}{f}<\frac{y+c}{2}<y.
\]
Finally $y\ge4c^4-1$ dominates every $O(c+p+1)$ coordinate because $c$ grows exponentially and $p=o(c)$. This checks all nineteen entries of~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:tuple}. Thus $\htop(\mathbf w)=y$ with strict dominance on a sufficiently late tail.

Extend the formulas to real $p\ge1$ by
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:realextension}
 \begin{gathered}
 c=\frac{\sinh(\alpha p)}{\sqrt\Delta},\quad m=pc,\quad
 \qaux=\sqrt\Delta\sinh(\alpha m),\quad S=p+2m,\\
 b_{\mathrm{aux}}=\arcosh\qaux,\quad
 y(p)=\frac{\sinh(Sb_{\mathrm{aux}})}{\sinh b_{\mathrm{aux}}}.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
Here $c(1)=m(1)=1$, $\qaux(1)=\Delta\ge3$, and $S(1)=3$. The functions $m$, $\qaux$, and $S$ increase strictly. For $S>1$ and $b>0$, $\sinh(Sb)/\sinh b$ increases in both arguments: the derivative of its logarithm in $b$ is $S\coth(Sb)-\coth b>0$, because $z\coth z$ increases on $(0,\infty)$. Thus $y(p)$ is strictly increasing, and the canonical family has no height ties after the fixed tail onset.

\subsection{An exponentially corrected expansion}
Put $d_0=\sqrt\Delta$ and $\ell_0=\log d_0$. From~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:realextension},
\[
 b_{\mathrm{aux}}=\alpha m+\ell_0+O(e^{-2\alpha m}),\qquad
 \log(2\sinh b_{\mathrm{aux}})=\alpha m+\ell_0+O(e^{-2\alpha m}).
\]
The logarithm of the numerator in $y$ contributes the remaining term $\log(1-e^{-2Sb_{\mathrm{aux}}})$, which is smaller. Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:logym}
 \log y=(2m+p-1)(\alpha m+\ell_0)
                 +O\bigl((m+p)e^{-2\alpha m}\bigr).
\end{equation}
The error is superexponentially small in $p$. Substituting
\[
 m=\frac{p}{2d_0}e^{\alpha p}(1-e^{-2\alpha p})
\]
gives the first two scales explicitly:
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:twoscales}
 \log y=C_0p^2e^{2\alpha p}
   +\frac{p}{2d_0}\bigl[\alpha(p-1)+\log\Delta\bigr]e^{\alpha p}
   +O(p^2).
\end{equation}
Divide by $C_0p^2e^{2\alpha p}$ and take the logarithm. The resulting first correction is
\[
 d_0\left(1+\frac{(\log\Delta)/\alpha-1}{p}\right)e^{-\alpha p},
\]
and the rest is $O(e^{-2\alpha p})$. This proves~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:heightmain}--\eqref{fup:hc:eq:heightfine}, including the coefficient $C_0=\alpha/(2\Delta)$.

\section{The real height cutoff and Lambert inversion}\label{fup:hc:sec:inverse}
Strict monotonicity gives, for every sufficiently large $B$, a unique real $p_B$ with $y(p_B)=B$. The exact height condition on the tail is $p\le p_B$.

Let $W$ be the positive real branch of the Lambert function, satisfying $W(z)e^{W(z)}=z$, and set
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:lambert}
 t_B=\frac1\alpha W\bigl(\sqrt{2\alpha\Delta\log B}\bigr).
\end{equation}
It solves the leading model exactly:
\[
 C_0t_B^2e^{2\alpha t_B}=\log B,\qquad
 2\alpha t_B+2\log t_B+\log C_0=\log\log B.
\]
Write $b_0=(\log\Delta)/\alpha-1$. Equation~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:heightfine}, with the positive derivative $2\alpha+2/p$ of the leading logarithmic model, first brackets $p_B-t_B$ by $O(e^{-\alpha t_B})$. Taylor expansion of the displayed smooth correction then gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:fineinverse}
 p_B=t_B-
 \frac{\sqrt\Delta(1+b_0/t_B)e^{-\alpha t_B}}{2\alpha+2/t_B}
       +O(e^{-2\alpha t_B}).
\end{equation}
The derivative of the first correction is $O(e^{-\alpha t_B})$ in this neighborhood; multiplying by the first bracket error produces the stated $O(e^{-2\alpha t_B})$ residual. In particular $p_B<t_B$ eventually.

A coarser expression is
\begin{align}\label{fup:hc:eq:inverseexp}
 p_B&=\frac1\alpha W\bigl(\sqrt{2\alpha\Delta\log B}\bigr)
   +O\left(e^{-W(\sqrt{2\alpha\Delta\log B})}\right)\notag\\
 &=\frac{\log\log B-2\log\log\log B+\log(8\alpha\Delta)}{2\alpha}
     +O\left(\frac{\log\log\log B}{\log\log B}\right).
\end{align}
The latter follows either by expanding $W$ or by substitution into the strictly increasing leading model. It is an inverse-height expansion, not a counting second term.

The difference $|p_B-t_B|$ is eventually smaller than $V$, so using $t_B$ instead of $p_B$ in a progression cutoff changes the count by at most one. It need not give equality at every jump. We keep $p_B$ in the exact floor formula below.

The density in Section~\ref{fup:hc:sec:window} already proves
\[
 N(B)\sim\frac{\drot}{V}p_B\sim\kappa\log\log B.
\]
If $p_j$ is the $j$th selected main index, then $p_j\sim Vj/\drot$. Inserting this in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:heightfine} proves $\log\log B_j\sim j/\kappa$. Only the double logarithm is substituted; an $o(p_j)$ index error must not be silently exponentiated into a multiplicative height equivalent.

% All-orders height and inverse refinement
\section{Height and cutoff expansions to every order}\label{fup:hc:sec:allorders}
The first correction above admits an all-orders refinement. It affects the continuous height and its inverse, without improving the rotation discrepancy. It is useful to separate a convergent analytic \emph{normal form} from the smaller remainder of the exact quantity.

Put $d_0=\sqrt\Delta$, $\epsilon=e^{-\alpha p}$, and define
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:AB}
 A_p=d_0(1-1/p),\qquad B_p=\frac{d_0\log\Delta}{\alpha p},\qquad
 G_0(p)=2\alpha p+2\log p+\log C_0.
\end{equation}
The symbols $A_p,B_p$ here are coefficient functions, distinct from the fixed Pell base $A$ and the height bound $B$.

\begin{proposition}[All orders at fixed parameters]\label{fup:hc:prop:allorders}
For each fixed integer $N\ge1$,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:allheight}
 \log\log y(p)=G_0(p)+\sum_{n=1}^N a_n(p)e^{-n\alpha p}
                         +O(e^{-(N+1)\alpha p}),
\end{equation}
where $a_n(p)$ is a polynomial in $1/p$ of degree at most $n$. Define
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:coefficientrec}
 S_0(C)=2,\quad S_1(C)=-C,\quad
 S_n(C)=-CS_{n-1}(C)+S_{n-2}(C),\quad
 a_n(p)=-\frac{S_n(A_p)+S_n(B_p)}n.
\end{equation}
For $t=t_B$ from~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:lambert}, the exact cutoff has, for every fixed $N$,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:allinverse}
 p_B=t+\sum_{n=1}^N c_n(t)e^{-n\alpha t}
                  +O(e^{-(N+1)\alpha t}).
\end{equation}
Each $c_n(t)$ is rational in $t$ and the fixed constants, bounded as $t\to\infty$, and analytic as a function of $1/t$ near zero. A triangular recurrence is given in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:inverserec}. Constants may depend on $N$ and all the fixed data.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The leading expression $F(p)=(2m+p-1)(\alpha m+\tfrac12\log\Delta)$ in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:logym} has the exact factorization
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:factorization}
 F(p)=C_0p^2\epsilon^{-2}
       (1+A_p\epsilon-\epsilon^2)(1+B_p\epsilon-\epsilon^2).
\end{equation}
Indeed, $m=p(\epsilon^{-1}-\epsilon)/(2d_0)$, so the two original factors respectively equal $(p/d_0)\epsilon^{-1}(1+A_p\epsilon-\epsilon^2)$ and $(\alpha p/(2d_0))\epsilon^{-1}(1+B_p\epsilon-\epsilon^2)$. Since $F(p)\sim2\alpha m^2$ and $m\ge p$, dividing the error in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:logym} by $F(p)$ and taking a logarithm gives
\begin{align}\label{fup:hc:eq:normalform}
 \log\log y(p)&=G_0(p)+\log(1+A_p\epsilon-\epsilon^2)
                  +\log(1+B_p\epsilon-\epsilon^2)+\mathcal R(p),\notag\\
 \mathcal R(p)&=O(e^{-2\alpha m}/m)=o(e^{-K\alpha p})
                                  \quad\text{for every fixed }K>0.
\end{align}

The roots $r_\pm(C)=(-C\pm\sqrt{C^2+4})/2$ satisfy
\[
 1+C\epsilon-\epsilon^2=(1-r_+(C)\epsilon)(1-r_-(C)\epsilon).
\]
Their power sums are $S_n(C)$ in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:coefficientrec}, and hence
\[
 \log(1+C\epsilon-\epsilon^2)
       =-\sum_{n\ge1}\frac{S_n(C)}n\epsilon^n
\]
whenever $|\epsilon|\max(|r_+|,|r_-|)<1$, taking branches that vanish at zero. For $p\ge1$, both $A_p$ and $B_p$ lie in a fixed bounded nonnegative interval. A constant $K_0'\ge1$ therefore bounds all four root moduli uniformly. For $|\epsilon|\le1/(2K_0')$, the sum of the two tails after order $N$ has absolute value at most
\[
 4\sum_{n>N}\frac{(K_0'|\epsilon|)^n}{n}
 \le\frac{8(K_0')^{N+1}}{N+1}|\epsilon|^{N+1}.
\]
This proves uniform convergence of the two-logarithm normal form. The recurrence shows that $S_n$ is a polynomial of degree at most $n$, proving the coefficient assertion and~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:allheight}.

For inversion put $p=t+u$ and $w_0=e^{-\alpha t}$. Define
\begin{align}\label{fup:hc:eq:Hnormal}
 H_t(u,w_0)={}&2\alpha u+2\log(1+u/t)\notag\\
 &+\log\bigl(1+A_{t+u}w_0e^{-\alpha u}-w_0^2e^{-2\alpha u}\bigr)\notag\\
 &+\log\bigl(1+B_{t+u}w_0e^{-\alpha u}-w_0^2e^{-2\alpha u}\bigr).
\end{align}
Introduce $z_0=1/t$, replacing $1/(t+u)$ by $z_0/(1+z_0u)$. The resulting function $H(u,z_0,w_0)$ is jointly analytic near $(0,0,0)$, using logarithms near $1$. It satisfies
\[
 H(0,z_0,0)=0,\qquad \partial_uH(0,z_0,0)=2\alpha+2z_0,
\]
which is nonzero near zero. The analytic implicit-function theorem supplies a unique analytic small root $\mathcal U(z_0,w_0)$ on a fixed neighborhood, with $\mathcal U(z_0,0)=0$. Its series in $w_0$ converges uniformly on a smaller polydisk and has uniformly bounded fixed-order remainders.

Explicitly, write $u_{<n}=\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}c_j(t)w_0^j$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:inverserec}
 c_n(t)=-\frac{[w_0^n]H_t(u_{<n},w_0)}{2\alpha+2/t}.
\end{equation}
Adding $c_n(t)w_0^n$ affects the coefficient of order $n$ only through the displayed linear derivative. Expansion of the rational functions, exponentials and logarithms proves inductively that these coefficients are rational in $t$ and the fixed constants; joint analyticity proves regularity and boundedness at $1/t=0$.

It remains to justify inversion for the exact height, rather than just its normal form. The already proved first-order inverse gives $u_{\mathrm{ex}}=p_B-t=O(w_0)$. Equation~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:normalform} gives
\[
 H_t(u_{\mathrm{ex}},w_0)=-\mathcal R(t+u_{\mathrm{ex}}).
\]
In this real neighborhood $\partial_uH_t\ge\alpha>0$ for large $t$. The mean value theorem bounds the distance from $u_{\mathrm{ex}}$ to $\mathcal U(1/t,w_0)$ by $\alpha^{-1}|\mathcal R(t+u_{\mathrm{ex}})|$. Since $m(t+u_{\mathrm{ex}})/m(t)\to1$, this is
\[
 O(e^{-\alpha m(t)}/m(t))=o(w_0^K)\quad\text{for every fixed }K>0.
\]
The uniformly convergent normal-form series therefore gives the actual fixed-order expansion~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:allinverse}.
\end{proof}

For reference, $a_1=A_p+B_p$ and $a_2=-2-(A_p^2+B_p^2)/2$, while
\[
 c_1(t)=-\frac{d_0[1+((\log\Delta)/\alpha-1)/t]}{2\alpha+2/t}.
\]
These reproduce the earlier first corrections. The convergent series sum to the \emph{normal forms}; the exact height and cutoff still differ by beyond-all-orders terms. Neither series is asserted to equal the exact quantity. Smooth expansions of arbitrarily high fixed order do not remove the counting discrepancy analyzed next.


\section{An eventual exact rotation count}\label{fup:hc:sec:exact}
We now strengthen the density argument using a standard effective theorem on linear forms in logarithms. For fixed positive algebraic numbers $z_1,\ldots,z_s$, there are effective constants $c,C>0$, depending on those numbers, such that every nonzero real form
\[
 \Lambda=\sum_{i=1}^s b_i\log z_i,\qquad b_i\in\Z,
\]
satisfies $|\Lambda|\ge c\mathcal B^{-C}$, where $\mathcal B=\max(3,|b_1|,\ldots,|b_s|)$. The sufficient statement is Theorem 1.1, equation (1.2), and its fixed-number consequence in Bugeaud~\cite{fup:hc:bugeaud}; Baker--W\"ustholz~\cite{fup:hc:baker} is a classical additional reference. No multiplicative-independence assumption is needed beyond nonvanishing of the particular form.

For $v=Y,Y+1$ and an integer $n$ near~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:nsize}, consider
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:boundaryform}
 \Lambda_v(p,n)=p\log\lambda-n\log\nu+
           \log\frac{\sqrt{D_P}}{2v\sqrt\Delta}.
\end{equation}
This form is nonzero. If it vanished, squaring the associated positive multiplicative equality would give
\[
 \lambda^{2p}=\frac{4v^2\Delta}{D_P}\nu^{2n}.
\]
Its left side lies in $\Q(\sqrt\Delta)$ and its right side in $\Q(\sqrt{D_P})$, hence both are rational. The norm-one property forces the positive rational $\lambda^{2p}$ to equal $1$, contrary to $p>0$.

The coefficients in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:boundaryform} are $O(p)$ by~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:nsize}. Thus, uniformly for either endpoint and all relevant large $p$,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:boundarylower}
 |\Lambda_v(p,n)|\ge c_1p^{-C_1}
\end{equation}
for effective fixed-data constants. This eventually exceeds the $O(e^{-2\alpha p})$ endpoint shifts in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:endpoint}. A disagreement between an exact and limiting indicator would require an allowed lattice point within that exponentially small distance of a limiting endpoint, contradicting~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:boundarylower}. Therefore only finitely many disagreements occur.

Enlarge $p_b$ within its progression beyond these exceptions and the positivity and height thresholds. Use the phase $\xi$ corresponding to this new $p_b$ in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:rotation}. For every $r\ge0$, the exact selection is now precisely~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:limtest}. Define
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:discrepancy}
 D(R)=\sum_{r=0}^{R-1}\mathbf 1_I(\{\xi+r\theta\})-\drot R
       \qquad(R\in\Z_{\ge0}),
\end{equation}
with the empty sum zero. In $D(R)$ and $R(B)$, $R$ denotes a nonnegative number of progression terms, not the restored coordinate $R=-p$ used in the construction. For all sufficiently large $B$,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:Rcut}
 R(B)=\max\left(0,1+\left\lfloor\frac{p_B-p_b}{V}\right\rfloor\right).
\end{equation}
The exact identity for this tail is
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:exactcount}
 N(B)=\drot R(B)+D(R(B)).
\end{equation}
This retains the fixed phase, the open strict-ratio interval and the true real cutoff. No asymptotic expression has been passed through a floor as if exact.

Combining~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:inverseexp} and~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:Rcut} gives, for $t=\log\log B$,
\[
 \drot R(B)=\kappa t-2\kappa\log t+O(1),\qquad
 R(B)\sim t/(2\alpha V),
\]
which proves~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:decomposition}. Changing to another finite initial convention merely adds a fixed count offset and does not remove the discrepancy.

\section{Effective errors and the second term obstruction}\label{fup:hc:sec:errors}
\subsection{A fixed parameter power saving}
For $h\ge1$, choose an integer $k_h$ nearest to $h\theta$. The nonzero form
\[
 hV\alpha-k_hL\beta
\]
has integer coefficients $O(h)$, and its nonvanishing follows from irrationality of $\theta$. The same logarithmic-form bound therefore supplies effective $c_2>0$ and $\mu\ge1$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:diophantine}
 \|h\theta\|\ge c_2h^{-\mu}.
\end{equation}
The exponent can be weakened to ensure $\mu\ge1$. The finite geometric sum gives
\[
 \left|\sum_{r=0}^{R-1}e^{2\pi ih(\xi+r\theta)}\right|
 \le\min\left(R,\frac1{2\|h\theta\|}\right)\ll h^\mu.
\]
The unnormalized Erd\H{o}s--Tur\'an inequality~\cite{fup:hc:et1,fup:hc:et2}, with an absolute implied constant, yields for each integer $K\ge1$
\begin{align}\label{fup:hc:eq:ET}
 |D(R)|&\ll\frac{R}{K+1}+
 \sum_{h=1}^K\frac1h
 \left|\sum_{r=0}^{R-1}e^{2\pi ih(\xi+r\theta)}\right|\notag\\
 &\ll\frac RK+K^\mu.
\end{align}
Taking $K$ of order $R^{1/(\mu+1)}$ gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:powersaving}
 D(R)=O(R^{1-\varepsilon}),\qquad \varepsilon=\frac1{\mu+1}>0.
\end{equation}
Since $R(B)$ is of order $t$ and $\varepsilon\le1/2$, the analytic $\log t$ correction is absorbed by this error. Equations~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:exactcount} and~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:decomposition} prove the counting part of~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:effective}.

There are no height ties, so $N(B_j)=j$. The leading law gives $t_j=\log\log B_j\sim j/\kappa$; substituting this into $j=\kappa t_j+O(t_j^{1-\varepsilon})$ proves the ranked error in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:effective}. This effective error may be very weak, and remains much larger than the scale $\log\log\log B$ of the putative second term.

\subsection{Why a bounded smooth remainder is impossible}
Kesten's interval bounded-remainder theorem states that an interval for an irrational rotation has bounded discrepancy if and only if its length belongs to $\Z+\theta\Z$~\cite{fup:hc:kesten,fup:hc:kelly}. It applies to a fixed phase: translating the interval absorbs that phase. Open versus half-open endpoint conventions change at most two orbit hits.

In our case $\drot\notin\Z+\theta\Z$. Otherwise integers $a_1,b_1$ would give
\[
 \delta=a_1L\beta+b_1V\alpha,\qquad
 \frac{Y+1}{Y}=\nu^{a_1L}\lambda^{b_1V}.
\]
The numbers $\lambda$ and $\nu$ and their inverses are algebraic integers, since their monic integer polynomials have constant term $1$. The right side is an algebraic unit. A rational algebraic unit must be $1$ or $-1$, whereas $(Y+1)/Y>1$. This contradiction proves the claim. Kesten's theorem now implies that $D(R)$ is unbounded.

As $B$ ranges through large real values, $R(B)$ attains every sufficiently large integer: take $B=y(p_b+V(R-1))$. The bounded residual in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:decomposition} cannot cancel an unbounded discrepancy. Hence
\[
 N(B)=\kappa\log\log B-2\kappa\log\log\log B+O(1)
\]
is false as an all-height assertion. The conclusion is about the claimed smooth remainder, not a failure of the leading law or of the precise height inverse.

\subsection{The exact unresolved criterion}
For this fixed rotation, the following statements are equivalent:
\begin{align}\label{fup:hc:eq:equiv}
 N(B)&=\kappa t-2\kappa\log t+o(\log t)
       &&\text{as all real }B\to\infty,\quad t=\log\log B;\notag\\
 D(R)&=o(\log R)&&\text{as integers }R\to\infty.
\end{align}
Sufficiency follows from~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:decomposition} and $R(B)\sim t/(2\alpha V)$. For necessity take $B=y(p_b+V(R-1))$. Then $R(B)=R$ exactly, and $\log t=\log R+O(1)$ by~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:heightfine}. Subtracting the smooth terms in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:decomposition} gives the required $o(\log R)$ estimate.

Unboundedness of $D(R)$ does not disprove this finer condition. An $O(\log R)$ bound alone would also be insufficient to identify the second coefficient, since its error has exactly that scale. Equidistribution supplies only $o(R)$, and the effective power saving~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:powersaving} is still too large. No $o(\log R)$ estimate is claimed.

\section{Comparison and further research}\label{fup:hc:sec:comparison}
\subsection{The scope differs from Report 25}
Report 25 classifies an entire fixed positive native fiber: seventeen supplied coordinates are forced, and all remaining tuples are parametrized by two Pell indices~\cite{fup:hc:r25}. Its height law follows from exact finite floor counts, exceptional baseline terms, hyperbola decomposition and Euler summation. In its notation the leading count is logarithmic in height, with a proved unsmoothed square-root second term and a smaller power error.

Here the input, compiler, repunit and prime-scale choice are fixed, but only the prescription $m=p\psi_A(p)$ and $S=p+2m$ is counted. The free main index runs through one CRT progression, selected by an irrational-rotation window. No classification of all admissible auxiliary indices or all signed19 witnesses has been proved. The count is double-logarithmic in height, and its second-term question is governed by the explicit discrepancy~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:discrepancy}. Report 25's two-index floor-sum error cannot be imported into this problem.

Pell growth, Binet expansion and careful height inversion are standard shared tools. The specific issue resolved here is the persistence of the exact rotation discrepancy through exponentially small strict-ratio endpoint perturbations, and the consequent bounded-remainder obstruction. This comparison is a statement of mathematical scope, not a literature-wide novelty claim.

\subsection{Concrete questions left open}
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{The finer discrepancy scale.} Determine whether $D(R)=o(\log R)$ for the explicit fixed $\theta,\xi,I$ in~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:rotation}. By~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:equiv}, this is exactly the missing estimate for the stated triple-logarithmic second coefficient. A result only on a subsequence or after averaging would not establish the all-height claim.
\item \textbf{Usable effective constants.} Specialize explicit logarithmic-form constants to one fixed compiler, input and scale, and bound the last possible endpoint disagreement. The present proof gives effectivity in principle, without a practical numerical exponent or onset. It does not materialize the enormous supplied tuples.
\item \textbf{Larger families.} Characterize other permitted auxiliary choices at a fixed main index, then count their union without duplication. The current canonical prescription offers a lower-bound subfamily inside the full fiber, not an upper bound or an exhaustive parametrization.
\item \textbf{Dependence on the scale.} Study how density and height constants vary over prime-scale choices, with genuinely uniform error bounds if possible. Summing fixed-scale asymptotics without such control is not justified by this report.
\end{enumerate}
The valid positive-index parent retains its positivity hypothesis. The separate raw29 and positive21 restoration questions remain outside the present theorem. No new universal operation count, degree bound, full numerical witness or machine-specific acceptance claim follows.
