\section{Transfer to asymmetric signed19 orientations}\label{fup:hc:sec:transfer}
A separate pinned source comparison~\cite{fup:hc:transfer} extends the applicability of this fixed family. At ProveIt commit
\begin{writenote}
The pinned sources of this appendix are research-tree files shipped inside archive~05 as byte copies and not shipped here: \rw{complete74_asymmetric_scale_transfer.md} and \rw{.json}, \rw{complete74_equation_orientation_census.md} and \rw{.json} (the latter 1,508,808 bytes), and \rw{review_complete74_equation_orientation_census.md}, all in the research tree's \rw{native-stream-queue/} directory and equal to the current files. The transfer proof itself is shipped as \rw{05-signed19-heights-evidence-TRANSFER-PROOF.md}. \emph{(Corrected in batch~83: the batch-82 write said Report~34 had not been reviewed.)} The programme's review \rw{review_incoming_counterexample_heights.md} (commit \texttt{ceb7222e9}) inspects all eight signed20 orientation sources and confirms this transfer to the eight matching signed19 children as a forward transfer only.
\end{writenote}
\begin{center}\small\code{9fa99be93fb479294d5d5a2458e59e5fba8444b6},\end{center}
the asymmetric scale replaces $X=wq^3$ by $X=wq$, retaining $Y=sq^3$. The supplied $w$ has no other consumer. Hence the coordinate map
\begin{equation}\label{fup:hc:eq:transfer}
 \Phi:\quad w_{\mathrm{new}}=q^2w_{\mathrm{old}}
\end{equation}
preserves $X$ and every subsequent computed quantity, including $R$, while leaving all other supplied coordinates unchanged.

There are four relevant equation orientations at each scale. Define
\[
 T_2=(ic^2)^2,\qquad K=\Delta(f^2-1),\qquad
 U_0=jc-R,\qquad U_1=of-c.
\]
Each orientation retains \emph{both} protecting comparisons $T_2=K$ and $U_0=U_1$, and selects its auxiliary norm from
\[
 C_e(U_v^2-y^2)=1-y^2,\qquad C_0=T_2,\quad C_1=K,
 \qquad(e,v)\in\{0,1\}^2.
\]
For each parent define the corresponding child by substituting $r=R=k-hXY-1$ everywhere and deleting only the resulting identical index comparison. These are mathematically defined descendants, not a claim that every eliminated packet has been published upstream.

For each \emph{matching} orientation, the scale map~\eqref{fup:hc:eq:transfer} preserves every residual, so
\[
 G_{(1,3)}^{(e,v)}(\Phi(\mathbf w))
       =G_{(3,3)}^{(e,v)}(\mathbf w)
\]
is an all-value polynomial identity over any commutative ring. Across \emph{different} orientations, the retained protecting comparisons give equality of comparison-zero loci over every commutative ring, and hence equality of sum-of-squares zero loci over the reals. This is not an assertion that the differently oriented sum-of-squares polynomials agree away from their zero sets.

The Report 33 tuples therefore give positive supplied-witness zeros with $R=-p$ in all four symmetric orientations and, after $\Phi$, all four asymmetric orientations. In the family of this report, $q$ and $w$ are fixed. The map changes just one fixed coordinate to another fixed value, while leaving $y(p)$ and every index-selection test unchanged. After increasing the finite height threshold if necessary, the maximum is still exactly $y$. All the canonical-family height and counting conclusions of Report 34 consequently transfer with the same constants and discrepancy, up to the finite-tail convention.

This uses only the forward map. It asserts neither an integral inverse on every asymmetric-child zero nor a classification of that child's full fiber. The valid twenty-coordinate parents still supply a positive $r$ and retain their nine comparisons. No conclusion for raw29, positive21, normalized87 or coupled88 is imported. No parent degree or operation ledger is assigned to these children.


\section{Evidence and the portable release}\label{fup:hc:sec:evidence}
The construction source is pinned to ProveIt commit
\begin{center}\small\code{2dde7850ffeb9c923a7bd92e3f2ee2b5af0e36ff}.\end{center}
The report was prepared from the frozen construction, its independent audit, and the separately audited analytic proof packet. The original reviewed analytic proof, before path normalization in the packaged copy, has SHA256 identity
\begin{center}\footnotesize
\code{ff9d116c90b058be082c2c9d6ee051cc}\\
\code{f537d80821c14d10b28aec9a96d5a533}.
\end{center}
The two lines concatenate to the complete digest. The whole-packet audit reports a mathematical pass for that exact version, including the effective error and the fixed-phase bounded-remainder application. \code{PROVENANCE.json} maps this original identity to the packaged digest and records every path-only portability normalization of the accompanying analytic notes.

The evidence has three distinct roles:
\begin{enumerate}
\item The inherited construction and its audit establish full child membership, positive supplied coordinates, and the fixed arithmetic progression.
\item The analytic proof and independent height, rotation and whole-packet checks establish the asymptotic formulas and external-theorem applications.
\item The local release verifier checks file identities; the offline build script rebuilds the PDF from the supplied LaTeX. These are packaging checks, not finite tests of the unbounded mathematical assertions.
\end{enumerate}
No executable mathematical checker is included or needed for this pure analytic follow-on. No upstream code, source arithmetic schedule or historical finite checker was run to establish its claims. The source component checks are analytic notes. No third-party paper PDF is redistributed in the archive; the bibliography links to the research sources. The report is a new release and does not alter Report 33.

\fupstart{37}{Part V: Report 37}
\part{Exact full negative zero obstruction for positive Diophantine interfaces}\label{fup:part:nz}
\noindent\wtag\ Source~15 of this report, manuscript~09 of batch~83: \emph{Research Report 37}, subtitled ``Deterministic packing recovery and complete positive witness reconstruction'', author line ``Mathematical research report'', dated 3 October 2026; archive \rw{Exact_Negative_Index_Obstruction_for_Positive_Diophantine_Interfaces_Package.zip} (arrival commit \texttt{3051d1446}, placed by \texttt{a51a439cd}); pin \texttt{d5bd4a67b}, targeted refinement \texttt{813c1cff4}, freshness check \texttt{0f7d618f4}. Its sections, statements and equations are numbered 37.$k$ here.
\begin{sourceabstract}
For the fixed-compiler raw29 and positive21 nonlinear-index interfaces, we give a necessary-and-sufficient predicate for a full strictly positive supplied tuple whose restored index is negative. The predicate completes the previously established reduction to two unbounded scale parameters. At each scale, a bounded representative determines at most one possible main Pell index. Packing then forces the marker and field word, and either of two input indices determines the remaining input marker. Every passing predicate admits explicit positive witnesses for all outer, first, main, input, strong and auxiliary equations. A necessary Binet window, intersected with the admissible index range, has length less than $q^{-3}+q^{-5}<1$ and admits outward rational evaluation. The odd-input wrapped sector is finite for each fixed compiler and input; the even-input sector obeys stronger bounds involving the actual outer marker. These results neither prove global restored-index positivity nor construct a full negative zero. Exact finite tests and separate audits corroborate the algebra and certified interval exclusions; they are not valid-compiler counterexamples or an unbounded search certificate.
\end{sourceabstract}
\begin{writenote}
\emph{What the title means.} The ``obstruction'' of the title is an exact necessary-and-sufficient predicate (Theorem~\ref{fup:nz:thm:main}), not a proof that no negative zero exists. Whether the predicate ever passes is open: a full negative zero of raw29 or positive21 is neither constructed nor excluded.

\emph{Place in this report.} Report~37 continues Appendix~\ref{fup:ir:app:raw} of Report~33, whose closing task (``to exclude or realize the negative branch while simultaneously meeting'' the window, the input dichotomy, the exact Pell ratio and every outer and input equation) it converts into this predicate with a complete positive inverse witness map. That task is therefore re-scoped, not closed (dated note at the end of Appendix~\ref{fup:ir:app:raw}). Its evidence packet carries two of Report~33's own evidence files as context, and its Section~\ref{fup:nz:sec:prior} restates Appendix~33.B and the research tree's refinement note; that section is printed as a recap, with pointers. Report~37 is printed as its own Part rather than as a third source of Part~IV because Part~IV is about the signed19 child, whereas Report~37 treats only raw29 and positive21.

\emph{The programme's review.} \rw{review_exact_negative_obstruction37.md} in the research tree's \rw{native-stream-queue/} directory (commit \texttt{308f14072}, with the helper \rw{review_exact_negative_obstruction37.py} and its receipt \rw{.json}) gives ``PASS within the stated fixed-compiler scope'': the predicate is necessary and sufficient and closes the conditional witness-reconstruction obligations, but proves neither that it has a solution nor that it has none (``Those two positive inverse problems remain unresolved''). Its scope precisions follow Theorem~\ref{fup:nz:thm:main}; its separation from the programme's universal polynomial follows Lemma~\ref{fup:nz:lem:packing}.

\emph{Notation in this Part.} Report~37 uses Report~33's letters (Section~\ref{fup:ir:sec:interface}) with these differences: $\dcell$ is Report~33's $d$ in $B=2^d$; $\zquot$ is Report~33's transport quotient $z$; the paid and native masks are rendered $\MFsrcV$ and $\MFnatV$, the objects written $\MFsrc$ and $\MFnat$ in Part~IV; $\Taux$ and $\ell$ are Report~33's $\Qa$ and $s_a$; the first-representative quotient $t$ is Report~33's $t_-$ (Appendix~\ref{fup:ir:app:raw}), not the binary exponent $t=dN$ of Section~\ref{fup:ir:sec:scale}; the input Pell index $e$ is Report~33's $v$ in Appendix~\ref{fup:ir:app:raw}; and $W$ is the positive supplied input marker. To keep one meaning per letter inside this Part, the merge renamed these symbols of the source (renderings otherwise unchanged, no normalization changed): $E^*_r\to\mathcal E_A(r)$, which is Report~33's $\ea(r)$ ($E$ stays $XY$); $Q_{h_0},h_0\to\mathcal Q_\theta,\theta$, which are Report~33's $\mathcal Q_b,b_a$ ($h$ stays the supplied quotient and $Q$ stays $q^2-1$); $a_\pm(V)$, $a_\pm$, $b_\pm\to\omega_\pm(V)$, $\omega^A_\pm$, $\omega^P_\pm$ ($a$ stays the register $Y(X+1)$ and $b$ the odd compiler constant); $r_\pm\to\xi_\pm$; $\alpha_*,\beta_*\to\lambda_A,\lambda_P$ ($\alpha$ stays the raw slack; $\lambda_A$ is Part~IV's unit $\lambda$); $L(p),U(p)\to\Lambda_-(p),\Lambda_+(p)$ ($L$ stays the compiler exponent, $U$ the auxiliary root); $J_{\log}\to I_{\mathrm{Bin}}$ ($J$ stays the repunit coordinate); and in the logarithm series of Section~\ref{fup:nz:sec:window} $r,k,t,z,j,N\to\vartheta,\nu,\vartheta_0,z_0,l,N_{\mathrm s}$ and the endpoints $l,h\to p_{\mathrm{lo}},p_{\mathrm{hi}}$, since $k,t,j,N,h$ are witnesses or quotients elsewhere in this Part. The inner radix $R_{\mathrm{rad}}=2^b$ and the defect $d_{\mathrm{def}}=2n-p$ keep the source's subscripts; $R$ alone is the restored index. Section~\ref{fup:fm:sec:notation} lists the letters that differ between Parts.

The source's files are shipped with the prefix \texttt{15-neg-obstruction-}.
\end{writenote}

\section{Conclusion and exact scope}\label{fup:nz:sec:scope}
The unresolved question is whether deleting an explicit positive-index coordinate from the two positive interfaces can introduce a full positive zero with a negative restored index. Earlier work reduced a putative negative zero to $R=-p$, where $p$ is the positive main Pell index, and bounded all index choices after two scale parameters are fixed. Necessary conditions alone did not settle whether omitted input or auxiliary sign constraints could prevent a reconstruction.

This report closes that reconstruction gap. A passing arithmetic predicate gives a complete strictly positive witness in \emph{both} interfaces, and every negative zero gives a passing predicate. The remaining problem is exactly the simultaneous feasibility of that predicate. Its emptiness and nonemptiness are both unresolved.

The source receipt uses the inherited mode names \code{raw30} and \code{positive22}. After the index elimination their supplied lists have respectively 29 and 21 positive coordinates; we call those children \emph{raw29} and \emph{positive21}. Their saved finalizers are sums of squared integer residuals. The signed19 child has different input-sign freedoms and is excluded throughout. A signed-interface negative construction does not supply the bounded positive markers required here.

\paragraph{What is new and what is inherited.}
The two-unbounded-parameter reduction, exact alternatives $R=\pm p$, evenness of $q$, the two input indices, defect exclusion, and at-most-one main index are prior results. The new conclusions are deterministic packing recovery, a full-zero equivalence with every sign obligation discharged, the finite wrapped-odd-input sector, the stronger even-input bounds, and a quantitative sub-unit necessary real window. The all-odd-index auxiliary construction is explicitly credited to the prior bootstrap. No operation count, universal polynomial bound, or new witness bound is claimed.
\begin{writenote}
In this report the prior results are Appendix~\ref{fup:ir:app:raw} of Report~33 and the research tree's \rw{complete74_negative_index_refinement.md} (commit \texttt{813c1cff4}), Sections~2--4; the bootstrap is the tree's \rw{review_complete74_nonlinear_index_bootstrap.md} (commit \texttt{93ad43375}). Section~\ref{fup:nz:sec:prior} gives the correspondence.
\end{writenote}

\paragraph{Source boundary.}
The mathematical target is the pinned ProveIt projection~\cite{fup:nz:projection} at commit
\begin{center}\small\code{d5bd4a67b41b89688a079997574a94bcd855bb83}.\end{center}
The five source files and their immutable URLs, byte counts, SHA256 digests and Git blob identities are supplied in \code{evidence/source_manifest.json}. The targeted refinement was last changed at commit \code{813c1cff42f9238a0158228f5e003a6a5405444a}. A separate freshness receipt records that all five relevant blobs were unchanged at the later inspected tip \code{0f7d618f43bcb6361cee432273ba644e7defd951}. This is a time-stamped source check, not a promise about future upstream changes. No upstream Python module or saved arithmetic schedule was executed for this report. Their role is read-only source evidence.
\begin{writenote}
The source manifest and the freshness receipt are shipped as \rw{data/15-neg-obstruction-evidence-source_manifest.json} and \rw{data/15-neg-obstruction-evidence-final_source_freshness.json}. The five source files themselves are byte copies of research-tree files and are not shipped; the bibliography of this Part gives their repository paths. At this write all five are byte-identical to the current files, and \texttt{d5bd4a67b} is an ancestor of the commit at which this Part was written.
\end{writenote}

\section{Fixed compiler contract and literal equations}\label{fup:nz:sec:contract}
\subsection{A genuine compiler slice rather than arbitrary numeral ports}
Fix a valid export of the complete75 modified half-binomial compiler~\cite{fup:nz:compiler} and an ordinary positive integer input $x$. Its fixed integers include
\[
 B=2^{\dcell},\qquad K_0=D_C+BD_R,\qquad \MC,\ \MFnatV,
 \qquad \MFsrcV=\MFnatV+B-1.
\]
The valid compiler contract, not a free choice of mask numerals, gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:compilerbounds}
 B\ge16,\quad 1\le b<B,\quad b\text{ odd},\quad
 0<D_C,D_R<B,\quad K_0\le B^2-1,\quad
 0<\MC,\MFnatV<B-1.
\end{equation}
Put
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:u}
 u=2\dcell x+b.
\end{equation}
Thus $u$ is odd and $u\ge3$.

For clarity, the fixed source recipe retains $b,L,\dcell=bL$ as powers of five, an inner radix $R_{\mathrm{rad}}=2^b$, and $B=R_{\mathrm{rad}}^L$. It retains the original separated native layout, selectors, duplicated center-clause bands, four anchors, and the optional high monomial ensuring $2D_C-D_R\not\equiv0\pmod5$. If $e_*$ is the specified ignored dummy exponent, the modified masks are
\[
 \MC=\MC^{\mathrm{old}}-2R_{\mathrm{rad}}^{e_*},\qquad
 \MFnatV=M_F^{\mathrm{old}}+4.
\]
The fixed radix is enlarged until
\[
 R_{\mathrm{rad}}\ge
 \max\!\left(4(K+2)\left(2\sum_e c_e+6\right)+8,\ 2\mu_{\mathrm{mask}}+4,\ 16\right).
\]
Here $K,c_e,\mu_{\mathrm{mask}}$ are the native compiler's support size, center coefficients and clause mask, not varying witnesses. The export is the modified \code{new_constants(compile_windows(...))} interface. Using only an inherited baseline \code{constants()} object would select the wrong masks. In particular the native bound $\MFnatV<B-1$ must not be applied to the shifted source coefficient $\MFsrcV$.

The source contract and sign-free bootstrap are inherited mathematical premises. This report is self-contained for the new obstruction and reconstruction under those premises; it does not reprove the fixed machine's complete compiler theorem or the generic ordinary auxiliary rank theorem. The supplied prior reduction explains the genuine coefficient bound: the sparse $D_C$ coefficients remain below the enlarged radix, its support and the $D_R$ exponent remain below $L$, and the modified export changes only the masks. Thus $D_C,D_R<B$ is provenance from the actual compiler, not a numerical-port assumption silently substituted for it.
\begin{writenote}
The coefficient bound is the one derived in Section~33.B (``Why the actual compiler gives the required coefficient bound'', equation~\eqref{fup:ir:eq:Kbound}), where $L$ is written $L_{\mathrm{comp}}$; in the radix bound the summation index $e$ and the coefficients $c_e$ are the compiler's, unrelated to the input index $e$ and the coordinate $c$ below. The programme's review stresses the same point: $K_0<B^2$ is a property of the actual numerals, not an assumption on arbitrarily chosen numeral ports.
\end{writenote}

\subsection{Scale notation and Pell sequences}
For positive scale coordinates $J,q,w,s$, set
\begin{align}\label{fup:nz:eq:scales}
 q&=(B-1)J+1,& X&=wq^3,&Y&=sq^3,&E&=XY,\\
 a&=Y(X+1),& A&=a+2,&\Delta&=A^2-1,&H&=4a+3,\\
 P&=2XY^2+1,&Q&=q^2-1,&M&=(\MC+q\MFsrcV)J.&&\nonumber
\end{align}
For every integer base $V\ge2$, define the integer Pell sequences by
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:pell}
 \chi_V(r)+\psi_V(r)\sqrt{V^2-1}
       =\left(V+\sqrt{V^2-1}\right)^r\qquad(r\ge0).
\end{equation}
They have initial values $\chi_V(0)=1$, $\chi_V(1)=V$, $\psi_V(0)=0$, $\psi_V(1)=1$ and recurrence $f(r+1)=2Vf(r)-f(r-1)$. Thus every comparison below is an effective comparison of integers, however large those integers may be.

The mathematical base $A=a+2$ must not be confused with the saved source register named \code{A}, which represents $\Delta$. The saved source name \code{d} represents the positive main root $D$, and \code{Jrep} represents $J$.

\subsection{The complete retained system}
The restored index is the expression $R=k-hE-1$, not a separately supplied positive coordinate. The outer comparisons are
\begin{align}\label{fup:nz:eq:outer}
 q&=(B-1)J+1,& C+\alpha+u-b&=q,\\
 (K_0+X)C&=F+\zquot(q-1),&C&=Z+W,\\
 R&=(q^2-Z-qF)Q+M.&&\nonumber
\end{align}
The first and main blocks impose
\begin{align}\label{fup:nz:eq:firstmain}
 \tau^2-XY^2(XY^2+1)k^2&=1,&c&=kY+\eta,&k&=\eta+\zeta,\\
 a&=Y(X+1),&D&=X+ac+\ga H,&D^2-\Delta c^2&=1.\nonumber
\end{align}
Writing $T=ic^2$ and $U=jc-R$, the ordinary strong and auxiliary comparisons are
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:auxsystem}
 T^2=\Delta(f^2-1),\qquad
 T^2(U^2-\yaux^2)=1-\yaux^2,\qquad U=of-c.
\end{equation}
Finally the entire input block is
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:inputsystem}
 \kappa=u+\delta\Delta,\qquad c=\kappa+\phi,\qquad
 \mu^2=1+\Delta\kappa^2,\qquad
 \mu=W+a\kappa+\rho H.
\end{equation}
All 29 supplied coordinates in the raw mode are strictly positive integers. In positive21, eight coordinates are eliminated by triangular source expressions; the retained comparisons and supplied positive coordinates force their same positive values. In particular the main quotient $\ga$ is independent of $\rho$. The split $\ga=\rho+\sigma$ belongs to the signed source and is not an equation of these two interfaces.
\begin{writenote}
Equation~\eqref{fup:nz:eq:outer} writes the raw bound as $C+\alpha+u-b=q$; Section~33.B writes the same equation as $C+\alpha+2dx=q$, since $u-b=2\dcell x$. The signed19 system of Section~\ref{fup:ir:sec:interface} instead has $\gamma=\rho+\sigma$ and a computed, possibly negative, $W=C-Z$.
\end{writenote}

\section{Inherited reduction and bounded indices}\label{fup:nz:sec:prior}
\begin{writenote}
\emph{A recap, not new.} This section restates, with the source's own credit, results already printed in this report or proved in the research tree. Equations~\eqref{fup:nz:eq:bootstrap}--\eqref{fup:nz:eq:firstclass} are the imported sign-free bootstrap~\eqref{fup:ir:eq:bootstrapraw} of Section~33.B, from the tree's \rw{review_complete74_nonlinear_index_bootstrap.md} (commit \texttt{93ad43375}). Equation~\eqref{fup:nz:eq:shifted} is the shifted-mask identity of Section~\ref{fup:ir:sec:interface}. Equation~\eqref{fup:nz:eq:absolute}, the exact alternatives $R=\pm p$ and the parity paragraph are the chain before~\eqref{fup:ir:eq:exactsigned}, equation~\eqref{fup:ir:eq:exactsigned} itself and the paragraph after it. Equations~\eqref{fup:nz:eq:discriminant}--\eqref{fup:nz:eq:twoinput} are the input dichotomy~\eqref{fup:ir:eq:inputdichotomy}, where the index is called $v$; it is also proved, independently, in Section~2 of the tree's \rw{complete74_negative_index_refinement.md} (commit \texttt{813c1cff4}; see the note after~\eqref{fup:ir:eq:inputdichotomy}). Equation~\eqref{fup:nz:eq:representative} and the bounds after it are the window~\eqref{fup:ir:eq:negativewindow}, where $t$ is written $t_-$. Equation~\eqref{fup:nz:eq:defect} and its argument are Section~3 of the refinement note, and the uniqueness of Lemma~\ref{fup:nz:lem:unique} is its Section~4. The ``prior raw/positive reduction'' and ``prior independent review'' that the source cites~\cite{fup:nz:prior} are Report~33's own evidence files (bibliography). The section is printed in full because the later sections cite its equations.
\end{writenote}
\subsection{The bootstrap input}
The prior sign-free bootstrap and its independent review~\cite{fup:nz:bootstrap,fup:nz:prior} classify every positive child zero as follows:
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:bootstrap}
 c=\psi_A(p),\quad D=\chi_A(p),\quad p\ge13\text{ odd},\qquad
 R\equiv p\text{ or }-p\pmod c,
\end{equation}
with $c>2p$ and $c>A^{12}$. It classifies the first norm by
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:firstclass}
 k=2\psi_P(n),\qquad \tau=\chi_P(n),\qquad
 \frac{p+1}{2}\le n\le p-1.
\end{equation}
The ratio $Y<c/k<Y+1$ supplies the index range. The ordinary strong norm and the mixed-sign auxiliary analysis, with its positive root checked before classification, supply the rank congruence. These are conclusions of the cited bootstrap, not consequences asserted from a finite sample.

\subsection{Exact signs and input representatives}
The shifted-mask identity is
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:shifted}
 R=(q^2-S')Q+T',\quad S'=Z+qF-1,\quad
 T'=\MC J+1+q(\MFnatV J-1),\quad 0<T'<Q.
\end{equation}
It gives $R\ne0$ and $R<q^4$. On the negative branch the actual positive outer markers, transport, and \eqref{fup:nz:eq:compilerbounds} sharpen the other direction to
\begin{align}\label{fup:nz:eq:absolute}
 -R&<(Z+qF)q^2<Cq^3(X+K_0+1/q)\nonumber\\
 &<CXq^3(1+1/q)<Xq^4.
\end{align}
The third comparison uses $K_0+1/q<q^2\le X/q$. Since $A>XY\ge Xq^3$, the Pell growth gives $c>2Xq^4$. Hence $|R|<c/2$ and $p<c/2$ turn \eqref{fup:nz:eq:bootstrap} into the exact alternatives $R=p$ or $R=-p$.

If $q$ were odd, its repunit equation with even $B$ would force $J$ even. Both terms in the original packing expression would then be even, contradicting odd $R=\pm p$. Therefore $q$ is even; so are $X,Y,A$, while $\Delta$ is odd. This does not prove that $q$ is a power of two.

The positive input norm gives $\kappa=\psi_A(e)$ and $\mu=\chi_A(e)$ for an integer $0<e<p$, using the positive gap $c>\kappa$. The recurrence modulo $\Delta$ gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:discriminant}
 \psi_A(r)\equiv
 \begin{cases}r& r\text{ odd},\\rA&r\text{ even}\end{cases}\pmod\Delta.
\end{equation}
The established size bounds give $p<Xq^4<\Delta$ and $0<u,uA<\Delta$. Reducing $\kappa=u+\delta\Delta$ and, in the even case, multiplying by $A$ proves
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:twoinput}
 e=u\quad\text{or}\quad e=uA.
\end{equation}
Here $u$ is odd and $uA$ is even. Both alternatives must be retained.

\subsection{The first representative and prior defect exclusion}
On $R=-p$, the first-index congruence is
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:representative}
 2n+p-1=tE,\qquad t\ge1.
\end{equation}
It initially gives $2p\le tE\le3p-3$. Combining $p<Xq^4$ with $tE<3Xq^4$ gives $ts<3q$, and in particular $s<3q$.
\begin{writenote}
\emph{(Batch 88.)} On the unwrapped odd-input sector Report~39 sharpens $ts<3q$ to $ts<3C$ (equation~\eqref{fup:nr:eq:tsC} in Section~\ref{fup:nr:sec:outerbound}).
\end{writenote}

The prior refinement~\cite{fup:nz:refinement} proves that the positive odd defect $d_{\mathrm{def}}=2n-p$ satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:defect}
 4^{d_{\mathrm{def}}}>X\ge q^3,\qquad d_{\mathrm{def}}\ge7,
 \qquad 2p+6\le tE\le3p-3.
\end{equation}
Its argument uses exact Pell growth, not a small-index approximation. From the ratio it obtains
\[
 a>\frac{X^{(p+d_{\mathrm{def}})/(2d_{\mathrm{def}})}}3.
\]
If $X\ge4^{d_{\mathrm{def}}}$, this would force $a>2^p$. Since $0<X<a$ and the main projection is $X\equiv2^p\pmod H$, both representatives would lie in $(0,H)$, forcing $X=2^p$. But then $E\le tE\le3p-3<2^p=X\le E$, a contradiction. This conditional no-wrap argument excludes the small-defect sector only; it is not permission to replace every exponential congruence by equality.

\section{An exact arithmetic predicate}\label{fup:nz:sec:predicate}
\subsection{Finite choices after two scale parameters}
Enumerate positive integers $q,w$ with
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:qranges}
 q\ge B,\qquad q\text{ even},\qquad q\equiv1\pmod{B-1},\qquad
 q>u-b+2.
\end{equation}
Define $J=(q-1)/(B-1)$ and the quantities in \eqref{fup:nz:eq:scales}. Choose only
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:stranges}
 1\le s\le3q-1,\qquad
 1\le t\le\left\lfloor\frac{3q-1}{s}\right\rfloor.
\end{equation}
The permissible main indices are odd integers in
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:prange}
 I=\left[
 \max\!\left(13,\left\lceil\frac{tE}{3}\right\rceil+1\right),
 \min\!\left(Xq^4-1,\left\lfloor\frac{tE-6}{2}\right\rfloor\right)
 \right].
\end{equation}
An empty interval is rejection. For each candidate define
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:n}
 n=\frac{tE+1-p}{2}.
\end{equation}
The $+1$ is essential. Since $E$ is even and $p$ odd, $n$ is integral; \eqref{fup:nz:eq:prange} implies $n\le p-1$ and $2n-p\ge7$.

Require the exact Pell ratio
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:ratio}
 2Y\psi_P(n)<\psi_A(p)<2(Y+1)\psi_P(n),
\end{equation}
and the filters
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:mainfilters}
 4^{2n-p}>X,\qquad 2^p\equiv X\pmod H.
\end{equation}
The defect test is necessary and useful, but its separate inclusion is not needed to make the later reconstruction sufficient.

\begin{lemma}[Prior uniqueness with an exact search]\label{fup:nz:lem:unique}
For fixed $q,w,s,t$, at most one odd $p\in I$ satisfies \eqref{fup:nz:eq:ratio}. There is a terminating exact procedure to find or reject that candidate.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
As $p$ increases by two, $n$ decreases by one. Thus the ratio $\psi_A(p)/(2\psi_P(n))$ strictly increases. More strongly, its numerator grows by more than $(2A-1)^2$ and its denominator decreases. Since $(2A-1)^2Y>Y+1$, two distinct odd indices cannot both lie in the target ratio interval. Binary search finds the first odd index, if any, that exceeds the lower threshold; only that index needs the upper-threshold test. Integer recurrence or powering gives all exact comparisons. This is an effectiveness statement, not a feasible running-time bound.
\end{proof}
\begin{writenote}
The uniqueness is the tree's (Section~4 of \rw{complete74_negative_index_refinement.md}, ``at most one main index per representative''); the factor-$(2A-1)^2$ argument and the exact-search formulation are the source's.
\end{writenote}

\subsection{Packing recovery and the two input tests}
Require the divisibility $Q\mid(p+M)$, and define
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:recover}
 N=\frac{p+M}{q^2-1},\qquad Z=N\bmod q\quad(0\le Z<q),\qquad
 F=q+\frac{N-Z}{q}.
\end{equation}
Reject $Z=0$. For each $e\in\{u,uA\}$, define the least nonnegative residue
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:W}
 W=2^e\bmod H,\qquad C=Z+W.
\end{equation}
Accept that branch exactly when
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:inputtests}
 e<p,\qquad 0<W<q,\qquad C+u-b<q,
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:transporttest}
 (K_0+X)C-F\quad\text{is a strictly positive multiple of }q-1.
\end{equation}
Together \eqref{fup:nz:eq:qranges}--\eqref{fup:nz:eq:transporttest} constitute the predicate. The Pell ratio is the sole exact Pell acceptance step in candidate extraction; modular powers can be evaluated without constructing their full exponential integers.

\begin{lemma}[Forced marker and field word]\label{fup:nz:lem:packing}
For fixed $q,w,s,t,p$, the values in \eqref{fup:nz:eq:recover} are the only possible $Z,F$ satisfying negative packing and $0<Z<q$. They satisfy $F\ge q$. If $F=q$, the shifted-mask remainder forces $Z\ge2$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The equation $-p=(q^2-Z-qF)Q+M$ is equivalent to $N=Z+qF-q^2$. Reduction modulo $q$ fixes $Z$ and then fixes $F$. Conversely these choices give the equation. Since $p,M>0$, $N>0$ and its nonnegative quotient by $q$ gives $F\ge q$. If $F=q$ and $Z=1$, then $S'=q^2$ in \eqref{fup:nz:eq:shifted}, giving $R=T'>0$, impossible.
\end{proof}
\begin{writenote}
Section~33.B already records the necessary conditions $F\ge q$ and, at $F=q$, $Z\ge2$ (after~\eqref{fup:ir:eq:negativewindow}); the unique recovery~\eqref{fup:nz:eq:recover} is new.

\emph{Separation from the programme's universal polynomial} (from the programme's review, commit \texttt{308f14072}). Every negative solution of this Part has $F\ge q$. The programme's normalized85 universal polynomial instead computes $C_{85}=q-F-Z-\alpha-2\dcell x$ and $N_t=(K_{\mathrm{const}}+w)C_{85}+q-F-t_{\mathrm{quot}}(q-1)$, with $K_{\mathrm{const}}$ its \code{Kconstant} and $t_{\mathrm{quot}}$ its \code{tquot}. At a positive zero of that polynomial $N_t$ is an integer unit, its second part $q-F-t_{\mathrm{quot}}(q-1)$ is at most zero and $K_{\mathrm{const}}+w\ge2$, so $C_{85}\ge0$ and $F+Z<q$, which is incompatible with $F\ge q$. This is a pretyping argument that comes before any native rank or index-sign recovery, and the review concludes that the normalized85 theorem is unaffected. The review names only normalized85; it predates by about five minutes the programme's 84-operation polynomial (commit \texttt{20aafb9a5}, \rw{complete84_scaled_strong_output.md}), which satisfies $F_{84}=\Delta\cdot F_{85}$ identically with $\Delta=(a+1)(a+3)>0$ on every allowed positive tuple. The two polynomials therefore have the same positive zeros, and the separation holds for the 84-operation polynomial too. (Here $\alpha$, $w$ and $t_{\mathrm{quot}}$ are that source's coordinates.)
\end{writenote}

\section{Full negative zero equivalence}\label{fup:nz:sec:theorem}
\begin{theorem}[Exact full-zero obstruction]\label{fup:nz:thm:main}
Fix a valid compiler slice and $x>0$ as in Section~\ref{fup:nz:sec:contract}. The following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
\item The raw29 child has a full strictly positive integer supplied tuple with $R<0$
\item The positive21 child has a full strictly positive integer supplied tuple with $R<0$
\item Some $q,w$ and finite choices $s,t,p,e$ pass the predicate of Section~\ref{fup:nz:sec:predicate}
\end{enumerate}
Every passing predicate reconstructs a full tuple in both modes. Only $q,w$ remain independently unbounded; this is not a globally finite decision procedure. Auxiliary witnesses need not be unique.
\end{theorem}
\begin{writenote}
\emph{Scope, as the programme's review records it} (\rw{review_exact_negative_obstruction37.md}, commit \texttt{308f14072}; PASS within the stated fixed-compiler scope). The theorem closes the conditional witness-reconstruction obligations; it proves neither that the predicate has a solution nor that it has none. The review adds these precisions.
\begin{itemize}
\item The mode labels remain \code{raw30} and \code{positive22}, while the child interfaces have 29 and 21 positive witnesses, 18 and 10 comparisons and 127 and 103 full sum-of-squares gates (the table of Section~33.1).
\item The omitted coordinate is restored as $R=k-hXY-1$ with the actual raw $k$ supplied.
\item Neither the inherited reduction nor Report~37 assumes that $q$ is a power of two on the negative branch.
\item $K_0<B^2$ is a property of the actual numerals, not an assumption on arbitrary ports.
\item The signed19 density theorem of Part~IV (Section~\ref{fup:ir:sec:density}) cannot be substituted for this question: it allows $W<0$ and lacks the retained positive gap.
\item Report~37 uses the symmetric scales $X=wq^3$, the ordinary strong equation and an independent $\ga$; it proves no asymmetric-scale transfer (contrast Section~\ref{fup:hc:sec:transfer} for signed19).
\item The odd-input split of Section~\ref{fup:nz:sec:sectors} does not eliminate the unbounded unwrapped sector, and the even branch remains open.
\item The Binet window of Section~\ref{fup:nz:sec:window} is necessary, not an exact ratio test.
\item The 3,536-case relaxed grid of Section~\ref{fup:nz:sec:checks} was not rerun or independently certified by the review and is not a set of genuine compiler instances.
\end{itemize}
The review writes $H=4A-5$, which is $H=4a+3$ since $A=a+2$. Its helper authenticates the archive (SHA-256 \texttt{018b960e\ldots 69f9}), four decisive evidence members and the five upstream sources, matches both literal witness and comparison inventories and both finalizer suffixes, checks the normalized85 separation (note after Lemma~\ref{fup:nz:lem:packing}), and adds 8 auxiliary divisibility and parity cases with $p\ge13$, 24 input-projection cases, 36 odd-quotient specializations and 16 deterministic packing recoveries. These are supplementary algebra cases, not valid compiler exports or full zeros, and the review claims no new universal bound.
\end{writenote}

\subsection{Necessity}
The prior results in Section~\ref{fup:nz:sec:prior} give $R=-p$, even $q$, the range \eqref{fup:nz:eq:prange}, the ratio, the defect filter and the two possible input indices. The source main-root equation gives $2^p\equiv X\pmod H$. The strict raw bound and $C=Z+W\ge2$ give $q\ge u-b+3$, which is exactly the preliminary bound in \eqref{fup:nz:eq:qranges}. The inequalities $s<3q$ and $ts<3q$ become \eqref{fup:nz:eq:stranges} without losing an endpoint.

Lemma~\ref{fup:nz:lem:packing} gives \eqref{fup:nz:eq:recover}. The input-root equation gives $W\equiv2^e\pmod H$; since $0<W<q<H$, $W$ is the unique least residue. The raw bound is precisely $C+\alpha+u-b=q$ with $\alpha>0$, and transport is precisely \eqref{fup:nz:eq:transporttest} with positive quotient $\zquot$. Thus all tests are necessary in both modes.

\subsection{First and main witnesses and every outer sign}
For a passing predicate put
\begin{align}\label{fup:nz:eq:mainwitness}
 \alpha&=q-C-u+b,&\zquot&=\frac{(K_0+X)C-F}{q-1},\\
 c&=\psi_A(p),&D&=\chi_A(p),\\
 k&=2\psi_P(n),&\tau&=\chi_P(n),\\
 \eta&=c-kY,&\zeta&=k-\eta,\\
 h&=\frac{k+p-1}{E},&\ga&=\frac{D-ac-X}{H}.\nonumber
\end{align}
The predicate makes $\alpha,\zquot,\eta,\zeta$ strictly positive integers. To check $h$, note $P\equiv1\pmod E$ and hence $\psi_P(n)\equiv n\pmod E$ by recurrence. Consequently $k+p-1\equiv2n+p-1=tE\equiv0\pmod E$. Its numerator is positive, so $h>0$ and $k-hE-1=-p$.

The Pell identities give the main norm and the first norm exactly:
\[
 D^2-\Delta c^2=1,\qquad
 \tau^2-1=(P^2-1)\psi_P(n)^2=XY^2(XY^2+1)k^2.
\]
The factor of four is absorbed by $k=2\psi_P(n)$; it cannot be omitted. Packing, scale and transport now hold by definition.

To prove the sign of $\ga$, define $\mathcal E_A(r)=\chi_A(r)-a\psi_A(r)$. The exact recurrence gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:E-star}
 \mathcal E_A(0)=1,\quad \mathcal E_A(1)=2,\quad \mathcal E_A(r)\equiv2^r\pmod H,\qquad
 \mathcal E_A(r)=2\psi_A(r)-\psi_A(r-1)>\psi_A(r)\quad(r\ge1).
\end{equation}
The congruence holds because $2^r$ satisfies the same recurrence modulo $H$: its characteristic residual is $4-4A+1=-H$. For $r\ge3$,
\[
 \psi_A(r)\ge4A^2-1>H+X\quad\text{and}\quad \psi_A(r)>H+q,
\]
since $a>X,q$. The main projection therefore makes $(D-ac-X)/H$ an integer greater than zero. The positive main root is explicitly $D=\chi_A(p)$, not an arbitrary sign of a square root.
\begin{writenote}
Equation~\eqref{fup:nz:eq:E-star} is Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:projection} of Report~33, where $\mathcal E_A(r)$ is written $\ea(r)$; the source writes $E^*_r$. The proof here is the source's own.
\end{writenote}

\subsection{The entire positive input block}
Define
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:inputwitness}
 \kappa=\psi_A(e),\quad\mu=\chi_A(e),\quad
 \delta=\frac{\kappa-u}{\Delta},\quad
 \phi=c-\kappa,\quad
 \rho=\frac{\mu-a\kappa-W}{H}.
\end{equation}
If $e=u$ is odd, \eqref{fup:nz:eq:discriminant} gives $\kappa\equiv u\pmod\Delta$. If $e=uA$ is even, it gives $\kappa\equiv uA^2\equiv u\pmod\Delta$. Hence $\delta$ is integral in both branches. Since $u\ge3$ and $e\ge u$, strict Pell growth gives $\kappa>u$, so $\delta>0$. The condition $e<p$ gives $\phi>0$.

The modulus $H$ is odd, so $W=2^e\bmod H$ is nonzero. Equation~\eqref{fup:nz:eq:E-star} gives $\mu-a\kappa>H+q$, while the predicate gives $W<q$. Thus $\rho$ is an integer and is strictly positive. The norm, discriminant increment, positive gap and exponent equation in \eqref{fup:nz:eq:inputsystem} all hold, including the positive sign of the supplied input root $\mu$.

\subsection{Ordinary strong and mixed-sign auxiliary completion}
We use the already verified bootstrap construction for \emph{every odd} $p$. Define
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:m}
 m=\begin{cases}cp,&p\equiv3\pmod4,\\2cp,&p\equiv1\pmod4,\end{cases}
 \qquad f=\chi_A(m),\quad \Taux=\Delta\psi_A(m),\quad
 i=\frac{\Taux}{c^2}.
\end{equation}
Next put
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:auxwitness}
 \ell=p+2m,\quad U=\frac{\chi_{\Taux}(\ell)}{\Taux},\quad
 \yaux=\psi_{\Taux}(\ell),\quad
 j=\frac{U-p}{c},\quad o=\frac{U+c}{f}.
\end{equation}
We verify integrality and positivity rather than simply invoking a solvable auxiliary norm.

Write $m=pv$, with $v=c$ or $2c$. The square-root coefficient of $(D+c\sqrt\Delta)^v$ is $\psi_A(m)$. Its first odd term has a factor $vc$, divisible by $c^2$, and every higher odd term has a factor $c^3$. Hence $c^2\mid\psi_A(m)$ and $i$ is a positive integer. The Pell identity gives
\[
 \Taux^2=\Delta(f^2-1).
\]
Since $A$ is even and $p$ odd, $c$ is odd. The choice \eqref{fup:nz:eq:m} makes $\ell\equiv1\pmod4$; in the second case the extra factor two is essential.

For odd $\ell=2\theta+1$, the quotient $\chi_T(\ell)/T$ is an integer polynomial $\mathcal Q_\theta(T^2)$. Its exact identities are
\[
 \mathcal Q_\theta(0)=(-1)^{\theta}(2\theta+1),\qquad
 \mathcal Q_\theta(1-A^2)=(-1)^{\theta}\psi_A(2\theta+1).
\]
Here $\theta=(\ell-1)/2$ is even. Because $c\mid\Taux$ and $c\mid m$, these identities give $U\equiv\ell\equiv p\pmod c$. Also $\Taux^2\equiv-\Delta=1-A^2\pmod f$, so $U\equiv\psi_A(\ell)\pmod f$. The Pell addition formulas imply
\[
 \chi_A(2m)\equiv-1\pmod f,\qquad
 \psi_A(2m)\equiv0\pmod f,
\]
and hence $\psi_A(p+2m)\equiv-c\pmod f$. This proves the integrality of $j$ and $o$.

Finally $\Taux\ge c^2$, $\ell\ge3$, and Pell growth give
\[
 U\ge4\Taux^2-3>p.
\]
Thus $j,o$ are positive. The Pell norm at base $\Taux$ reads
\[
 \Taux^2(U^2-\yaux^2)=1-\yaux^2,
\]
and $U=jc+p=jc-R=of-c$. These are all comparisons in \eqref{fup:nz:eq:auxsystem}. The construction supplies positive $f,i,j,o,\yaux$ and discharges the auxiliary sign obligations. No uniqueness is asserted for this completion.
\begin{writenote}
\emph{Credit.} The construction is Section~5 of the tree's bootstrap note \rw{review_complete74_nonlinear_index_bootstrap.md} (commit \texttt{93ad43375}), ``A parametric positive auxiliary realization of $r=-p$'', as the source says. Report~33 used its case $p\equiv3\pmod4$ with the same tools: Lemmas~\ref{fup:ir:lem:oddquotient} and~\ref{fup:ir:lem:divisibility} and Section~\ref{fup:ir:sec:aux}, where $\Taux$, $\ell$, $\theta$ and $\yaux$ are written $\Qa$, $s_a$, $b_a$ and $y$. Report~37 re-verifies the integrality and positivity of $i,j,o$ for every odd $p$, including $p\equiv1\pmod4$ with $m=2cp$, inside the full raw29 and positive21 systems.
\end{writenote}

The raw29 tuple, in saved source order after the stated aliases, is
\begin{align*}
 (&C,F,J,W,Z,a,\alpha,c,D,\delta,\eta,f,\ga,h,i,j,k,\kappa,\mu,o,\phi,\\
 &q,\rho,s,\tau,w,\yaux,\zeta,\zquot).
\end{align*}
The positive21 tuple is
\[
 (J,F,\alpha,\zquot,f,h,i,j,o,s,w,\tau,\eta,\zeta,\ga,\yaux,Z,W,\delta,\phi,\rho).
\]
The omitted coordinates are exactly the positive triangular expressions established above. The raw mode has 18 retained comparisons; positive21 retains 10 of them. Table~\ref{fup:nz:tab:residuals} accounts for each raw comparison. The positive21 comparisons correspond to raw indices $1,2,3,5,10,11,12,13,15,16$; the other eight become source definitions.

Hence vanishing of this complete list gives vanishing of the saved integer polynomial in either mode. This finishes the constructive direction and Theorem~\ref{fup:nz:thm:main}.

\section{Input and packing consequences}\label{fup:nz:sec:sectors}
\subsection{The finite wrapped odd sector}
On the odd branch $e=u$, the integer $2^u$ is fixed once the compiler and input are fixed. If $H>2^u$, then $W=2^u$ exactly. Since $Z,\alpha\ge1$, the raw bound gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:unwrapped}
 q\ge2^u+u-b+2.
\end{equation}
If instead $H\le2^u$, parity improves it to $H\le2^u-1$. Substituting the exact $H$ gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:wrapped}
 4swq^6+4sq^3+4\le2^u,\qquad
 w\le\left\lfloor\frac{2^u-4sq^3-4}{4sq^6}\right\rfloor,
 \qquad4q^6<2^u.
\end{equation}
There are consequently only finitely many $q,w,s,t,p$ in this wrapped odd sector at each fixed compiler and input. Enumerating those exact bounds and applying the predicate decides that sector. This does not require factoring $H$ or finding a multiplicative order.

\begin{corollary}[Odd-branch forbidden interval]
There is no odd-input negative zero satisfying both
\[
 4q^6\ge2^u\qquad\text{and}\qquad q<2^u+u-b+2.
\]
Moreover $u\le6\dcell+2$ excludes the wrapped odd sector. In the actual compiler, inputs $x=1,2$ have no wrapped odd branch.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
The first claim separates the exhaustive wrapped and unwrapped cases. Since $q\ge B=2^{\dcell}$, wrapping requires $2^{6\dcell+2}<2^u$. Finally $\dcell=bL$ with $L\ge1$ gives $b\le\dcell$, so for $x=1,2$ one has $u\le5\dcell<6\dcell+2$.
\end{proof}
No finite enumeration at the actual, potentially huge compiler constants has been performed. The even branch is unaffected by this corollary.

\subsection{The even branch uses the same outer marker}
For $e=uA$ we have $uA<p$. Combining this with \eqref{fup:nz:eq:absolute} and $A>sXq^3$ gives
\[
 us<C(1+1/q)<C+1,
\]
because $C<q$. Integrality and the strict raw bound therefore imply
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:even}
 us\le C\le q-u+b-1,\qquad
 u(s+1)\le q+b-1,\qquad
 s\le\left\lfloor\frac{q-u+b-1}{u}\right\rfloor.
\end{equation}
Also $uA<p<tE/2$ and $A>E$ imply
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:event}
 t>2u,\qquad\text{hence }t\ge2u+1.
\end{equation}
The improvement over $us<q$ is that the same $C=Z+W$ must simultaneously pass packing, transport and the ordinary input's raw bound.
\begin{writenote}
The bound $us<q$ improved here is the one recorded at the end of Section~33.B, after~\eqref{fup:ir:eq:inputdichotomy}.
\end{writenote}

\subsection{A local exclusion independent of the sign}
No full child zero of either sign has $3\mid q$. Indeed this would make $3\mid X,Y,H$, while the main projection $2^p\equiv X\pmod H$ would make a power of two divisible by three. This elementary filter can be applied before candidate extraction. It is not the full obstruction theorem.

\section{A certified sub unit real window}\label{fup:nz:sec:window}
Exact Pell powering is definitive but may involve very large integers. A necessary interval can reject parameter choices without materializing those integers. The interval is stronger than the prior uniqueness statement in its quantitative width, but is not an exact acceptance test.

\subsection{Rational bounds for the Binet formula}
For an integer $V\ge2$ put
\[
 \omega_-(V)=2V-1/V,\qquad \omega_+(V)=2V-1/(2V).
\]
Squaring shows
\[
 V-1/V<\sqrt{V^2-1}<V-1/(2V),\quad
 \omega_-(V)<V+\sqrt{V^2-1}<\omega_+(V).
\]
Abbreviate $\omega^A_\pm=\omega_\pm(A)$ and $\omega^P_\pm=\omega_\pm(P)$, and define positive rational prefactors
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:rpm}
 \xi_- =\frac{P-1/P}{2A}\left(1-(2A-1)^{-26}\right),\qquad
 \xi_+ =\frac{P}{2(A-1/A)}\frac1{1-(2P-1)^{-14}}.
\end{equation}
With $\lambda_A=A+\sqrt{A^2-1}$ and $\lambda_P=P+\sqrt{P^2-1}$, Binet's identity is exactly
\[
 \frac{\psi_A(p)}{2\psi_P(n)}=
 \frac{\sqrt{P^2-1}}{2\sqrt{A^2-1}}
 \frac{\lambda_A^p}{\lambda_P^n}
 \frac{1-\lambda_A^{-2p}}{1-\lambda_P^{-2n}}.
\]
For $p\ge13$ and $n\ge7$, the exponents 26 and 14 in \eqref{fup:nz:eq:rpm} uniformly bound the two tails. Thus
\begin{align}\label{fup:nz:eq:LU}
 \Lambda_-(p)&=\log \xi_-+p\log \omega^A_- -n\log \omega^P_+
 <\log\frac{\psi_A(p)}{2\psi_P(n)}\nonumber\\
 &<\log \xi_++p\log \omega^A_+-n\log \omega^P_-=\Lambda_+(p).
\end{align}
These bounds make no assumption that $p/A$ is small.

Substitute $n=(tE+1-p)/2$. The strict ratio requires
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:logwindow}
 \frac{\log Y-\log \xi_+ +(tE+1)\log \omega^P_-/2}
 {\log \omega^A_+ +\log \omega^P_-/2}
 <p<
 \frac{\log(Y+1)-\log \xi_- +(tE+1)\log \omega^P_+/2}
 {\log \omega^A_- +\log \omega^P_+/2}.
\end{equation}
Both denominators are positive. Let $I_{\mathrm{Bin}}$ denote this open real interval.

\begin{theorem}[Sub-unit necessary window]\label{fup:nz:thm:window}
The intersection $I_{\mathrm{Bin}}\cap I$, with $I$ as in \eqref{fup:nz:eq:prange}, has length less than $q^{-3}+q^{-5}<1$. It contains at most one integer, before odd parity is imposed. Every ratio-admissible index lies in that intersection.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The exponential-base errors satisfy
\[
 \log(\omega^A_+/\omega^A_-)=\log\left(1+\frac1{4A^2-2}\right)<\frac1{3A^2},
 \qquad \log(\omega^P_+/\omega^P_-)<\frac1{3P^2}.
\]
Here $P>A$. The square-root-prefactor log error is less than
\[
 \frac1{A^2-1}+\frac1{P^2-1}<\frac3{A^2},
\]
and the two logarithmic tail errors sum to less than $1/A^2$. For instance $-\log(1-z)<2z$ at each present $0<z<1/2$, and each resulting tail is below $1/(2A^2)$. On $I$, where $n<p$ and $p\ge13$, this yields
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:errorwidth}
 \Lambda_+(p)-\Lambda_-(p)<\frac{p+n}{3A^2}+\frac4{A^2}
 <\frac{p}{A^2}<\frac1{Xq^2}\le\frac1{q^5}.
\end{equation}
The penultimate comparison uses the actual outer bound $p<Xq^4$ and $A>XY\ge Xq^3$.

Take any two real points $p_1<p_2$ in \emph{the intersection} $I_{\mathrm{Bin}}\cap I$. At $p_1$, $\Lambda_+(p_1)>\log Y$, so $\Lambda_-(p_1)>\log Y-q^{-5}$. At $p_2$, $\Lambda_-(p_2)<\log(Y+1)$. Hence
\[
 \Lambda_-(p_2)-\Lambda_-(p_1)<\log(1+1/Y)+q^{-5}<q^{-3}+q^{-5}.
\]
The affine slope of $\Lambda_-$ is $\log \omega^A_-+\tfrac12\log \omega^P_+>1$. Dividing proves the claimed width. Restriction to $I$ is essential because the uniform error estimate was proved there.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Outward rational evaluation and exact fallback}
For a positive rational $\vartheta$, range-reduce $\vartheta=2^{\nu}\vartheta_0$ with $1\le \vartheta_0<2$. Set $z_0=(\vartheta_0-1)/(\vartheta_0+1)$. The elementary series gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nz:eq:atanh}
 \log \vartheta_0=2\sum_{l=0}^{N_{\mathrm s}-1}\frac{z_0^{2l+1}}{2l+1}+\varepsilon_{N_{\mathrm s}},
 \qquad
 0\le\varepsilon_{N_{\mathrm s}}\le
 \frac{2z_0^{2N_{\mathrm s}+1}}{(2N_{\mathrm s}+1)(1-z_0^2)}.
\end{equation}
The same formula at $\vartheta_0=2$ encloses $\log2$. All endpoints and errors are rational. Correct signed interval arithmetic for $\nu\log2$, and division only after the denominator's positive lower bound has been certified, give outward enclosures of \eqref{fup:nz:eq:logwindow}.

An outward lower endpoint $p_{\mathrm{lo}}$ and upper endpoint $p_{\mathrm{hi}}$ safely restrict strict integer membership to $\lfloor p_{\mathrm{lo}}\rfloor+1\le p\le\lceil p_{\mathrm{hi}}\rceil-1$, followed by the index-range and odd-parity restrictions. If that set is empty the parameter choice is rejected exactly. A surviving integer, or an endpoint that cannot be separated by the present enclosure, still needs exact Pell comparison. Refinement isolates or rejects; it does not by itself certify strict ratio acceptance.

The logarithmic precision depends on the parameters. Exact final acceptance can still require extremely large Pell integers, and no uniform polynomial-time complexity or nonzero separation bound is proved. Binary search from Lemma~\ref{fup:nz:lem:unique} remains a terminating exact fallback regardless of an unresolved real endpoint.

\section{Checked evidence and reproducibility}\label{fup:nz:sec:checks}
\begin{writenote}
\emph{Shipped names.} The packet's files are shipped with the prefix \texttt{15-neg-obstruction-} and the delivery directories flattened into the name (README): \rw{evidence/check_exact_obstruction.py} is \rw{code/15-neg-obstruction-evidence-check_exact_obstruction.py}, \rw{evidence/independent/audit_exact_obstruction.py} and \rw{audit_log_windows.py} are \rw{code/15-neg-obstruction-evidence-independent-audit_exact_obstruction.py} and \rw{code/15-neg-obstruction-evidence-independent-audit_log_windows.py}, and their frozen receipts are the three \rw{data/15-neg-obstruction-evidence-*expected_*.json}. The packet's \rw{evidence/sources/} and \rw{evidence/context/} hold byte copies of repository files and are not shipped; the checkers read them, so the README gives a rerun route that restores them from the repository. The delivered release verifier refuses to run on NTFS, whose file modes it cannot match; at placement the three mathematical programs, run directly on a copy, passed in normal and optimized Python, with receipts equal to the frozen ones (up to CRLF line ends of the author receipt on Windows). The programme's review did not rerun the 3,536-case grid below.
\end{writenote}
\subsection{What the finite checks establish}
The new mathematical checker authenticates the five cached upstream files by SHA256 and Git blob SHA1, reads the source JSON as data, and verifies both exact supplied witness lists and comparison inventories. The independent checker additionally verifies both complete sum-of-squares finalizer suffixes. Neither evaluates a saved source schedule.

Table~\ref{fup:nz:tab:checks} records the author's exact test families. All integer and interval decisions use exact arithmetic. The floating maximum-window-width value in the receipt is a display statistic only; the asserted rational bound is $1/100000$.
\begin{table}[!ht]\centering\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{.67\textwidth}r@{}}
\toprule Check family & Count\\\midrule
Negative outer-packing arithmetic reconstructions & 18,360\\
Input discriminant and residue fixtures & 288 each\\
Fully materialized auxiliary-only tuples & 4\\
Large-index auxiliary divisibility and parity fixtures & 16\\
Direct rational Binet envelope cases & 450\\
Relaxed scale grid choices with empty odd-index windows & 3,536\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{Author checker results, with subsystem and scale scopes retained}\label{fup:nz:tab:checks}
\end{table}
The full grid takes $q\in\{16,20,32\}$, $w\in\{1,2,3,4\}$, every $1\le s\le3q-1$ and every $1\le t\le\lfloor(3q-1)/s\rfloor$. It uses 36-term outward rational series bounds. Every one of the 3,536 necessary windows has no odd integer; the maximum outward width is strictly less than $1/100000$. This is a relaxed arithmetic scale grid, \emph{not} 3,536 valid compiler instances. No surviving main-index candidate or full negative compiler zero was materialized.

The four complete auxiliary-only fixtures include $p=1$ and $p=3$, covering both odd residue classes modulo four. They are outside the full main-index condition $p\ge13$. The larger-index modular fixtures and the general construction address that condition without claiming to have materialized a full tuple.

\subsection{Independent corroboration}
The independent review~\cite{fup:nz:audit} concludes that the full-negative-zero equivalence, finite-sector consequences and sub-unit necessary interval are valid under the stated compiler and prior-bootstrap premises. Its exact checker records:
\begin{itemize}
\item Five source identity checks and both full witness, comparison and finalizer inventories
\item 61 odd-quotient polynomial identities; 1,620 main/input projection, discriminant and growth instances; 360 positive input-delta cases
\item 240 auxiliary divisibility/parity cases, six auxiliary modular completions with exact $f$, and three materialized auxiliary-only tuples
\item 9,030 index-range boundary cases and 448 packing reconstructions, including $F=q$
\item 162 rational Binet/error cases, 126 representative uniform-error cases, and 1,728 wrapped-sector threshold cases
\end{itemize}
The three materialized independent auxiliary tuples all have $p=3$; their $U$ bit lengths are 7,939, 56,928 and 214,545. They are explicitly subsystem evidence.

A separate independent interval implementation uses 48-term rational logarithm bounds on the \emph{full rational prefactors}, rather than the author's split-tail implementation. It checks 16 selected scale points covering all three $q$ values, interior choices and extreme representatives. All 16 independently exclude odd integers. This corroborates selected points; it does not replace or inflate the author's 3,536-case full-grid count.

\subsection{Portable execution contract}
The package preserves the complete pinned evidence packet, including the proof, prior-context snapshots, independent review, static upstream sources, three new mathematical executables and frozen expected receipts. The relevant executable paths are
\begin{quote}\small
\code{evidence/check_exact_obstruction.py}\\
\code{evidence/independent/audit_exact_obstruction.py}\\
\code{evidence/independent/audit_log_windows.py}.
\end{quote}
Each defaults to canonical JSON on stdout only. The \code{--expect} option checks a separately frozen receipt; \code{--output} permits an external destination and rejects an in-packet write. Normal Python and \code{python3 -O} are both supported because all checks use explicit exceptions rather than removable assertions. The cached upstream Python file is inert source evidence, not an executable of this package; its historical assertions are not altered or used as a release gate.

The release verifier checks pinned payload and executable identities, exact recursive JSON types and values, and copied-source preservation. Its normal/optimized replays run from an external copy, with outputs outside the packet. Tamper and malformed-input regressions test failure paths. The archive tools enforce exact inventory, unique safe paths, and payload identities. The PDF build disables shell escape, runs outside the release tree and checks reproducible bytes. The README gives the executable commands; the integrity record separates these reproducibility properties from the mathematical scope. Reproducible checks corroborate a proof under stated premises; they do not prove the unresolved existential conclusion.

\section{What remains open and useful next directions}\label{fup:nz:sec:future}
Theorem~\ref{fup:nz:thm:main} reduces the unresolved full-zero question to a precisely specified intersection. Its two unbounded sectors are:
\begin{enumerate}
\item The odd branch with the exact fixed marker $W=2^u$ and $q\ge2^u+u-b+2$
\item The even branch with $us\le C\le q-u+b-1$ and $t\ge2u+1$
\end{enumerate}
The finite wrapped odd sector is an additional, decidable component at each fixed compiler and input. Finite decidability here need not mean practical enumeration.

A useful next proof would exploit the simultaneous constraints on the uniquely recovered $Z,F$, the two modular exponent tests, and the exact ratio. A contradiction in one of the listed sectors would eliminate that sector; a candidate would need to pass \emph{every} test and then use the explicit reconstruction. The full witness formulas show that an auxiliary sign obstacle will not independently eliminate a passing candidate.

The certified interval suggests an exact rejection-first implementation: apply local filters, isolate the possible main index with rational bounds, then pay for exact Pell acceptance only if a candidate survives. A meaningful complexity theorem would need explicit precision/separation estimates or a different exact comparison algorithm. None is presently supplied.

Some tempting shortcuts fail. Replacing $W\equiv2^u\pmod H$ by $W=2^u$ discards the finite wrapped sector, and is unjustified on the even branch. Density at a fixed scale does not force an index into the bounded representative range. The signed19 progression lets a marker grow freely, whereas $0<Z,W<C<q$ and deterministic recovery constrain it here. Arbitrary small mask ports are not genuine compiler exports, even if they satisfy a few numerical inequalities.

The decisive remaining question is therefore unchanged in its yes-or-no form: does any valid fixed-compiler choice admit a passing full predicate, or can all such choices be excluded? The contribution is an exact obstruction with a complete inverse witness map, explicit sector boundaries and certifiable finite rejection machinery. It is neither a global positivity theorem nor a counterexample.
\begin{writenote}
\emph{Re-scoped (batch 88).} Part~\ref{fup:part:nr} (Report~39) works in the first unbounded sector above. At $q=B^{2x+2}$, with $W=2^u$, $Z=1$, $C=W+1$ and $s=t=1$, it proves that for every valid compiler and input infinitely many scales satisfy every condition of the predicate except the main congruence $2^p\equiv X\pmod H$ (Theorem~\ref{fup:nr:thm:family}); that an odd-index hit of that congruence requires the Jacobi symbol $\mathcal J(w,4q^3+3)$ to be $-1$ (Lemma~\ref{fup:nr:lem:jacobi}), a fixed-modulus test of the kind suggested above; and that on a thinned progression with symbol $+1$ infinitely many scales pass every other condition and fail the congruence (Theorem~\ref{fup:nr:thm:misses}). No sector is eliminated: the scales of that family with symbol $-1$, the rest of the odd sector and the whole even sector remain, and the yes-or-no question above is still open. The programme's review of Report~39 checks the Jacobi filter but does not recertify its analytic existence argument (note at the start of Part~\ref{fup:part:nr}).
\end{writenote}

\fupstart{39}{Part VI: Report 39}
\part{Nonredundant main comparisons in positive Diophantine certificates}\label{fup:part:nr}
\noindent\wtag\ Source~16 of this report, manuscript~01 of batch~88: \emph{Research Report 39}, subtitled ``Infinite genuine compiler counterfamilies after deleting one comparison'', author line ``Mathematical research report'', dated 3 October 2026; archive \rw{Nonredundant_Main_Comparisons_in_Positive_Diophantine_Certificates_Package.zip} (arrival commit \texttt{c5612efa1}, placed by \texttt{992aaafb3}); pin \texttt{d5bd4a67b}, the pin of Report~37. Its sections, statements and equations are numbered 39.$k$ here.
\begin{sourceabstract}
For every valid fixed complete75 compiler export and ordinary input $x\ge1$, we construct infinitely many positive tuples violating exactly one retained comparison: the main projection in raw29, or the main norm in positive21. First, exact affine packing and a quantitative shrinking target argument construct a broader family satisfying every condition of Report 37's negative-zero predicate except $2^p\equiv X\pmod{4Y(X+1)+3}$. The compiler numerals stay genuine and fixed, with $q=B^{2x+2}$, $W=2^{2d_{\mathrm{cell}}x+b}$, $Z=1$, $C=W+1$ and $s=t=1$. A later Jacobi-symbol argument proves the fixed-modulus necessary filter $(w/(4q^3+3))=-1$ for any odd-index congruence hit. Restricting $w=1+(q-1)(4q^3+3)j$ instead gives symbol $+1$ and certifies misses. For every sufficiently large $N$, at least $\delta_0N/(32L_0\log N)$ such scales with $N\le j<2N$ satisfy every other condition. Their exact raw sum of squares is $\rmain^2>0$; the positive21 sum is $[\rmain(2D-\rmain)]^2>0$. This proves nonredundancy of the indicated comparison in each interface. It supplies no full negative zero and no global restored-index positivity theorem. No enormous genuine tuple is numerically materialized, and no practical threshold is claimed.
\end{sourceabstract}
\begin{writenote}
\emph{What the title means.} The comparisons are \emph{nonredundant} in a precise and limited sense: deleting the raw29 main projection, or the positive21 main norm, from the respective interface admits infinitely many strictly positive tuples that violate the deleted comparison (Theorem~\ref{fup:nr:thm:misses}). These tuples are zeros of the comparison-deleted systems, not of raw29 or positive21. The yes-or-no question of Part~V, whether a full positive zero with negative restored index exists (Section~\ref{fup:nz:sec:future}), stays open; the source says so in its first section and in Section~\ref{fup:nr:sec:questions}.

\emph{Place in this report.} Report~39 continues Part~V. It works inside the first of the two unbounded sectors that Section~\ref{fup:nz:sec:future} lists, the odd branch with the fixed marker $W=2^u$, at the explicit scale $q=B^{2x+2}$ with $Z=1$, $C=W+1$ and $s=t=1$, and it settles what that section calls ``a useful next proof'' for one infinite subfamily: every condition of Part~V's predicate except the main congruence holds infinitely often (Theorem~\ref{fup:nr:thm:family}), and a fixed-modulus Jacobi symbol, one of the ``modular exponent tests'' that section asks to exploit, excludes the main congruence on a thinned progression (Lemma~\ref{fup:nr:lem:jacobi}, Theorem~\ref{fup:nr:thm:misses}). Part~V's note at the end of Section~\ref{fup:nz:sec:future} records this re-scoping. Section~\ref{fup:nr:sec:contract} restates Part~V's compiler contract and predicate (Sections~\ref{fup:nz:sec:contract} and~\ref{fup:nz:sec:predicate}); Section~\ref{fup:nr:sec:reconstruction} specializes Part~V's positive reconstruction. Both are printed in full, because later equations cite them, with pointers and no novelty claim. The source carried Report~37's manuscript as context (a byte copy of the text printed as Part~V; not shipped).

\emph{The programme's review.} The Hilbert's-tenth programme reviewed this archive with Reports~41, 43, 45 and~46 in \rw{review_incoming_arithmetic_reports_39_46.md} (research tree, commit \texttt{5d8889017}, with the receipt \rw{.json}). It checks the Jacobi identity~\eqref{fup:nr:eq:jacobiidentity} and finds it valid, calls the thinning to multiples of $h_0$ or $4h_0$ ``a concrete obstruction, not an inference from a search'', and finds that the transformed second derivative and discrepancy estimates of Section~\ref{fup:nr:sec:misses} have the expected scaling. \emph{It did not read the parent analytic proof or its long independent audit in full, and does not recertify the complete analytic existence argument}: the shrinking-target construction of Sections~\ref{fup:nr:sec:lattice}--\ref{fup:nr:sec:theorem}, on which Theorems~\ref{fup:nr:thm:family} and~\ref{fup:nr:thm:misses} rest, is certified only by the source's own independent review (shipped as \rw{16-nonredundant-evidence-independent-INDEPENDENT-REVIEW.md}). The review's verdict on scope: the result ``supplies neither a full zero of a smaller polynomial nor an improved universal operation count''. A second reviewer's provenance cross-read does not recertify these analytic dependencies either. The two analytic estimates of Section~\ref{fup:nr:sec:discrepancy} are cited from the literature, as the source states.

\emph{A historical statement in the shipped evidence.} \rw{16-nonredundant-evidence-README.md} (line~9) says that no genuine candidate has been proved either to satisfy or to fail the omitted congruence. That sentence belongs to the first frozen stage and was accurate at its freeze; the later Jacobi addendum, integrated here as Sections~\ref{fup:nr:sec:jacobi}--\ref{fup:nr:sec:misses}, proves infinitely many certified failures. The source says this itself (``Historical evidence stages'', Section~\ref{fup:nr:sec:scope}). The delivered file is kept byte for byte. No main-congruence \emph{hit} is proved anywhere.

\emph{Notation in this Part.} Report~39 uses Part~V's letters (and Part~V's macros $\MFsrcV$, $\MFnatV$ for the masks), with no symbol renamed by the merge. Its own letters that differ from Part~V: $\lambda_A(X),\lambda_P(X)$ are \emph{logarithms} of the Pell units (Part~V's $\lambda_A,\lambda_P$ are the units themselves); $L_0=q(q-1)Q$ is a lattice modulus (Part~V's $L$ is the compiler exponent in $\dcell=bL$); $N$ bounds the progression index and $N_{\mathrm{pack}}$ is Part~V's packing quotient $N$; $h_0=4q^3+3$ is a fixed Jacobi modulus, while the odd-quotient index that Part~V writes $\theta$ is $h_{\mathrm{aux}}$ here; $E^*_j$ is Part~V's $\mathcal E_A(j)$; $K(X)$, $S(X)$, $G(z,v)$, $g(r)$, $a_0=\log(2Y)$, $\kappa_0$, $\delta_0$, $K_*$ belong to the analytic argument and are unrelated to the compiler constant $K_0$, the free coefficient $S$ of Part~VIII, the input coefficient $\kappa$ and the register $a$; $\acent$ is the analytic centre (not the field word $F$); $T_{\mathrm{thin}}$ is a thinning factor (not $\Taux$); $\faux$ is Part~V's auxiliary root $f$. The letter $j$ is the thinned progression index in Sections~\ref{fup:nr:sec:jacobi}--\ref{fup:nr:sec:misses} and the supplied auxiliary coordinate in Section~\ref{fup:nr:sec:reconstruction}; the source warns about this. Section~\ref{fup:fm:sec:notation} lists the letters that differ between Parts.

The source's files are shipped with the prefix \texttt{16-nonredundant-}.
\end{writenote}

\section{Conclusion and boundaries of the result}\label{fup:nr:sec:scope}
The raw main projection and the positive21 main norm are genuinely nonredundant comparisons. For each actual fixed compiler and input slice, deleting the respective comparison admits infinitely many strictly positive tuples that violate it, with restored index $R=-p$. These are counterfamilies for the \emph{comparison-deleted} systems. They are not zeros of the original full interfaces and do not by themselves resolve whether deleting the explicit positive-index coordinate can introduce a full negative zero.

The proof has two stages. The broad family theorem realizes the exact affine packing lattice, positive input loading, exact Pell ratio, bounded first-index conditions and all remaining sign obligations. A separate arithmetic argument then filters by a fixed-modulus Jacobi symbol and thins the progression to a certified infinite miss subfamily. Both stages retain genuine compiler numerals throughout.

This report proves a precise nonimplication of one comparison by the others on positive integer tuples. It does not assert unrestricted logical independence of axioms, a full zero, global positivity, a complete parametrization, or a feasible search threshold. Abstract finite fixtures with small numeral ports are not actual compiler exports and are not the genuine counterfamilies proved here.

\paragraph{Historical evidence stages.}
The frozen first-stage proof stated that it had established neither a hit nor a miss of the omitted congruence. That statement remains accurate for its freeze. The separately frozen Jacobi addendum supplies the later exclusion argument, and this report integrates both without changing either source packet. No main-congruence hit is proved. The symbol $-1$ sector and full negative-zero existence remain unresolved.

\paragraph{Relationship to Report 37.}
Report 37 gives an exact necessary-and-sufficient arithmetic predicate for a full strictly positive supplied tuple with restored index $R=-p$ in the raw29 and positive21 interfaces. The present report proves a new sufficiency result for its one-condition deletion. The source/compiler contract, sign-free bootstrap and prior full-zero equivalence remain inherited premises. The analytic proof and the later Jacobi extension are given in full, and every positive witness required for the reduced system is reconstructed below. Neither the complete universal compiler theorem nor the generic ordinary auxiliary rank theorem is reproved here.

\paragraph{Literal mode names.}
The preserved projection names the modes \code{raw30} and \code{positive22}. After eliminating the explicit index coordinate, their supplied lists contain 29 and 21 positive coordinates; we call them raw29 and positive21. Both finalizers are sums of squared integer residuals. The signed19 interface is excluded. A signed-interface construction does not discharge the positive markers required here.

\paragraph{Fixed parameters before asymptotics.}
The compiler, input, $q$, and hence $Y,\delta_0,L_0$ are fixed before $r$ or $N$ tends to infinity. All implicit constants and the eventual threshold may depend on those fixed data. There is no uniform assertion for a growing compiler or growing $q$.

\section{The inherited compiler and predicate}\label{fup:nr:sec:contract}
\subsection{The genuine source contract}
Fix an actual valid export of the complete75 modified half-binomial compiler and $x\ge1$. Retain its fixed integers
\[
 B=2^{\dcell},\qquad K_0=D_C+BD_R,\qquad \MC,\MFnatV,
 \qquad\MFsrcV=\MFnatV+B-1.
\]
The inherited contract supplies
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:contract}
 \begin{gathered}
 B\ge16,\qquad \dcell=bL\ge4,\qquad 1\le b\le\dcell,\qquad b\text{ odd},\\
 0<D_C,D_R<B,\qquad K_0\le B^2-1,\qquad
 0<\MC,\MFnatV<B-1.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
In the genuine recipe $b,L,\dcell$ are powers of five, the inner radix is $R_{\mathrm{rad}}=2^b$, and $B=R_{\mathrm{rad}}^L$. The separated native layout, selectors, duplicated center-clause bands, four anchors and optional high monomial are retained. The last ensures $2D_C-D_R\not\equiv0\pmod5$. For the specified ignored dummy exponent $e_*$, the modified masks are
\[
 \MC=\MC^{\mathrm{old}}-2R_{\mathrm{rad}}^{e_*},\qquad
 \MFnatV=M_F^{\mathrm{old}}+4.
\]
The radix is enlarged until
\[
 R_{\mathrm{rad}}\ge
 \max\!\left(4(K+2)\left(2\sum_e c_e+6\right)+8,\ 2\mu_{\mathrm{mask}}+4,\ 16\right).
\]
Here $K,c_e,\mu_{\mathrm{mask}}$ belong to the fixed native compiler, not to varying witnesses. This is the modified \code{new_constants(compile_windows(...))} export, not the baseline \code{constants()} masks. In particular, $\MFnatV<B-1$ must not be misapplied to the shifted coefficient $\MFsrcV$.

These conditions are provenance from a genuine export, not a license to choose mask numerals freely. The source snapshots and Report 37 theorem are immutable evidence in the accompanying packet; no upstream module or saved arithmetic schedule is executed.

\subsection{Source specific evenness of the main mask}\label{fup:nr:sec:parity}
The present construction additionally uses the source-specific fact that $\MC$ is even. This is not deduced merely from the abbreviated numerical bounds in \eqref{fup:nr:eq:contract}. The modified compiler note explicitly states it in equations (2)--(3), lines 34--46~\cite{fup:nr:compiler}. The retained materializer formula, lines 73--76~\cite{fup:nr:materializer}, is
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:MCparity}
 \MC^0=B-1-\sum_{\substack{e\in\mathcal P\\e\ne1}}R_{\mathrm{rad}}^e,
 \qquad \MC=\MC^0-2R_{\mathrm{rad}}^{e_*}.
\end{equation}
The position construction retains the Start selector $0$ exactly once and every other retained exponent is positive. Since $R_{\mathrm{rad}}$ is even, the sum in \eqref{fup:nr:eq:MCparity} has exactly one odd term, $R_{\mathrm{rad}}^0=1$. Both it and $B-1$ are odd, so $\MC^0$ is even, and the modification preserves parity. No stronger congruence for $\MC$ is assumed. Both source files are cached as inert text with immutable commit and digest references in Appendix~\ref{fup:nr:app:provenance}.

\subsection{Notation and Pell sequences}
Put
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:u}
 u=2\dcell x+b,
\end{equation}
so $u$ is odd and at least $3$. For positive scale coordinates let
\begin{align}\label{fup:nr:eq:scales}
 q&=(B-1)J+1,&X&=wq^3,&Y&=sq^3,&E&=XY,\\
 a&=Y(X+1),&A&=a+2,&\Delta&=A^2-1,&H&=4a+3,\nonumber\\
 P&=2XY^2+1,&Q&=q^2-1,&M&=(\MC+q\MFsrcV)J.&&\nonumber
\end{align}
For each integer $V\ge2$, define the integer Pell sequences by
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:pell}
 \chi_V(j)+\psi_V(j)\sqrt{V^2-1}
 =\bigl(V+\sqrt{V^2-1}\bigr)^j\qquad(j\ge0).
\end{equation}
They satisfy the recurrence $f(j+1)=2Vf(j)-f(j-1)$ with
\[
 \chi_V(0)=1,\quad\chi_V(1)=V,\quad
 \psi_V(0)=0,\quad\psi_V(1)=1.
\]
The mathematical base $A$ differs from the source register \code{A}, which holds $\Delta$. We write $D$ for the main positive Pell root, $k_{\mathrm{first}}$ for the first coefficient, $\alpha_{\mathrm{src}}$ for the raw loading slack, $\faux$ for the auxiliary root, and $\acent$ for the analytic center. Thus neither an analytic logarithm nor a lattice quotient is confused with a supplied coordinate.

\subsection{The exact predicate being reduced}
Report 37 enumerates $q,w>0$ with
\[
 q\ge B,\quad q\text{ even},\quad q\equiv1\pmod{B-1},\quad q>u-b+2,
\]
and finite choices
\[
 1\le s\le3q-1,\qquad
 1\le t\le\left\lfloor\frac{3q-1}{s}\right\rfloor.
\]
Its candidate main indices are the odd integers in
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:range}
 I=\left[
 \max\!\left(13,\left\lceil\frac{tE}{3}\right\rceil+1\right),
 \min\!\left(Xq^4-1,\left\lfloor\frac{tE-6}{2}\right\rfloor\right)
 \right],\qquad n=\frac{tE+1-p}{2}.
\end{equation}
The tests are
\begin{align}\label{fup:nr:eq:predicateratio}
 2Y\psi_P(n)&<\psi_A(p)<2(Y+1)\psi_P(n),\\
 4^{2n-p}&>X,\qquad 2^p\equiv X\pmod H.\label{fup:nr:eq:predicatefilters}
\end{align}
Require $Q\mid p+M$ and recover
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:recover}
 N_{\mathrm{pack}}=\frac{p+M}{Q},\quad
 Z=N_{\mathrm{pack}}\bmod q\quad(0\le Z<q),\quad
 F=q+\frac{N_{\mathrm{pack}}-Z}{q}.
\end{equation}
Reject $Z=0$. For an input branch $e\in\{u,uA\}$, put
\[
 W=2^e\bmod H\quad(0\le W<H),\qquad C=Z+W.
\]
Finally require
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:inputtests}
 e<p,\qquad 0<W<q,\qquad C+u-b<q,
\end{equation}
and that $(K_0+X)C-F$ be a strictly positive multiple of $q-1$.

The reduced predicate keeps every one of these conditions except $2^p\equiv X\pmod H$. In particular it keeps the defect test even though Report 37's necessity proof for that filter uses the main congruence. Here the filter will be proved directly from the constructed indices. The construction uses the odd-input branch $e=u$ throughout.
\begin{writenote}
This section restates Part~V.
