\section{The fixed scale and exact affine lattice}\label{fup:nr:sec:lattice}
\subsection{An explicit admissible scale}
Set $W=2^u=2^bB^{2x}$. Choose any even $q$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:qchoice}
 q\equiv1\pmod{B-1},\qquad 3\nmid q,\qquad q\ge W+u-b+2.
\end{equation}
The convenient choice for every genuine compiler and input is
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:explicitq}
 q=B^{2x+2}.
\end{equation}
Indeed $b\le\dcell$ gives $W\le B^{2x+1}$, while
\[
 q-W\ge(B-1)B^{2x+1}>2\dcell x+1=u-b+1.
\]
For the final strict inequality, $2^{\dcell(2x+1)}>\dcell(2x+1)>2\dcell x+1$ since $\dcell\ge4$. The desired integer bound follows. The power of two in \eqref{fup:nr:eq:explicitq} is even, is not divisible by $3$, and is $1$ modulo $B-1$.

Fix from now on
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:fixed}
 \begin{gathered}
 J=\frac{q-1}{B-1},\quad Q=q^2-1,\quad M=(\MC+q\MFsrcV)J,\\
 C=W+1,\quad Z=1,\quad \alpha_{\mathrm{src}}=q-C-u+b,\quad
 s=t=1,\quad Y=q^3,\quad L_0=q(q-1)Q.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
Then $\alpha_{\mathrm{src}}\ge1$, $0<W<q$, $C+u-b<q$, $C$ is odd, and $M$ is even. These are exact facts before any asymptotic argument. All compiler constants remain unchanged.

\subsection{A common lattice for every progression index}
For integer $r\ge1$ define
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:progression}
 w_r=1+(q-1)r,\qquad X_r=q^3w_r,
 \qquad E_r=X_rY,
\end{equation}
and the fixed integers
\begin{align}\label{fup:nr:eq:lattice}
 P_0&=Q\bigl((1+q(K_0+q^3))C-q^2-W\bigr)-M,\\
 p_0&=P_0\bmod L_0,\quad 0\le p_0<L_0,\qquad p=p_0+L_0 k.\nonumber
\end{align}
Since $Q,C$ are odd and $q,W,M$ are even, $P_0$ is odd. Since $L_0$ is even, $p_0$ is odd and nonzero. Every index in this lattice is odd; no coprimality or extra favorable mask residue is needed.

\begin{lemma}[Exact packing and transport lattice]\label{fup:nr:lem:lattice}
For every $r\ge1$, the lattice in \eqref{fup:nr:eq:lattice} is equivalent to the combined packing and transport congruence at the fixed markers in \eqref{fup:nr:eq:fixed}. With
\[
 \ell_{\mathrm{lat}}=\frac{p-P_0}{L_0}\in\Z,
\]
the recovered coordinates are exactly
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:affine}
 F=(K_0+q^3)C+(q-1)\ell_{\mathrm{lat}},\qquad
 \zquot=q^3Cr-\ell_{\mathrm{lat}}.
\end{equation}
Their remaining sign conditions are strict linear inequalities in $r,\ell_{\mathrm{lat}}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Use $\mathcal D_r=1+q(K_0+X_r)$, not the Pell root $D$. Direct expansion gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:latticediff}
 Q(\mathcal D_rC-q^2-W)-M=P_0+L_0q^3Cr.
\end{equation}
Therefore $p\equiv P_0\pmod{L_0}$ is precisely
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:outercong}
 p+M\equiv Q(\mathcal D_rC-q^2-W)\pmod{L_0}.
\end{equation}
It first makes $N_{\mathrm{pack}}=(p+M)/Q$ integral. Dividing the congruence by $Q$ and reducing modulo $q$ gives
\[
 N_{\mathrm{pack}}\equiv C-W=1\pmod q.
\]
The recovered least marker is exactly $Z=1$. Thus
\[
 F=\frac{N_{\mathrm{pack}}-1+q^2}{q},\qquad
 \zquot=\frac{(K_0+X_r)C-F}{q-1}.
\]
Substituting $p=P_0+L_0\ell_{\mathrm{lat}}$ gives \eqref{fup:nr:eq:affine}, proving every division integral. Conversely those formulas give exactly
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:outeridentities}
 -p=(q^2-1-qF)Q+M,\qquad (K_0+X_r)C=F+\zquot(q-1).
\end{equation}
The two forms of the lattice coordinate differ only by the fixed integer $(P_0-p_0)/L_0$. The analytic argument below chooses an index in this already-compatible lattice; it does not introduce another congruence problem.
\end{proof}

\section{The exact ratio center and its curvature}\label{fup:nr:sec:curvature}
\subsection{The center is defined from the exact Binet expression}
With $Y=q^3$ fixed, let $X$ be a sufficiently large positive real variable and define
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:analyticdefs}
 \begin{gathered}
 A(X)=Y(X+1)+2,\qquad P(X)=2XY^2+1,\\
 \lambda_A(X)=\log\bigl(A+\sqrt{A^2-1}\bigr),\qquad
 \lambda_P(X)=\log\bigl(P+\sqrt{P^2-1}\bigr),\\
 K(X)=\frac{\sqrt{P^2-1}}{2\sqrt{A^2-1}},\quad
 \delta_0=\frac12\log(1+1/Y)>0,\quad
 S(X)=\lambda_A(X)+\frac12\lambda_P(X).
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
At integer $X,p,n$ with $p,n>0$ and $n=(XY+1-p)/2$, Binet's formula gives the exact identity
\begin{align}\label{fup:nr:eq:logratio}
 \log\frac{\psi_A(p)}{2\psi_P(n)}
 &=\log K+p\lambda_A-n\lambda_P+\mathcal T(X,p),\\
 \mathcal T(X,p)
 &=\log(1-e^{-2p\lambda_A})-\log(1-e^{-2n\lambda_P}).\nonumber
\end{align}
The non-tail expression has a smooth real extension; its center is
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:center}
 \acent(X)=\frac{(XY+1)\lambda_P(X)/2+\log Y+\delta_0-\log K(X)}{S(X)}.
\end{equation}
Its non-tail log-ratio equals $\log Y+\delta_0$, the midpoint of the target log interval. At a different $p$, the non-tail part is exactly
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:affinelog}
 \log Y+\delta_0+S(X)\bigl(p-\acent(X)\bigr).
\end{equation}
\subsection{An analytic decomposition with controlled derivatives}
Let $a_0=\log(2Y)>0$, $z=1/X$, and
\[
 v=\frac1{3\log X+4a_0},\qquad a_1=\frac{Y+2}{Y},\quad b_1=\frac1{2Y^2}.
\]
Write
\[
 \lambda_A=\log X+a_0+\epsilon_a(z),\quad
 \lambda_P=\log X+2a_0+\epsilon_b(z),\quad
 \log K=\log Y+\epsilon_K(z).
\]
The remainders have the following exact real-analytic descriptions near $z=0$:
\begin{align}\label{fup:nr:eq:eps}
 \epsilon_a(z)&=\log\frac{1+a_1z+\sqrt{(1+a_1z)^2-z^2/Y^2}}2,\\
 \epsilon_b(z)&=\log\frac{1+b_1z+\sqrt{(1+b_1z)^2-z^2/(4Y^4)}}2,\nonumber\\
 \epsilon_K(z)&=\frac12\log\frac{(1+b_1z)^2-z^2/(4Y^4)}{(1+a_1z)^2-z^2/Y^2}.\nonumber
\end{align}
Each logarithm has argument $1$ at $z=0$, and each square root uses the real branch equal to $1$ there. Hence all three functions are analytic with zero constant terms.

Put $\epsilon_D=2\epsilon_a+\epsilon_b$ and $\epsilon_{\mathrm{diff}}=\epsilon_b-\epsilon_a$. Direct rearrangement of \eqref{fup:nr:eq:center} gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:split}
 \acent(X)=\frac{YX}3+\frac{2Ya_0}3\frac{X}{3\log X+4a_0}
             +\frac13+vG(z,v),
\end{equation}
where the exact two-variable analytic function is
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:G}
 G(z,v)=\frac{
 \begin{aligned}
 &\frac{2Y}3\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{diff}}(z)}z
 +\frac23\bigl(a_0+\epsilon_{\mathrm{diff}}(z)\bigr)
 +2\delta_0-2\epsilon_K(z)\\[-1pt]
 &\hspace{25pt}-\frac{2Ya_0}3v\frac{\epsilon_D(z)}z
 \end{aligned}}{1+v\epsilon_D(z)}.
\end{equation}
The divided numerators vanish at zero, so their singularities are removable analytically. The denominator is $1$ at $(z,v)=(0,0)$. This establishes analyticity in two independent variables, not just a formal asymptotic series along a curve.

For completeness, the rearrangement may be verified without asymptotic manipulation. Substitute $\log X=(v^{-1}-4a_0)/3$ in twice the numerator and denominator of \eqref{fup:nr:eq:center}. After clearing the common denominator and multiplying by $3vz$, the identity becomes
\begin{align*}
 &(Y+z)(1+2a_0v+3\epsilon_bv)+6\delta_0vz-6\epsilon_Kvz\\
 &=(Y+2Ya_0v+z)(1+v\epsilon_D)\\
 &\quad+v\bigl(2Y\epsilon_{\mathrm{diff}}+2z(a_0+\epsilon_{\mathrm{diff}})
       +6\delta_0z-6\epsilon_Kz-2Ya_0v\epsilon_D\bigr).
\end{align*}
This polynomial identity is checked by both independent algebra implementations in the packet.

\begin{lemma}[Controlled second derivative]\label{fup:nr:lem:curvature}
As $X\to\infty$ with $Y$ fixed,
\begin{align}\label{fup:nr:eq:centerasymp}
 \acent(X)&=\frac{YX}3+\frac{2Ya_0}3\frac{X}{3\log X+4a_0}
                  +\frac13+O(1/\log X),\\
 \acent''(X)&=-\frac{2Ya_0}{9X(\log X)^2}
                  \left(1+O(1/\log X)\right).\label{fup:nr:eq:fsecond}
\end{align}
The second assertion follows by controlled differentiation of an analytic remainder.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Analyticity of $G$ yields bounded partial derivatives through order two on a sufficiently small closed neighborhood of $(0,0)$. Set $R(X)=vG(z,v)$. Along the curve,
\[
 z'=-X^{-2},\quad z''=2X^{-3},\quad
 v'=-3v^2/X,\quad v''=(3v^2+18v^3)/X^2.
\]
As a function of two variables, $R$ has derivative bounds
\[
 R_z=O(v),\quad R_v=O(1),\quad R_{zz}=O(v),\quad
 R_{zv}=O(1),\quad R_{vv}=O(1).
\]
The multivariable chain rule gives
\begin{align*}
 R''&=R_{zz}(z')^2+2R_{zv}z'v'+R_{vv}(v')^2+R_z\,z''+R_v\,v''\\
     &=O\!\left(\frac v{X^3}+\frac{v^2}{X^2}\right)
      =O\!\left(\frac1{X^2(\log X)^2}\right),
\end{align*}
using $X^{-1}=O(v)$ eventually. The curved explicit term in \eqref{fup:nr:eq:split} has exact second derivative
\[
 \frac{d^2}{dX^2}\left(\frac{2Ya_0}3Xv\right)
 =-\frac{2Ya_0v^2(1-6v)}X.
\]
The linear term and the constant have second derivative zero. This proves \eqref{fup:nr:eq:fsecond}; \eqref{fup:nr:eq:centerasymp} follows directly from boundedness of $G$. In particular, the proof does not differentiate an uncontrolled $O(1/\log X)$ term.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Curvature along the actual lattice progression}
Let
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:g}
 g(r)=\frac{\acent(X_r)-p_0}{L_0},\qquad
 c_0=q^3(q-1),\qquad X_r=q^3+c_0r.
\end{equation}
The chain rule and Lemma~\ref{fup:nr:lem:curvature} yield
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:gsecond}
 g''(r)=-\frac{K_*}{r(\log r)^2}
        \left(1+O(1/\log r)\right),\qquad
 K_* =\frac{2Ya_0c_0}{9L_0}>0.
\end{equation}
The analytic constant $K_*$ is unrelated to the compiler numeral $K_0$. Because $q$ was fixed, $\log X_r=\log r+O(1)$. There are therefore fixed $a_*,b_*>0$ and an eventual threshold such that on each sufficiently large dyadic interval,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:dyadicderivative}
 \frac{a_*}{N(\log N)^2}\le |g''(r)|\le
 \frac{b_*}{N(\log N)^2}\qquad (N\le r\le2N).
\end{equation}
The constants do not depend on $N$ or on the Fourier frequency introduced next.

\section{A quantitative shrinking target argument}\label{fup:nr:sec:discrepancy}
\subsection{The two analytic estimates used}
We invoke two classical estimates, with precisely the uniformity needed here.

\begin{lemma}[Second derivative estimate]\label{fup:nr:lem:vdc}
If a real $C^2$ phase $h$ on an interval containing $N$ consecutive integers satisfies $\lambda\le|h''|\le C\lambda$, then
\[
 \left|\sum e^{2\pi i h(r)}\right|
 \ll_C N\sqrt\lambda+\lambda^{-1/2}.
\]
The constant depends only on the fixed comparability ratio $C$.
\end{lemma}
This is the second-derivative theorem stated by Robert, Theorem 1, Section 3.1, printed page 5~\cite{fup:nr:robert}. Translation of the interval does not change the bound.

\begin{lemma}[Interval discrepancy]\label{fup:nr:lem:ET}
For any real sequence $x_1,\ldots,x_N$, any interval $I$ in the unit circle, and any integer $H_f\ge1$,
\[
 \left|\#\{j:\{x_j\}\in I\}-N|I|\right|
 \ll\frac{N}{H_f+1}+
 \sum_{h=1}^{H_f}\frac1h\left|\sum_{j=1}^Ne^{2\pi ihx_j}\right|.
\]
The implied constant is absolute and uniform in the interval $I$.
\end{lemma}
The normalized Erd\H{o}s--Tur\'an form in Mukhopadhyay, Ramar\'e and Viswanadham, Lemma 6, printed page 5~\cite{fup:nr:discrepancy}, implies this displayed unnormalized form. These primary sources were checked for the stated hypotheses and uniformity. Their estimates are analytic inputs; neither is inferred from finite numerical samples in this project.

\subsection{Uniformity in every frequency being summed}
For $1\le h\le H_f$, apply Lemma~\ref{fup:nr:lem:vdc} to $hg(r)$ on $N\le r<2N$, taking
\[
 \lambda=\frac{a_*h}{N(\log N)^2},\qquad C=b_*/a_*.
\]
Multiplication by $h$ leaves the comparability ratio fixed. Uniformly over all these frequencies,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:frequencies}
 \left|\sum_{N\le r<2N}e^{2\pi ihg(r)}\right|
 \ll\frac{\sqrt{hN}}{\log N}+
       \frac{\sqrt N\log N}{\sqrt h}.
\end{equation}
There is no hidden dependence on $h$ in the implied constant. The weighted sum in Lemma~\ref{fup:nr:lem:ET} is bounded by
\[
 \frac{\sqrt N}{\log N}\sum_{h\le H_f}h^{-1/2}
 +\sqrt N\log N\sum_{h\le H_f}h^{-3/2}
 \ll\frac{\sqrt{NH_f}}{\log N}+\sqrt N\log N.
\]
Choose $H_f=\lfloor(\log N)^4\rfloor$. Uniformly over every target interval, including intervals depending on $N$, the count error is
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:error}
 O\!\left(\frac N{(\log N)^4}+\sqrt N\log N\right)
 =o(N/\log N).
\end{equation}
This quantitative estimate is the essential improvement over qualitative equidistribution. A fixed-interval density statement would not suffice for windows of width $1/\log r$.

\subsection{The lower count with its explicit fixed constant}
Let
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:kappa0}
 \kappa_0=\frac{\delta_0}{8L_0}>0,
 \qquad I_N=[0,\kappa_0/\log(2N)).
\end{equation}
For sufficiently large $N$, this is a valid interval in $[0,1)$. Its leading count is $\kappa_0N/\log(2N)$. The error in \eqref{fup:nr:eq:error} is eventually less than half that fixed positive leading term. Since $\log(2N)\le2\log N$ for $N\ge2$, there are at least
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:count}
 \frac{\kappa_0N}{2\log(2N)}\ge
 \frac{\delta_0}{32L_0}\frac N{\log N}
\end{equation}
integers $r\in[N,2N)$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:target}
 0\le\{g(r)\}<\frac{\kappa_0}{\log r}.
\end{equation}
Indeed $r<2N$ makes the interval $I_N$ narrower than the pointwise interval in \eqref{fup:nr:eq:target}. Fractional part zero is allowed and causes no endpoint difficulty. The threshold may be enormous because $\kappa_0$ is a very small fixed constant. No numerical value or practical complexity estimate is asserted.

\section{Exact Pell acceptance and every strict bound}\label{fup:nr:sec:acceptance}
For each sufficiently large $r$ selected by \eqref{fup:nr:eq:target}, take
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:indices}
 k_{\mathrm{lat}}=\lfloor g(r)\rfloor,\qquad
 p=p_0+L_0k_{\mathrm{lat}},\qquad
 n=\frac{E_r+1-p}{2}.
\end{equation}
The lattice makes $p$ odd. Since $E_r$ is even, $n$ is an integer. The rounding error is exactly controlled:
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:rounding}
 0\le\acent(X_r)-p=L_0\{g(r)\}
 <\frac{\delta_0}{8\log r}.
\end{equation}

\subsection{The exact ratio rather than a necessary real window}
As $S(X_r)\sim\tfrac32\log r$, eventually $S(X_r)\le2\log r$. By \eqref{fup:nr:eq:affinelog}, the non-tail logarithm at $p$ lies between
\[
 \log Y+\tfrac34\delta_0\quad\text{and}\quad\log Y+\delta_0.
\]
Equations \eqref{fup:nr:eq:centerasymp} and \eqref{fup:nr:eq:rounding} imply that $p$ and $n$ tend to infinity linearly with $X_r$. The two tail arguments in \eqref{fup:nr:eq:logratio} tend to zero exponentially in $X_r\log X_r$. For example, $|\log(1-z)|\le2z$ for $0\le z\le1/2$, so for all sufficiently large selected $r$ the sum of the absolute tail terms is below $\delta_0/4$. Hence the full exact log-ratio is strictly between $\log Y$ and $\log Y+2\delta_0=\log(Y+1)$. Exponentiating gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:exactratio}
 Y<\frac{\psi_A(p)}{2\psi_P(n)}<Y+1.
\end{equation}
This proves exact acceptance by integer Pell values, not merely passage through a necessary approximate interval.

\subsection{The positive deviation above one third}
The center expansion yields the stronger asymptotic information
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:pdev}
 p=\frac{E_r}3+
 \frac{2Ya_0}3\frac{X_r}{3\log X_r+4a_0}+\frac13+O(1/\log X_r).
\end{equation}
In particular,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:ratios}
 \frac p{E_r}\longrightarrow\frac13,\qquad
 p-\frac{E_r}3\longrightarrow+\infty,\qquad
 \frac n{E_r}\longrightarrow\frac13,
 \qquad d_{\mathrm{def}}=2n-p=E_r+1-2p\sim\frac{E_r}3.
\end{equation}
The fixed positive coefficient of $X_r/\log X_r$ matters: the limit $p/E_r\to1/3$ alone would not justify the strict upper endpoint $E_r\le3p-3$.

\begin{proposition}[All eventual index conditions]\label{fup:nr:prop:bounds}
One finite threshold, enlarged if necessary, ensures that every selected $r$ above it satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:allbounds}
 \begin{gathered}
 p\ge13\text{ odd},\qquad u<p,\qquad
 \frac{p+1}2\le n\le p-1,\qquad 2n+p-1=E_r,\\
 2p+6\le E_r\le3p-3,\qquad p<X_rq^4,
 \qquad 4^{d_{\mathrm{def}}}>X_r.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
Thus $p$ lies in the precise interval \eqref{fup:nr:eq:range} with $t=1$, including its integer endpoints.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The first two assertions follow from $p\to\infty$ and fixed $u$. The quantity $E_r-2p\sim E_r/3$ tends to infinity, yielding $2p+6\le E_r$. The deviation in \eqref{fup:nr:eq:ratios} yields $3p-E_r\to+\infty$ and therefore $E_r\le3p-3$. These give the stated bounds on $n$ through \eqref{fup:nr:eq:indices}. They also give $p\ge\lceil E_r/3\rceil+1$ and $p\le\lfloor(E_r-6)/2\rfloor$ without losing an endpoint. The identity $2n+p-1=E_r$ is exact; its $+1$ cannot be discarded. Further,
\[
 \frac p{X_rq^4}\longrightarrow\frac1{3q}<1.
\]
Since $p$ and $X_rq^4$ are integers, eventual strict inequality also gives $p\le X_rq^4-1$. Finally $d_{\mathrm{def}}\sim E_r/3$ grows linearly in $X_r$, so $d_{\mathrm{def}}\log4-\log X_r\to+\infty$. This proves the defect filter directly, independently of the omitted congruence. A finite list of eventual bounds has a common enlarged threshold.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Every outer and input sign}
The exact recovery formula has asymptotic
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:Fasymp}
 \frac F{X_r}\longrightarrow\frac{Y}{3qQ}
 =\frac{q^2}{3(q^2-1)}<1.
\end{equation}
Indeed $M,q,Q$ are fixed and $p/X_r\to Y/3$. Thus $F>q$ eventually. Since $C=W+1>1$,
\[
 \frac{(K_0+X_r)C-F}{X_r}
 \longrightarrow C-\frac{q^2}{3(q^2-1)}>0.
\]
The quotient $\zquot$ in Lemma~\ref{fup:nr:lem:lattice} is therefore a strictly positive integer. The loading inequalities and $\alpha_{\mathrm{src}}>0$ already hold by \eqref{fup:nr:eq:qchoice}. Also $H>q>W=2^u$ for every $r\ge1$, so $W$ is exactly the least input residue $2^u\bmod H$. With $e=u<p$, every input test is met. Finally $s=t=1$ satisfies both finite ranges in Section~\ref{fup:nr:sec:contract}.

\section{The infinite family theorem}\label{fup:nr:sec:theorem}
\begin{theorem}[Infinite candidates with one congruence omitted]\label{fup:nr:thm:family}
Under the genuine fixed compiler and bootstrap premises inherited from Report 37, fix any valid complete75 modified compiler export and any $x\ge1$. Choose $q$ as in \eqref{fup:nr:eq:qchoice}, for example $q=B^{2x+2}$, and fix the witnesses in \eqref{fup:nr:eq:fixed}. There is $N_0$ such that for every integer $N\ge N_0$, at least
\[
 \frac{\delta_0N}{32L_0\log N}
\]
distinct scales $w_r=1+(q-1)r$ with $N\le r<2N$ admit odd indices $p$ satisfying every condition of Report 37's exact negative-zero predicate except
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:unresolved}
 2^p\equiv X_r\pmod{4Y(X_r+1)+3}.
\end{equation}
Every compiler numeral is unchanged. The reduced predicate has infinitely many solutions on every such fixed compiler/input slice.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The discrepancy argument gives the lower count of $r$ in \eqref{fup:nr:eq:count}. Lemma~\ref{fup:nr:lem:lattice} places the selected odd index in the exact packing/transport lattice. Section~\ref{fup:nr:sec:acceptance} proves the exact ratio, all interval and defect conditions, and all outer/input tests with positive markers. Enlarge one threshold to cover every eventual claim. The progression is injective in $r$, so these are distinct scales. Taking arbitrarily large disjoint dyadic intervals supplies infinitely many solutions. None of these steps invokes \eqref{fup:nr:eq:unresolved}.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{What the count measures.}
Here $N$ measures the progression index $r$, not $w$ and not the bit length of the data. The counted actual scales lie in
\[
 [\,1+(q-1)N,\ 1+2(q-1)N\,).
\]
The constant and threshold may depend on the fixed compiler/input/$q$. The theorem says at least the displayed real number of integer scales, equivalently the count is an integer no smaller than it. It asserts neither a density bounded away from zero nor a practical first accepted scale.

\paragraph{Why this is stronger than outer compatibility.}
The affine formulas alone do not force the narrow Pell ratio. The center has a curved $X/\log X$ correction to its linear term, and this curvature quantitatively distributes its lattice coordinate on the circle. The resulting $o(N/\log N)$ discrepancy error is small enough for the shrinking index windows. The old fixed-$X$ bounded window cannot be used as though it were an unbounded density problem.


\section{A fixed modulus Jacobi filter}\label{fup:nr:sec:jacobi}
The broad family theorem does not decide the main congruence. The new arithmetic input does decide it on a carefully chosen infinite subfamily. Write $\mathcal J(a,m)$ for the Jacobi symbol with positive odd denominator $m$, allowing the value zero when $a,m$ are not coprime.

\begin{lemma}[Fixed modulus character identity]\label{fup:nr:lem:jacobi}
Let $q$ be an even square with $3\nmid q$. For every positive integer $w$, put
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:h0}
 h_0=4q^3+3,\qquad X=q^3w,\qquad H=4q^6w+h_0.
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:jacobiidentity}
 \mathcal J(X,H)=\mathcal J(w,h_0).
\end{equation}
Consequently, for every positive odd $p$,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:jacobifilter}
 2^p\equiv X\pmod H\quad\Longrightarrow\quad \mathcal J(w,h_0)=-1.
\end{equation}
No denominator is required to be prime or squarefree.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Both $H$ and $h_0$ are $3$ modulo $8$. Since $H\equiv3\pmod q$ and $3\nmid q$, we have $\gcd(q,H)=1$. The integer $q^3$ is a square, so its Jacobi symbol modulo $H$ is $1$. Thus $\mathcal J(X,H)=\mathcal J(w,H)$. Also
\[
 \gcd(w,H)=\gcd(w,h_0).
\]
If this gcd is greater than one, both sides of \eqref{fup:nr:eq:jacobiidentity} are zero. Otherwise write $w=2^\nu z$ with $z$ positive and odd. The supplementary law gives
\[
 \mathcal J(2,H)=\mathcal J(2,h_0)=-1.
\]
If $z=1$, these equal factors finish the identity. If $z>1$, apply reciprocity only to the coprime odd pairs. Since $H\equiv h_0\pmod z$ and $H,h_0\equiv3\pmod4$,
\begin{align*}
 \mathcal J(z,H)
 &=(-1)^{(z-1)/2}\mathcal J(H,z)\\
 &=(-1)^{(z-1)/2}\mathcal J(h_0,z)
 =\mathcal J(z,h_0).
\end{align*}
The two reciprocity signs cancel. Multiplying by the equal factors from $2^\nu$ proves \eqref{fup:nr:eq:jacobiidentity}, including even $w$. Finally, for odd $p$,
\[
 \mathcal J(2^p,H)=\mathcal J(2,H)^p=-1.
\]
Jacobi symbols depend only on numerator residue classes, proving the necessary filter.
\end{proof}
The laws used here are multiplicativity, the supplementary law for $2$, and quadratic reciprocity for Jacobi symbols. NIST DLMF Section 27.9 explicitly extends the supplementary laws and reciprocity to relatively prime positive odd composite denominators~\cite{fup:nr:dlmf}. The noncoprime case was handled before either reciprocity step. Symbol $-1$ is only a necessary test, not a sufficient criterion for a congruence hit.

\subsection{Two fixed thinnings that force a miss}
For a genuine compiler and input, choose the explicit scale
\[
 q=B^{2x+2}=(B^{x+1})^2.
\]
It is an even square coprime to $3$, as required by Lemma~\ref{fup:nr:lem:jacobi}. Keep all markers and lattice constants from Sections~\ref{fup:nr:sec:lattice}--\ref{fup:nr:sec:theorem}. Choose either fixed thinning factor
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:thinfactor}
 T_{\mathrm{thin}}=h_0\qquad\text{or}\qquad T_{\mathrm{thin}}=4h_0,
\end{equation}
and restrict
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:thinprogression}
 r=T_{\mathrm{thin}}j,\qquad
 w_j=1+(q-1)T_{\mathrm{thin}}j\equiv1\pmod{h_0},\qquad j\ge1.
\end{equation}
Then $\mathcal J(w_j,h_0)=1$, so \eqref{fup:nr:eq:unresolved} fails for \emph{every odd} $p$ at these scales. The choice $T_{\mathrm{thin}}=h_0$ retains more progression indices and can give even $w$. The factor $4h_0$ from the original proposal also works; it has $w\equiv1\pmod{4h_0}$ and permits a shorter proof with individually positive reciprocity signs.

Arithmetic exclusion alone would not show that any of these scales meets the exact Pell ratio. That requires a fresh shrinking-target argument on the thinned sequence, proved next. One may not assume that the already-selected parent hits distribute among residue classes.

\section{Infinitely many certified misses after fixed thinning}\label{fup:nr:sec:misses}
Define
\[
 g_T(j)=g(T_{\mathrm{thin}}j).
\]
All fixed data, including $T_{\mathrm{thin}}$, precede the limit. Equation~\eqref{fup:nr:eq:gsecond} and the exact chain rule give
\begin{align}\label{fup:nr:eq:thinsecond}
 g_T''(j)&=T_{\mathrm{thin}}^2g''(T_{\mathrm{thin}}j)\nonumber\\
 &=-\frac{K_*T_{\mathrm{thin}}}{j(\log j)^2}
       \left(1+O(1/\log j)\right).
\end{align}
Thus the curvature coefficient is multiplied by $T_{\mathrm{thin}}$, not by its square after changing variables in the asymptotic denominator. The derivative-comparability ratio remains independent of the Fourier frequency.

Repeat Lemmas~\ref{fup:nr:lem:vdc}--\ref{fup:nr:lem:ET} on $N\le j<2N$. Uniformly for $1\le h\le\lfloor(\log N)^4\rfloor$,
\[
 \left|\sum_{N\le j<2N}e^{2\pi ihg_T(j)}\right|
 \ll\frac{\sqrt{hN}}{\log N}+\frac{\sqrt N\log N}{\sqrt h}.
\]
The implied constant may now depend on the fixed thinning factor. The interval-count error is still
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:thinerror}
 O\!\left(\frac N{(\log N)^4}+\sqrt N\log N\right)=o(N/\log N).
\end{equation}
Use the narrower interval
\[
 I_{T,N}=\left[0,\frac{\kappa_0}{\log(2T_{\mathrm{thin}}N)}\right).
\]
Its leading count is $\kappa_0N/\log(2T_{\mathrm{thin}}N)$. Since $T_{\mathrm{thin}}$ is fixed, this dominates \eqref{fup:nr:eq:thinerror} eventually, leaving at least half the leading count. Enlarge the threshold so $N\ge2T_{\mathrm{thin}}$; then $\log(2T_{\mathrm{thin}}N)\le2\log N$, and the count is at least
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:thincount}
 \frac{\kappa_0N}{2\log(2T_{\mathrm{thin}}N)}
 \ge\frac{\delta_0N}{32L_0\log N}.
\end{equation}
Every counted $j$ satisfies
\[
 0\le\{g(T_{\mathrm{thin}}j)\}
 <\frac{\kappa_0}{\log(T_{\mathrm{thin}}j)}.
\]
This is exactly the earlier pointwise hypothesis \eqref{fup:nr:eq:target} at $r=T_{\mathrm{thin}}j$. Thus all interior-margin, Binet-tail, strict-bound and positive-reconstruction arguments apply unchanged.

\begin{theorem}[Infinite genuine comparison counterfamilies]\label{fup:nr:thm:misses}
Under the inherited genuine compiler and bootstrap premises, fix any valid compiler export, any $x\ge1$, and $q=B^{2x+2}$. Choose $T_{\mathrm{thin}}=h_0$ or $4h_0$ as above. For all sufficiently large integers $N$, at least $\delta_0N/(32L_0\log N)$ indices $j\in[N,2N)$ yield distinct scales \eqref{fup:nr:eq:thinprogression} satisfying every condition of the reduced Report 37 predicate, with
\[
 2^p\not\equiv X\pmod H.
\]
Their positive reconstructions have restored index $R=-p$ and strictly positive remainder $1\le\rmain<H$. In raw29 they satisfy every comparison except the main projection, which they violate. In positive21 they satisfy every retained comparison except the main norm, which they violate. Hence each indicated comparison is nonredundant relative to its own remaining conjunction on strictly positive integer tuples.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Equation~\eqref{fup:nr:eq:thincount} supplies infinitely many exact-ratio candidates after fixed thinning. The Jacobi identity excludes the main congruence for every selected odd $p$. Sections~\ref{fup:nr:sec:reconstruction}--\ref{fup:nr:sec:residual} provide all positive supplied coordinates and the literal residual values. Their nonzero remainder makes the indicated residual nonzero in each mode, while all others vanish.
\end{proof}
Here $N$ counts $j$. The corresponding original progression indices and actual scales lie in
\[
 [T_{\mathrm{thin}}N,2T_{\mathrm{thin}}N),\qquad
 [1+(q-1)T_{\mathrm{thin}}N,\ 1+2(q-1)T_{\mathrm{thin}}N).
\]
The same displayed constant does not mean that the parent $r$-count was silently reused. It follows from a fresh discrepancy proof on $j$, and its threshold now depends on $T_{\mathrm{thin}}$. The result is not a positive natural density claim.

\section{Complete strictly positive witness reconstruction}\label{fup:nr:sec:reconstruction}
This section spells out the literal completion, so the result is not dependent on an unexamined sign assertion. The only potentially unsatisfied raw comparison will be the main projection comparison. The omitted congruence is not used anywhere in the completion.
\begin{writenote}
This reconstruction specializes Part~V's (Section~\ref{fup:nz:sec:theorem}): \eqref{fup:nr:eq:firstwitness} is the first and main block of~\eqref{fup:nz:eq:mainwitness}, \eqref{fup:nr:eq:inputwitness} the input block~\eqref{fup:nz:eq:inputwitness} on the branch $e=u$, and \eqref{fup:nr:eq:mchoice}--\eqref{fup:nr:eq:auxwitness} the auxiliary block~\eqref{fup:nz:eq:m}--\eqref{fup:nz:eq:auxwitness}, which Part~V in turn credits to the research tree's bootstrap note. The recurrence~\eqref{fup:nr:eq:Estar}--\eqref{fup:nr:eq:projectionrecurrence} is~\eqref{fup:nz:eq:E-star}, that is, Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:projection} of Report~33; the identities~\eqref{fup:nr:eq:Qidentities} are Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:oddquotient}; and the divisibility $c^2\mid\psi_A(m)$ is Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:divisibility}. The residual Table~\ref{fup:nr:tab:residuals} specializes Part~V's Table~\ref{fup:nz:tab:residuals}. What is new here is that every coordinate is positive on the family without the main congruence, and the two residual formulas of Section~\ref{fup:nr:sec:residual}.
\end{writenote}

\subsection{The literal retained system}
The restored index is $R=k_{\mathrm{first}}-hE-1$. The outer system is
\begin{align}\label{fup:nr:eq:literalouter}
 q&=(B-1)J+1,& C+\alpha_{\mathrm{src}}+u-b&=q,\\
 (K_0+X)C&=F+\zquot(q-1),& C&=Z+W,\nonumber\\
 R&=(q^2-Z-qF)Q+M.&&\nonumber
\end{align}
The first and main comparisons are
\begin{align}\label{fup:nr:eq:literalfirst}
 \tau^2-XY^2(XY^2+1)k_{\mathrm{first}}^2&=1,&
 c&=k_{\mathrm{first}}Y+\eta,\\
 k_{\mathrm{first}}&=\eta+\zeta,& a&=Y(X+1),\nonumber\\
 D&=X+ac+\ga H,&D^2-\Delta c^2&=1.\nonumber
\end{align}
The ordinary strong and auxiliary comparisons, with $T=ic^2$ and $U=jc-R$, are
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:literalaux}
 T^2=\Delta(\faux^2-1),\qquad
 T^2(U^2-\yaux^2)=1-\yaux^2,\qquad U=o\faux-c.
\end{equation}
The input block is
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:literalinput}
 \kappa=u+\delta_{\mathrm{in}}\Delta,\quad c=\kappa+\phi,\quad
 \mu_{\mathrm{in}}^2=1+\Delta\kappa^2,\quad
 \mu_{\mathrm{in}}=W+a\kappa+\rho H.
\end{equation}
In these two interfaces $\ga$ and $\rho$ are independent positive coordinates. The relation $\ga=\rho+\sigma$ belongs to a different signed interface and must not be inserted here.

\subsection{First block and restored negative index}
For a selected candidate set
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:firstwitness}
 \begin{gathered}
 c=\psi_A(p),\quad D=\chi_A(p),\quad
 k_{\mathrm{first}}=2\psi_P(n),\quad\tau=\chi_P(n),\\
 \eta=c-k_{\mathrm{first}}Y,\quad
 \zeta=k_{\mathrm{first}}-\eta,\quad
 h=\frac{k_{\mathrm{first}}+p-1}{E}.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
The exact ratio gives $\eta,\zeta>0$. Since $P\equiv1\pmod E$, the recurrence implies $\psi_P(n)\equiv n\pmod E$. Hence
\[
 k_{\mathrm{first}}+p-1\equiv2n+p-1=E\equiv0\pmod E.
\]
Its numerator is positive, so $h$ is a positive integer and $R=-p$ exactly. The two Pell identities give
\[
 D^2-\Delta c^2=1,\qquad
 \tau^2-1=(P^2-1)\psi_P(n)^2
          =XY^2(XY^2+1)k_{\mathrm{first}}^2.
\]
The factor $2$ in $k_{\mathrm{first}}$ is essential for the first norm. Every outer comparison holds by the definitions and the exact lattice identities.

\subsection{A recurrence for the exponent projections}\label{fup:nr:sec:Estar}
Define $E_j^*=\chi_A(j)-a\psi_A(j)$. Since $a=A-2$, the Pell recurrence gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:Estar}
 E_0^*=1,\quad E_1^*=2,\quad
 E_j^*=2\psi_A(j)-\psi_A(j-1)>\psi_A(j)\quad(j\ge1).
\end{equation}
Moreover
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:projectionrecurrence}
 E_j^*\equiv2^j\pmod H.
\end{equation}
Indeed $2^j$ has the same initial values and satisfies the same recurrence modulo $H$, because $4-4A+1=-H$. For $j\ge3$, monotonicity of the Pell sequence gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:Estargrowth}
 \psi_A(j)\ge\psi_A(3)=4A^2-1>H+X,
 \qquad \psi_A(j)>H+q.
\end{equation}
Here $a>X,q$, since $X,Y\ge q^3$. These inequalities will ensure positive quotients without assuming the main projection is divisible.

\subsection{Every positive input coordinate}
Use the fixed odd input index $e=u$ and set
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:inputwitness}
 \kappa=\psi_A(u),\quad\mu_{\mathrm{in}}=\chi_A(u),\quad
 \delta_{\mathrm{in}}=\frac{\kappa-u}{\Delta},\quad
 \phi=c-\kappa,\quad
 \rho=\frac{\mu_{\mathrm{in}}-a\kappa-W}{H}.
\end{equation}
The recurrence modulo $\Delta$, where $A^2\equiv1$, gives
\[
 \psi_A(j)\equiv
 \begin{cases}j,&j\text{ odd},\\jA,&j\text{ even}\end{cases}
 \pmod\Delta.
\]
Thus $\delta_{\mathrm{in}}$ is integral; $u\ge3$ and strict Pell growth give $\kappa>u$, so it is positive. Since $u<p$, $\phi>0$. Equation~\eqref{fup:nr:eq:projectionrecurrence} and $W=2^u\bmod H$ make $\rho$ integral. Equation~\eqref{fup:nr:eq:Estargrowth} and $W<q$ make it positive. The supplied root $\mu_{\mathrm{in}}$ is the positive Pell root. All four input comparisons hold exactly.

\subsection{Auxiliary completion for both odd index classes}
The all-odd-index construction inherited from Report 37 may be verified directly. Define
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:mchoice}
 m=\begin{cases}cp,&p\equiv3\pmod4,\\2cp,&p\equiv1\pmod4,
 \end{cases}\qquad
 \faux=\chi_A(m),\quad\Taux=\Delta\psi_A(m),\quad
 i=\frac{\Taux}{c^2}.
\end{equation}
Let
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:auxwitness}
 \ell=p+2m,\qquad
 U=\frac{\chi_{\Taux}(\ell)}{\Taux},\quad
 \yaux=\psi_{\Taux}(\ell),\quad
 j=\frac{U-p}{c},\quad o=\frac{U+c}{\faux}.
\end{equation}
Here $j$ denotes the supplied auxiliary coordinate, not the progression index of Section~\ref{fup:nr:sec:misses}. All these auxiliary quantities are positive integers, as follows.

Write $m=pv_0$, with $v_0=c$ or $2c$. The square-root coefficient of $(D+c\sqrt\Delta)^{v_0}$ is $\psi_A(m)$. Its first odd binomial term contains $v_0c$, divisible by $c^2$; every later odd term contains $c^3$. Therefore $c^2\mid\psi_A(m)$ and $i>0$ is integral. The norm gives
\[
 \Taux^2=\Delta(\faux^2-1).
\]
Because $A$ is even and $p$ is odd, $c$ is odd. The two choices in \eqref{fup:nr:eq:mchoice} both give $\ell\equiv1\pmod4$. In particular, the factor two for $p\equiv1\pmod4$ cannot be replaced by the other branch.

For every odd $\ell=2h_{\mathrm{aux}}+1$, the quotient $\chi_T(\ell)/T$ is an integer polynomial $Q_{h_{\mathrm{aux}}}(T^2)$ satisfying
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:Qidentities}
 Q_{h_{\mathrm{aux}}}(0)=(-1)^{h_{\mathrm{aux}}}(2h_{\mathrm{aux}}+1),\qquad
 Q_{h_{\mathrm{aux}}}(1-A^2)=(-1)^{h_{\mathrm{aux}}}\psi_A(2h_{\mathrm{aux}}+1).
\end{equation}
These identities hold for all indices: $Q_0(t)=1$, $Q_1(t)=4t-3$ and
\[
 Q_{h_{\mathrm{aux}}+1}(t)=2(2t-1)Q_{h_{\mathrm{aux}}}(t)-Q_{h_{\mathrm{aux}}-1}(t).
\]
The evaluations in \eqref{fup:nr:eq:Qidentities} follow by the same recurrence and initial values, with the sign $(-1)^{h_{\mathrm{aux}}}$ in the second identity. Thus the proof is not limited to the finitely many polynomial fixtures.

Here $h_{\mathrm{aux}}$ is even. Since $c\mid\Taux$ and $c\mid m$, the first identity gives $U\equiv\ell\equiv p\pmod c$. Modulo $\faux$, the strong norm gives $\Taux^2\equiv-\Delta=1-A^2$; the second identity gives $U\equiv\psi_A(\ell)$. Pell addition formulas give
\[
 \chi_A(2m)\equiv-1\pmod{\faux},\qquad
 \psi_A(2m)\equiv0\pmod{\faux},
\]
so $\psi_A(p+2m)\equiv-c\pmod{\faux}$. This proves integrality of $j,o$. Since $\Taux\ge c^2$, $\ell\ge3$ and Pell growth gives $c>p$,
\[
 U\ge4\Taux^2-3>p.
\]
Consequently $j,o>0$. Finally the Pell norm at base $\Taux$ is
\[
 \Taux^2(U^2-\yaux^2)=1-\yaux^2,
\]
and $U=jc+p=jc-R=o\faux-c$. This verifies every comparison in \eqref{fup:nr:eq:literalaux} with positive $\faux,i,j,o,\yaux$. No uniqueness or reasonable size of these auxiliary witnesses is claimed.

\section{The literal one residual and the two different sums of squares}\label{fup:nr:sec:residual}
Set
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:residue}
 \ga=\left\lfloor\frac{D-ac-X}{H}\right\rfloor,\qquad
 \rmain=D-ac-X-\ga H.
\end{equation}
By \eqref{fup:nr:eq:Estar}--\eqref{fup:nr:eq:Estargrowth}, $D-ac>H+X$, so $\ga\ge1$. By Euclidean division and \eqref{fup:nr:eq:projectionrecurrence},
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:mainres}
 0\le\rmain<H,\qquad \rmain=(2^p-X)\bmod H.
\end{equation}
Thus every supplied raw coordinate is positive even without knowing whether $\rmain$ is zero.

\subsection{The exact supplied tuples}
The raw29 tuple in saved order, using the aliases introduced above, is
\begin{align*}
 (&C,F,J,W,Z,a,\alpha_{\mathrm{src}},c,D,\delta_{\mathrm{in}},\eta,\faux,\ga,h,i,j,
 k_{\mathrm{first}},\kappa,\mu_{\mathrm{in}},o,\phi,\\
 &q,\rho,s,\tau,w,\yaux,\zeta,\zquot).
\end{align*}
The positive21 supplied tuple is
\[
 (J,F,\alpha_{\mathrm{src}},\zquot,\faux,h,i,j,o,s,w,\tau,\eta,\zeta,
 \ga,\yaux,Z,W,\delta_{\mathrm{in}},\phi,\rho).
\]
The eliminated positive coordinates in positive21 are recovered by triangular expressions. In particular its main root expression is
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:Dprime}
 D'=X+ac+\ga H=D-\rmain>0,
\end{equation}
which may differ from the raw supplied Pell root $D$.

\subsection{Complete accounting of the raw comparisons}
The following table records every raw residual. Positive21 retains the comparisons with raw indices $1,2,3,5,10,11,12,13,15,16$; the other eight are triangular definitions.

Static inspection of the saved source shows that $\ga$ affects only raw comparison $9$. Its finalizer contains exactly one square of each of the 18 residuals. Therefore its exact value on this tuple is
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:rawsos}
 \operatorname{SOS}_{\mathrm{raw29}}=\rmain^2.
\end{equation}
This identity holds on the entire broad family. On the thinned family of Theorem~\ref{fup:nr:thm:misses}, its value is strictly positive because $\rmain\ge1$.

\subsection{The triangular main norm in positive21}
The positive21 retained main norm uses $D'$ from \eqref{fup:nr:eq:Dprime}, not $D$. Hence its single possible residual is
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:positive21res}
 (D')^2-\Delta c^2-1
 =(D-\rmain)^2-D^2=-\rmain(2D-\rmain).
\end{equation}
The remaining nine retained comparisons vanish. Static dependency inspection confirms that $\ga$ affects only positive21 comparison $4$, which is raw comparison $10$ after triangular elimination. Its full ten-term finalizer therefore has value
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:pos21sos}
 \operatorname{SOS}_{\mathrm{positive21}}=
 [\rmain(2D-\rmain)]^2.
\end{equation}
Since $D>H>\rmain$, this vanishes if and only if $\rmain=0$. The two SOS formulas \eqref{fup:nr:eq:rawsos} and \eqref{fup:nr:eq:pos21sos} are distinct and must not be conflated.

\begin{corollary}[The remaining obstruction on this family]
Each selected candidate in the broad family becomes a full negative zero in both interfaces if and only if \eqref{fup:nr:eq:unresolved} holds. The thinned family of Theorem~\ref{fup:nr:thm:misses} is excluded: its two SOS values are respectively $\rmain^2>0$ and $[\rmain(2D-\rmain)]^2>0$. No congruence hit or full negative zero is supplied.
\end{corollary}
The finite residual fixtures have nonzero residues, but they are explicitly main-block subsystem checks without the exact ratio and genuine compiler realization. They do not establish the genuine-family nonredundancy claim; that claim follows from the analytic existence proof together with the Jacobi obstruction.

\section{A separate sharpened outer estimate}\label{fup:nr:sec:outerbound}
For every putative full negative zero in the unwrapped odd-input sector, the input inequalities imply
\[
 q>W=2^bB^{2x}\ge2B^2>K_0.
\]
Since $X\ge q^3$, it follows that
\[
 K_0(q^2-1)<q^3\le X,
 \qquad q(q^2-1)(K_0+X)<Xq^3.
\]
Positive transport gives $F<(K_0+X)C$. Since $Z<q<q^2$ and $M>0$, negative packing gives the strict chain
\begin{align}\label{fup:nr:eq:sharper}
 p&<Q\bigl(Z+q(K_0+X)C-q^2\bigr)-M\nonumber\\
  &<qQ(K_0+X)C<CXq^3.
\end{align}
Combining this with $tE\le3p-3$ and $E=sXq^3$ yields
\begin{equation}\label{fup:nr:eq:tsC}
 ts<3C.
\end{equation}
This strengthens the prior $ts<3q$ and the looser estimate involving $C(1+1/q)$. It is separate from the family theorem and does not force the sign of $R$. The family constructed here satisfies \eqref{fup:nr:eq:sharper} eventually and has $s=t=1$.
\begin{writenote}
The prior bound $ts<3q$ is Part~V's, derived after~\eqref{fup:nz:eq:representative}; Part~V's note there points here. The sharper~\eqref{fup:nr:eq:tsC} is proved only on the unwrapped odd-input sector, where $W=2^u$ exactly.
\end{writenote}

\section{Exact enumeration and unresolved questions}\label{fup:nr:sec:questions}
\subsection{An integer enumeration independent of real endpoint decisions}
The analytic selection proves existence but need not be the computational definition of the candidate set. Enumerate $r\ge1$, set $w=w_r$, $s=t=1$, and use Report 37's finite exact ratio search within \eqref{fup:nr:eq:range}. Increasing odd $p$ by two increases $\psi_A(p)$ and decreases $\psi_P(n)$, so the ratio strictly increases. Its numerator grows by more than $(2A-1)^2$, and $(2A-1)^2Y>Y+1$; thus at most one odd $p$ at each scale can pass \eqref{fup:nr:eq:predicateratio}. Exact integer binary search finds or rejects it. Retain it only if $p\equiv P_0\pmod{L_0}$ and every fixed-marker test passes.

The broad theorem guarantees infinitely many retained scales. Restricting this enumeration to $r=T_{\mathrm{thin}}j$ still leaves infinitely many, and each is a certified congruence miss by Theorem~\ref{fup:nr:thm:misses}. This enumeration never has to decide whether a transcendental fractional part equals a shrinking-window endpoint. It uses enormous exact Pell integers if needed; termination at a fixed scale is not a practical complexity guarantee, and the theorem does not provide a feasible first-hit bound.

\subsection{Questions that remain open}
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{The symbol minus one sector.} The fixed-character filter eliminates $\mathcal J(w,h_0)\in\{0,1\}$. Does the broad exact-ratio family contain a main-congruence hit in the necessary sector $\mathcal J(w,h_0)=-1$? The filter is not sufficient, and no hit is proved.
\item \textbf{A numerically materialized genuine tuple.} Can one materialize a genuine compiler export and an accepted large-$r$ tuple, then compute its exact positive residue in the miss subfamily? Existence and nonzeroness are already proved there, but no enormous example or residue numeral is supplied by the finite tests.
\item \textbf{Effective thresholds.} Can the analytic constants, Binet-tail estimates and all strict inequalities be made explicit enough for a useful numerical threshold? Finiteness of the threshold does not make it feasible.
\item \textbf{Additional modular obstructions.} The fixed Jacobi test can be computed without factoring $h_0$ or constructing Pell integers. Are there further fixed-modulus or character tests that constrain the unresolved sector? Any claimed joint distribution with the varying exponential modulus must be proved, not inferred from center discrepancy alone.
\item \textbf{Scope beyond this slice.} Other scales, markers, $s,t$, input branches and progression classes remain outside this family's parametrization. The present exclusions settle neither full raw29/positive21 negative-zero existence nor global restored-index positivity.
\end{enumerate}
A hit in any passing full predicate would yield a full negative zero by Report 37 and the positive reconstruction. No such hit is claimed. The infinite certified misses prove nonredundancy of two specific comparisons in their respective systems; they do not prove emptiness of the full predicate.
\begin{writenote}
At this write (batch~88) all five questions are open, and so is Part~V's yes-or-no question; the research tree records raw29 and positive21 as unresolved. A main-congruence hit with symbol $-1$ in this family would, by Theorem~\ref{fup:nz:thm:main}, be a full negative zero.
\end{writenote}

\section{Reproducible evidence and its exact scope}\label{fup:nr:sec:repro}
\begin{writenote}
\emph{Shipped names.} The packet's files are shipped with the prefix \texttt{16-nonredundant-}, the delivery directories flattened into the name (README): \rw{ALL-BUT-MAIN-PROJECTION.md} is \rw{16-nonredundant-evidence-ALL-BUT-MAIN-PROJECTION.md}, the independent review \rw{16-nonredundant-evidence-independent-INDEPENDENT-REVIEW.md}, the Jacobi addendum and its audit \rw{16-nonredundant-jacobi-JACOBI-ADDENDUM.md} and \rw{16-nonredundant-jacobi-INDEPENDENT-AUDIT.md}, and the three checkers \rw{code/16-nonredundant-evidence-check_unwrapped_family.py}, \rw{code/16-nonredundant-evidence-independent-check_audit.py} and \rw{code/16-nonredundant-jacobi-check_jacobi_addendum.py}. The four context snapshots are byte copies (three of research-tree files, one of the Report~37 manuscript) and are not shipped. At placement the three checkers, run directly on a copy of the delivery layout, passed in normal and optimized Python with receipts byte-identical to the frozen ones; the release verifier refuses to run on NTFS, whose file modes it cannot match. The README gives a rerun route. The programme's review executed none of these programs.
\end{writenote}
\subsection{Frozen scientific evidence}
The accompanying release preserves the complete follow-on packet and the separate later Jacobi addendum as immutable evidence. Its original proof is \code{ALL-BUT-MAIN-PROJECTION.md}; the independent review explains the derivative control, strict bounds, auxiliary signs and literal residual dependencies. The scientific author checker uses the Python standard library. The independent checker uses exact symbolic algebra with SymPy, tested with version 1.14.0. They authenticate the four context snapshots as inert data. A third own checker corroborates the Jacobi addendum using exact integer arithmetic and a trial-factorization/Euler-criterion oracle. None of the three imports an upstream module or evaluates a saved arithmetic schedule.

The frozen scientific receipts are deterministic canonical JSON. The release replay compares stdout byte for byte with the expected files in both normal and optimized Python. Explicit exception checks remain active under \code{-O}. These replays corroborate the listed algebra and finite inventories, not the unbounded analytic theorem.

\subsection{Finite checks and their limitations}
The author receipt contains:\nopagebreak[4]
\begin{itemize}
\item Five formal multivariate polynomial identities: affine transport, negative packing, lattice progression, the positive21 shifted-root residual, and the analytic $G$ rearrangement after clearing denominators
\item 36 structural exponent/loading-bound fixtures, without materializing any actual fixed compiler export
\item 864 outer algebra/parity fixtures, with no exact Pell-ratio or full-zero claim
\item 48 exact main Pell/floor-residual fixtures, all with nonzero residues, explicitly limited to the main subsystem
\item Authentication of the Report 37 TeX, projection JSON and two parity-source snapshots, plus static main-root checks
\end{itemize}
The independent portable receipt additionally contains exact symbolic curvature and remainder identities, both complete supplied-coordinate inventories and SOS suffixes, 432 abstract bound/lattice fixtures, 20 modular auxiliary fixtures with $p\ge13$ and $p\equiv1\pmod4$, and 25 exact odd-quotient polynomial identities. The review also preserves historical evidence of five authenticated cached source identities and a 43-file unchanged Report 37 tree. That historical record is not a fresh five-source network check or a replay of all of Report 37.

The Jacobi receipt adds 21,109 binary-symbol/oracle comparisons, including 11,495 composite-denominator cases; 2,328 general-filter cases split into 994 symbol $-1$, 990 symbol $+1$ and 344 zero cases; 11,640 odd-exponent checks; 384 thinning fixtures; and exact chain-factor, count-constant and residual identities. Its 1,192 even-$w$ general-filter fixtures and 96 even-$w$ fixtures in the $T_{\mathrm{thin}}=h_0$ family guard the parity boundary. Boundary controls exercise nonsquare $q$, even $p$ and $3\mid q$. These are abstract arithmetic fixtures, not genuine compiler/Pell-ratio tuples.

The infinite count depends on the mathematical proof and the two stated analytic estimates. No numerical frequency experiment, floating-point sample, or symbolic check of finitely many indices establishes that count. No selected large-$r$ genuine-compiler tuple has been materialized in this packet.

\subsection{Authentication and execution boundaries}
The release separates two questions. A manifest, exact source pins and archive checks establish which bytes are being reviewed or executed. The mathematical arguments and their assumptions establish what those bytes claim and why the new theorem follows. A passing digest check is not a proof of mathematical truth; a valid theorem does not authenticate a substituted file.

The enclosing verifier authenticates the release and frozen source identities before executing the three own scientific checkers in an external temporary copy. It checks exact canonical output and strict receipt types rather than accepting a generic status string. Output paths are guarded so an enclosing replay cannot overwrite a packet, an expected receipt or an existing output. The frozen author checker itself allows replacement of an existing external output path; this limitation is left unchanged and is avoided by the enclosing safe workflow. The independent checker requires a fresh external output file. Default scientific runs use stdout only.

The release includes regression checks for tampered bytes/receipts and hostile ZIP members, plus deterministic PDF and ZIP build helpers. Archive validation rejects unsafe member paths and ambiguous inventories before extraction or execution. A release's authenticity must ultimately be anchored by a digest obtained through a trusted channel; a manifest bundled with adversarial replacement content cannot authenticate itself. Detailed commands, exact replay gates and build dependencies are documented in the release's \code{README.md} and \code{INTEGRITY.md}.

\paragraph{No mutation of inherited evidence.}
The original follow-on packet, later Jacobi packet and sealed Report 37 remain unchanged. The reproducible archive is external to the new release directory. Cached source scripts remain inert. PDF generation uses only the report's own TeX and the permitted TeX toolchain, with no shell escape. Deterministic build checks and all-page visual inspection concern artifact quality, not an independent proof of the infinite theorem.

\section{A compact map of the proof dependencies}
The theorem uses the following logical chain:
\begin{enumerate}
\item Genuine compiler and immutable mask recipe imply the loading bounds and even $\MC$
\item Fixed $q,W,C,Z$ and the scale progression give a common odd index lattice and exact affine outer coordinates
\item The exact Binet center has a controlled analytic remainder and nonzero curvature of order $1/(r\log^2r)$
\item A frequency-uniform second-derivative estimate plus interval discrepancy yields a count of order $N/\log N$ in the shrinking target
\item Interior margin and exact tail control give the integer Pell ratio
\item The positive $X/\log X$ deviation and the lattice formulas give every strict index, outer and input condition
\item Pell and auxiliary identities reconstruct every positive coordinate, leaving one possible raw residual
\item Literal SOS dependencies give the two distinct residual-value formulas for the broad family
\end{enumerate}
The Jacobi addendum then proves the fixed-character identity, selects a progression with symbol $+1$, and repeats the shrinking-target argument after fixed thinning. This certifies nonzero residuals and comparison nonredundancy. The symbol $-1$ sector and full negative-zero question remain open.

\fupstart{41}{Part VII: Report 41}
\part{Failure of first index deletion: a full positive counterfamily for the 81- and 82-operation candidates}\label{fup:part:fd}
\noindent\wtag\ Source~17 of this report, manuscript~02 of batch~88: \emph{Research Report 41}, subtitled ``A full positive counterfamily for the 81 and 82 operation candidates'', author line ``Mathematical research report'', dated 3 October 2026; archive \rw{Failure_of_First_Index_Deletion_Package.zip} (arrival commit \texttt{c5612efa1}, placed by \texttt{992aaafb3}); pins \texttt{3f4a974a5} (the two parent sources and the deletion scout) and \texttt{93c34e817} (the scaled-obstruction note, context only). Its sections, statements and equations are numbered 41.$k$ here.
\begin{sourceabstract}
Deleting the first-index quotient from the validated normalized 85-operation and ordinary 86-operation sources produces syntactically valid 81-operation and 82-operation circuits, but destroys their intended ordinary-input soundness. For every unchanged genuine admissible six-numeral compiler instance and every positive ordinary input, we construct all seventeen supplied witnesses as strictly positive integers and make every one of the six surviving literal factors equal to $+1$. The construction uses a factorial exponent, a controlled radix and Chinese remaindering to fit an exact dyadic Pell family into the actual shifted-mask packing, sheared transport, input norm and shared quotient constraints. Both retained strong auxiliary forms are completed with the actual positive auxiliary quotient. The omitted coordinate would have to be $h=(k-R-1)/(XY)$, whose least nonnegative numerator remainder is $25u-1>0$. Thus no integer restoration is possible. This is a symbolic existence proof, not a materialized enormous numerical tuple. The 81/82 counts and degrees 168/124 describe rejected candidates; the established 85-operation minimum among the validated constructions is unchanged.
\end{sourceabstract}
\begin{writenote}
\emph{Which polynomials.} The parents are the research tree's normalized85 universal polynomial (\rw{complete85_auxiliary_bezout_projection.md}, $85$ operations, exact degree 175) and ordinary86 polynomial (\rw{complete86_ordinary_auxiliary_projection.md}, $86$ operations, exact degree 131), at the pin \texttt{3f4a974a5}; the deletion candidates are those of the tree's \rw{first_index_quotient_deletion_scout.md}. The ``82-operation candidate'' of this Part, ordinary82 ($82=45\mathrm M+37\mathrm A$, 17 witnesses, degree 124), is \emph{not} the square/product 82-operation candidate of Part~\ref{fup:part:cand} ($82=45\mathrm M+37\mathrm A$, 18 witnesses, degree 185), although the operation split is the same; Table~\ref{fup:fm:tab:candidates} keeps the names apart.

\emph{A stale record figure (dated note, batch~88).} The abstract's closing clause, that ``the established 85-operation minimum among the validated constructions is unchanged'', and the same sentence in the shipped \rw{17-index-deletion-evidence-README.md} (line~6, ``established85-operation upper bound''), were correct at the pin \texttt{3f4a974a5} (3 October 2026, 13:55~PDT). Since commit \texttt{20aafb9a5} (15:08~PDT the same day) the research tree's record is \emph{84 operations} ($84=47\mathrm M+37\mathrm A$, 18 positive witnesses, uniform exact degree 187), with $F_{84}=\Delta\cdot F_{85}$ (Section~\ref{fup:fm:sec:tree}). The substance stands: Report~41 changes no record, and the 85- and 86-operation parents remain valid. The delivered text is kept.

\emph{What the research tree did with it.} The programme's review \rw{review_incoming_arithmetic_reports_39_46.md} (commit \texttt{5d8889017}) read the full counterfamily and the source correspondence and finds ``the central arithmetic is consistent'': the factorial totients, the CRT choices, $55\mid R$, the dyadic resonance, the strict ratio, the shared split $\rho,\sigma$, the transport quotient and the auxiliary index $2cp$; the remainder $25u-1$ gives full positive zeros at every input, including a rejecting program; and the false strict endpoint at index~2 in the earlier note (the erratum in Section~\ref{fup:fd:sec:repro}) is not used by the family. It called the result ``an actionable transfer to the current84 source''. The same evening the tree carried it out: \rw{complete80_first_index_deletion_collapse.md} (with \rw{.py} and \rw{.json}, commit \texttt{5d8889017}) deletes the same four first-index rows from the 84-operation polynomial, obtaining a candidate with $80=45\mathrm M+35\mathrm A$ operations, 17 positive witnesses and exact degree 180, proves the all-ring identities $F_{80}=\Delta\cdot F_{81}$ and $F_{84}+\Delta=(F_{80}+\Delta)N_k$ with $N_k=k-hE-R$, and transfers Report~41's normalized81 family to it. Its independent review \rw{review_complete80_first_index_deletion_collapse.md} passes, with fresh normal and optimized receipts. So the complete80 candidate, too, is refuted on every positive input of every authentic compiler slice, the empty-set compiler included; the intake review records that no deletion is incorporated into the established universal polynomial. The tree's result is a transfer of Theorem~\ref{fup:fd:thm:main}, with Report~41 credited as its source, not an independent duplicate. The intake review does not discuss the smooth-radix refinement of Section~\ref{fup:fd:sec:smooth}, which the source itself says received only a coordinating review.

\emph{Relation to the other Parts.} This is the same kind of failure as Part~\ref{fup:part:ir} (``Failure of positive index restoration''), for a different interface: there the restored index of the signed19 child is negative; here $R=p>0$ and the deleted quotient $h$ is nonintegral. The source's ``Report~33'' is Part~\ref{fup:part:ir}, and its ``Reports~37 and~39'' are Parts~\ref{fup:part:nz} and~\ref{fup:part:nr}. Lemma~\ref{fup:fd:lem:oddpoly} is Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:oddquotient} of Report~33 (also re-proved in Parts~\ref{fup:part:nr} and~\ref{fup:part:cand}); Lemma~\ref{fup:fd:lem:g} is the projection recurrence of Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:projection}, written for $g_j=(\ea(j)-2^j)/H$; the scaled subsystem family of Section~\ref{fup:fd:sec:history} is of the same kind as the tree's \rw{first_index_scaled_obstruction.md} (pin \texttt{93c34e817}, family $p=t(t+2)$), and the theorem does not use it.

Letters that differ from Part~V: $u=R/55$ is a family parameter, \emph{not} Part~V's input index (here the input index is $I=\ell x+b$, which is Part~V's $u$, and $W=2^I$); $t=L_*!$ is the binary radix exponent with $q=2^t$ (Part~V's $t$ is the first-representative quotient); $m$ is the odd part of $t$; $e$ is a residue exponent, not an input index; $L_0=2XY$ and $M_0=4XY^2$ are dyadic bases (Part~\ref{fup:part:nr}'s $L_0$ is a lattice modulus); $a_0=Y(X+1)$ is Part~V's $a$; $\tr$ is the supplied transport quotient (Part~V's $\zquot$; Part~VIII writes $t_{\mathrm{tr}}$); $T$ is the supplied auxiliary quotient and $S=\Delta\psi_A(\ma)$ a computed auxiliary coefficient, unlike the supplied coefficient $S$ of Part~VIII's free-coefficient candidate; $\mathcal S$ is the literal strong coefficient; $N_1,\ldots,N_6$ are the six retained factors (Part~VIII names them $N_{\mathrm{first}},\ldots$).

The source's files are shipped with the prefix \texttt{17-index-deletion-}.

\section{Conclusion and exact scope}\label{fup:fd:sec:scope}
The first-index equation is essential in these two exact sources. The counterfamily below preserves their compiler constants and the ordinary input. It satisfies the complete retained system, including the paid packing and final product subtraction, with positive supplied integers throughout.

\begin{theorem}[Full collapse of both deletion candidates]\label{fup:fd:thm:main}
Fix any genuine admissible six-numeral compiler instance of the pinned normalized85 or ordinary86 source, and any ordinary input $x\in\Z_{>0}$. Its corresponding normalized81 or ordinary82 first-index-deleted candidate has seventeen strictly positive integer witnesses for which all six retained factors are $+1$ and the final polynomial is zero. For this same retained tuple the unique possible restored coordinate
\[
 h=\frac{k-R-1}{E},\qquad E=XY,
\]
is positive rational and nonintegral. More precisely, for a positive integer $u\equiv1\pmod4$ constructed from the exact paid packing,
\[
 R=p=55u,\quad n=40u,\quad E=2^{88u-1},\qquad
 (k-R-1)\bmod E=25u-1.
\]
\end{theorem}

The conclusion has two distinct levels. First, the positive zero-set projection obtained by dropping $h$ has no inverse at the constructed tuple. Second, the deletion candidates accept \emph{every} positive input for every genuine compiler instance. Taking a correct compiler for the empty computably enumerable set therefore refutes ordinary-input soundness of the proposed universal representation. For a compiler whose language is all positive integers, the acceptance statement is of course not itself an input-language contradiction; the failed coordinate restoration still holds.

\paragraph{What is unchanged.}
The normalized85 and ordinary86 parent results remain valid. Their retained first-index factor excludes this family. There is no new universal operation bound, no assertion that 85 is an absolute mathematical lower bound over all possible circuits, and no claim that the rejected candidates preserve the intended language merely because their arrays are live and topologically well formed.

\paragraph{Relationship to other interfaces.}
Report 33 refuted a restored-index positivity claim for signed19. Reports 37 and 39 concern raw29 and positive21, possible \emph{negative} restored $R$, and their main comparisons; the global full-negative-zero question there remains open. Here $R=p>0$ and the obstruction is the \emph{nonintegrality} of the deleted first-index quotient $h$ in different, validated 85/86 parents. This report neither settles the raw29/positive21 sign question nor invalidates those parents.

\paragraph{Evidence boundary.}
The theorem is proved by exact mathematical definitions and inequalities. No astronomical full tuple is numerically materialized, and no saved arithmetic schedule or upstream program is executed. Finite checks verify components and source correspondence; they are supplementary evidence rather than the proof of the universal quantifiers.

\section{The exact source and fixed compiler contract}\label{fup:fd:sec:source}
\subsection{Authentic numerals and positive ports}
The six fixed source ports, in order, are
\[
 \code{Bm1},\ \code{Kconstant},\ \code{twice_cell_bits},\
 \code{inner_bits},\ \code{MC},\ \code{MF}.
\]
Their mathematical values are $B-1,K,2d,b,\MC,\MFsrcV$, respectively. Fix these values once and for all from an actual admissible compiler export. The proof uses the inherited properties
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:contract}
 \begin{gathered}
 B=2^d\ge16,\qquad \ell=2d,\qquad K>0,\qquad b>0\text{ odd},\\
 \MC\equiv2\pmod4,\qquad \MFsrcV=\MFnatV+B-1,\qquad
 0<\MC,\MFnatV<B-1.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
The literal \code{MF} is the \emph{shifted} mask; the bound $\MFsrcV<B-1$ would be false. The actual recipe also gives $\MFnatV\equiv4\pmod8$ and further compiler restrictions. They remain satisfied because none of the fixed numerals is altered. Conditions~\eqref{fup:fd:eq:contract} are properties of authentic exports, not a proposed replacement definition allowing arbitrary toy masks.

The provenance is explicit. The pinned coupled-index note states $\MC\equiv2\pmod4$ and the mask contract. The half-binomial compiler note defines the modified $\MC$, native $\MFnatV$, and shifted export. The fixed raw universal proof and half-binomial note choose $b,L,d$ as powers of five with $d=bL$, hence $b$ is odd. These immutable primary notes are included in the source packet and cited in Appendix~\ref{fup:fd:app:provenance}.

The ordinary positive input is $x$. The seventeen supplied witness ports are exactly
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:ports}
 \begin{gathered}
 \code{Jrep},\ F,\ \alpha,\ \code{transport_quotient},\ f,\ i,
 \ \code{auxiliary_quotient},\ s,\ w,\\
 \code{tau_root},\ \eta,\ \zeta,\ \code{y_aux},\ Z,\ \delta,\ \rho,\ \sigma.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
We denote the two supplied quotients by $\tr$ and $T$, and the root and auxiliary coordinate by $\tau$ and $\yaux$. Divisions, factorials, exponentials and Pell recurrences used to \emph{define} these witnesses are mathematical existence constructions. They are not additional counted circuit instructions or extra supplied ports.

\subsection{Shared literal expressions}
For the witnesses just listed, the retained source computes
\begin{align}\label{fup:fd:eq:literal}
 q&=(B-1)J+1,&X&=wq,&Y&=sq^3,&E&=XY,\\
 k&=\eta+\zeta,&c&=kY+\eta,&a_0&=Y(X+1),&A&=a_0+2,\nonumber\\
 \Delta&=A^2-1,&H&=4a_0+3,&D&=X+a_0c+(\rho+\sigma)H.&&\nonumber
\end{align}
The mathematical Pell base $A$ must be distinguished from the literal register \code{A}, whose value is $\Delta$. With
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:outerliteral}
 \begin{gathered}
 I=\ell x+b,\qquad C=q-F-Z-\alpha-\ell x,\qquad W=C-Z,\\
 \kappa=I+\delta\Delta,\qquad \mu=W+a_0\kappa+\rho H,\\
 Q=q^2-1,\quad M=(\MC+q\MFsrcV)J,\quad
 R=(q^2-Z-qF)Q+M,\\
 V=c(Tf-1)-Rf^2,
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
the six surviving factors are
\begin{align}\label{fup:fd:eq:factors}
 N_1&=\tau^2-XY^2(XY^2+1)k^2,\\
 N_2&=D^2-\Delta c^2,\nonumber\\
 N_3&=\mu^2-\Delta\kappa^2,\nonumber\\
 N_4&=\mathcal S(V^2-\yaux^2)+\yaux^2,\nonumber\\
 N_5&=(K+w)C+q-F-\tr(q-1),\nonumber\\
 N_6^{\mathrm N}&=f^2-\Delta(ic^2)^2,
 &\mathcal S^{\mathrm N}&=\Delta^2(ic^2)^2,\nonumber\\
 N_6^{\mathrm O}&=1+(ic^2)^2-\Delta(f^2-1),
 &\mathcal S^{\mathrm O}&=\Delta(f^2-1).\nonumber
\end{align}
The normalized mode uses the N superscript; the ordinary mode uses O. These are not alternative conventions for one register: they are the two actual strong source forms.

The source's first product is exact: \code{first_root_base} is $XY^2k$ and \code{first_next} is $XY^2k+k$, so their product is $XY^2(XY^2+1)k^2$. There is no omitted factor of two. The product cone is
\[
 N_1N_2\longrightarrow N_1N_2N_3
 \longrightarrow N_1N_2N_3N_4
 \longrightarrow N_1N_2N_3N_4N_5
 \longrightarrow \prod_{j=1}^6N_j.
\]
The final subtraction is paid, and the candidate polynomial is
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:finalizer}
 \mathcal F_{\mathrm{cand}}=\prod_{j=1}^6N_j-1.
\end{equation}
The historical register name \code{seven_units} contains six surviving factors.

\subsection{The literal deletion and its cost}
The original first-index factor is $N_h=k-hE-R$. Its unique consumer chain is
\[
 h\longrightarrow\code{hpm1}\longrightarrow\code{index_difference}
 \longrightarrow\code{norm_index}\longrightarrow\code{norm_product}.
\]
Only the four named registers are deleted, and \code{all_units} is rewired from \code{norm_product} to \code{norm_four}. Every other definition and final subtraction remains. Thus, as a polynomial identity for arbitrary ring values,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:productidentity}
 \mathcal F_{\mathrm{parent}}+1
 = (\mathcal F_{\mathrm{cand}}+1)(k-hE-R).
\end{equation}
At a candidate zero, restoration would require $k-hE-R=1$, so the unique possible inverse is $(k-R-1)/E$.

\midrule
Normalized81 & 46 & 35 & 81 & 168\\
