Both have seventeen positive witness ports. Degree assigns one to $x$ and each supplied witness and zero to each fixed numeral. These are exact syntactic ledgers of \emph{rejected candidates}, not sound universal bounds.

\section{Pell identities used in the construction}\label{fup:fd:sec:pell}
For an integer $A\ge2$ define
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:pell}
 \chi_A(j)+\psi_A(j)\sqrt{A^2-1}
 =\bigl(A+\sqrt{A^2-1}\bigr)^j\quad(j\ge0).
\end{equation}
Both sequences are integral and satisfy $u_{j+2}=2Au_{j+1}-u_j$, with initial values $(1,A)$ for $\chi$ and $(0,1)$ for $\psi$. The norm identity is
\[
 \chi_A(j)^2-(A^2-1)\psi_A(j)^2=1.
\]
For $j\ge3$, monotonicity and the recurrence give
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:pellbounds}
 (2A-1)^{j-1}\le\psi_A(j)<(2A)^{j-1}.
\end{equation}
Indeed consecutive positive $\psi$ terms increase, so each new term is greater than $(2A-1)$ times its predecessor and smaller than $2A$ times it. The initial step is $\psi_A(1)=1$ and $\psi_A(2)=2A$.

Two further identities will be needed with their signs intact.
\begin{lemma}[Odd auxiliary polynomials]\label{fup:fd:lem:oddpoly}
There are integer polynomials $Q_v(T)$, $v\ge0$, such that
\[
 \chi_S(2v+1)=S Q_v(S^2),\qquad
 Q_v(0)=(-1)^v(2v+1),
\]
and
\[
 Q_v(1-A^2)=(-1)^v\psi_A(2v+1).
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Take $Q_0=1$, $Q_1=4T-3$ and
$Q_{v+2}=(4T-2)Q_{v+1}-Q_v$. The doubled-step Pell recurrence proves the first identity. Evaluation at zero gives the second identity by induction. At $T=1-A^2$ the recurrence coefficient is $2-4A^2$; alternating the sign of the odd-index $\psi_A$ sequence gives exactly this recurrence and the same two initial values. This proves the third identity.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Positive shared projection quotients]\label{fup:fd:lem:g}
For $a_0=A-2$ and $H=4a_0+3=4A-5$, put
\[
 z_j=\chi_A(j)-a_0\psi_A(j),\qquad
 g_j=\frac{z_j-2^j}{H}.
\]
Then every $g_j$ is an integer, $g_0=g_1=0$, $g_2=1$, and
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:grecurrence}
 g_{j+2}=2Ag_{j+1}-g_j+2^j.
\end{equation}
The sequence is strictly increasing for $j\ge1$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The sequence $z_j$ has initial values $1,2$ and the Pell recurrence. Subtracting the sequence $2^j$ yields the forcing term $(4A-5)2^j=H2^j$, proving the recurrence and integrality. If $g_{j+1}>g_j\ge0$, then
\[
 g_{j+2}-g_{j+1}=(2A-1)g_{j+1}-g_j+2^j>0.
\]
The base $g_2=1>g_1=0$ completes the induction.
\end{proof}

\section{A factorial radix and controlled residue exponent}\label{fup:fd:sec:factorial}
Now fix the genuine compiler and input from Theorem~\ref{fup:fd:thm:main}. Define
\[
 I=\ell x+b,\qquad W=2^I.
\]
Choose any integer
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:Lchoice}
 L_*\ge\max(1100d,16,K,W,\ell x),
\end{equation}
and set
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:radix}
 \begin{gathered}
 t=L_*!,\qquad N=t/d,\qquad q=2^t=B^N,
 \qquad J=\frac{q-1}{B-1},\\
 t=2^\nu m\quad(m\text{ odd}),\qquad
 Q=q^2-1,\qquad M=(\MC+q\MFsrcV)J.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
Since $1100d\le L_*$, the integer $1100d$ divides $L_*!$. Thus $N$ is a positive multiple of 1100, $t\ge64$, $\nu\ge4$, and $K,W,\ell x\le t$. In particular $J$ is a positive integer.

\begin{lemma}[The odd part of a factorial divides the radix minus one]\label{fup:fd:lem:factorial}
The choices in~\eqref{fup:fd:eq:radix} satisfy $q\equiv1\pmod m$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For every odd prime power $r^v$ dividing $L_*!$, both $r^{v-1}$ and $r-1$ divide $L_*!$. The first divisibility follows from the $r$-adic valuation; the second from $r\le L_*$. These two integers are coprime, so
\[
 \varphi(r^v)=r^{v-1}(r-1)\mid t.
\]
Euler's theorem therefore gives $2^t\equiv1\pmod{r^v}$. Chinese remaindering over the odd prime powers gives $q\equiv1\pmod m$. No assertion that $\varphi(t)$ divides $t$ is needed.
\end{proof}

Let $e_0$ be the residue of $M$ in $[0,m-1]$. Exactly one of
$e_0,e_0+m,e_0+2m,e_0+3m$ is $3$ modulo $4$, because $m$ is odd. Choose that integer $e$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:e}
 e\equiv M\pmod m,\qquad e\equiv3\pmod4,
 \qquad 3\le e<4m\le t/4.
\end{equation}
Let $Z$ be the least strictly positive representative of $e-M$ modulo $2^\nu$. Thus
\[
 1\le Z\le2^\nu\le t.
\]
Because $q\equiv0\pmod4$ and $J=1+B+\cdots+B^{N-1}\equiv1\pmod4$, the genuine mask residue gives $M\equiv\MC\equiv2\pmod4$. Hence $Z\equiv1\pmod4$.

Define the outer witnesses and exact packed value by
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:outerconstruction}
 \begin{gathered}
 C=W+Z,\qquad F=(K+2^e)C,\\
 \alpha=q-F-2Z-W-\ell x,\\
 R=(q^2-Z-qF)Q+M.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
Substitution into the literal definitions~\eqref{fup:fd:eq:outerliteral} gives the intended $C$ and $W$ exactly. The positivity and arithmetic of these witnesses are established next, rather than assumed.

\section{Outer positivity and exact packed arithmetic}\label{fup:fd:sec:packing}
\subsection{All required outer margins}
Since $C\le2t$, $K\le t$ and $e<t/4$,
\[
 F\le2t(t+2^{t/4}),\qquad 2Z+W+\ell x\le4t.
\]
For every $t\ge64$,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:margin}
 2t^2+2t\,2^{t/4}+4t<2^t=q.
\end{equation}
Here is an elementary uniform verification. The function $2^{t/8}/t$ increases for $t\ge64$ and is at least one there, so $t\le2^{t/8}$. Consequently
\[
 2t^2+4t\le4t^2\le2^{t/2},\qquad
 2t\,2^{t/4}\le2^{t/2}.
\]
Their sum is at most $2^{t/2+1}<2^t$. Thus $\alpha>0$, and $F,Z,C$ are positive integers. In particular $F+Z<q$.

Set $G=q^2-Z-qF$. Positivity and $F+Z<q$ give
\[
 2q-1\le G\le q^2-q-1.
\]
The shifted mask bounds in~\eqref{fup:fd:eq:contract} imply
\[
 0<M<(B-1)(1+2q)J=(q-1)(1+2q).
\]
These exact inequalities yield
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:Rbounds}
 (2q-1)(q^2-1)<R<q^4-q^3.
\end{equation}
For the upper bound, expand $(q^2-q-1)(q^2-1)$ and add the strict upper bound $2q^2-q-1$ for $M$; all lower-order terms cancel. The lower bound follows from $G\ge2q-1$ and $M>0$.

In particular $R>q>t$. Also $2^t>5t+5$ for $t\ge64$: it holds at 64 and is preserved under $t\mapsto t+1$ since $2(5t+5)>5(t+1)+5$. Hence
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:Rlarge}
 R>5t+5.
\end{equation}
These are the actual pretyping packed-index bounds; they are not a substitute for the packed equation, which already holds by definition.

\subsection{The three simultaneous congruences}
Modulo $m$, Lemma~\ref{fup:fd:lem:factorial} gives $Q\equiv0$, hence $R\equiv M\equiv e$. Modulo $2^\nu$, the radix $q=2^t$ is zero, $Q\equiv-1$, and therefore
\[
 R\equiv Z+M\equiv e\pmod{2^\nu}.
\]
Since $m$ is odd, Chinese remaindering proves
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:Rcrt}
 R\equiv e\pmod t.
\end{equation}

Moreover $2^{20}\equiv1\pmod{55}$, so $B^{20}\equiv1\pmod{55}$. The geometric sum $J$ has $N$ terms, where $1100=55\cdot20$ divides $N$. Grouping into blocks of 20, each block has the same residue modulo 55 and the number of blocks is divisible by 55. Thus
\[
 55\mid J,\qquad q=B^N\equiv1\pmod{55},
 \qquad55\mid M,Q,R.
\]
This argument does not divide a congruence by $B-1$, which need not be invertible modulo 55. Finally,~\eqref{fup:fd:eq:Rcrt}, $4\mid t$ and~\eqref{fup:fd:eq:e} give $R\equiv3\pmod4$.

Set
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:u}
 u=R/55.
\end{equation}
Then $u$ is a strictly positive integer and $u\equiv1\pmod4$, because $55u\equiv3\pmod4$. This parameter $u$ is distinct from the fixed ordinary input index $I$.

\section{An exact dyadic first and main family}\label{fup:fd:sec:dyadic}
Define
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:dyadic}
 \begin{gathered}
 p=R=55u,\qquad n=40u,\qquad 2n-p=25u,\\
 X=2^p,\qquad Y=2^{33u-1},\qquad
 w=2^{p-t},\qquad s=2^{33u-1-3t},\\
 E=XY,\quad a_0=Y(X+1),\quad A=a_0+2,\quad
 \Delta=A^2-1,\quad H=4a_0+3,\quad P=2XY^2+1,\\
 D=\chi_A(p),\qquad c=\psi_A(p),\qquad
 \tau=\chi_P(n),\qquad k=2\psi_P(n).
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
By~\eqref{fup:fd:eq:Rlarge}, $p>t$ and
\[
 33u-1=3p/5-1>3t.
\]
Thus $w,s$ are strictly positive integers, and the genuine asymmetric scales $X=wq$ and $Y=sq^3$ hold. The first and main Pell norms are exactly $+1$: indeed $P^2-1=4XY^2(XY^2+1)$, matching $k=2\psi_P(n)$ in $N_1$.

\subsection{A strict ratio proved without asymptotics}
Put $L_0=2XY=2^{88u}$ and $M_0=4XY^2=2^{121u}$. The exponent identity
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:balance}
 \frac{L_0^{p-1}}{2M_0^{n-1}}=Y
\end{equation}
is exact: its exponent is $88u(55u-1)-1-121u(40u-1)=33u-1$. Also
\begin{align*}
 2A-1&=L_0\left(1+\frac1X+\frac{3}{2XY}\right),&
 2A&=L_0\left(1+\frac1X+\frac2{XY}\right),\\
 2P&=M_0\left(1+\frac1{2XY^2}\right),&2P-1&>M_0.
\end{align*}
The Pell bounds~\eqref{fup:fd:eq:pellbounds} and~\eqref{fup:fd:eq:balance} give
\[
 \frac{c}{kY}\ge
 \frac{(1+1/X+3/(2XY))^{p-1}}
 {(1+1/(2XY^2))^{n-1}}>1.
\]
The last inequality follows because the numerator base exceeds the denominator base and $p>n$.

For the upper bound let $\varepsilon=1/X+2/(XY)<2/X$. Since $p\ge55$, we have $2p/2^p<1/2$ and therefore $(p-1)\varepsilon<1/2$. For any positive integer $j$ with $j\varepsilon<1/2$, the binomial coefficient estimate gives
\[
 (1+\varepsilon)^j\le\frac1{1-j\varepsilon}
 <1+2j\varepsilon.
\]
Combining with the upper Pell bound and~\eqref{fup:fd:eq:balance},
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:ratio}
 0<\frac ck-Y<\frac{4pY}{X}
 =110u\,2^{-22u}<1.
\end{equation}
The last bound holds at $u=1$ and decreases: the ratio of successive values is $(u+1)/(u2^{22})<1$. Set
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:slacks}
 \eta=c-kY,\qquad\zeta=k-\eta.
\end{equation}
Both are strictly positive integers, and they reproduce the exact literal source values $k=\eta+\zeta$, $c=kY+\eta$.

\section{The actual input norm and the shared split}\label{fup:fd:sec:input}
The input is still the prescribed $x$, so $I=\ell x+b$ is odd and at least 3. Define
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:inputconstruction}
 \begin{gathered}
 \kappa=\psi_A(I),\qquad\mu=\chi_A(I),\qquad
 \delta=\frac{\kappa-I}{\Delta},\\
 \rho=g_I,\qquad\sigma=g_p-g_I,
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
using Lemma~\ref{fup:fd:lem:g}. The coefficient of $\sqrt\Delta$ in the binomial expansion of $(A+\sqrt\Delta)^I$ gives
\[
 \psi_A(I)\equiv I A^{I-1}\equiv I\pmod\Delta,
\]
because $I$ is odd and $A^2\equiv1\pmod\Delta$. Since $\psi_A(I)>I$ for $I\ge3$, the supplied $\delta$ is a positive integer.

We have $2^I=W\le t$, hence $I<t<p$. Lemma~\ref{fup:fd:lem:g} therefore gives $\rho>0$ and $\sigma>0$. It also gives exactly
\begin{align}\label{fup:fd:eq:inputidentities}
 D&=X+a_0c+(\rho+\sigma)H,\\
 \kappa&=I+\delta\Delta,\nonumber\\
 \mu&=W+a_0\kappa+\rho H.\nonumber
\end{align}
Indeed $X=2^p$, $W=2^I$, and $\rho+\sigma=g_p$. The Pell identity now proves $N_3=\mu^2-\Delta\kappa^2=1$.

This is the input norm with the \emph{same} $\rho$ required by the main root, and with both supplied parts of its split positive. It is not an independent substitute exponent witness, and no rescaling or change of the input has occurred.

\section{The literal sheared transport quotient}\label{fup:fd:sec:transport}
By~\eqref{fup:fd:eq:Rcrt}, $p\equiv e\pmod t$. Since $q=2^t$ and $w=2^{p-t}$,
\[
 w\equiv2^e\pmod{q-1}.
\]
Moreover $p>5t+5$ and $e<t/4$ imply $p-t>e$, so $w>2^e$. The supplied transport quotient
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:tr}
 \tr=1+\frac{C(w-2^e)}{q-1}
\end{equation}
is therefore a strictly positive integer. Using $F=(K+2^e)C$, direct substitution gives
\[
 (K+w)C+q-F-\tr(q-1)=1.
\]
Thus $N_5=1$. The coefficient is the actual \emph{sheared} $K+w$, not an older $K+X$ coefficient. No signed quotient or raw-mask substitution is used.

\section{Both strong modes and the positive auxiliary quotient}\label{fup:fd:sec:aux}
At the constructed $p=R\equiv3\pmod4$, define
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:auxconstruction}
 \begin{gathered}
 \ma=2cp,\qquad f=\chi_A(\ma),\qquad
 i_{\mathrm N}=\frac{\psi_A(\ma)}{c^2},\qquad
 S=\Delta\psi_A(\ma),\\
 \yaux=\psi_S(p),\qquad V=\frac{\chi_S(p)}S,\\
 o=\frac{V+c}{f},\qquad j=\frac{V+p}{c},\qquad
 T=\frac{o+pf}{c}.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
Every quotient in this display is a positive integer, as we now prove.

\subsection{Divisibility and the exact signs}
Expand $(\chi_A(p)+c\sqrt\Delta)^{2c}$. Its $\sqrt\Delta$ coefficient is $\psi_A(2cp)$. The term with one $c\sqrt\Delta$ factor is $2c^2\chi_A(p)^{2c-1}$; every higher odd term contains at least $c^3$. Therefore $c^2\mid\psi_A(\ma)$ and $i_{\mathrm N}>0$ is integral. The Pell norm gives
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:S}
 S^2=\Delta(f^2-1).
\end{equation}
In particular $S>c^2$ and $c\mid S$.

Set $v=(p-1)/2$, which is odd. Lemma~\ref{fup:fd:lem:oddpoly} gives integral $V=Q_v(S^2)$. Modulo $f$, equation~\eqref{fup:fd:eq:S} says $S^2\equiv1-A^2$. Modulo $c$, it says $S^2\equiv0$. Hence, retaining the minus signs,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:Vcongruences}
 V\equiv-c\pmod f,\qquad V\equiv-p\pmod c.
\end{equation}
Thus $o$ and $j$ are integers. Since $p\ge3$, Pell growth gives
\[
 V\ge\frac{\chi_S(3)}S=4S^2-3>c>p,
\]
so $o,j>0$.

We also have $f^2\equiv1\pmod c$. From $of=V+c$ and $V+p=jc$,
\[
 of+p=c(j+1).
\]
Multiplying this congruence modulo $c$ by $f$ gives $o+pf\equiv0\pmod c$. Thus the actual supplied $T$ in~\eqref{fup:fd:eq:auxconstruction} is a strictly positive integer. As $R=p$,
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:Vliteral}
 c(Tf-1)-Rf^2=of-c=V.
\end{equation}
The Pell identity at base $S$ is exactly
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:auxnorm}
 S^2(V^2-\yaux^2)+\yaux^2=1.
\end{equation}

\subsection{Normalized source}
Supply $i=i_{\mathrm N}$. Then
\[
 f^2-\Delta(ic^2)^2=1,\qquad
 \Delta^2(ic^2)^2=S^2.
\]
The literal normalized strong factor is $N_6^{\mathrm N}=1$, and its literal auxiliary coefficient is $\mathcal S^{\mathrm N}=S^2$. Equations~\eqref{fup:fd:eq:Vliteral}--\eqref{fup:fd:eq:auxnorm} therefore give $N_4=1$.

\subsection{Ordinary source}
Instead supply $i=\Delta i_{\mathrm N}$. Now
\[
 (ic^2)^2=\Delta(f^2-1)=S^2,
\]
so the ordinary strong factor $1+(ic^2)^2-\Delta(f^2-1)$ is $+1$, and the literal auxiliary coefficient $\mathcal S^{\mathrm O}$ is again $S^2$. The same positive $f,T,V,\yaux$ satisfy the auxiliary equation.

The quantities $o,j$ are mathematical intermediates proving positive integrality of $T$. They are \emph{not} extra witness ports and are not supplied instead of $T$. Neither mode uses the missing $h$.

\section{The full zero and the failed restoration}\label{fup:fd:sec:finish}
We have constructed all seventeen positive integers in the precise order
\[
 J,F,\alpha,\tr,f,i,T,s,w,\tau,\eta,\zeta,\yaux,Z,\delta,\rho,\sigma.
\]
Their literal register values match~\eqref{fup:fd:eq:literal}--\eqref{fup:fd:eq:outerliteral}. The first/main norms, actual input norm, auxiliary norm, sheared transport factor and the appropriate strong factor all equal $+1$. The fully paid finalizer~\eqref{fup:fd:eq:finalizer} is therefore exactly zero.

Since $P=2XY^2+1\equiv1\pmod E$, reduction of the $\psi_P$ recurrence gives $\psi_P(n)\equiv n\pmod E$, hence $k\equiv2n\pmod E$. Therefore
\[
 k-R-1\equiv2n-p-1=25u-1\pmod E.
\]
Here $E=2^{88u-1}>25u$ and $25u-1>0$, so this is the least nonnegative remainder. Also
\[
 k=2\psi_P(40u)\ge80u>55u+1=R+1.
\]
Consequently $(k-R-1)/E$ is positive rational but nonintegral. Equation~\eqref{fup:fd:eq:productidentity} proves that no integer $h$ can restore a parent zero at this retained tuple. This completes the proof of Theorem~\ref{fup:fd:thm:main}.

\section{An optional smaller radix for the genuine recipe}\label{fup:fd:sec:smooth}
This section is a later refinement of the factorial proof, preserved in its own frozen source packet. It received a separate coordinating review and author checks; it is not part of the earlier independent review. It changes neither the candidate arrays nor the conclusion. The original factorial construction remains intact in Sections~\ref{fup:fd:sec:factorial}--\ref{fup:fd:sec:finish}; the new argument replaces only the choice of $t$ and its outer positivity estimate.

The genuine recipe has the stronger property $d=5^r$, with $r\ge1$. Set
\[
 h_K=\left\lceil\log_2(K+1)\right\rceil,\qquad
 H_0=\max(I,h_K).
\]
Choose the least integer $\lambda\ge0$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:smootht}
 t=4400d\,2^\lambda\ge4H_0.
\end{equation}
The doubling count $\lambda$ is only a mathematical selection parameter, not another supplied coordinate. Then
\[
 t=2^{\lambda+4}5^{r+2}11,\qquad
 \nu=\lambda+4,\qquad m=275d,\qquad
 N=t/d=4400\,2^\lambda.
\]
Both Euler periods $\varphi(5^{r+2})=4\cdot5^{r+1}$ and $\varphi(11)=10$ divide $t$, so $2^t\equiv1\pmod m$. Also $1100\mid N$. Thus all radix and CRT arguments remain valid with
\[
 3\le e<4m=1100d\le t/4.
\]
Keep $q=2^t$, $J$, $Q$, $M$, $Z$, $C$, $F$, $\alpha$ and the paid $R$ defined exactly as before. The same two reductions give $R\equiv e\pmod t$, while the 1100-block argument gives $55\mid R$ and the actual mask residues give $R\equiv3\pmod4$.

The former bounds $K,W\le t$ are no longer asserted. Instead,~\eqref{fup:fd:eq:smootht} gives
\[
 I\le t/4,\qquad K<2^{h_K}\le2^{t/4},\qquad
 W=2^I\le2^{t/4},\qquad Z\le2^\nu\le t.
\]
Since $t\ge22000>64$, we have $t\le2^{t/4}$ and $3t\le2^{t/4}$. Therefore
\begin{align*}
 F=(K+2^e)(W+Z)&<2^{t/2+2},\\
 2Z+W+\ell x&\le3t+2^{t/4}\le2^{t/4+1}.
\end{align*}
Both exponents $t/2+2$ and $t/4+1$ are smaller than $t-1$. Their sum is less than $2^t$, proving $\alpha>0$. The same shifted-mask estimates yield the original packed bounds and $p=R>5t+5$. We also have $I\le t/4<t<p$, so the shared input split remains positive. Every subsequent dyadic, ratio, transport, auxiliary and restoration argument is unchanged.

\begin{corollary}[The radix can be singly exponential at fixed compiler]
For each genuine compiler and positive input, Theorem~\ref{fup:fd:thm:main} also holds with the smooth exponent~\eqref{fup:fd:eq:smootht}. The least choice of $\lambda$ satisfies
\[
 t\le\max(4400d,8H_0).
\]
Consequently the chosen radix $q=2^t$ is at most singly exponential in $x$ when the compiler is fixed.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
If $\lambda=0$, then $t=4400d$. If $\lambda>0$, minimality gives $t/2<4H_0$, so $t<8H_0$. At fixed compiler, $I=2dx+b$ is linear in $x$ and $h_K$ is constant. The already verified replacements prove all seventeen witness conditions and the same nonzero remainder $25u-1$.
\end{proof}
This is a bound on the radix selection only, not a small bound on the downstream Pell witnesses. It is not an operation-count improvement. The addendum has its own exact modular/CRT and finite-margin checks and separate review attribution; it does not retroactively change the independently reviewed factorial draft.

\section{Earlier reductions and why the completion matters}\label{fup:fd:sec:history}
The chronological stages are worth separating because a valid subsystem counterexample alone would not establish the theorem.

\subsection{The noncircular bootstrap}
The earlier reduction, preserved in \code{PROOF.md} with a separate audit, proves that \emph{every} full positive zero of either deletion candidate satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{fup:fd:eq:defectreduction}
 \begin{gathered}
 p=R,\qquad R\equiv3\pmod4,\qquad R/2<n<R,\\
 d_{\mathrm{def}}=2n-R\text{ odd},\qquad1\le d_{\mathrm{def}}\le R-2<E,\\
 (k-R-1)\bmod E=d_{\mathrm{def}}-1,\qquad
 \frac{k-R-1}{E}>0.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
Its missing-coordinate question was therefore exactly whether the retained equations force $d_{\mathrm{def}}=1$. It did not assume the deleted equation or import a parent zero. Our full construction realizes $d_{\mathrm{def}}=25u>1$ and settles that question negatively.

Here is the logical structure of that independent reduction. A product equal to one makes all six factors units. Congruences modulo 4 exclude negative units in the main, input, strong and auxiliary norms. A minimal-solution descent excludes the negative first norm. Thus transport is also $+1$, giving the genuine outer bounds before any dyadic decoding. Positive Pell classification and the strict ratio imply $p/2<n<p$.

The projection $X\equiv2^p\pmod H$, together with the actual scales, excludes $p\le5$ by elementary inequalities. Consequently $p\ge6$ and
\[
 c=\psi_A(p)>A\Delta^2,\qquad c>2p,\qquad c>2R.
\]
These are the noncircular bounds needed to apply the normalized rank argument or the ordinary relaxed-rank lemma. They yield a strong Pell index $m$ with $p\mid m$, $c\mid m$ and $m>2p$. The retained positive $T$ then excludes the negative auxiliary branch and makes its derived $o,j$ positive.

Odd auxiliary polynomials and the unsquared minus congruences recover $p=R$ and $p\equiv3\pmod4$. Finally $P\equiv1\pmod E$ gives~\eqref{fup:fd:eq:defectreduction}. The full derivation, including the ordinary relaxed-rank proof and its smaller-fundamental-unit case, is retained in the accompanying source packet. The full counterfamily proof in Sections~\ref{fup:fd:sec:factorial}--\ref{fup:fd:sec:finish} does not depend on this classification theorem: it constructs its coordinates directly.

\subsection{A genuinely scaled subsystem family}
An intermediate family takes $r\ge5$ with $r\equiv1\pmod4$ and
\[
 p=r(2r+1),\quad n=(3r^2+r)/2,\quad
 v=\frac{(2r+1)(r+1)}2-1,\quad X=2^p,\quad Y=2^v.
\]
It has defect $2n-p=r^2$ and exact dyadic balance
\[
 \frac{(2XY)^{p-1}}{2(4XY^2)^{n-1}}=Y.
\]
The same Pell estimates yield $Y<c/k<Y+1$ because
\[
 4p\,2^{v-p}<1,
 \qquad p-v=r^2-(r-1)/2.
\]
The projection quotient is integral and positive, and the canonical auxiliary block can be completed. For a dyadic $q\ge16$, choosing $r=q^2/2+1$ also gives the true scales $X=wq$, $Y=sq^3$ and the scalar bounds
\[
 (2q-1)(q^2-1)<p<q^4-q^3.
\]
But scalar bounds are not the actual fixed-mask packed equation. That stage did not complete the same input, transport and packing simultaneously. The smaller materialized fixture $r=5$, $p=55$, $n=40$, $X=2^{55}$, $Y=2^{32}$, $q=16$ has remainder 24, but fails the scalar lower bound on $R$. It is only a subsystem check.

The upstream scaled-obstruction note at commit \nolinkurl{93c34e817c7bf726becd47326d22ec5e511a4251} likewise leaves the input and auxiliary constraints unfinished. Its matching small fixture is prior subsystem context. The advance proved here is the factorial--CRT completion at the \emph{actual paid} $R$, for every unchanged genuine compiler and input, with the shared $\rho$ and both auxiliary source modes.

\section{Reproducibility and the scope of computation}\label{fup:fd:sec:repro}
\begin{writenote}
\emph{Shipped names.} The packet's files are shipped with the prefix \texttt{17-index-deletion-} (README): the frozen reduction note \rw{PROOF.md} is \rw{17-index-deletion-evidence-PROOF.md}, kept byte for byte with the erratum below; the final counterfamily proof is \rw{17-index-deletion-evidence-FULL_COUNTERFAMILY.md}, the smooth-radix proof \rw{17-index-deletion-smooth-SMOOTH_RADIX.md}, and the independently reviewed drafts \rw{17-index-deletion-evidence-audit_bootstrap-original_sources-FULL_COUNTERFAMILY.reviewed.md} and \rw{...-SCALED_FAMILY.reviewed.md}. Their SHA-256 digests were measured equal to the ones quoted below. The two reviewer snapshot ledgers \rw{reviewed_snapshot.sha256} and \rw{counterfamily_reviewed_snapshot.sha256} are not shipped; besides the files above they record the reviewer's snapshot of the preliminary \rw{PROOF.md}, SHA-256 \texttt{8d61c1eb4b367e3d\ldots2b5a87}, which differs from the shipped note and is historical. At placement the five checkers, run directly on a copy, passed in normal and optimized Python with receipts equal to the frozen ones after CRLF line ends were normalized; the release verifier refuses to run on NTFS (file modes) and compares stdout bytes that Windows writes with CRLF. The programme executed none of them.
\end{writenote}
The accompanying release contains the editable LaTeX source, deterministic PDF, the frozen factorial packet and separate smooth-radix addendum, a release manifest and hash inventory, and explicit replay/build/archive tools. The manifest authenticates the complete allowed regular-file inventory before a copied checker is invoked. The release verifier checks source pins and typed expected receipts before running newly authored active checkers in normal and optimized Python. It rejects extra or changed files rather than silently blessing them.

The active scientific checkers have canonical JSON stdout. Their \code{--expect} interface compares exact bytes; \code{--output} is optional and accepts only a fresh external path. The copied scientific packet and all predecessor sources remain immutable. A replay from a safely extracted and moved directory demonstrates independence from the original absolute workspace paths. Hostile archive and tamper tests are separate from mathematical evidence.

\paragraph{What the finite checks establish.}
The reduction checker performs static literal deletion/interface checks and exact mathematical recurrence, projection, rank, step-down, small-index, parity and scaled-subsystem checks. The full-construction checker tests factorial odd parts, literal CRT arithmetic, outer margins, shared input splits and both auxiliary forms. The separately authored smooth-radix checker adds 625 exact period cases, 27 height-selection and CRT cases, and 1009 finite margin checks. Its necessary-residue fixtures are not complete compiler instances. The independent auditor uses separately authored mathematical expressions and tests the full proof's vulnerable arithmetic and positivity steps. Normal and optimized runs must match their receipts exactly.

\paragraph{What they do not establish.}
The outer arithmetic fixtures use explicitly mock necessary-residue ports. They are not represented as complete compiler instances. The modest Pell fixtures do not simultaneously instantiate the entire factorial-radix family. No finite enumeration can replace the uniform theorem, and no recorded source arithmetic array is interpreted by a checker. Historical upstream check totals embedded in inert source documents describe those documents only; they are not new executions in this release.

\paragraph{A narrow erratum in the preserved preliminary note.}
The frozen reduction note \code{PROOF.md} states the strict bound $\psi_A(j)<(2A)^{j-1}$ for $j\ge2$. At $j=2$ equality holds. The correct strict range is $j\ge3$, with the non-strict bound valid for $j\ge1$. That note uses the estimate only at indices 4 and 5; the full counterfamily uses $p\ge55$ and $n\ge40$. Thus neither conclusion changes. Equation~\eqref{fup:fd:eq:pellbounds} states the correct range, and the frozen source bytes are preserved with this explicit erratum.

\paragraph{Independent mathematical review.}
The independent full-counterfamily audit reports PASS for the complete construction, including factorial totient divisibility, exact CRT, genuine shifted masks, packing margins, input/shared-split positivity, literal transport, both strong modes, positive $T$, and the final nonzero remainder. The independently reviewed draft had SHA256 \nolinkurl{789e0d876393c4cd74076c41b9d030d3e192939346505217db13cfd599ed48a0}; those exact bytes, original audit and original receipts are preserved. The final text corrects the peripheral base-check phrase from $p=4$ to $p=5$ (the constructed range is $p\ge55$) and spells out the already-used final inequalities $E=2^{88u-1}>25u$ and $k>R+1$. The coordinating review checked these final bytes. Later active copies of the independent checkers change portability and output handling only, with before/after hashes and separately attributed author replay receipts. The independent reviewer did not review the subsequent packaging. The final proof SHA256 is
\hash{dc886e8991e32c331b9135b4ea6b3f73733656be3df6c2aa01576e1732fb83e1}
The separately frozen smooth-radix proof SHA256 is
\hash{259ec165067555228eb97fd6ca37180feaba6861df4ce1cce94d73917b12d8ca}
The manifests and replays establish byte identity; the mathematical arguments and their explicitly attributed reviews establish the theorem.

\part{Candidates below the 84-operation polynomial: the free-coefficient 83 and square/product 82 charts}\label{fup:part:cand}
\noindent\wtag\ This Part prints three manuscripts about two candidate reductions of the research tree's 84-operation universal polynomial ($84=47\mathrm M+37\mathrm A$, 18 positive witnesses, exact degree 187; commit \texttt{20aafb9a5}, Section~\ref{fup:fm:sec:tree}). Report~43 (Sections 43.$k$) studies the \emph{free-coefficient 83} candidate of commit \texttt{0d9d1e017} ($83=46\mathrm M+37\mathrm A$, 18 witnesses, source degree 111), which supplies the auxiliary coefficient $S$ instead of paying $S=i\Delta c^2$. Report~45 (Sections 45.$k$) refutes the \emph{square/product 82} candidate of commit \texttt{6ce2dcaaf} ($82=45\mathrm M+37\mathrm A$, 18 witnesses, exact degree 185), which supplies the products $f^2$ and $Tf$. Report~46 (Sections 46.$k$) measures the height of Report~45's certificates and proves that none of Report~45's outer tuples extends to the free-coefficient interface of Report~43. Report~46 imports both of the others and cannot be read without them, so the three form one Part, as Reports~33 and~34 form Part~\ref{fup:part:ir}.
\begin{writenote}
\emph{Status of both candidates at this write.} Both are refuted on their inherited compiler recipe, so neither lowers the record of 84 operations:
\begin{itemize}
\item The free-coefficient 83 candidate is refuted by the research tree's \rw{free_coefficient83_prime_outer_collapse.md} (commit \texttt{8cf6239b6}, 3 October 2026, 18:33~PDT, with \rw{.py}, \rw{.json} and the independent review \rw{review_free_coefficient83_prime_outer_collapse_math.md}): on every valid fixed-program slice it has infinitely many full positive zeros at every positive ordinary input, using prime main indices $p\equiv1\pmod4$. Report~43 is pinned to \texttt{0d9d1e017} and predates that commit; its statements that the ordinary-input language is open are superseded (dated note below). Report~46 cites the collapse (Section~\ref{fup:eh:sec:scope}) and ships its independent audit.
\item The square/product 82 candidate is refuted twice, by different constructions: by the tree's \rw{complete82_all_input_outer_collapse.md} (commit \texttt{dc03992d9}, 18:26~PDT, with \rw{.py}, \rw{.json} and two independent reviews that pass), and by Report~45, whose audit observation is dated 00:44~UTC on 4 October (17:44~PDT on 3 October), about forty minutes earlier, and which does not cite the tree's note. Neither makes a priority claim; Report~45's theorem is printed as an independent second route (note at Theorem~\ref{fup:sp:thm:main}).
\end{itemize}
``83'' and ``82'' each name more than one polynomial in the programme; Table~\ref{fup:fm:tab:candidates} lists them. In particular the \emph{independent-gamma} 83-operation candidate ($83=47\mathrm M+36\mathrm A$, 18 witnesses, degree 187) is a different polynomial, whose ordinary-input language is unresolved; none of these three reports treats it, and the programme's review notes that Report~43's lemmas ``do not apply automatically'' to it.

\emph{The programme's review.} \rw{review_incoming_arithmetic_reports_39_46.md} (commit \texttt{5d8889017}) reviewed all three archives within a stated scope; it executed no archived program. It did \emph{not} review in full: Report~43's inner-family addenda (Sections~\ref{fup:fc:sec:completion}--\ref{fup:fc:sec:interface} and Appendix~\ref{fup:fc:app:11}) and its other squarefree addenda (Lemmas~\ref{fup:fc:lem:firstcoord} and~\ref{fup:fc:lem:smalltarget} and Theorem~\ref{fup:fc:thm:squarefree}); Report~45's archived finite receipts, which it did not replay; and Report~46's analytic expansions (Sections~\ref{fup:eh:sec:expansion} and~\ref{fup:eh:sec:allinverse}) and their claimed precision. Those parts rest on the sources' own audits, which are shipped. Its findings are printed in the notes at the start of each source below.

\emph{Notation in this Part.} The three sources share the macros for the Pell sequences $\chi_A(r),\psi_A(r)$ and for the named factors, but not all their letters. Inside the sections of one source a letter has that source's meaning; Table~\ref{fup:cand:tab:letters} lists the letters that change between the three sources or differ from Part~V. One rendering was changed: Report~46 writes the free-coefficient auxiliary and strong factors $N_{\mathrm a}$ and $N_{\mathrm s}$, and Reports~43 and~45 write the same factors $N_{\mathrm{aux}}$ and $N_{\mathrm{strong}}$; all three are printed as $\Na$ and $\Ns$. No other symbol was renamed. The three sources write the shifted and native masks $M_F$ and $M_F^{\mathrm{native}}$, $M_F^0$ or $M_{F,0}$; these are Part~V's $\MFsrcV$ and $\MFnatV$.
\end{writenote}

$Q$, $Q_s$ & $Q=q^2-1$ throughout; $Q_v$, $Q_j$, $Q_h$ odd quotient polynomials; 45, 46: $Q_s=\Fa-\Delta i^2c^4$. & ---\\
$P$, $P_5$ & $P=2XY^2+1$ throughout; 45, 46: $P_5$ the product of the five retained factors and $P_{82}$ the polynomial. & ---\\
$\epsilon$, $\varepsilon$ & 45: $\varepsilon=\pm1$, the common sign of the index and transport factors; 46: the same sign written $\epsilon$, and $\varepsilon_h$, $\varepsilon_0$ error terms. & ---\\\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
}

\fupsourcehead{Report 43. Structural obstructions and nonnative inner families for the free coefficient 83 candidate}
\noindent\wtag\ Source~18 of this report, manuscript~05 of batch~88: \emph{Research Report 43}, author line ``Research Report 43'', dated 4 October 2026; archive \rw{Structural_Obstructions_for_the_Free_Coefficient_83_Candidate_Package.zip} (arrival commit \texttt{c5612efa1}, placed by \texttt{992aaafb3}); pins \texttt{0d9d1e017} (the candidate) and \texttt{53c34b012} (the credited native-alias precursor). Its sections, statements and equations are numbered 43.$k$ here.
\begin{sourceabstract}
The free-coefficient 83-operation arithmetic candidate is obtained from the
verified scaled 84-operation construction by supplying its auxiliary
coefficient directly. This report proves two complementary results. First,
every full strictly positive candidate zero on a genuine fixed-compiler
slice has auxiliary factor $\Na=z^2>0$, where $z^2\mid\D$ and
$z=\gcd(|V|,y)$. On the additional squarefree-discriminant slice, the main,
input and auxiliary factors are all $1$. Second, an explicit infinite
power-of-17 family satisfies the intended first, main, index, auxiliary
and strong equations with main Pell rank $p\ne R$, and admits compatible
positive input loading with the shared projection whenever $p>I$.
Its completion to the genuine compiler is equivalent to a
specified packing congruence, transport congruence and strict positivity
test in two integer parameters. No solution of that interface at a rejected
ordinary input is supplied. Ordinary-input language soundness remains
\textbf{open}; the established universal construction remains
$84=47\mathrm M+37\mathrm A$, with 18 positive witnesses and exact degree
187. The results use classical Pell arithmetic. No priority claim or new
universal-operation bound is made.
\end{sourceabstract}
\begin{writenote}
\emph{Superseded open status (dated note, batch~88).} The abstract's ``Ordinary-input language soundness remains \textbf{open}'' and question~1 of Section~\ref{fup:fc:sec:conclusions} were open at the pin \texttt{0d9d1e017}. They are answered: the research tree's \rw{free_coefficient83_prime_outer_collapse.md} (commit \texttt{8cf6239b6}, 3 October 2026, 18:33~PDT, which is 4 October, 01:33~UTC, the date Report~43 carries) proves that on every valid fixed-program slice the candidate has infinitely many full positive zeros at every positive ordinary input, so the specialization to a compiler of the empty set gives a false acceptance; its independent review passes. The answer does not come through Report~43's interface: the tree's family uses prime main indices $p\equiv1\pmod4$ and a different outer tuple, not the power-of-17 family of Theorem~\ref{fup:fc:thm:family}, so questions~2--4 of Section~\ref{fup:fc:sec:conclusions} (the family's mask condition and residual congruences, and the remaining divisor branches) stay open as mathematical questions, while question~5 presupposes a general soundness proof, which this candidate cannot have. The source's remark that a prime-progression shortcut does not close the problem (end of Section~\ref{fup:fc:sec:interface}) concerns its own interface and is not contradicted. No theorem of Report~43 is refuted. The open status is kept byte for byte in the delivered package: the shipped \rw{data/18-free83-MANIFEST.json}, \rw{data/18-free83-checks-MATH.normal.json}, \rw{data/18-free83-checks-QA.json} and \rw{data/18-free83-source-receipts-inner_replay.json} record \code{"ordinary_input_language": "OPEN"}, and the unshipped delivery README says the same. The programme's review: ``The README's open-language status is historical.''

\emph{The programme's review} (\rw{review_incoming_arithmetic_reports_39_46.md}, commit \texttt{5d8889017}) read the three structural notes (shipped as \rw{18-free83-source-structure-early_auxiliary_norm_lemma.md}, \rw{-exceptional_negative_aux_exclusion.md} and \rw{-even_parameter_divisor_classification.md}): the small-norm descent of Lemma~\ref{fup:fc:lem:smallnorm}, the reduction of a negative auxiliary factor to the single branch $f=1$, $S=A$, $\Ns=-1$, $\Na=-\Delta$ and its exclusion for both parities of the main index (Theorem~\ref{fup:fc:thm:aux}), and the even-$A$ divisor classification of Theorem~\ref{fup:fc:thm:even}, which ``classifies values, not which values satisfy all other source constraints''. All consistent. The inner-family and other squarefree addenda were not reviewed in full (Part~VIII's opening note lists them).

\emph{Credits and overlaps.} Section~\ref{fup:fc:sec:completion} extends the conditional auxiliary CRT completion of the tree's \rw{free_coefficient83_native_alias.md} (commit \texttt{53c34b012}), which the source credits in Section~\ref{fup:fc:sec:scope}. Lemma~\ref{fup:fc:lem:quotient} is Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:oddquotient} of Report~33 with the extra value $Q_v(1)=1$; it is re-proved in Parts~\ref{fup:part:nr} and~\ref{fup:part:fd} and in Report~46. Lemma~\ref{fup:fc:lem:smallnorm} is restated and proved again in Report~46 as Lemma~\ref{fup:eh:lem:smallnorm} (a second proof, printed there). The source's ``Report~41'' is Part~\ref{fup:part:fd}. The tree's \rw{complete83_free_coefficient_scout.md} records degree 111 as source data; Report~43 does not use it. A nonunit zero that the tree materialized (commit \texttt{8d465637d}, factor values $(1,1,1,1,1,-675,-1)$ on numerals that are not a genuine compiler slice) is consistent with Report~43's genuine-slice theorems; Report~43 does not cite it.

\emph{Layout.} The source's table of contents is omitted. Its release verifier is the only one of the five batch-88 verifiers that runs on Windows as delivered (README). The source's files are shipped with the prefix \texttt{18-free83-}.
\end{writenote}

\section{Scope and the exact arithmetic object}
\label{fup:fc:sec:scope}
\subsection{Three distinct questions}
The following statements must be kept separate throughout.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{A full positive arithmetic zero} assigns positive integers to all
18 supplied witnesses and the ordinary input, retaining the six fixed
compiler numerals, and makes the complete candidate polynomial zero.
\item \emph{An intended inner completion} satisfies selected literal
factors at their intended values and the indicated projection, while
treating the packed index $R$ as a target port. It need not realize the
genuine outer packing and transport equations.
\item \emph{A language counterexample} is a full positive arithmetic zero
at an ordinary input known to be rejected by the unchanged genuine
compiler. Neither extra witnesses at an already accepted input nor an
inner completion alone is a language counterexample.
\end{enumerate}
The structural theorems concern the first notion. The infinite families
initially concern the second. The exact interface in Section~\ref{fup:fc:sec:interface}
states what remains to pass from the second to the first. A separate
rejected-input certificate would then be needed for the third.

The established 84-operation baseline is the previously independently
audited construction, not an unrestricted lower bound. The present
83-operation candidate is also distinct from Report 41's first-index
deletion and its 81/82-operation failures. Here the first-index factor is
retained and is verified explicitly.

\subsection{Relation to the existing native alias construction}
The upstream note \emph{Free-coefficient native index mismatch, and a
four-bit mask obstruction}, at commit
\code{53c34b0129bbde7b5d79e520ff3e004cf6f7c07a}, already extends the
family $p=t(t+2)$, $n=t(t+1)$, $R=2n-1$ to a positive retained index
witness $h$ and a conditional auxiliary CRT completion at $p\ne R$.
Its condition $\gcd(c,p)\mid R-p$ is equivalent to
$\gcd(c,p)\mid R$. That note also excludes its entire family at the
$B=16$ necessary-mask interface by a congruence modulo 17; it does not
supply input or transport witnesses. Thus neither a wrong-index inner
extension nor the conditional CRT idea is claimed here as a new invention.

The present synthesis adds the explicit infinitely many gcd-favorable
11- and 17-power parameters, the every-odd-$p$ completion theorem, positive
ordinary-input loading with its shared projection, the exact genuine
outer interface, and the structural divisor obstructions. The predecessor's
$B=16$ argument does not automatically apply to these different families
or to the genuine bases with $d$ a power of 5. These comparisons are with
the identified source, not a claim of priority over the literature.

\subsection{Compiler slice and positive supplied domain}
The six fixed ports are
\[
 B-1,\quad K,\quad \ell,\quad b,\quad M_C,\quad M_F.
\]
A \emph{genuine slice} means these are produced by the complete inherited
compiler recipe, not arbitrary numerals satisfying a few inequalities.
In particular $B=2^d$, $\ell=2d$, $d$ and $b$ are powers of 5 with
$d,b\ge5$ and $b\le d$, and $K>0$. The source uses the shifted numeral
\[
 M_F=M_F^{\rm native}+B-1,\qquad
 0<M_C,M_F^{\rm native}<B-1.
\]
That shift is essential. Throughout, $x\ge1$ is the ordinary input, and
the 18 witnesses
\[
 J,F,\alpha,t_{\rm tr},f,h,S,T,s,w,\tau,\eta,\zeta,y,Z,
 \delta,\rho,\sigma
\]
are strictly positive integers. Derived quantities may initially be signed.
In particular we do not assume that $V$, $C$, $W$ or the input norm's root
is positive.

Define the paid expressions
\begin{align}
 q&=(B-1)J+1,&X&=wq,&Y&=sq^3,&E&=XY,\\
 a&=Y(X+1)=E+Y,&A&=a+2,&\D&=A^2-1,&H&=4a+3,\\
 k&=\eta+\zeta,&c&=kY+\eta,&D&=X+ac+(\rho+\sigma)H.\label{fup:fc:eq:base}
\end{align}
Thus $A\ge3$, $\D\ge8$, $D>0$ and $kY<c<k(Y+1)$.
The remaining definitions are
\begin{align}
 C&=q-F-Z-\alpha-\ell x,&W&=C-Z,\\
 I&=\ell x+b,&\kappa&=I+\delta\D,&\mu&=W+a\kappa+\rho H,\\
 G&=q^2-qF-Z,&M&=(M_C+qM_F)J,&R&=G(q^2-1)+M,\\
 V&=c(Tf-1)-Rf^2.\label{fup:fc:eq:ports}
\end{align}
The seven literal factors are
\begin{align}
 \Nf&=\tau^2-XY^2(XY^2+1)k^2,\\
 \Nm&=D^2-\D c^2,&\Ni&=\mu^2-\D\kappa^2,\\
 \Na&=S^2V^2-(S^2-1)y^2,&\Nk&=k-hE-R,\\
 \Nt&=(K+w)C+q-F-t_{\rm tr}(q-1),\\
 \Ns&=\D f^2-S^2.
\end{align}
The complete polynomial is
\begin{equation}
 F_{83}=\Nf\Nm\Ni\Na\Nk\Nt\Ns-\D.\label{fup:fc:eq:full}
\end{equation}
Consequently each factor at a zero is nonzero and divides $\D$; in
particular its absolute value is at most $\D$. No general unit
normalization follows merely from this product equation.

\subsection{Literal deletion and what the inverse says}
In the verified 84-operation source, $S=i\D c^2$ is a paid multiplication
from the supplied positive witness $i$. Replacing $i$ by the supplied
positive $S$ and deleting that one row gives the exact all-ring identity
\begin{equation}
 F_{83}(S=i\D c^2)=F_{84}.\label{fup:fc:eq:forward}
\end{equation}
The other rows and the seven-operation finalizer remain. The arithmetic
ledger is therefore $83=46\mathrm M+37\mathrm A$, with 18 supplied
witnesses. The source's degree claim is 111; the independently audited
84 baseline has degree 187. Degree 111 is not needed by any theorem below
and is not a substitute for soundness.

For $\D c^2\ne0$, the literal rational inverse is uniquely
\begin{equation}
 i=\frac{S}{\D c^2}.\label{fup:fc:eq:inverse}
\end{equation}
On the positive domain it is positive, but it need not be integral.
Equation~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:forward} proves the candidate accepts every input
accepted by the 84 parent. Failure of integrality in~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:inverse}
disproves this particular witness-set inverse, not equality of the
ordinary-input languages.

\section{Pell notation and elementary tools}
For integer $A\ge2$ define
\[
 \ch A r+\ps A r\sqrt{A^2-1}
  =(A+\sqrt{A^2-1})^r\qquad(r\ge0).
\]
Both sequences are integral, with initial values $(1,0)$ and $(A,1)$,
and recurrence $u_{r+1}=2Au_r-u_{r-1}$. The norm is 1. Addition and
subtraction identities follow by multiplying the displayed powers.
In particular
\begin{align}
 \ps A {r+s}&=\ch A r\ps A s+\ps A r\ch A s,\\
 \ch A {r+s}&=\ch A r\ch A s+(A^2-1)\ps A r\ps A s,\\
 \ps A {2r}&=2\ch A r\ps A r.\label{fup:fc:eq:dup}
\end{align}
For $r\ge1$, the positive sequences increase strictly, and for $r\ge3$
\begin{equation}
 (2A-1)^{r-1}\le\ps A r<(2A)^{r-1}.\label{fup:fc:eq:growth}
\end{equation}
These inequalities follow by induction from the recurrence; the lower
inequality uses $\ps A {r-1}<\ps A r$. Pell classification for
$u^2-(A^2-1)v^2=1$ uses the fundamental positive pair $(A,1)$: any pair
with $v\ge1$ has $u\ge A$, and multiplication by the inverse of that
unit descends until $(1,0)$. Thus all nonnegative solutions are the above
pairs. We also use the usual integer Pell-unit classification for a
general positive nonsquare discriminant in one conditional theorem.

\begin{lemma}[Odd quotient identities]\label{fup:fc:lem:quotient}
There are polynomials $Q_v(Z)\in\mathbb Z[Z]$ such that
\[
 \frac{\ch S {2v+1}}S=Q_v(S^2),\quad
 Q_v(0)=(-1)^v(2v+1),\quad
 Q_v(1-A^2)=(-1)^v\ps A {2v+1},\quad Q_v(1)=1.
\]
The quotient is an integer for every positive integer $S$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Set $Q_0=1$, $Q_1=4Z-3$ and
$Q_{v+1}=(4Z-2)Q_v-Q_{v-1}$. The odd subsequence of $\chi_S$ obeys
this recurrence, proving the first identity. At $Z=0$ the recurrence
and first two values give $(-1)^v(2v+1)$; at $Z=1$ they give 1.
At $Z=1-A^2$, multiplying the $v$th term by $(-1)^v$ gives the recurrence
and initial values of $\psi_A(2v+1)$, proving the remaining identity.
These are polynomial identities, so reducing them modulo an integer
does not require $S$ to be invertible.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Residues of Pell second coordinates]\label{fup:fc:lem:residue}
For $A\ge2$, $m\ge1$ and $t\ge0$, modulo $\ps A m$ the value $\ps A t$
is either 0 or $\pm\ps A j$ for some $1\le j<m$. If $m$ is odd and
$t$ is even, $j$ may be chosen even.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Write $t=qm+j$ with $0\le j<m$. Addition and the norm identity give
$\ps A t\equiv\ch A m^q\ps A j$ and $\ch A m^2\equiv1$.
For even $q$ use $j$ itself; for odd $q$ use
$\ch A m\ps A j\equiv-\ps A {m-j}$. If $j=0$ the residue is zero.
When $m$ is odd and $t$ even, $j$ is even for even $q$, while $m-j$
is even for odd $q$.
\end{proof}

\section{An unconditional auxiliary square theorem}
\label{fup:fc:sec:aux}
\begin{theorem}\label{fup:fc:thm:aux}
Every full strictly positive zero of~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:full} on a genuine
fixed-compiler slice satisfies
\[
 \Na=z^2>0,\qquad z^2\mid\D,\qquad z=\gcd(|V|,y).
\]
No parity assumption on $q$ or $A$, and no assumption $p=R$, is used.
\end{theorem}

\subsection{Descent for a quadratic form near a square}
\begin{lemma}\label{fup:fc:lem:smallnorm}
Let $H_0\ge2$, $v\ge0$ and $y>0$ be integers, and put
$N=H_0v^2-(H_0-1)y^2$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item If $N<0$, then $N\le-(H_0-1)$.
\item If $0<N<H_0$, then $N=z^2$ and $\gcd(v,y)=z$ for a positive $z$.
Every such solution descends to $(z,z)$ by an integral unimodular map.
\item The solutions with $N=-(H_0-1)$ are exactly
\[
 v=2(H_0-1)\ps {2H_0-1}r,\quad
 y=\ch {2H_0-1}r,\qquad r\ge0.
\]
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
When $y>v$, put $t=y-v\ge1$. Direct expansion gives
\begin{equation}
 -N=(H_0-1)t^2+v\bigl(2(H_0-1)t-v\bigr).\label{fup:fc:eq:descentvalue}
\end{equation}
If $N<0$ and $v\le2(H_0-1)t$, the right side is at least $H_0-1$.
Otherwise negativity gives
\[
 2(H_0-1)t<v<\bigl(H_0-1+\sqrt{H_0(H_0-1)}\bigr)t<(2H_0-1)t.
\]
The transformation
\begin{equation}
 v'=(2H_0-1)v-2(H_0-1)y=v-2(H_0-1)t,
 \quad y'=(2H_0-1)y-2H_0v=(2H_0-1)t-v\label{fup:fc:eq:descent}
\end{equation}
preserves $N$ and has $0<v'<v$, $y'>0$. Induction proves the negative gap.

If $0<N<H_0$ and $v\ge y+1$, then
$N=H_0(v^2-y^2)+y^2\ge H_0$, a contradiction. If $v=y$, then $N=v^2$.
Otherwise~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:descentvalue} and $N>0$ give $v>2(H_0-1)t$;
$N<H_0$ gives $v<(2H_0-1)t$, since the quadratic in $v$ is increasing
beyond its positive root and takes value $H_0t^2$ at that endpoint.
Thus~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:descent} descends until a diagonal $(z,z)$. Its determinant
is 1, so both it and its integral inverse preserve the gcd. This proves
the second assertion, including the exact gcd.

At $N=-(H_0-1)$, descent ends where $v\le2(H_0-1)t$.
Equation~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:descentvalue} forces $t=1$ and
$v=0$ or $v=2(H_0-1)$. The latter maps to $(0,1)$.
The inverse matrix is
\[
 \begin{pmatrix}2H_0-1&2(H_0-1)\\2H_0&2H_0-1\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Its nonnegative iterates of $(0,1)$ give exactly the formula in part 3.
Conversely, every displayed pair is an integral positive-$y$ solution by
the Pell norm identity. This proves both inclusion and exhaustiveness.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Reduction to one negative branch}
At a full zero write $d_s=\Ns$ and $N=\Na$. The product gives
$|d_sN|\le\D$. The case $S=1$ is impossible: then
$d_s=\D f^2-1\ge7$, $d_s\mid\D$, and $\gcd(d_s,\D)=1$.
Hence $S\ge2$ and $H_0=S^2\ge4$.

Suppose $N<0$. Lemma~\ref{fup:fc:lem:smallnorm} gives
\begin{equation}
 |d_s|(S^2-1)\le\D.\label{fup:fc:eq:budget}
\end{equation}
If $f\ge2$, then $S^2=\D f^2-d_s\ge3\D$, contradicting the budget.
Thus $f=1$. If $d_s=-b_s<0$, then
$S^2=\D+b_s$ and $b_s(\D+b_s-1)\le\D$, forcing $b_s=1$.
If $d_s>0$, then $\D=S^2+d_s$, and the budget becomes
$(d_s-1)(S^2-2)\le2$. For $d_s=1$ this would require
$A^2-S^2=2$, impossible. For $d_s\ge2$ the only possible square
$S^2\ge4$ is $S^2=4$, $d_s=2$, giving $A^2=7$, also impossible.
Therefore the only negative possibility is
\begin{equation}
 f=1,\qquad S=A,\qquad\Ns=-1,\qquad\Na=-\D.\label{fup:fc:eq:exceptional}
\end{equation}

For $N>0$, one always has $N<S^2$. Indeed $N\le\D/|d_s|$.
For $f\ge2$ use $S^2\ge3\D$; for $f=1,d_s<0$ use $S^2>\D$.
For $f=1,d_s>0$ the value $d_s=1$ was excluded, and
$d_sS^2>S^2+d_s=\D$ for $d_s\ge2,S^2\ge4$.
Lemma~\ref{fup:fc:lem:smallnorm} already proves the theorem on the positive branch.
It remains to exclude~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:exceptional} on the genuine source.

\subsection{What can legitimately be recovered in that branch}
In~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:exceptional}, $\Ns\Na=\D$. Thus the other five integer
factors have product 1 and are individually units. Only at this point
are unit arguments available.

The first norm cannot be $-1$. Put $u_0=XY^2>1$. A solution
$\tau^2-u_0(u_0+1)k^2=-1$ has
$u_0k<\tau<(u_0+\tfrac12)k$ for every $k\ge1$, since $u_0>1$.
The inverse unit gives
$0<(2u_0+1)k-2\tau<k$ and an integral companion root preserving the norm.
Taking its nonzero absolute value yields an impossible infinite descent.
Main and input norms cannot be $-1$ modulo 4, since $\D\equiv0$ or 3.
Hence
\begin{equation}
 \Nf=\Nm=\Ni=1,\qquad\Nk=\Nt=\epsilon\in\{1,-1\}.\label{fup:fc:eq:fiveunits}
\end{equation}

Rewrite transport as
\[
 \Nt=(K+w)C+1-F-(t_{\rm tr}-1)(q-1).
\]
Since $K+w\ge2$ and the final terms are nonpositive, $C\le-1$ would
give $\Nt\le-2$. Thus $C\ge0$ and $F+Z<q$. The shifted mask bounds give
\[
 2q-1\le G\le q^2-q-1,\qquad
 0<M<(q-1)(1+2q).
\]
It follows directly that
\begin{equation}
 (2q-1)(q^2-1)<R<q^4-q^3,\quad
 0<R<a<\D,\quad R+2<E.\label{fup:fc:eq:packingbounds}
\end{equation}
These are untyped integer bounds: $q$ has not been assumed even or a power
of $B$.

Put $P=2XY^2+1$. The fundamental unit for
$\tau^2-XY^2(XY^2+1)k^2=1$ has pair $(P,2)$: coefficient 1 places a
square strictly between consecutive squares, coefficient 2 gives $P$,
and the inverse unit descends any larger solution. Thus
$\tau=\ch P n$, $k=2\ps P n$, $n\ge1$.
Since $P\equiv1\pmod E$, the recurrence gives $\ps P n\equiv n\pmod E$.
From the index unit and~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:packingbounds},
\[
 2n=R+\epsilon+vE,\quad v\ge0,\qquad n\ge(R-1)/2\ge24.
\]
The main positive root gives $D=\ch A p$, $c=\ps A p$.
Since $P>A$ and $c>k$, growth forces $p>n$, hence $p\ge25$.
Also $c>2R$, and independently
\begin{equation}
 c>A\D^2,\qquad
 \ps A6-A\D^2=A(31A^4-30A^2+5)>0.\label{fup:fc:eq:csize}
\end{equation}
No strong-rank theorem or equality $p=R$ has been used.

\subsection{Excluding both parities of the main rank}
Let $B_0=2A^2-1$. Lemma~\ref{fup:fc:lem:smallnorm} and the literal $V$ give
\begin{equation}
 V=\pm2\D\ps {B_0}r\quad(r\ge0),\qquad V\equiv-R\pmod c.\label{fup:fc:eq:badresidue}
\end{equation}
For odd $p$, $\gcd(A,c)=1$, because $\ps A p\equiv\pm1\pmod A$.
Duplication gives $2A\ps {B_0}r=\ps A{2r}$.
Lemma~\ref{fup:fc:lem:residue} with its parity refinement therefore makes $V$
congruent modulo $c$ to zero or
\[
 \pm\frac{\D\ps A j}{A},\qquad 2\le j\le p-1,\quad j\text{ even}.
\]
Every nonzero displayed magnitude is at least $2\D$ and is less than
$c/2$. For the upper bound it suffices to use $j=p-1$ and
\[
 Ac=A^2\ps A{p-1}+A\ch A{p-1}>2\D\ps A{p-1}.
\]
Thus none is the least-absolute residue $-R$, since
$0<R<\D<c/2$. Zero is excluded as well.

For even $p=2m$, let $b_0=\ps {B_0}m$, so $c=2Ab_0$.
Equation~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:badresidue} forces $R$ even. Divide by 2 and reduce
modulo $b_0$:
\[
 \pm\D\ps {B_0}r\equiv-R/2\pmod{b_0}.
\]
The left side is zero or a signed $\D\ps {B_0}j$, $1\le j<m$.
Every nonzero magnitude is at least $\D$ and less than $b_0/2$, since
\[
 b_0>(2B_0-1)\ps {B_0}{m-1}>2\D\ps {B_0}{m-1}.
\]
By~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:csize}, $b_0>\D^2/2>R$. Again
$0<R/2<\min(\D,b_0/2)$ is impossible. Both signs of $V$ and $r=0$
are covered. This excludes the only negative branch and completes
Theorem~\ref{fup:fc:thm:aux}; divisibility $z^2\mid\D$ follows from the full product.

\section{Conditional divisor classification and squarefree obstructions}
\label{fup:fc:sec:squarefree}
\begin{theorem}[Even parameter norm values]\label{fup:fc:thm:even}
Let $A\ge2$ be even, $\D=A^2-1$, and suppose the nonzero represented
integer $N=x_0^2-\D y_0^2$ divides $\D$. Then either $N$ is a positive
square, or $N<0$ and $\D/|N|$ is a square. This is a necessary
classification of represented norm values, not a sufficiency assertion
for arbitrary listed divisors or for full compiler witnesses.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Write $\D=d_0m^2$ with $d_0$ squarefree, and $|N|=rz^2$ with $r$
squarefree. Since $\D\equiv3\pmod4$, $m,d_0$ are odd and
$d_0\equiv3\pmod4$. Divisibility gives $z\mid m$, $z\mid x_0$.
Set $X_0=|x_0|/z$, $Y_0=m|y_0|/z$, and $\varepsilon=\operatorname{sgn}N$.
Then $X_0^2-d_0Y_0^2=\varepsilon r$.
Each prime of $r$ divides $X_0$ because $|N|\mid x_0^2$. If such a prime
did not divide $d_0$, it would divide $Y_0$ too, contradicting the
squarefree valuation on the right. Hence $r\mid d_0$ and $r\mid X_0$.

The integers
\[
 U=\frac{X_0^2+d_0Y_0^2}{r},\qquad W_0=\frac{2X_0Y_0}{r}
\]
satisfy $U^2-d_0W_0^2=1$. The norm is odd, so $X_0,Y_0$ have opposite
parity; $U$ is odd. Let $\alpha+\beta\sqrt{d_0}$ be the fundamental
positive integer Pell unit. Since $A+m\sqrt{d_0}$ has even first
coordinate, $\alpha$ is even: if $\alpha$ were odd, $\beta$ would be
even, and all powers would have odd first coordinate. With $\alpha$ even,
$\beta$ odd and first-coordinate parity alternates with the exponent.
Thus $U=\ch\alpha{2h}$ for some $h\ge0$.

For $\varepsilon=1$,
$X_0^2/r=(U+1)/2=\ch\alpha h^2$, forcing $r=1$.
For $\varepsilon=-1$,
$(d_0/r)Y_0^2=(U+1)/2=\ch\alpha h^2$, forcing $r=d_0$.
This yields $N=z^2$ or $N=-d_0z^2$ and
$\D/|N|=(m/z)^2$, respectively. Zero-coordinate cases obey the same
identities, or follow immediately from $N\mid\D$.
\end{proof}

If $\D$ is squarefree then $A$ must be even: odd $A\ge3$ would make
$8\mid\D$. Theorems~\ref{fup:fc:thm:aux} and~\ref{fup:fc:thm:even} give
\begin{equation}
 \Nm,\Ni\in\{1,-\D\},\qquad\Ns\in\{-1,\D\},\qquad\Na=1.\label{fup:fc:eq:squarefreevalues}
\end{equation}

\begin{lemma}[A first-coordinate divisibility obstruction]\label{fup:fc:lem:firstcoord}
For $A\ge2$, $m\ge3$ and $p\ge0$,
\[
 \ps A m\mid\ch A p+\epsilon,\quad\epsilon\in\{1,-1\}
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad 2m\mid p,\quad\ch A p\equiv1\pmod{\ps A m}.
\]
Conversely $2m\mid p$ gives the displayed residue 1, hence divisibility
of $\ch A p-1$. For $m=2$, divisibility by $\ps A2=2A$ of
$\ch A p\pm1$ at least forces $p$ even.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Put $f=\ps A m$, $D_m=\ch A m$ and $p=qm+j$, $0\le j<m$.
Modulo $f$, $\ch A p$ is $\ch A j$ for even $q$ and
$\ch A{m-j}$ for odd $q$. For $1\le j<m$, the interior values lie
strictly between 1 and $f-1$, since
$f-\ch A{m-1}=A\ps A{m-1}\ge2$.
At $j=0,q$ odd, the residue of $D_m=Af-\ps A{m-1}$ is
$f-\ps A{m-1}$, again strictly between 1 and $f-1$ for $m\ge3$.
Only $j=0,q$ even gives $\pm1$, and it gives $+1$.
The reverse implication follows from addition and $D_m^2\equiv1$.
For $m=2$, an odd $p$ has $\ch A p\equiv0\pmod A$, excluding $\pm1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Small target quotient obstruction]\label{fup:fc:lem:smalltarget}
Let $S\ge2$, $k_0\ge6$, $c=\ch S{k_0}$, and $1<L<S^2-1$.
There is no odd positive $t$ and no sign $\epsilon$ such that
\[
 \epsilon\frac{\ch S t}S\equiv-L\pmod c.
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The Pell pair at $2k_0$ is $(-1,0)$ modulo $c$. Period and reflection
therefore reduce $\ch S t$ to a signed $\ch S j$ with $j$ odd and
$1\le j\le k_0$. If $k_0$ is even, $\gcd(S,c)=1$; division modulo $c$
gives representatives $\pm\ch S j/S$ with $j<k_0$, each of magnitude
less than $c/2$. If $k_0$ is odd, divide modulo $c/S$; $j=k_0$ becomes
zero and the remaining magnitudes are less than $(c/S)/2$.
In either case the smallest nonzero magnitude is 1, and the next is
\[
 \ch S3/S=4(S^2-1)+1>L.
\]
Finally $c\ge\ch S6>S(2S-1)^5>S(S^2-1)^2>2SL$.
Thus $L$ itself is below half the applicable modulus and lies in the
forbidden gap between the representatives. It is neither zero nor 1.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Squarefree main and input normalization]\label{fup:fc:thm:squarefree}
Every full strictly positive candidate zero on a genuine slice with
squarefree $\D$ satisfies $\Nm=\Ni=\Na=1$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
By~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:squarefreevalues}, if either main or input equals $-\D$,
the other is 1, $\Ns=-1$, and the first, index and transport factors are
units. The first is $+1$ by descent, and index and transport have the
same sign. Thus~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:packingbounds} applies, in particular
$1<R<a<\D$ and $C\ge0$.

Suppose first $\Ni=-\D$. Squarefreeness gives $\D\mid\mu$;
write $\mu=\D b_0$. Then $\kappa^2-\D b_0^2=1$, so
$\kappa=\ch A t$ for $t\ge0$. Modulo $\D$ this is $A^t$, hence 1 or
$A$. But $\kappa\equiv I\pmod\D$, whereas genuine input loading and
$C\ge0$ give
\[
 1<I=2dx+b<q+d<2q<A<\D.
\]
Here $2dx<q$, $b\le d$, and $q\ge B=2^d>d$.
The residue $I$ is neither 1 nor $A$, a contradiction.

Now suppose $\Nm=-\D$. Squarefreeness yields
$c=\ch A p$ and $D=\D\ps A p$. The retained first/index units give
$k=2\ps P n$ and $n\ge24$. Since
$c>kY>\ps A n>\ch A{n-1}$, one has $p\ge n\ge24$.
The strong equation gives $S=\ch A m$, $f=\ps A m$, $m\ge1$.
The auxiliary equation gives
\[
 V=\epsilon\ch S t/S,\qquad t>0\text{ odd},\quad\epsilon=\operatorname{sgn}V.
\]
Lemma~\ref{fup:fc:lem:quotient} and $S^2=1+\D f^2$ imply
$V\equiv\epsilon\pmod f$. The literal $V$ implies $V\equiv-c\pmod f$,
so $f\mid c+\epsilon$.

For $m\ge3$, Lemma~\ref{fup:fc:lem:firstcoord} gives $2m\mid p$ and
$c\equiv1\pmod f$. Also $c\ge\ch A{2m}=1+2\D f^2$.
Since $R<\D$ and $T\ge1$,
\[
 V\ge c(f-1)-Rf^2>c(f-1)-c/2>0.
\]
Thus $\epsilon=1$, forcing $c\equiv-1\pmod f$ as well, impossible
because $f>2$.
For $m=2$, Lemma~\ref{fup:fc:lem:firstcoord} forces $p=2k_0$ with $k_0\ge12$;
for $m=1$ put $k_0=p\ge24$. In either case $c=\ch S{k_0}$.
Put $L=Rf^2$. Then $1<L<\D f^2=S^2-1$, while the literal quotient gives
$V\equiv-L\pmod c$. Lemma~\ref{fup:fc:lem:smalltarget} excludes both cases.
\end{proof}

Two alternatives remain on this slice. If $\Ns=\D$, then
$\Nf=1$ and $\Nk=\Nt\in\{1,-1\}$. If $\Ns=-1$, then
\begin{equation}
 \Nf\Nk\Nt=-\D.\label{fup:fc:eq:remaining}
\end{equation}
The latter divisor/sign equation is unresolved. The former still has
the free-coefficient rank problem. Neither is automatically a parent zero.

\section{Auxiliary completion with a weaker rank condition}
\label{fup:fc:sec:completion}
\begin{theorem}\label{fup:fc:thm:completion}
Let $A\ge2$, $p\ge3$ odd, $c=\ps A p$, $D=\ch A p$ and $R>0$.
If $\gcd(c,p)\mid R$, there exist positive integers $f,S,T,y$ satisfying
the literal auxiliary and strong equations $\Na=1$, $\Ns=\D$.
The construction does not require $p=R$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Set
\[
 f=\ch A{2p},\qquad b_0=\ps A{2p}=2Dc,\qquad S=\D b_0.
\]
Then $f^2-\D b_0^2=1$, so $\Ns=\D$, $c\mid S$,
$f^2\equiv1\pmod c$, and $\gcd(c,f)=1$. Odd $p$ gives odd $c$.
Let $j_0=1$ if $p\equiv3\pmod4$ and $j_0=3$ if $p\equiv1\pmod4$.
Choose a positive solution of
\begin{equation}
 z\equiv j_0p\pmod{8p},\qquad z\equiv R\pmod c.\label{fup:fc:eq:crt}
\end{equation}
The gcd of the moduli is $\gcd(c,p)$, and it divides $j_0p$.
The CRT therefore says that~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:crt} is compatible
\emph{if and only if} $\gcd(c,p)\mid R$. A positive representative
exists whenever it is compatible. It satisfies $z\equiv3\pmod4$.

Write $z=2v+1$, with $v$ odd, and set
\[
 V=\ch S z/S=Q_v(S^2),\qquad y=\ps S z.
\]
By Lemma~\ref{fup:fc:lem:quotient}, $V\equiv-z\equiv-R\pmod c$.
Coefficientwise in the quadratic ring modulo $f$, the Pell pair at
$4p$ is $(-1,0)$ and that at $8p$ is $(1,0)$. Hence
$\ps A z\equiv\ps A{j_0p}\equiv c\pmod f$; for $j_0=3$ use
$\ps A{3p}=(2f+1)c$.
Since $S^2=\D(f^2-1)\equiv1-A^2\pmod f$, the odd quotient identity
gives $V\equiv-c\pmod f$.
It follows that
\begin{equation}
 T=\frac{V+c+Rf^2}{cf}\label{fup:fc:eq:T}
\end{equation}
is a positive integer: its numerator is divisible by each of the
coprime numbers $c,f$. Equation~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:T} is exactly
$V=c(Tf-1)-Rf^2$. Finally the Pell norm at $S$ gives
$S^2V^2-(S^2-1)y^2=1$.
\end{proof}

The inverse~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:inverse} in this construction is $2D/c$.
It is nonintegral because $c>1$ is odd and $\gcd(c,D)=1$.
The gcd hypothesis is necessary and sufficient for this particular CRT
pair; the theorem does \emph{not} claim it is necessary for every possible
auxiliary completion. For example $A=2,p=3$ gives $c=15$; $R=1$
fails that recipe's congruence compatibility modulo 3, without ruling
out other recipes.

When $p\equiv3\pmod4$ and $\gcd(c,p)=1$, a simpler choice is
\begin{equation}
 f=D,\qquad S=\D c,\qquad
 z\equiv p\pmod{4p},\quad z\equiv R\pmod c.\label{fup:fc:eq:simpleaux}
\end{equation}
The pair at $2p$ is $(-1,0)$ modulo $f$, and that at $4p$ is $(1,0)$;
the same proof gives~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:T}, $\Na=1$, $\Ns=\D$.
Here the inverse is simply $1/c$.
These divisions define existential integer witnesses; they do not add
instructions to the saved arithmetic circuit.

\section{The power of 17 inner family}
\label{fup:fc:sec:family}
\begin{theorem}\label{fup:fc:thm:family}
For every $j\ge0$, set
\begin{align}
 u&=17^{2j},&p&=35u,&n&=30u,&R&=60u-1,\\
 X&=2^p,&Y&=2^{7u-1},&E&=XY,&P&=2XY^2+1,\\
 a&=Y(X+1),&A&=a+2,&\D&=A^2-1,&H&=4a+3,\\
 D&=\ch A p,&c&=\ps A p,&\tau&=\ch P n,&k&=2\ps P n.
 \label{fup:fc:eq:17family}
\end{align}
Positive $\eta,\zeta,h,\gamma,f,S,T,y$ exist such that
\[
 k=\eta+\zeta,\quad c=kY+\eta,\quad D=X+ac+\gamma H,
 \quad\Nf=\Nm=\Nk=\Na=1,\quad\Ns=\D.
\]
Here $p\ne R$, and the literal inverse $S/(\D c^2)=1/c$ is
nonintegral. The parameters are not yet a genuine outer completion.
\end{theorem}

\subsection{Exact resonance and strict witness inequalities}
The Pell norms give $\Nm=1$ and, because $P^2-1=4XY^2(XY^2+1)$,
$\Nf=1$. Put $L_0=2XY$ and $M_0=4XY^2$. Exponent arithmetic gives
\begin{equation}
 L_0^{p-1}=2Y M_0^{n-1}.\label{fup:fc:eq:resonance}
\end{equation}
Indeed the exponent equality is equivalent to
$p(p-n)=7u(2n-p)$, and $35\cdot5=7\cdot25$.
Moreover
\begin{align*}
 2A-1&=L_0\left(1+\frac1X+\frac{3}{2XY}\right),&
 2A&=L_0\left(1+\frac1X+\frac2{XY}\right),\\
 2P&=M_0\left(1+\frac1{2XY^2}\right),&2P-1&>M_0.
\end{align*}
Use~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:growth} and~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:resonance}. The numerator
perturbation in $2A-1$ exceeds the denominator perturbation in $2P$,
and $p>n$, giving $c>kY$. For the upper bound let
$\varepsilon=1/X+2/(XY)<2/X$. Since $(p-1)\varepsilon<1/2$,
\[
 (1+\varepsilon)^{p-1}
 \le\frac1{1-(p-1)\varepsilon}<1+2(p-1)\varepsilon.
\]
Here the first inequality follows by bounding binomial coefficients by
powers of $p-1$. Thus
\begin{equation}
 0<\frac ck-Y<\frac{4pY}{X}=70u\,2^{-28u}<1.\label{fup:fc:eq:ratio}
\end{equation}
The last inequality holds for all $u\ge1$, for example from
$u\le2^{u-1}$ and $70<2^{27u+1}$.
Therefore $\eta=c-kY$ and $\zeta=k-\eta$ are positive integers.

As $P\equiv1\pmod E$, $k\equiv2n\pmod E$.
Since $2n=R+1$ and $\ps P n>n$,
\begin{equation}
 h=(k-R-1)/E=(k-2n)/E\in\mathbb Z_{>0},\qquad\Nk=1.\label{fup:fc:eq:index}
\end{equation}
This verifies the retained index equation; no index deletion is involved.

\subsection{The shared main projection}
Define $z_r=\ch A r-a\ps A r$. Then $z_0=1$, $z_1=2$, and
$z_{r+1}=2Az_r-z_{r-1}$. The sequence $2^r$ obeys this recurrence
modulo $H=4A-5$, since its discrepancy is a multiple of $H$.
Hence $z_r\equiv2^r\pmod H$.
For $r\ge1$, positivity and strict increase give
\begin{equation}
 z_{r+1}>(2A-1)z_r.\label{fup:fc:eq:z-growth}
\end{equation}
It follows that $z_p>2^p=X$ and
\begin{equation}
 \gamma=(z_p-X)/H\in\mathbb Z_{>0}.\label{fup:fc:eq:gamma}
\end{equation}
The literal main projection is therefore satisfied.

\subsection{Coprimality at every parameter value}
Since $17^2\equiv1\pmod{24}$, $u\equiv1\pmod{24}$.
The exact expression
\[
 A=2^{42u-1}+2^{7u-1}+2
\]
gives $A\equiv3\pmod5$, $A\equiv0\pmod7$ and $A\equiv0\pmod{17}$.
Modulo 5, the recurrence coefficient $2A$ is 1 and the $\psi$ sequence
has period six $0,1,1,0,-1,-1$. As $p\equiv5\pmod6$, $c\equiv-1\pmod5$.
Modulo 7 and 17 the coefficient is 0; $p\equiv3\pmod4$ gives
$c\equiv-1$ in both. The only possible prime divisors of $p=35\cdot17^{2j}$
are 5, 7 and 17. Therefore
\begin{equation}
 \gcd(c,p)=1\quad\text{for every }j\ge0.\label{fup:fc:eq:coprime}
\end{equation}
The simplified auxiliary extension~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:simpleaux} now completes
the theorem. Since $R-p=25u-1>0$, the ranks differ for every parameter.

\section{Ordinary input loading within the inner family}
\label{fup:fc:sec:loader}
Fix a genuine compiler and a positive ordinary input $x$. Its input
rank $I=\ell x+b$ is odd and at least 3. For any family member with
$p>I$, put $W=2^I$ and define
\begin{align}
 \kappa&=\ps A I,&\mu&=\ch A I,&
 \delta&=\frac{\kappa-I}{\D},\label{fup:fc:eq:delta}\\
 \rho&=\frac{z_I-W}{H},&\sigma&=\gamma-\rho.
 \label{fup:fc:eq:loader}
\end{align}
For odd $I$, $\ps A I\equiv I\pmod\D$: the recurrence has
$A^2\equiv1\pmod\D$, or this follows from the power expansion.
Also $\ps A I>I$, so $\delta$ is a positive integer.
The projection congruence and~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:z-growth} make $\rho$ a positive
integer. For $p>I$, monotonicity gives
\[
 z_p-z_I\ge z_p-z_{p-1}>(2A-2)z_{p-1}>2^p=X.
\]
Therefore
$\sigma=(z_p-z_I-X+2^I)/H>0$.
These definitions simultaneously satisfy
\[
 \kappa=I+\delta\D,\quad\mu=W+a\kappa+\rho H,
 \quad D=X+ac+(\rho+\sigma)H,
 \quad\Ni=1.
\]
This is a shared positive projection, not two unrelated Pell equations.
It uses no acceptance fact, native computation history or mask decoding.
It does not yet realize the register $W=C-Z$ with actual outer witnesses.

\section{The exact remaining compiler interface}
\label{fup:fc:sec:interface}
Fix all six genuine numerals and $x$, and choose $j\ge0$ in
Theorem~\ref{fup:fc:thm:family}. Choose a second integer $N\ge1$ and put
\begin{equation}
 t=dN,\quad q=2^t=B^N,\quad
 3t\le7u-1,\quad p>I.\label{fup:fc:eq:scalerestrictions}
\end{equation}
Then
\[
 w=2^{p-t},\quad s=2^{7u-1-3t},\quad
 J=\frac{q-1}{B-1},\quad Q=q^2-1,\quad
 M=(M_C+qM_F)J,\quad e=p\bmod t.
\]
Both $w,s$ are positive integers, $X=wq$, $Y=sq^3$, and $0\le e<t$.

\begin{theorem}[Intended-factor interface equivalence]\label{fup:fc:thm:interface}
With the choices above and $W=2^I$, the family realizes its prescribed
$R=60u-1$ and $\Nt=1$ with all remaining outer witnesses positive if
and only if the following tests hold:
\begin{align}
 R&\equiv M\pmod Q,\label{fup:fc:eq:test1}\\
 G&=(R-M)/Q,\quad Z=(-G)\bmod q\in\{1,\ldots,q-1\},
 \quad F=(q^2-Z-G)/q\ge1,\label{fup:fc:eq:test2}\\
 F&\equiv(K+2^e)(W+Z)\pmod{q-1},\label{fup:fc:eq:test3}\\
 F+2Z+W+\ell x&<q.\label{fup:fc:eq:test4}
\end{align}
The residue in~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:test2} is the least nonnegative residue and
is required to be nonzero. In particular $F,Z$ are uniquely forced
by $(j,N)$ whenever the tests succeed.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
For necessity, the literal packing equation implies
\eqref{fup:fc:eq:test1} and $G=q^2-qF-Z$. Positivity with $W=C-Z$ gives
$\alpha=q-F-2Z-W-\ell x>0$, hence $1\le Z<q$ and~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:test4}.
Reducing the packing identity modulo $q$ forces exactly the residue and
$F$ in~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:test2}. The equation $\Nt=1$ is equivalent to
\[
 (K+w)(W+Z)-F=(t_{\rm tr}-1)(q-1).
\]
Since $w=2^{p-t}\equiv2^e\pmod{q-1}$, this implies~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:test3}.

Conversely suppose all four tests hold. Set
\begin{equation}
 \alpha=q-F-2Z-W-\ell x,\qquad
 t_{\rm tr}=1+\frac{(K+w)(W+Z)-F}{q-1}.\label{fup:fc:eq:outer-witnesses}
\end{equation}
The first is positive integral by~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:test4}; the second is integral
by~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:test3}. The scale restriction implies $p>2t$, so $w>q$.
Since $0<F<q$ and $W+Z\ge1$, its numerator is positive, proving
$t_{\rm tr}>0$. The definitions give $C=W+Z$, recover the exact packing
$R$, and make $\Nt=1$. Sections~\ref{fup:fc:sec:family}--\ref{fup:fc:sec:loader}
supply all other positive witnesses. Thus the six factors other than
strong are 1, strong is $\D$, and~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:full} vanishes.
\end{proof}

The iff is deliberately limited to this family with its prescribed $R$
and intended transport factor 1. It does not classify accidental product
zeros obtained by changing $R$, abandoning these family coordinates, or
using different divisor values. In a generic zero, $q=B^N$ has not been
proved. Here it is a construction choice.

\subsection{Necessary restrictions that cannot be discarded}
Because $J\mid Q$ and $J\mid M$, packing requires $J\mid R$.
For the power-of-17 family:
\begin{enumerate}
\item $N$ must be odd. If $N$ were even, odd $d$ gives $q\equiv1$,
$J\equiv0$ and $Q\equiv0\pmod3$, forcing $R\equiv0$; but
$R=60u-1\equiv2\pmod3$.
\item For odd $N$, one has $q\equiv2$, $J\equiv1\pmod3$ and $Q\equiv0$.
Thus the genuine shifted masks must satisfy
\begin{equation}
 M_C+2M_F\equiv2\pmod3.\label{fup:fc:eq:maskcondition}
\end{equation}
This has not been shown automatic from the compiler recipe.
\item If $3\mid N$, then $B$ has order 3 modulo 7 and $7\mid J$.
Since $R\equiv4\cdot2^j-1\pmod7$, this requires $j\equiv1\pmod3$.
\end{enumerate}
These restrict this family, not every positive candidate zero.

Strict positivity sharpens the generic packing lower bound. From
$F=q-2Z-W-\ell x-\alpha$ one gets
\[
 G=(2q-1)Z+q(W+\ell x+\alpha)
 \ge q(W+\ell x+3)-1.
\]
Therefore any intended family completion must satisfy
\begin{equation}
 R\ge [q(W+\ell x+3)-1](q^2-1)+M.\label{fup:fc:eq:sharpbound}
\end{equation}
The algebraic lower bound is attained at $Z=\alpha=1$ when the resulting
$F$ is positive; this does not assert transport or packing congruence
solvability there.

For $j=0$, the 17-family has $u=1$, $R=59$ and $Y=2^6$.
The scale bound demands $t\le2$, whereas a genuine compiler requires
$t=dN\ge5$. This smallest exact inner fixture cannot complete on any
genuine positive-input slice. Large exact Pell integers in a fixture are
therefore not evidence of a full compiled witness.

\subsection{Why a prime progression shortcut does not close the problem}
Replacing a power parameter by primes might simplify a local gcd test,
but packing uses the full modulus $Q=q^2-1$, not merely its divisor $J$.
For the 17-family shape the congruence is $60u\equiv M+1\pmod Q$;
after removing $\gcd(60,Q)$, the modulus still has size comparable to
$q^2$. For the original 11-family it is $416u\equiv M+1\pmod Q$.
On an unwrapped transport branch $F=L(W+Z)$ with $L=K+2^e$ fixed,
\[
 R=Q\{q^2-qLW-(1+qL)Z\}+M.
\]
The progression step as $Z$ varies is $Q(1+qL)$, at least of order
$q^3$, within a packing range below $q^4$. No prime-existence argument
at these actual moduli is supplied. This identifies a gap in a shortcut;
it proves neither solvability nor insolvability of the interface.

\section{Conclusions and sharply posed next questions}
\label{fup:fc:sec:conclusions}
The structural theorem excludes all negative auxiliary factors on every
genuine slice, and on a squarefree discriminant excludes both swapped
main/input values $-\D$. It does not normalize all factors on a general
zero. In particular a nonunit auxiliary square $z^2\mid\D$ remains
possible when $\D$ is not squarefree, and~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:remaining} remains
unsettled even on the squarefree slice.

The inner family proves that the intended first/main/index/auxiliary/
strong block, with the exact main projection and compatible positive
ordinary-input loading, does not force $p=R$. The old strong-rank proof
relied on the deleted divisibility $\D c^2\mid S$; importing that proof
after freeing $S$ would assume what is missing.

The principal remaining questions are therefore concrete.
\begin{enumerate}
\item For an actual genuine compiler and a known rejected $x$, do there
exist $j,N$ satisfying~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:scalerestrictions} and all four tests
of Theorem~\ref{fup:fc:thm:interface}? An affirmative result plus the rejection
certificate would give a language counterexample for this candidate.
\item Does the exact compiler recipe force or forbid the shifted-mask
condition~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:maskcondition}? This can eliminate genuine slices
for this family without changing any numeral.
\item Can the residual congruences be excluded for every $j,N$ in the
17-family or the original 11-family? Such an exclusion would be useful,
but it would not establish general soundness.
\item Can~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:remaining} be resolved, and can nonsquarefree
nonunit auxiliary squares and other remaining divisor branches be
controlled without presuming units or $p=R$?
\item If a general soundness proof exists, can it recover a native
ordinary-input witness by a new decoding argument even though the literal
positive integer witness inverse fails?
\end{enumerate}
Theorems about the entire positive zero set, intended-family constructions,
finite checks and inherited compiler facts have different scopes. Keeping
those boundaries explicit is essential. No 83-operation universal
representation and no new false-accepted ordinary input is established.

\fupappendix
\section{The original power of 11 family}
\label{fup:fc:app:11}
The earlier independently reviewed family remains useful as a distinct
construction. For $j\ge0$ let
\[
 u=11^{12j+1},\quad p=247u,\quad n=208u,\quad R=416u-1,
 \quad X=2^p,\quad Y=2^{57u-1}.
\]
Define $E,a,A,\D,H,P,D,c,\tau,k$ as in~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:17family}.
The same proof gives all inner equations and the shared projection.
Here the resonance is
\[
 p(p-n)=57u(2n-p),\qquad247\cdot39=57\cdot169=9633,
\]
and the strict ratio estimate is
\[
 0<c/k-Y<4pY/X=494u\,2^{-190u}<1.
\]
Thus $\eta,\zeta>0$, $h=(k-R-1)/E>0$, and $\Nk=1$.
Projection and input loading are exactly those of
Sections~\ref{fup:fc:sec:family} and~\ref{fup:fc:sec:loader}.

To prove $\gcd(c,p)=1$ for every $j$, the only primes to exclude are
11, 13 and 19. Put
\[
 L=\operatorname{lcm}(12,18,13(13^2-1),19(19^2-1))=622440.
\]
Integer arithmetic gives $11^{12}\equiv1\pmod L$, so $u\equiv11\pmod L$.
For primes 13 and 19, this fixes $A$ modulo the prime through the binary
exponents and fixes $p$ modulo the order bound
$|\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb F_\ell)|=\ell(\ell^2-1)$ for the determinant-one
Pell companion matrix. At $u=11$ the exact residues are
\[
 (A,c)\equiv(4,1)\pmod{13},\qquad(A,c)\equiv(11,18)\pmod{19}.
\]
They persist at every $j$.
Modulo 11, $u\equiv1\pmod{10}$ gives $A\equiv8$ and $\D\equiv8$.
In the quadratic ring in characteristic 11, Frobenius gives
\[
 \ps A{11r}\equiv\D^5\ps A r=-\ps A r\pmod{11}.
\]
Since $\ps8{247}\equiv10$ and $12j+1$ is odd, $c\equiv1\pmod{11}$.
This proves the universal gcd claim. Theorem~\ref{fup:fc:thm:completion} supplies
the auxiliary extension at $R=416u-1$ with inverse $2D/c$ nonintegral.

The exact interface is Theorem~\ref{fup:fc:thm:interface} with
$3dN\le57u-1$ and these new $p,R$. Its unavoidable restrictions include
$\gcd(N,6)=1$. Indeed $d$ is an odd power of 5 with $5\mid d$, so
$B\equiv-1\pmod{11}$; even $N$ would give $11\mid J$ but
$R\equiv-1\pmod{11}$. Also $B$ has order 3 modulo 7, so $3\mid N$
would give $7\mid J$, while $u\equiv4$ and $R\equiv4\pmod7$.

The smallest materialized fixture is $u=11$, $p=2717$, $n=2288$,
$R=4575$, with $Y$ exponent 626. Its main coefficient has 9,082,305
bits, but it still cannot complete on any genuine positive-input slice:
$I\ge15$ gives $W\ge32768$, and positivity forces $q>W$.
Even the relaxed values $q\ge32769$, $\ell x\ge10$, $M\ge1$ in
\eqref{fup:fc:eq:sharpbound} force $R>10^{18}$.
The proof and independent review of this original family are preserved
in the reproducibility packet. No extra offset family is used here.
