\fupstart{45}{Part VIII: Report 45}
\fupsourcehead{Report 45. Full positive collapse of the auxiliary square product 82 candidate}
\noindent\wtag\ Source~19 of this report, manuscript~06 of batch~88: \emph{Research Report 45}, author line ``Research Report 45'', dated 4 October 2026; archive \rw{Full_Positive_Collapse_of_the_Square_Product_82_Candidate_Package.zip} (arrival commit \texttt{c5612efa1}, placed by \texttt{992aaafb3}); pin \texttt{6ce2dcaaf} (the candidate and its review); its audit's source observation was made at repository HEAD \texttt{6bf7f30d0}. Its sections, statements and equations are numbered 45.$k$ here.
\begin{sourceabstract}
For every fixed genuine numeral tuple emitted by the inherited compiler
of the auxiliary square/product 82-operation candidate, and every
positive ordinary input, we construct infinitely many strictly positive
18-witness tuples that zero its complete polynomial. The actual packed
index is controlled by a power-of-five Chinese remainder construction.
The shared input projections and retained first-index factor are restored.
When the fixed mask residue is one modulo three, the index and transport
factors are both $-1$, so their signs cancel. A direct Pell extension at
the actual packed index completes the auxiliary block with even main
coordinate $c$, and its supplied auxiliary coordinate is nonsquare.
Specialization to the genuine empty-set compiler proves failure of the
inherited ordinary-input representation. A different numeral compiler is
not ruled out. The validated universal baseline remains
$84=47\mathrm M+37\mathrm A$, with 18 positive witnesses and degree 187.
The full proof, explicit source pins, research and independent
audit packets, and bounded path-preserving replay are supplied. This is
neither a smaller universal bound nor a priority claim.
\end{sourceabstract}
\begin{writenote}
\emph{The same theorem twice.} Theorem~\ref{fup:sp:thm:main} and Corollary~\ref{fup:sp:cor:language} state the result of the research tree's \rw{complete82_all_input_outer_collapse.md} (commit \texttt{dc03992d9}, 3 October 2026, 18:26~PDT): the square/product 82 candidate has infinitely many full positive zeros at every positive input on every inherited compiler slice, the empty-set compiler included. The two proofs are different constructions. The tree's family keeps the original positive outer slack and makes all five retained factors $+1$. Report~45 uses a power-of-five radix and Chinese remaindering with the two branches $(p,n)=(4u,3u)$ and $(20v,14v)$; when the mask residue $m_3$ is~1 its index and transport factors are \emph{both} $-1$; its main coordinate $c$ is even, so it needs a new auxiliary completion with $i=1$, $\Fa=\Delta c^4+1$ and the actual packed index $R$ as the auxiliary Pell index (Lemma~\ref{fup:sp:lem:aux}). Report~45 is therefore printed as a second, independent route to the tree's theorem. Its audit observation (Appendix~\ref{fup:sp:app:pins}) is dated 00:44~UTC on 4 October, 17:44~PDT on 3 October, about forty minutes before \texttt{dc03992d9}; Report~45 does not cite the tree's note, and neither text claims priority. The programme's review (\rw{review_incoming_arithmetic_reports_39_46.md}, commit \texttt{5d8889017}) read the complete counterfamily and calls it ``a different construction of full false-input zeros for the exact 82-row source already refuted by the WIP all-input proof''; its even main rank and the matching negative signs are ``useful new structure'', not a normalization theorem for every zero and not a saving in a valid universal polynomial. The review cross-read the branch CRT bounds and positivity estimates algebraically and \emph{did not replay the archived finite receipts}.

\emph{A superseded sentence (dated note, batch~88).} Section~\ref{fup:sp:sec:scope} and question~3 of Section~\ref{fup:sp:sec:questions} say that Report~43's ordinary-input language question remains open. It was answered by the tree's \rw{free_coefficient83_prime_outer_collapse.md} (commit \texttt{8cf6239b6}): the free-coefficient candidate is refuted on every input (note at the start of Part~\ref{fup:part:cand}). The source's statement that its own theorem settles neither direction for that candidate remains true.

\emph{A reconstructed proof.} A workspace replacement interrupted the preparation of this report (Appendix~\ref{fup:sp:app:pins}). The research proof (shipped as \rw{19-square-product82-counterfamily-COUNTERFAMILY.md}) and the audit packet were recovered from retained records and checked again; \rw{data/19-square-product82-RECOVERY.json} and \rw{data/19-square-product82-counterfamily-HISTORICAL_LOST_RELEASE.json} separate byte-identical recoveries from reconstructed files. The reconstructed proof is authenticated by fresh hashes and by the fresh independent audit (\rw{19-square-product82-audit-audit.md}), not by the historical hashes of the lost release, which no longer match by design.

\emph{Credits and overlaps.} The candidate, its gate ledger, its degree 185, the forward identity~\eqref{fup:sp:eq:forward}, the conditional odd-$c$ auxiliary extension and the nonsquare zeros at accepted inputs are the tree's (commit \texttt{6ce2dcaaf}), as the source's Attribution section says; Lemma~\ref{fup:sp:lem:aux} extends that extension to even $c$. Lemma~\ref{fup:sp:lem:pellbounds} is the Pell estimate~\eqref{fup:fd:eq:pellbounds} of Report~41 and~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:growth} of Report~43; Lemma~\ref{fup:sp:lem:projection} is Lemma~\ref{fup:fd:lem:g} of Report~41, and through it Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:projection} of Report~33; the odd polynomials of~\eqref{fup:sp:eq:oddpoly} are Lemma~\ref{fup:ir:lem:oddquotient}; the strict ratio window of Lemma~\ref{fup:sp:lem:ratio} uses the method of Section~\ref{fup:fd:sec:dyadic}. Report~45 cites Report~41 (``first-index-deleted circuit'', Part~\ref{fup:part:fd}) but none of these lemmas. Report~46 (below) imports Report~45's whole construction.

Its Appendix~\ref{fup:sp:app:pins} read the pin table from the file \rw{source_pins.tex} and Appendix~\ref{fup:sp:app:rows} the row list from \rw{source_rows.txt}; both are shipped unchanged as \rw{data/19-square-product82-source_pins.tex} and \rw{data/19-square-product82-source_rows.txt}, and their contents are printed in place here, so that this article builds on its own. The source's files are shipped with the prefix \texttt{19-square-product82-}; Report~46 shipped a byte copy of the whole Report~45 release, which is not shipped again.

\section{The theorem and its scope}
\label{fup:sp:sec:scope}
\subsection{The exact candidate}
The object is \code{complete82_auxiliary_square_product_chart} in
Vladimir Reshetnikov's \emph{ProveIt} repository, frozen at commit
\begin{center}\small\code{6ce2dcaaf49d28de43ce9396a094643308fb0595}.\end{center}
Its source and independent repository review establish a complete
$82=45\mathrm M+37\mathrm A$ arithmetic circuit, 18 strictly positive
supplied witnesses, and uniform exact degree 185
\cite{fup:sp:candidate,fup:sp:review}. The two freed coordinates replace the parent
products $f^2$ and $Tf$. The first-index equation remains in the circuit.

A \emph{genuine inherited compiler tuple} is a fixed numeral tuple
produced by the unchanged fixed-program compiler inherited through the
validated 84-operation parent, including its half-binomial mask
convention. It is not an arbitrary numerical tuple satisfying some
necessary mask tests. Write $P_{82,\mathcal C}$ for the polynomial
specialized to such a fixed compiler tuple $\mathcal C$.

\begin{writenote}
\emph{Second route.} The research tree proved the same statement in \rw{complete82_all_input_outer_collapse.md} (commit \texttt{dc03992d9}) by a different construction; the note at the start of this source compares the two.
\end{writenote}
\begin{theorem}[Full positive counterfamily]
\label{fup:sp:thm:main}
For every genuine inherited compiler tuple $\mathcal C$ and every
$x\in\pos$, there are infinitely many distinct
$\boldsymbol\xi\in\pos^{18}$ such that
\[
P_{82,\mathcal C}(x,\boldsymbol\xi)=0.
\]
All six fixed numeral ports and the ordinary input are unchanged.
The constructed tuples have unbounded repunit coordinate $J$. Each has
even main Pell coordinate $c$, main Pell index $p\ne R$ for the actual
packed index $R$, and nonsquare supplied auxiliary coordinate $\Fa$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{corollary}[Inherited language failure]
\label{fup:sp:cor:language}
Every genuine inherited compiler slice of this candidate accepts all
positive inputs. In particular, the genuine compiler of the empty
computably enumerable set gives a false positive at every positive
input. Thus the candidate fails to preserve the inherited universal
representation.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
The theorem gives acceptance of every $x\in\pos$. The validated parent
represents the empty set with its genuine compiler tuple, so it has no
positive witnesses at any positive input. The candidate uses those
same fixed numerals and has positive witnesses at each such input.
\end{proof}

The empty-set specialization does not require materializing its
astronomically large fixed constants. It follows from the theorem's
quantifiers. This proves more than failure of a particular inverse on
witnesses at an already accepted input. It does not prove that the same
polynomial could never be universal under some different, unprovided
numeral compiler or another allowed numeral domain. Indeed the arithmetic
construction works for every fixed tuple satisfying the explicit
consequences in Section~\ref{fup:sp:sec:recipe}; it does not classify tuples
outside those hypotheses.

\subsection{The neighboring reports remain distinct}
Report 41 concerns a first-index-deleted circuit and its failures.
Here the index factor, the strictly positive quotient $h$, and the
coefficient $S=i\D c^2$ are retained and verified. Report 43 concerns
the separate free-coefficient 83-operation candidate; its ordinary-input
language question remains open. Neither its soundness nor its failure
follows from the present theorem.

The validated universal baseline remains
$84=47\mathrm M+37\mathrm A$, with 18 positive witnesses and uniform
exact degree 187 \cite{fup:sp:baseline}. These are achieved counts, not lower
bounds on all arithmetic circuits. The failed candidate still has
formal degree 185 and 82 paid operations; those source facts do not make
it a universal representation.

\subsection{What was previously established and what is added}
The repository introduced the candidate and proved its exact forward
identity, a conditional auxiliary extension for odd $c$, and nonsquare
candidate zeros at already accepted parent inputs. Its independent
review explicitly left ordinary-input soundness unresolved. The present
counterfamily completes the genuine outer packing, input and transport
conditions for every positive input and every inherited compiler,
including all three fixed-mask residues modulo three. The repository's
earlier claims remain credited within their stated scope.

This report contains the entire quantified proof. Exponentiation,
division by proved divisors, and Pell powering are definitions of
existential witnesses, not additional free circuit operations. Full
enormous witnesses need not be printed. Source Python and saved
arithmetic rows are treated only as inert data; neither is run or
interpreted as a schedule. The reproducible checks are bounded
corroboration of the proof, not a finite-search substitute for it.

\section{The complete source and its retained factors}
\label{fup:sp:sec:source}
\subsection{Fixed ports and all witness coordinates}
The six fixed ports, in source order, are
\[
\code{Bm1},\ \code{Kconstant},\ \code{twice_cell_bits},\
\code{inner_bits},\ \code{MC},\ \code{MF},
\]
with respective values $B-1,K,\ell,b,M_C,M_F$. The ordinary input
is $x$. The 18 supplied witness values, in source order, will be
\begin{align*}
&J,F,\alpha,\ttt,\Fa,h,i,\Ua,s,w,\\
&\tau,\eta,\zeta,\yaux,Z,\delta,\rho,\sigma.
\end{align*}
Their literal source names are
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{llllll}
\code{Jrep}&\code{F}&\code{alpha}&\code{transport_quotient}&\code{L16}&\code{h}\\
\code{i}&\code{auxiliary_Tf}&\code{s}&\code{w}&\code{tau_root}&\code{eta}\\
\code{zeta}&\code{y_aux}&\code{Z}&\code{delta}&\code{rho}&\code{sigma}
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The packing witness $F$ is distinct from $\Fa=\code{L16}$.
The source register \code{A} is $\D$, not the Pell parameter $A$ below.
The source register \code{R14} is the main root $D$;
\code{R10a} is $c$, and \code{r_lhs} is the actual packed $R$.

The exact paid definitions are
\begin{align}
q&=(B-1)J+1,&X&=wq,&Y&=sq^3,&E&=XY,\label{fup:sp:eq:XY}\\
a_0&=Y(X+1),&A&=a_0+2,&H&=4a_0+3,&\D&=A^2-1,\label{fup:sp:eq:params}\\
k&=\eta+\zeta,&c&=kY+\eta,&
D&=X+a_0c+(\rho+\sigma)H.\label{fup:sp:eq:main}
\end{align}
The source computes $\D=a_0^2+H$, equal to $(a_0+2)^2-1$.
The packing and input relations are
\begin{align}
C&=q-F-Z-\alpha-\ell x,&W&=C-Z,\label{fup:sp:eq:C}\\
I&=\ell x+b,&\kappa&=I+\delta\D,&\mu&=W+a_0\kappa+\rho H,\label{fup:sp:eq:input}\\
G&=q^2-qF-Z,&Q&=q^2-1,&M&=(M_C+qM_F)J,\label{fup:sp:eq:packing}\\
R&=GQ+M.\label{fup:sp:eq:R}
\end{align}
In particular $R$ is not a freely supplied target. It must be restored
by its actual paid expression.

\subsection{The full output identity}
The five retained factors are
\begin{align}
\Nf&=\tau^2-XY^2(XY^2+1)k^2,\label{fup:sp:eq:first}\\
\Nm&=D^2-\D c^2,\\
\Ni&=\mu^2-\D\kappa^2,\\
\Nk&=k-hE-R,\label{fup:sp:eq:index}\\
\Nt&=(K+w)C+q-F-\ttt(q-1).\label{fup:sp:eq:transport}
\end{align}
Write $P_5=\Nf\Nm\Ni\Nk\Nt$. The actual auxiliary expressions are
\begin{align}
S&=i\D c^2,&V&=c(\Ua-1)-R\Fa,\label{fup:sp:eq:aux}\\
\Na&=S^2V^2-(S^2-1)\yaux^2,&\Ns&=\D\Fa-S^2.\label{fup:sp:eq:auxnorm}
\end{align}
The complete output is $P_5\Na\Ns-\D$. With the proof expression
$Q_s=\Fa-\D i^2c^4$, this gives the all-value identity
\begin{equation}
\boxed{P_{82}=\D\bigl(P_5\Na Q_s-1\bigr).}
\label{fup:sp:eq:final}
\end{equation}
Indeed $\Ns=\D Q_s$ identically. No zero-only substitution or division
is used. A product equation is never silently replaced by the assertion
that each of its factors equals $+1$.

The core costs $39\mathrm M+36\mathrm A=75$ operations; the full
finalizer costs $6\mathrm M+1\mathrm A=7$. All 82 rows and all 25
supplied ports are live in the repository review. The complete inert
row contract is reproduced in Appendix~\ref{fup:sp:app:rows}. The gate ledger,
formal degree, and original forward identity are source/review results,
not conclusions inferred from numerical tests in this report.

\subsection{The forward map and its inverse restriction}
The removed parent rows are $\code{L16}=f\cdot f$ and
$\code{auxiliary_Tf}=T\cdot f$. All other rows are retained. Thus
\begin{equation}
P_{82}(\Fa=f^2,\Ua=Tf)=P_{84}
\label{fup:sp:eq:forward}
\end{equation}
over every commutative ring \cite{fup:sp:candidate,fup:sp:review}.
On positive integer coordinates, the literal inverse requires $\Fa$
to be a square and its positive square root to divide $\Ua$. The
candidate supplies these coordinates without enforcing those
conditions. The later nonsquare interval shows that every tuple in
our family falls outside that literal image, while the all-input
theorem separately establishes the language failure.

\section{The inherited recipe consequences}
\label{fup:sp:sec:recipe}
Fix a genuine compiler once and for all. Its recipe gives
\begin{equation}
B=2^d,\quad d=5^a\ (a\ge1),\quad \ell=2d,\quad
b\in\{5,25,125,\ldots\},\quad K>0.
\label{fup:sp:eq:recipe1}
\end{equation}
The inherited radix bound gives $2^b\ge16$; together with the
power-of-five choice it forces $b\ge5$. Only odd $b\ge3$ is
needed below. The symbol $a$ denotes the exponent in $d=5^a$;
$a_0$ denotes the paid Pell expression in~\eqref{fup:sp:eq:params}.
For the native second mask $M_F^0$ after the half-binomial modification,
\begin{equation}
M_F=M_F^0+B-1,\qquad
0<M_C,M_F^0<B-1,\qquad M_C\equiv2\pmod4.
\label{fup:sp:eq:recipe2}
\end{equation}
Here $M_F^0$ is not a supplied port. The source uses the shifted $M_F$,
which can exceed $B$.

The residue follows directly from the authenticated recipes. The
baseline native first mask is
\[
M_C^{\rm old}=B-1-\sum_{e\in\mathcal E,\ e\ne1}(2^b)^e.
\]
The Start exponent $e=0$ occurs and contributes $1$. All other
subtracted powers are zero modulo four, while $B-1\equiv3\pmod4$.
Thus $M_C^{\rm old}\equiv2\pmod4$. The half-binomial compiler subtracts
$2(2^b)^{e_*}$ at a nonzero ignored dummy exponent, which leaves that
residue unchanged. It also modifies the native second mask and then
supplies its shifted value as in~\eqref{fup:sp:eq:recipe2}
\cite{fup:sp:recipe76,fup:sp:recipe75}. The baseline chooses $b$ and the cell-block
length to be powers of five, so their product $d$ is a power of five,
and the inherited input width is $\ell=2d$.

Every other genuine compiler restriction stays in force because none
of the six fixed numerals is changed. Only
\eqref{fup:sp:eq:recipe1}--\eqref{fup:sp:eq:recipe2} are used in the construction.
This is why the proof applies to every genuine compiler while the
bounded diagnostic masks need not themselves encode genuine programs.

\section{A power of five radix and the two CRT constructions}
\label{fup:sp:sec:crt}
Fix any $x\in\pos$, and set $I=\ell x+b$ and $W=2^I$.
The number $I$ is odd and at least three. Choose any integer $r\ge a$
large enough that
\begin{equation}
t=5^r\ge4\max\{I,\operatorname{bitlength}(K),61,16\}.
\label{fup:sp:eq:tsize}
\end{equation}
Here $\operatorname{bitlength}(K)=\lfloor\log_2K\rfloor+1$.
In particular $t\ge625$. Put
\begin{equation}
q=2^t,\quad N=5^{r-a},\quad J=\frac{q-1}{B-1},\quad
Q=q^2-1,\quad M=(M_C+qM_F)J.
\label{fup:sp:eq:radix}
\end{equation}
Then $q=B^N$ and $J=1+B+\cdots+B^{N-1}\in\pos$. The length $N$ is odd.

\begin{lemma}[Radix and mask residues]
\label{fup:sp:lem:residues}
Let $m_3\in\{0,1,2\}$ be the residue of $M_C+2M_F$ modulo three.
Then
\begin{align}
&q\equiv2\pmod5,\quad Q\equiv3\pmod5,\quad
q\in\{2,4\}\pmod7,\quad Q\in\{3,1\}\pmod7,\label{fup:sp:eq:57}\\
&q\equiv2\pmod3,\quad J\equiv1\pmod3,\quad M\equiv m_3\pmod3,\label{fup:sp:eq:3}\\
&q\equiv0\pmod4,\quad J\equiv1\pmod4,\quad M\equiv2\pmod4.\label{fup:sp:eq:4}
\end{align}
Thus $Q$ is a unit modulo five and seven.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Since $t=5^r\equiv1\pmod4$, $2^t\equiv2\pmod5$.
Since $t$ is one or two modulo three, $2^t$ is two or four modulo
seven. The numbers $t,d,N$ are odd, so $q,B\equiv-1\pmod3$;
the odd-length alternating repunit sum is one modulo three.
Since $B,q$ are divisible by four, the repunit is one modulo four,
and the last congruence uses $M_C\equiv2\pmod4$.
\end{proof}

The variable $Z$ will control the actual $R$ through an affine
expression. The CRT conditions simultaneously arrange $R\equiv3\pmod4$,
a transport residue $p\equiv e\pmod t$, and exact matching
$2n=R+\varepsilon$.

\subsection{The nonzero residues modulo three}
\label{fup:sp:sec:branchA}
If $m_3=2$ or $m_3=1$, put
\[
\varepsilon=\begin{cases}1,&m_3=2,\\-1,&m_3=1,\end{cases}
\qquad
e=\begin{cases}8,&K\not\equiv1\pmod5,\\9,&K\equiv1\pmod5.\end{cases}
\]
Define
\begin{equation}
L=K+2^e,\quad \beta=Q(1+qL),\quad
A_*=Q\bigl(q^2-q(LW+1-\varepsilon)\bigr)+M.
\label{fup:sp:eq:AstarA}
\end{equation}
For $e=8$, $1+qL\equiv3+2K\pmod5$, which vanishes only at $K\equiv1$.
In that exceptional case $e=9$ gives $1+qL\equiv2\pmod5$.
Therefore $\beta$ is a unit modulo $t=5^r$.

Choose the unique positive representative $Z\le4t$ satisfying
\begin{equation}
Z\equiv1\pmod4,\qquad
A_*-\beta Z\equiv3e\,2^{-1}-\varepsilon\pmod t.
\label{fup:sp:eq:crtA}
\end{equation}
The notation $2^{-1}$ denotes the modular inverse of two, not a
nonintegral coordinate. The second condition fixes one class modulo
$t$, and $\gcd(4,t)=1$ permits CRT. Define
\begin{align}
F&=L(W+Z)+1-\varepsilon,\label{fup:sp:eq:F}\\
\alpha&=q-F-2Z-W-\ell x,\label{fup:sp:eq:alpha}\\
R&=(q^2-qF-Z)Q+M=A_*-\beta Z.\label{fup:sp:eq:affineR}
\end{align}
Uniform positivity will be proved below. These definitions restore
the source's actual $C=W+Z$ and actual packed $R$.

Since $Z\equiv1\pmod4$, Lemma~\ref{fup:sp:lem:residues} gives
$R\equiv3\pmod4$. Also $Q\equiv0\pmod3$, so $R\equiv m_3\pmod3$.
Consequently
\begin{equation}
u=\frac{R+\varepsilon}{6},\quad
p=4u,\quad n=3u,\quad y=2u-1
\label{fup:sp:eq:familyA}
\end{equation}
are integers. For $\varepsilon=1$, $R\equiv11\pmod{12}$ and $u$ is
even; for $\varepsilon=-1$, $R\equiv7\pmod{12}$ and $u$ is odd.
Multiplying the second congruence in~\eqref{fup:sp:eq:crtA} by $2/3$
in $\mathbb Z/t\mathbb Z$ gives
\begin{equation}
p\equiv e\pmod t,\qquad 2n=R+\varepsilon.
\label{fup:sp:eq:coupleA}
\end{equation}
Both two and three are units modulo $t$. The actual integer $p$
is defined by~\eqref{fup:sp:eq:familyA}; modular division introduces no
nonintegral witness.

\subsection{The zero residue modulo three}
\label{fup:sp:sec:branchB}
If $m_3=0$, choose $h_0\in\{1,\ldots,12\}$ such that, with $e=5h_0$,
\begin{equation}
1+q(K+2^e)\not\equiv0\pmod5,\qquad
1+q(K+2^e)\not\equiv0\pmod7.
\label{fup:sp:eq:eB}
\end{equation}
Modulo five, $2^{5h_0}\equiv2^{h_0}$ takes four distinct values as
$h_0$ runs over its classes modulo four; at most one is forbidden.
Modulo seven, $2^{5h_0}\equiv2^{2h_0}$ takes three distinct values
over classes modulo three; at most one is forbidden. CRT modulo
twelve therefore leaves at least $(4-1)(3-1)=6$ valid choices.
In particular $5\le e\le60$.

Put $\varepsilon=1$ and
\begin{equation}
L=K+2^e,\quad\beta=Q(1+qL),\quad
A_*=Q(q^2-qLW)+M,\quad T=t/5.
\label{fup:sp:eq:AstarB}
\end{equation}
The coefficient $\beta$ is invertible modulo seven and $T$.
The moduli $4,7,T$ are pairwise coprime, so choose the unique positive
representative $Z\le28T$ with
\begin{equation}
\begin{split}
Z&\equiv1\pmod4,\\
A_*-\beta Z&\equiv-1\pmod7,\\
A_*-\beta Z&\equiv7h_0-1\pmod T.
\end{split}
\label{fup:sp:eq:crtB}
\end{equation}
Define $F,\alpha,R$ by~\eqref{fup:sp:eq:F}--\eqref{fup:sp:eq:affineR}, with
$\varepsilon=1$. Then $R\equiv-1\pmod{28}$, so
\begin{equation}
v=\frac{R+1}{28},\quad p=20v,\quad n=14v,\quad y=15v-1
\label{fup:sp:eq:familyB}
\end{equation}
are integers. The last congruence yields $28v\equiv7h_0\pmod T$.
Because $T$ is a power of five, division by seven is legitimate and
gives $4v\equiv h_0\pmod T$. Multiplication by five now gives
\begin{equation}
p\equiv5h_0=e\pmod t,\qquad 2n=R+1=R+\varepsilon.
\label{fup:sp:eq:coupleB}
\end{equation}
The residue $R\equiv0\pmod3$ only implies $v\equiv1\pmod3$.
There is no attempted inversion modulo three in this branch.
In both branches $y$ denotes the exponent for $Y=2^y$ and is
distinct from the auxiliary witness $\yaux$.

\section{Uniform positivity and the real packed index interval}
\label{fup:sp:sec:bounds}
\begin{lemma}[Outer positivity and size margins]
\label{fup:sp:lem:bounds}
Both CRT constructions give positive integers $J,F,Z,\alpha$, with
\begin{equation}
1\le Z\le6t,\qquad F+Z<q,\qquad
(2q-1)(q^2-1)<R<q^4-q^3.
\label{fup:sp:eq:outerbounds}
\end{equation}
Also $R>q^3>100t$, and
\begin{equation}
p>I,\qquad p>t+e,\qquad y\ge3t.
\label{fup:sp:eq:thresholds}
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The CRT bounds are $Z\le4t$ or $Z\le28t/5$, both at most $6t$.
Put $U=2^{t/4}$, a positive real used only for estimates.
The sizing condition and $e\le60$ give $W,K,2^e\le U$.
For $t\ge625$, $13t<U$: the starting value is verified by
$(13\cdot625)^4<2^{625}$, and $2^{t/4}/t$ increases thereafter
because its logarithmic derivative is $(\log2)/4-1/t>0$.
Thus $W+Z<2U$, $L\le2U$, and
\[
0<F\le4U^2+2,\qquad
2Z+W+\ell x\le13t+U<2U.
\]
As $U>4$, $4U^2+2+2U<U^4=q$. Equation~\eqref{fup:sp:eq:alpha} proves
$\alpha>0$ and also $F+Z<q$. Integrality gives $F+Z\le q-1$.

The actual $G=q^2-qF-Z$ obeys
\begin{equation}
2q-1\le G\le q^2-q-1.
\label{fup:sp:eq:Gbounds}
\end{equation}
For the lower bound, $F\le q-1-Z$ implies
$G\ge q+(q-1)Z\ge2q-1$; the upper bound uses $F,Z\ge1$.
The shifted masks satisfy
\begin{equation}
0<M<(q-1)(1+2q),
\label{fup:sp:eq:Mbounds}
\end{equation}
because $M_C<B-1$, $M_F<2(B-1)$, and $J=(q-1)/(B-1)$.
Thus the strict lower $R$ bound follows, and the upper endpoint is
\[
(q^2-q-1)(q^2-1)+(q-1)(1+2q)=q^4-q^3.
\]
For $q\ge3$, $(2q-1)(q^2-1)>q^3$ since the difference is
$q^3-q^2-2q+1>0$. Hence $R>2^{3t}>100t$.

In the first branch $u=(R+\varepsilon)/6>R/7>14t$;
in the second $v=(R+1)/28>R/28>3t$.
Thus $u\ge3$ or $v\ge2$. The first branch gives $p>56t$
and $y>28t-1$; the second gives $p>60t$ and $y>45t-1$.
Using $I\le t/4$ and $e\le60<t$ proves~\eqref{fup:sp:eq:thresholds}.
\end{proof}

Define the supplied scales
\begin{equation}
w=2^{p-t},\qquad s=2^{y-3t}.
\label{fup:sp:eq:scales}
\end{equation}
They are positive integers, and the actual source computes
\begin{equation}
X=wq=2^p,\qquad Y=sq^3=2^y.
\label{fup:sp:eq:dyadic}
\end{equation}
There is no circular choice: first fix the compiler and input, then
$t,q,J$, then CRT data $Z,F,\alpha,R$, then $p,n,y$, and finally
the scales and Pell coordinates.

\section{Pell coordinates and exact resonance}
\label{fup:sp:sec:pell}
For any integer $A\ge2$, define $\ch A j,\ps A j$ for $j\ge0$ by
\begin{equation}
(A+\sqrt{A^2-1})^j=\ch A j+\ps A j\sqrt{A^2-1}.
\label{fup:sp:eq:pell-def}
\end{equation}
Equivalently these are coordinates in the quadratic ring. They are
integers, positive for $j\ge1$, and satisfy
\begin{align}
\ch A j^2-(A^2-1)\ps A j^2&=1,\label{fup:sp:eq:pellnorm}\\
\ps A0=0,\quad \ps A1&=1,\quad
\ps A{j+2}=2A\ps A{j+1}-\ps A j.\label{fup:sp:eq:pellrec}
\end{align}

\begin{lemma}[Pell growth bounds]
\label{fup:sp:lem:pellbounds}
The sequence $\ps A j$ is strictly increasing for $j\ge0$.
For $j\ge3$,
\begin{equation}
(2A-1)^{j-1}\le\ps A j<(2A)^{j-1}.
\label{fup:sp:eq:pellbounds}
\end{equation}
Also $\ps A j>j$ for $j\ge2$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The initial values $0,1,2A$ increase. If the two latest positive
terms increase, the recurrence gives
$\ps A{j+1}>(2A-1)\ps A j>\ps A j$ for $j\ge2$.
Iteration from $\ps A1=1$ proves the lower bound.
The upper inequality $\ps A{j+1}<2A\ps A j$ becomes strict when
the subtracted term is positive, giving the stated strict bound for
$j\ge3$. The lower estimate and the initial case $\ps A2=2A>2$
give $\ps A j>j$.
\end{proof}

Using the actual $X,Y$ from~\eqref{fup:sp:eq:dyadic}, define
\begin{align}
a_0&=Y(X+1),&A&=a_0+2,&\D&=A^2-1,&H&=4a_0+3,\\
P&=2XY^2+1,&D&=\ch A p,&c&=\ps A p,\\
\tau&=\ch P n,&k&=2\ps P n,&E&=XY.
\label{fup:sp:eq:pellchoices}
\end{align}
Here $P$ is a Pell parameter, distinct from $P_{82}$. The norm
identities give exactly
\begin{equation}
D^2-\D c^2=1,\qquad
\tau^2-XY^2(XY^2+1)k^2=1,
\label{fup:sp:eq:norms}
\end{equation}
since $P^2-1=4XY^2(XY^2+1)$ and $k=2\ps P n$.

Put $L_0=2XY$ and $M_0=4XY^2$. Both families satisfy the exact resonance
\begin{equation}
L_0^{p-1}=2Y M_0^{n-1}.
\label{fup:sp:eq:resonance}
\end{equation}
Taking exponents of two shows that this is equivalent to
\[
p(p-n)=(y+1)(2n-p).
\]
For~\eqref{fup:sp:eq:familyA}, both sides are $4u^2$; for
\eqref{fup:sp:eq:familyB}, both sides are $120v^2$.
Thus this is an exact integer identity rather than an asymptotic
estimate. In both families $p>n$.

\section{The strict positive ratio and the retained index factor}
\label{fup:sp:sec:ratio}
\begin{lemma}[A strict one-unit ratio window]
\label{fup:sp:lem:ratio}
The coordinates in~\eqref{fup:sp:eq:pellchoices} satisfy
\begin{equation}
0<\frac ck-Y<1.
\label{fup:sp:eq:ratio}
\end{equation}
Thus $\eta=c-kY$ and $\zeta=k-\eta$ are positive integers and restore
$k=\eta+\zeta$, $c=kY+\eta$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The exact identities
\begin{align*}
2A-1&=L_0\left(1+\frac1X+\frac3{2XY}\right),\\
2A&=L_0\left(1+\frac1X+\frac2{XY}\right),\\
2P&=M_0\left(1+\frac1{2XY^2}\right),\qquad 2P-1>M_0
\end{align*}
and Lemma~\ref{fup:sp:lem:pellbounds}, together with~\eqref{fup:sp:eq:resonance},
give
\[
\frac c{kY}>
\frac{(1+1/X+3/(2XY))^{p-1}}
     {(1+1/(2XY^2))^{n-1}}>1.
\]
The numerator base is larger, both bases exceed one, and $p>n$.

For the upper bound put $z=1/X+2/(XY)<2/X$, using $Y>2$.
The upper Pell estimate for $c$ and lower estimate for $\ps P n$
give $c/(kY)<(1+z)^{p-1}$. Since $p\ge12$,
$(p-1)z<2p/2^p<1/2$. For an integer $j\ge1$ and $0<jz<1/2$,
the bound $\binom jk\le j^k$ gives
\[
(1+z)^j\le\sum_{k\ge0}(jz)^k
=\frac1{1-jz}<1+2jz.
\]
Consequently
\begin{equation}
0<\frac ck-Y<\frac{4pY}{X}.
\label{fup:sp:eq:ratiogap}
\end{equation}
The right side is $8u\,2^{-2u}<1$ in the first branch for $u\ge3$,
and $40v\,2^{-5v}<1$ in the second for $v\ge2$.
The starting comparisons are $24<64$ and $80<1024$; successive-term
ratios are $(u+1)/(4u)<1$ and $(v+1)/(32v)<1$.
This proves~\eqref{fup:sp:eq:ratio}. Multiplying by $k>0$ gives
$0<\eta<k$, and hence $\zeta=k-\eta>0$.
\end{proof}

As $P=2XY^2+1\equiv1\pmod E$, the recurrence for $\ps P n$ reduces
modulo $E$ to the recurrence for the sequence $n$. Thus
$\ps P n\equiv n\pmod E$. Define
\begin{equation}
h=\frac{k-2n}{E}.
\label{fup:sp:eq:h}
\end{equation}
This is an integer and is strictly positive because $n\ge2$ and
$\ps P n>n$. The actual retained source factor becomes
\begin{equation}
\Nk=k-hE-R=2n-R=\varepsilon.
\label{fup:sp:eq:index-sign}
\end{equation}
Its possible value $-1$ is a factor sign in the complete polynomial,
not a nonpositive supplied witness. No first-index equation or
domain condition has been deleted.

\section{The exact shared main and input projections}
\label{fup:sp:sec:input}
The input root uses $\rho$, while the main root uses $\rho+\sigma$.
Solving two Pell equations separately would not suffice; these
shared projections must also be restored with positive coordinates.

\begin{lemma}[Increasing integral projection quotients]
\label{fup:sp:lem:projection}
With $a_0=A-2$ and $H=4A-5$, set
\[
z_j=\ch A j-a_0\ps A j,\qquad
g_j=\frac{z_j-2^j}{H}.
\]
Then every $g_j$ is integral, $g_0=g_1=0$, $g_2=1$, and
\begin{equation}
g_{j+2}=2Ag_{j+1}-g_j+2^j.
\label{fup:sp:eq:grec}
\end{equation}
For every $j\ge1$, $g_{j+1}>g_j\ge0$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The sequence $z_j$ has initial values $1,2$ and satisfies the Pell
recurrence. The sequence $2^j$ satisfies it modulo $H$, since
\[
2^{j+2}-2A\,2^{j+1}+2^j=-(4A-5)2^j=-H2^j.
\]
Thus $z_j\equiv2^j\pmod H$, proving integrality. Subtracting the
recurrences gives~\eqref{fup:sp:eq:grec}. The value $g_2=1$ follows from
$z_2=4A-1$. If $g_{j+1}>g_j\ge0$, then
\[
g_{j+2}-g_{j+1}=(2A-1)g_{j+1}-g_j+2^j>0.
\]
Induction begins with $g_2>g_1=0$.
\end{proof}

Because $p>I\ge3$, put
\begin{equation}
\rho=g_I,\quad \sigma=g_p-g_I,\quad
\kappa=\ps A I,\quad\mu=\ch A I,\quad
\delta=\frac{\kappa-I}{\D}.
\label{fup:sp:eq:inputchoices}
\end{equation}
The first two are positive integers. As $I$ is odd, the binomial
expansion of the Pell power gives
\[
\ps A I=\sum_{j=0}^{(I-1)/2}
\binom I{2j+1}A^{I-2j-1}\D^j
\equiv IA^{I-1}\equiv I\pmod\D,
\]
using $A^2\equiv1\pmod\D$. Since $\ps A I>I$, $\delta$ is a
positive integer. These choices restore
\begin{align}
\kappa&=I+\delta\D,\\
\mu&=2^I+a_0\kappa+\rho H=W+a_0\kappa+\rho H,\\
D&=2^p+a_0c+g_pH=X+a_0c+(\rho+\sigma)H,\\
\Ni&=\mu^2-\D\kappa^2=1.
\label{fup:sp:eq:restoredinput}
\end{align}
The $W$ here is the actual $C-Z$ because~\eqref{fup:sp:eq:alpha} fixes
$C=W+Z$. The prescribed ordinary input is never changed or
replaced by an independent exponent port.

\section{Positive transport and its matching sign}
\label{fup:sp:sec:transport}
The two CRT branches gave $p\equiv e\pmod t$ and $p>t+e$.
Thus $p-t-e$ is a strictly positive multiple of $t$. Since $q=2^t$,
\[
w=2^{p-t}\equiv2^e\pmod{q-1},\qquad w>2^e.
\]
Set
\begin{equation}
\ttt=1+\frac{(W+Z)(w-2^e)}{q-1}.
\label{fup:sp:eq:ttr}
\end{equation}
This is a strictly positive integer. Using the literal $C=W+Z$ and
$F=(K+2^e)C+1-\varepsilon$,
\begin{align}
\Nt
&=(K+w)C+q-F-\ttt(q-1)\notag\\
&=(K+w)C+q-\bigl((K+2^e)C+1-\varepsilon\bigr)
 -(q-1)-C(w-2^e)\notag\\
&=\varepsilon.
\label{fup:sp:eq:transport-sign}
\end{align}
Combining the first, main, input, index and transport factors yields
\begin{equation}
P_5=1\cdot1\cdot1\cdot\varepsilon\cdot\varepsilon=1.
\label{fup:sp:eq:P5}
\end{equation}
For $m_3=1$, both the index and transport factors are $-1$.
This matched sign is essential to covering that entire fixed-mask
residue class. Both quotients $h$ and $\ttt$ are strictly positive
there. Requiring all five individual factors to be $+1$ would miss
this branch of the all-input proof.

\section{Direct auxiliary completion including even c}
\label{fup:sp:sec:aux}
The repository's conditional extension assumed odd $c$ to combine
conditions modulo $c$ and four. Every tuple here has even $c$.
The actual $R\equiv3\pmod4$ lets us use $R$ itself as the auxiliary
Pell index, with no coprimality assumption on $c$.

\begin{lemma}[All-parity auxiliary completion]
\label{fup:sp:lem:aux}
Let $\D,c,R$ be positive integers with $\D c^2>1$ and
$R\equiv3\pmod4$. Define
\begin{equation}
i=1,\quad S=\D c^2,\quad \Fa=\D c^4+1,\quad
V=\frac{\ch S R}{S},\quad \yaux=\ps S R.
\label{fup:sp:eq:auxchoices}
\end{equation}
Then $V\in\pos$, $V\equiv-R\pmod c$, and
\begin{equation}
\Ua=\frac{V+R\Fa}{c}+1
\label{fup:sp:eq:Uaux}
\end{equation}
is a positive integer. The literal argument $V=c(\Ua-1)-R\Fa$
is restored, with $\Na=1$ and $Q_s=1$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Write $R=2j+1$. There are integer polynomials $Q_j(T)$ satisfying
\begin{align}
\ch S{2j+1}&=S Q_j(S^2),\label{fup:sp:eq:oddpoly}\\
Q_0(T)&=1,\qquad Q_1(T)=4T-3,\notag\\
Q_{j+2}(T)&=(4T-2)Q_{j+1}(T)-Q_j(T),\notag\\
Q_j(0)&=(-1)^j(2j+1).\label{fup:sp:eq:constant}
\end{align}
The first identity and recurrence follow from the first-coordinate
Pell recurrence at increments of two, whose coefficient is
$2\ch S2=4S^2-2$. The powers one and three give the initial
values. Evaluation at zero and induction prove~\eqref{fup:sp:eq:constant}.

Therefore the division defining $V$ is integer-polynomial division.
It is positive because the Pell coordinate is positive.
Since $R\equiv3\pmod4$, $j$ is odd. Because $c\mid S$,
\[
V=Q_j(S^2)\equiv Q_j(0)=-R\pmod c.
\]
Also $\Fa\equiv1\pmod c$, so $V+R\Fa$ is divisible by $c$.
This numerator is positive, proving~\eqref{fup:sp:eq:Uaux} integral and
positive. Rearrangement restores the literal $V$.
Finally the Pell norm at $S$ gives
\[
S^2V^2-(S^2-1)\yaux^2=1,
\]
and $Q_s=\Fa-\D i^2c^4=1$ by definition.
\end{proof}

Apply the lemma to the already constructed actual $\D,c,R$.
No oddness condition on $c$ is invoked. In fact $p$ is divisible
by four in both families, and the recurrence modulo two gives
$\ps A p\equiv p\pmod2$. Hence $c$ is even. The main index $p$
is even whereas the actual $R$ is odd, so $p\ne R$.

The positive main norm also proves that $\Fa$ is nonsquare.
Here $c>1$, $\D>1$ and $D^2-\D c^2=1$, so $D>c$. Directly,
\begin{align}
(cD)^2-\Fa&=c^2-1>0,\\
\Fa-(cD-1)^2&=c(2D-c)>0.
\label{fup:sp:eq:nonsquare}
\end{align}
Thus
\begin{equation}
(cD-1)^2<\Fa<(cD)^2.
\label{fup:sp:eq:nonsquareinterval}
\end{equation}
These are consecutive squares. This describes how the witnesses
fail the literal parent inverse; the language failure itself
already follows from the all-input theorem.

\section{All eighteen witnesses and completion of the proof}
\label{fup:sp:sec:assembly}
The full tuple is listed below in exact source order. No new port is
introduced by these mathematical definitions.
\begin{longtable}{@{}p{0.10\textwidth}p{0.53\textwidth}p{0.28\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
Witness&Definition&Integrality and positivity\\\midrule\endhead
$J$&$(q-1)/(B-1)$ with $q=B^{5^{r-a}}$&Repunit identity\\
$F$&$(K+2^e)(W+Z)+1-\varepsilon$&Lemma~\ref{fup:sp:lem:bounds}\\
$\alpha$&$q-F-2Z-W-\ell x$&Lemma~\ref{fup:sp:lem:bounds}\\
$\ttt$&$1+(W+Z)(w-2^e)/(q-1)$&Section~\ref{fup:sp:sec:transport}\\
$\Fa$&$\D c^4+1$&Lemma~\ref{fup:sp:lem:aux}\\
$h$&$(k-2n)/E$&Section~\ref{fup:sp:sec:ratio}\\
$i$&$1$&Immediate\\
$\Ua$&$(V+R\Fa)/c+1$&Lemma~\ref{fup:sp:lem:aux}\\
$s$&$2^{y-3t}$&Lemma~\ref{fup:sp:lem:bounds}\\
$w$&$2^{p-t}$&Lemma~\ref{fup:sp:lem:bounds}\\
$\tau$&$\ch P n$&Positive Pell coordinate\\
$\eta$&$c-kY$&Lemma~\ref{fup:sp:lem:ratio}\\
$\zeta$&$k-\eta$&Lemma~\ref{fup:sp:lem:ratio}\\
$\yaux$&$\ps S R$&Positive Pell coordinate\\
$Z$&Positive CRT representative&Section~\ref{fup:sp:sec:crt}\\
$\delta$&$(\ps A I-I)/\D$&Section~\ref{fup:sp:sec:input}\\
$\rho$&$g_I$&Lemma~\ref{fup:sp:lem:projection}\\
$\sigma$&$g_p-g_I$&$p>I$ and Lemma~\ref{fup:sp:lem:projection}\\\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{fup:sp:thm:main}]
Choose any sufficiently large $r$ satisfying~\eqref{fup:sp:eq:tsize}.
The applicable CRT branch and Lemma~\ref{fup:sp:lem:bounds} supply positive
outer witnesses and scaling margins. The exact Pell resonance and
bounds give positive $\eta,\zeta,h$ and first/main norms equal to one.
The shared projection lemma and odd input index give positive
$\delta,\rho,\sigma$, restoring both root definitions and the input
norm. The positive transport quotient gives the same sign as the
retained index factor, so $P_5=1$. Lemma~\ref{fup:sp:lem:aux} supplies
the remaining positive coordinates with $\Na=Q_s=1$. The complete
paid identity~\eqref{fup:sp:eq:final} therefore gives $P_{82}=\D(1-1)=0$.

Every supplied coordinate is in the list above. All fixed numerals
and the ordinary input $x$ were held fixed. Infinitely many $r$
satisfy the sizing condition, and
$J=(2^{5^r}-1)/(B-1)$ strictly increases with $r$. Thus the witness
tuples are distinct and infinite. Section~\ref{fup:sp:sec:aux} establishes
even $c$, $p\ne R$, and nonsquare $\Fa$ for every member.
\end{proof}

No numerical mask fixture was used as a genuine compiler in this
argument. No full compiler execution or explicit rejection search
is needed to specialize it to the genuine empty-set compiler.

\section{Consequences and further research questions}
\label{fup:sp:sec:questions}
The earlier repository projection and nonsquare extension were
correct within their stated scope. The all-input construction shows
that their auxiliary freedom can be coupled to the actual packing,
input and transport constraints for every inherited compiler.
Both the matched negative branch and the direct even-$c$ extension
are necessary to cover the full quantifier proved here.

Several questions remain open.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{A repair below the validated baseline.}
Can a different paid arithmetic restriction replace the two deleted
products with enough information to recover soundness at a lower
total count? Restoring exactly $f^2$ and $Tf$ returns to 84 operations,
but no optimality theorem follows. A repair needs a complete source
and a proof on its entire positive existential zero set.
\item \textbf{A different numeral compiler.}
The theorem covers every tuple satisfying the displayed arithmetic
consequences, and hence the full inherited compiler family. A proposal
outside that family must specify a different effective compiler and
prove both language directions. Merely changing a convenient
residue does not provide such a compiler.
\item \textbf{The free-coefficient 83 candidate.}
Its auxiliary structure is different. Does its outer interface admit
a full false positive at a genuinely rejected input, or does another
constraint restore soundness? Report 43's language question is still
open and is not settled by transferring an operation count.
\item \textbf{Sign control.}
Requiring both index and transport factors to equal $+1$ would block
this family's $m_3=1$ branch, but not its $m_3=0,2$ branches.
A sign-based repair therefore needs more than an observation about
the matched negative factors.
\item \textbf{Smaller examples and formal verification.}
A smaller explicit counterexample on an independently certified genuine
compiler slice, or a formalized proof of the quantified construction,
would be useful additional evidence. Neither is needed for the
empty-set specialization. Finite subsystem checks should continue
to be labeled separately from full compiler examples.
\end{enumerate}
These questions are not completed repairs, improved universal bounds,
or novelty claims. The pins identify the object and evidence studied;
they do not constitute a global priority survey.

\section{Replay and the limits of executable evidence}
\label{fup:sp:app:replay}
\subsection{Proof and bounded corroboration}
Sections~\ref{fup:sp:sec:recipe}--\ref{fup:sp:sec:assembly} prove the unbounded
quantifiers. The independent mathematical audit returned PASS after
checking all CRT branches, strict positivity, source-register
restoration, shared projections, the direct even-$c$ auxiliary extension,
and the paid finalizer. Its independent checker provides corroboration,
not a finite proof of the theorem.

The counterfamily checker records all eight source pins, all 82
literal row definitions in order, the 18-witness interface, and the
$45\mathrm M+37\mathrm A$ ledger. Its handwritten sparse polynomial
cuts include the complete 17-term output identity. Its bounded evidence
comprises:
\begin{itemize}
\item 70 exponent-choice residue cases and all five nonzero-branch
$K\bmod5$ cases;
\item 1,683 outer diagnostic cases, 561 per $m_3$ branch, reaching
$t=15{,}625$ and 62,500-bit packed $R$;
\item 21 exact small Pell families, 63 shared input cases, 180
auxiliary extensions including 90 with even $c$, and 150 projection cases.
\end{itemize}
The separately authored independent checker records 70 exponent-control
cases, 2,520 outer diagnostic cases (840 per branch), 18 exact Pell
subsystem fixtures, five exact even-$c$ auxiliary completions, 288
all-parity auxiliary congruences, three source pins, and 19 critical
literal rows. Its outer packed $R$ values reach 12,500 bits.
\begin{writenote}
The two packets are shipped with the shorter prefixes \texttt{19-square-product82-counterfamily-} and \texttt{19-square-product82-audit-} (README). The path-preserving replay below therefore runs only in an extraction of the arrival archive on a POSIX system: on Windows the delivered verifier and \rw{replay.py} build inventory keys with backslashes, and the audit checker writes its receipt with CRLF line ends. At placement both passed once these two portability defects were bypassed in a scratch copy, and the checkers run directly passed in normal and optimized Python with receipts equal to the frozen ones up to CRLF. The programme did not replay these receipts.
\end{writenote}

The large outer fixtures stop at manageable integers such as
$q,R,p,n,y$. They never construct the associated $2^p$ or enormous
Pell coordinates. Their numerical masks satisfy necessary arithmetic
conditions but are not certified genuine compiler outputs.
The small exact Pell fixtures test subsystem lemmas and are not
presented as full genuine compiler counterexamples. The language
conclusion comes from the general proof specialized to the genuine
empty-set compiler.

\subsection{Path-preserving replay}
The two research packets are sibling directories under \code{packets/}:
\begin{center}\small
\code{square-product82-counterfamily-recovered-20261004}\\
\code{square-product82-independent-audit-recovered-20261004}.
\end{center}
The sibling layout matters: the audit checker locates the counterfamily
source relative to its own directory. Renaming a packet or extracting
only a checker breaks that contract. The report wrapper preserves both
names and copies them into a temporary replay directory before running
the checkers. The audit checker's intentional receipt write is thereby
isolated from the frozen release copy; the reproduced receipt must
match the saved receipt.

From any working directory, replace \code{REPORT45} below with the
absolute extracted release path:
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\small]
python3 REPORT45/replay.py
python3 -O REPORT45/replay.py
python3 REPORT45/build_pdf.py --output /tmp/Report45.pdf
python3 REPORT45/verify_release.py
python3 REPORT45/archive_release.py --output /tmp/Report45.zip
\end{Verbatim}
Choose new PDF and ZIP output paths if those files already exist.
The utilities preserve existing outputs rather than silently overwriting
them. Standard-library Python is sufficient for replay, inventory
verification and archive construction. Rebuilding the PDF additionally
requires the recorded \code{pdflatex} toolchain and packages.

The wrapper checks packet hashes, runs each inspected independent
checker normally and with \code{-O}, verifies the saved receipts,
and checks all packet bytes again afterward. Upstream Python is
authenticated as bytes only; the saved 82-row arithmetic schedule
is never imported or interpreted. No compiler recipe, physical
experiment, repository write, or public publication is part of replay.

The LaTeX build uses a fixed source epoch and suppresses volatile PDF
metadata. The archive uses sorted paths, fixed timestamps, fixed
permissions, and fixed compression settings. Byte-identical PDF and ZIP
reproduction is checked with the recorded toolchain. Different TeX or
compression versions may change rendering bytes without changing the
mathematics or source pins. The final release records the independent
manuscript review, all-page visual inspection and isolated replay;
temporary page images and TeX intermediates are omitted from the compact
ZIP.

\fupstart{46}{Part VIII: Report 46}
\fupsourcehead{Report 46. Exact certificate height and an even-rank obstruction}
\noindent\wtag\ Source~20 of this report, manuscript~08 of batch~88: \emph{Research Report 46}, author line ``Research Report 46'', dated 4 October 2026; archive \rw{Exact_Certificate_Height_and_an_Even_Rank_Obstruction_Package.zip} (arrival commit \texttt{c5612efa1}, placed by \texttt{992aaafb3}); pins \texttt{6ce2dcaaf} (square/product 82), \texttt{0d9d1e017} (free-coefficient 83) and \texttt{8cf6239b6} (the tree's prime-index collapse). Its sections, statements and equations are numbered 46.$k$ here.
\begin{sourceabstract}
We quantify the square/product82 counterfamily constructed in Report45.
For every fixed outer tuple in that family, the displayed auxiliary
completion uniquely minimizes the maximum of all 18 positive supplied
witnesses, even when every positive auxiliary multiplier is allowed.
Its largest coordinate is $\psi_{\D c^2}(R)$, with exact bit length
$(R-1)(2p(p+y+1)-1)+1$. This is an exact cubic in the actual packed
index $R$, with leading coefficient $4/3$ or $25/14$. An 18-coordinate
ledger, uniform error bounds, and compiler/input bounds retain the
power-of-five radix jumps. The numerical witness height is doubly
exponential in the radix exponent, not triply exponential. A separately
audited extension gives a convergent all-orders forward log-height
formula, a convergent algebraic inverse, an explicit first exponential
inverse correction, and a convergent all-orders Lagrange inverse
algorithm with a geometric tail bound.

Separately, no even-main-rank outer tuple with $A\ge4$ even,
$c=\psi_A(p)$ for even $p\ge2$, odd $R$, and retained factor product
$P_5=1$ extends to a zero of the literal free-coefficient83 interface.
The proof covers all positive auxiliary variables, both norm signs,
and nonsquarefree $\D=A^2-1$. We prove the required small-norm descent
and the correct underlying Pell-lattice reduction. These results
do not minimize over different outer tuples. The even-rank obstruction
is compatible with a separately audited odd-prime-index collapse of
the unchanged inherited free83 recipe. Frozen source packets,
independent audits, inspected
bounded replay, and reproducible LaTeX accompany the proofs.
\end{sourceabstract}
\begin{writenote}
\emph{What it imports.} The source's ``Report45'' is Report~45 above (Sections~45.$k$) and its ``Report43'' is Report~43 (Sections~43.$k$). Section~\ref{fup:eh:sec:setup} restates the construction of Sections~\ref{fup:sp:sec:recipe}--\ref{fup:sp:sec:assembly} as a recap, and imports Theorem~\ref{fup:sp:thm:main} as a proved result; Theorem~\ref{fup:eh:thm:free} is about the free-coefficient interface~\eqref{fup:fc:eq:full} of Report~43. The ``Report41'' it distinguishes is Part~\ref{fup:part:fd}. The certificates whose height is measured belong to a chart that is already refuted; the programme's review calls the result ``controlled witness-size information for one already-refuted chart''.

\emph{The programme's review} (\rw{review_incoming_arithmetic_reports_39_46.md}, commit \texttt{5d8889017}) read the full even-rank proof, its source notes, the height proof and the auxiliary-minimum proof. It finds that the proof of Theorem~\ref{fup:eh:thm:free} correctly passes to the fundamental Pell unit (Lemma~\ref{fup:eh:lem:lattice}) ``rather than incorrectly assuming every strong solution is a power of $A+\sqrt\Delta$'', that the theorem is scoped to a fixed outer tuple and does not contradict the tree's odd-prime counterfamily, ``which changes the outer tuple'', and it summarizes Theorems~\ref{fup:eh:thm:height} and~\ref{fup:eh:thm:min} (11 exact lengths and 7 upper bounds; $8\mid c$ and $0<R<c/2$ in the minimality argument). On growth it agrees with the source: the height is doubly exponential in $t$ and, eventually for a fixed compiler, in the numerical input $x$, not in the binary length of $x$. \emph{The analytic logarithm and inverse expansions of Sections~\ref{fup:eh:sec:expansion} and~\ref{fup:eh:sec:allinverse} and their claimed precision were not reviewed}; they rest on the source's own independent audits, shipped as \rw{20-cert-height-expansion-audit-AUDIT.md} and \rw{20-cert-height-inverse-audit-AUDIT.md}.

\emph{Same lemma twice.} Lemma~\ref{fup:eh:lem:smallnorm} is Lemma~\ref{fup:fc:lem:smallnorm} of Report~43, with $H_0$ written $H$; Report~46 restates and proves it so that its theorem is self-contained, and credits Report~43. Its macro \verb|\bl| (bit length, $\bl(z)=\lfloor\log_2z\rfloor+1$) is kept; it is the function written $\bits$ in Parts~I and~II. Table~\ref{fup:cand:tab:letters} lists its other letters.
